After: Ilvane +9.2, Quill +7.9, Maren +6.2, Obsidia +5.9, the rest unchanged. Maren is now a woman named **Valeria** (first Ashencourte, then Valeria, Robert 2026-10-24) (display name only; the id stays `knight` so saves and art keys keep working; the model got ash-pale hair). Ragnar's trait (about 0) is still open. The "Glasswaste" realm gets a darker name (options sent to Robert, not renamed yet).
149. **The Glasswaste is now The Hollow Crown (Robert, 2026-10-24: "something dark and sinister", picked from eight options) →** realm display name only (id `starfall`, venue `glass` unchanged); Obsidia is "Spellblade of the Hollow Crown". Older entries (#140) keep the old name as history.
150. **Valeria has real art (Robert, 2026-10-24: "her name is now going to be Valeria") →** the knight (id `maren`) is **Valeria**, and her hero figure is the standing pose from Robert's own concept sheet (`assets-src/valeria`, cut with `tools/pack-valeria.ps1` into `figures/hero-knight.png`, art key `hero:knight`; the procedural model stays as the fallback). The sheet looks AI-generated: Steam asks for AI disclosure on shipped content, so decide before the Steam release. The sheet's four attack-animation rows are too small to use (about 120px tall); a bigger sheet would let her lunge and strike with real frames.
151. **Three Legendaries per Riftwalker, for good (Robert, 2026-10-24: "go back down to 3 ... only keep 3 going forward") →** the 0.12.0 set of five (#144) is cut to **three**: each hero keeps the two they had before 0.12.0 plus one of the three added then (a payoff skill or attack, so the three are not all Powers): Bastion of Dawn, Drone Apotheosis, Bloodmoon Hunt, The Long Ambush, Ten Thousand Cuts, The Spirit Council, Eye of the Storm, Shatterstorm, Grave Harvest, The Twelve-Gauge Opera. The other 20 are deleted (nothing else referenced them). The deck limit of two Legendaries stays.
152. **Ragnar is Sehra again (Robert, 2026-10-24: "rename ragnar back to Sehra since shes a girl now") →** the stance hero (id `sehra`) is displayed as **Sehra**; her new art is a woman. Display name only (`RIFTWALKERS.sehra.hero.name`).
153. **The assassin is Sylas (Robert, 2026-10-24: "quill ... almost sounds like a bow and arrow character") →** the knife-showman hero (id `assassin`, new art is a man) is displayed as **Sylas** (was Scarlet; `Quill` in the docs). Display name only.
154. **The basic cards are Attack and Defend (Robert, 2026-10-24: "all be the exact same. they should just say Attack or Defend") →** every Riftwalker's starter already shared one Neutral Strike card (6 damage) and one Defend card (5 Barrier); they are now **named** Attack and Defend (were Slash and Guard; ids `strike` and `defend` unchanged). Event and prelude text that said Slash or Guard now says Attack or Defend. Hero-specific starters (Dawn Smite, Quick Jab...) are separate cards and stay.
155. **Snares are Traps (Robert, 2026-10-24: "change Snare cards to be Trap cards instead, it makes more sense") →** every player-facing word changes (card kind label, tips, Learn page, event text, card names like Hollow Trap and Trap Net, the Traps lane). Identifiers stay (`kind: 'snare'`, `fireSnare`, card ids) so saves and the engine are untouched; older entries in this file keep the old word.
156. **Real card art, by sheet (Robert, 2026-10-24: "another prompt to have ai generate our card art") →** `pnpm cardprompts` writes `docs/card-art/<hero>.md`: nine cards to a sheet (3 x 3), best cards first, anchored on the Valeria image; `tools/pack-cards.ps1` cuts `assets-src/cards/<sheet>.png` into `public/art/cards/<id>.jpg` (found by the navy gutters) and lists them in the manifest, which `realCardArt` already reads. A card with no picture keeps its painted placeholder, so sheets can arrive in any order. Also: cards in the hand are about 10% smaller (`handCardWidth`), a flyer's head never rises above 640 stage px, and a next-move icon never goes above the screen.
157. **Valeria's third lane is Devotion (Robert, 2026-10-24: "change the knights 'sanctified' build to a different name") →** the heal-and-Shielded lane was called Sanctified, which also names the Sanctum event that re-rolls a deck (`sanctify.ts`, kept). Lane text, pool headings, `tools/src/lanes.ts` and the roster doc now say **Devotion**. Name only.
158. **Figures never overlap, and errors can be copied (Robert, 2026-10-24: "make sure theres no overlap of the characters"; "we also need to be able to copy paste that text") →** each side is laid out from the figures' measured widths (`fight/lineLayout.ts`, tested): left to right, a flyer or a figure on a different height may tuck 20% into its neighbour, and if a side still does not fit every figure on it shrinks (down to 55%) instead of overlapping. The fight and run error screens show the error in a selectable box with a Copy button (message, build, browser). A page left open across a deploy (\"Failed to fetch dynamically imported module\") reloads itself once instead of failing.
159. **Card art in batches, commons stay painted (Robert, 2026-10-24: "basic cards can be hand drawn for now, maybe we just do that for all common cards") →** `pnpm cardprompts` now writes AI sheets only for Uncommon, Rare, Legendary and Ancestral cards (676 cards, 81 sheets); Basic and Common cards keep their painted placeholder until they are hand-drawn. The prompts come as `docs/card-art/batch-NN.md` (batch 01 = the first sheet of every character: Legendary and best Rares; batch 02 = the second sheet of each; ...), pasted one prompt at a time, because pasting a whole file was too much for the image AI.
160. **A run is three stages (Robert, 2026-10-24: "change the campaign to be 3 stages that are a bit longer") →** was five (four realms and a one-floor Vorthax stage). Now: **Stage 1 and 2** are two of the four realms (shuffled by the seed), **seven floors + that realm's Warden** each (was five); **Stage 3 is the Rift**: **eight floors** drawing on the two unvisited realms, always a Shrine and a Merchant before **Vorthax**. Elites may reach three per stage on the longer maps. The enemy ramp is `STAGE_SCALE` 82/86, 98/98, 114/110 (HP/damage %, was five steps from 80/85 to 130/125; measured with `pnpm runsim -- --n 40`: 37% of bot runs cleared Normal against 42% for the five-stage run, within the noise of about 8 points). The two unvisited realms' Wardens are not fought in the main run for now. `FINAL_STAGE = 3` is the one constant. Saves from the old five-stage runs are accepted at stage 1-3 and rejected beyond; re-measure with `pnpm runsim`.
161. **PvP, first version (Robert, 2026-10-24: "simple match making system and allow players to battle with decks that they've banked") →** built as in `docs/11_PVP.md`: a Cloudflare Worker with two Durable Objects (`apps/server`): a **Matchmaker** (the queue; closest Rift Tier first, anyone after 20 s) and one **MatchRoom** per code (friend codes and queue matches share it). The room is a referee: both browsers run the real engine from the same seed, a player sends only their move, the room checks turn, legality and move number with the engine and tells both the accepted move plus a state hash. The pure rules (deck check, queue, `Match`) live in `packages/pvp` with tests (every Riftwalker finishes a PvP match against another). **Banked decks** are kept in a Vault in the browser (`apps/web/src/pvp/vault.ts`, 5 slots, a full Vault asks which to give up); every Riftwalker's starting deck is also always offered for testing. Turn clock 75 s, two missed turns forfeit, 60 s to reconnect. Seats are drawn by lot. Known limit: each browser holds the whole state, so hidden information is not hidden yet (the server still decides every move); fixing it means the room keeping the state and sending each player only `viewFor`. No accounts or ratings yet.
162. **Who goes first in PvP (Robert, 2026-10-24: ask both players; going second gives +10 HP; same answers get a coin flip) →** once both players are in a room each is asked *go first* or *go second (+10 HP)* (20 s). Different answers are granted; the same answer, or no answer, is settled by the server's coin, shown to both players as a coin that spins and lands on the portrait and name of the player who goes first. Whoever goes second always starts with `SECOND_PLAYER_HP` = 10 more HP and max HP (shared `setupFor`), on top of the engine's existing second-player +1 Mana and +1 card. Measured with `pnpm pvpbalance 6` (bot vs bot, starter decks): moving first won 57% before, 48% after (moving second 52%), so the trade is close to even. Simultaneous turns were considered and set aside for now.
163. **PvP is called the Arena (Robert, 2026-10-24) →** the title-screen button, the lobby heading and the Vault text say Arena; the route is `#/arena` (`#/pvp` still works). Code keeps `pvp` in its names (`packages/pvp`, `apps/server`); the painted battlefields (`duel/scene/arena.ts`) are unrelated scenery and keep their name.
164. **The Vault is saved online, under a Vault code (Robert, 2026-10-24: "are players able to save decks in a database ... and see them in the duel screen?" / "yes") →** banked decks are kept in the browser and also on the server, so they show in the Arena on any device that has the player's **Vault code**: a random 16-character code made on first need, kept in localStorage, shown (and copyable) in the Arena. There are no accounts; the code is the only key (decision #161's "no accounts" still holds). Stored in a **Durable Object per code** (SQLite table, named by a hash of the code) rather than in the D1 database `shattered-rifts-db`: it needs no migration run by hand, a wrong binding cannot break the Workers Build, and nothing here needs a cross-player query yet. The server re-checks every deck with the same rules as a match (`checkEntry`, 5 slots). The browser copy still works if the server is down. Loading another code replaces the browser's list with that Vault's. Limit: a deck removed while offline returns at the next sync.
165. **Sign in with Google, no personal data (Robert, 2026-10-24: "real sign in, just google or discord ... save decks associated with their account ... i dont want any personal data") →** Google first (Discord later). OAuth in the Worker (`apps/server/src/auth.ts`), scope `openid` only; the only thing kept is `SHA-256("google:" + sub)`, which names the account's Vault Durable Object. The session is an HttpOnly signed cookie, so sign-in works on shatteredrifts.app only (the GitHub Pages copy stays guest). The Vault Code remains for guests. Secrets live in Cloudflare, never in the repo. No ratings or profiles yet.
166. **Arena decks are never lost (Robert, 2026-10-24: "players dont lose their decks if they lose, they keep them forever unless they decide to go further in the campaign") →** a banked deck is a snapshot in the Vault (`seed:tier`). Nothing in a match changes the Vault: a match is played with a copy, so a loss, a forfeit or a disconnect cost nothing. A deck leaves the Vault only when the player removes it, or gives it up to bank a new one into a full Vault. Choosing **Continue** after banking starts the next Rift Tier with the live deck, which can change or be lost in the run; the banked snapshot stays as it was banked (the next bank is a separate entry, `seed:t<tier+1>`). Ratings or rewards that change decks are not planned.
167. **The Arena needs a banked deck (Robert, 2026-10-24: "get rid of the starter decks for arena ... players need to have saved decks to play") →** the starting decks offered for testing (decision #161) are removed from the Arena screen. With an empty Vault it shows why and links to a New Run. The server still accepts any legal deck (it cannot tell a banked deck from a built one), so this is a rule of the screen, not a security check; bot tests use `pnpm pvpsmoke` and the engine tests directly.
168. **Save a deck after each stage, in test builds only (Robert, 2026-10-24: "include option 2 for now but the real game wont have that, it will only be once players have cleared a tier") →** the Stage cleared screen (after stage 1 and 2) offers "Save this deck for the Arena": a copy goes into the Vault (id `seed:t<tier>:s<stage>`, shown as "after stage N") and the run carries on unchanged. It is behind one flag, `MID_RUN_SAVES` in `run/MidRunSave.tsx`, and is to be removed for release; the real rule stays decision #166 and the Bank at a cleared Rift Tier. It uses the normal five slots, and a full Vault says so.
169. **The test-build save is a run-bar button, once, after stage 1 (Robert, 2026-10-24: "replace the export run button with the save button ... only available after stage 1 ... right before starting the next stage") →** supersedes the Stage cleared panel of #168: the run bar's **Save deck** replaces Export run (Export returns when `MID_RUN_SAVES` is false) and is enabled only on the Stage 1 cleared screen (`canSaveForArena`); at any other time it is greyed out with a hint. The Export button on the banked screen is unchanged.
170. **A deck viewer, and Boons saved with the deck (Robert, 2026-10-24: "a deck viewer ... like team fight tactics post game report ... sorted by card rarity descending ... dated ... also need to show boons saved on the deck") →** a Saved decks page (`#/decks`) and a short list in the Arena (see `docs/11_PVP.md`). Decks are unnamed, dated and sorted by rarity descending with small stacked cards. A saved deck now carries the run's Boons (`VaultDeck.boons`, also stored by the server, added to old Vaults by an `ALTER TABLE` on first use); they are shown only: **PvP matches do not apply Boons** (decision #161 and docs/03 say Boons never go into PvP), so whether they should is a design question for Robert. The limit on saved decks stays at 5 and is now shown on both screens; the Arena no longer removes decks (only the Saved decks page, with a confirmation).
171. **Boons are in effect in the Arena, and the Vault holds 10 decks (Robert, 2026-10-24: "lets keep boons in pvp and limit 10 decks per player") →** reverses "Boons never go into PvP" (docs/03, decision #170's note). A banked or saved deck carries its run's Boons into a match: `Entry.boons` (checked in `checkEntry`: only real Boon ids, no repeats, at most 8; unknown ids are left out, not refused), passed to both seats by `setupFor`; the engine already applied Boons to any hero side. A test plays every Boon through a whole match. Boons are run rewards, so a deck with better Boons is stronger: a balance question for `pnpm pvpbalance` and the matchmaker's tier pairing, not yet measured. Vault slots go from 5 to 10 (`V2_DECK_RULES.vaultSlots`, also `DECK_RULES` in the campaign).
172. **Where the test-build save works (Robert, 2026-10-24: "before the start of a new stage that is beyond stage 1, except if its further than tier 1, then stage 1 tier X is ok") →** supersedes #169's "stage 1 only". `savePoint` in `run/MidRunSave.ts`: allowed on the Stage cleared screen after stage 1 or 2 (the next stage is beyond stage 1), and on a Rift Tier beyond the first at the Waymark just before its stage 1 begins (right after Continue). Never mid-stage, and not at the start of tier 1. A copy's id ends `:s<N>` (N = stages cleared; 0 = start of a tier), shown as "after stage N" or "at the start". Still test-only (`MID_RUN_SAVES`).
173. **Pocket a deck and park the run (Robert, 2026-10-24: "save a deck and choose to continue the campaign ... you pocket the deck for later, go play other runs ... come back to this one and continue the campaign and it might be lost"; "save at the end of a run and at the very start of the new tier, both"; up to 3 runs) →** Bank no longer ends a run. On the cleared-tier screen **Save this deck** copies the deck into the Vault (id `seed:t<tier>`) and the run stays; then **Continue** (next tier, the live deck at risk, the saved copy untouched, as in #166) or **Leave it for now**. The run bar's **Save deck** works at that screen and at the start of the next tier before its stage 1 (`savePoint`, `run/deckSave.ts`); the between-stage points stay test-only (#168, #172). A **Home** button leaves any run parked. A player keeps up to **3** unfinished runs (`RUN_SLOTS`, `run/saves.ts`; an older single save becomes the first slot); the home page lists them with Continue and Abandon, and starting a fourth run is blocked until one is finished or abandoned. The engine's `bank()` and the Banked screen remain only for saves made before this change. Each run keeps to its own slot (the run screen remembers the slot id and only writes there), so two windows cannot overwrite each other's runs; an abandoned run is not brought back; nothing is ever evicted to make room, and a new or imported run is refused when all 3 slots are taken (slots that cannot be read still count and are kept). The duel-left-unfinished marker is kept per run.
174. **Playtest round (Robert, 2026-10-24) →** (a) A Riftwalker's three card **lanes** are now called **Builds** in everything the player reads (code keeps the word lane). (b) Merchant: a hold can be let go (`releaseHold`): a card held at this visit goes back on sale at full price, one waiting from an earlier visit is turned down; the deposit is not refunded. (c) The map never has two Merchants in a row on any path (the earlier becomes a Duel). (d) The choice at the start of a run now says what chance decided (which card was upgraded, added, removed, which Boon or Potion), and results show in green while refusals stay red. (e) Stances show as a banner and a coloured aura on the character (Radiant adds rays and sparks). (f) A card's hover explains every status or keyword named in its rules text, and what those explanations name in turn (Fervor explains Radiant). (g) Event and Waymark options are written as full sentences ("a random card in your deck is upgraded"). (h) The owner icon on a card is smaller and sits bottom right.
175. **Second playtest round (Robert, 2026-10-24) →** (a) A merchant can hold up to **3** cards for later (`MAX_HELD`), not one; older saves with one held card load as a list. (b) A card that summons can be played with every companion slot taken: the player names the companion that **leaves** (`play.release`); it simply leaves: a release is not a death, so no "when an ally dies" effect fires and it is not remembered for Revive. The server passes `release` through. (c) Aiming: while a target is wanted, only the figures that can be chosen are hit-tested, with a roomier reach, and a click that misses keeps the card chosen (right-click, Esc or choosing another card lets go). (d) The Obsidian Blade's sharpness shows as a counter beside the resources and a gold tag on every sharpened card. (e) **Grave Harvest** (Legendary): Doom 12 → 18, and its upgrade costs 1 (was 2). (f) The last node before a Warden is **always a Shrine** (every node on that floor); a stage with no Merchant by chance gets one in the middle floors, so a Merchant is still guaranteed.
176. **Third playtest round: log, phones, notices (Robert, 2026-10-24) →** (a) The fight screen has a **battle log** (right side, above End turn; scrollable; folded by default on a phone): events become short lines as they play (`fight/logLines.ts`, pure), noise left out. (b) A card whose cost is not the printed one shows it on the gem: green with a down arrow when cheaper, red with an up arrow when dearer. (c) **Messages are green when good, red when bad**: run results and refusals, the fight banners (your turn / your Trap green; enemy turn, a refused play, reconnecting red), Vault-code notes, the save note. (d) **Phones**: landscape is the way to fight (portrait shows a turn-your-phone overlay); below 600px tall or 700px wide the root font and the hand shrink (`handCardWidth(w, h)`); on touch a first tap lifts a card and shows it big with its notes beside it, the second tap plays it; the Card library book is sized to the room left in the window and can never be wider than it; the run page can no longer be pushed wider than the screen. Released-companion rule changed: releasing is not a death (#175b).
177. **The levels are named Rift Tier > Stage > Floor (Robert, 2026-10-24: tried "Floors" for the tiers, then "Rooms", then "lets go back to Rift Tiers > Stage > Floor") →** a **Rift Tier** is one trip through the three stages ("Rift Tier 2 · Stage 1/3"); a **Stage** is one realm's map; a **Floor** is a row of nodes on a stage map ("Floor 4/9"). Nothing changed in the end: the names are as they were before this round. The code keeps `tier` and `floor` as the field names.
178. **Weekly and monthly leaderboards (Robert, 2026-10-24: "weekly and monthly leaderboard for pvp and run progression"; pvp sorts by most wins, the campaign by how far each got) →** an Arena board (wins, win-loss record, the characters played) and a campaign board (furthest run, in stages cleared), each for the current week (Monday UTC) and month (the 1st, UTC). Storage is a new Durable Object (`Leaderboard`, migration v3; Robert chose this over D1), names are the typed Arena name, campaign progress is trusted from the browser for now (Robert's choice; server replay-verification is a later step), and farming is limited (3 counted wins per winner/opponent pair per day). Builds are not shown yet. Details in docs/11_PVP.md.

179. **Ghost battles (Robert, 2026-10-24: "a players deck that is piloted by AI ... from a deck that has been entered into the arena ... upper half of wins ... a backup matchup if there are no active players ... after 20 seconds they are offered a fight against a ghost ... monitor the leaderboards of the ghosts ... how many wins players have against ghosts"; and "players should also be able to queue up for a ghost match from the arena screen") →** done as described in docs/11_PVP.md. Decisions made on the way: ghost matches are played **on the server** (the room runs the ghost's moves), not in the browser, so the results are trustworthy; a ghost's strength is its wins as a person's deck plus as a ghost; ghosts do not count on the Arena board (kept separate, with their own boards); the pilot is a new beam-search bot, not the greedy one. Not done: matching a ghost's Rift Tier to the player's, and ghosts for Boons not in the game any more (a deck that no longer passes the deck check is simply not played).

180. **Leaderboards as tables, top 100, with your own place (Robert, 2026-10-24: "I prefer the table layout with a max of 100, and would like to show where the logged in player is") →** every board is a table of up to 100 (`BOARD_SIZE`); a player the server knows (Vault code or account) also gets their rank and line, shown after a gap if they are below the 100th. The server computes the full ranking and cuts it, via POST `/pvp/board/view`. Ghost matches keep their own boards.

181. **Sanctified cards are permanent, and the Sanctum happens once (Robert, 2026-10-24) →** a sanctified card (`hi~`/`lo~`) can no longer be removed (Shrine or Merchant), and the Sanctum event cannot come up once a run has sanctified (`RunState.sanctified`, or a sanctified card in the deck). Also fixed: a sanctified card's picture fell back to the painted placeholder because the art lookup did not strip the `hi~`/`lo~` prefix.

182. **A collapsed run loses its saved Arena deck (Robert, 2026-10-24) →** when a run ends in defeat, every deck saved from that run is removed from the Vault and the online copy (`loseSavedDecks`). Banking the run keeps it. This makes saving a snapshot you must protect, in line with the power-plateau goal.

183. **Arena HP is tripled (Robert, 2026-10-24) →** both heroes (players and ghosts) have `ARENA_HP_MULT = 3` times their usual HP; the second seat's bonus is tripled too (10 to 30). Matches will run longer: re-measure `pnpm ghostsim` and `pnpm pvpbalance` (open).

184. **No third-party art (Robert, 2026-10-24: "remove the dependency on those in the credits entirely if we have alternatives") →** the enemy figures were already the AI redo sheets, so the Tania Herrero, Maaot and Jitsu-Koan credits were stale; the cave and moss venue code and packs were unused behind the painted arenas and are deleted; rarity gems and the card back are drawn in CSS (Dumivid's pack removed); the fire, frost and portal sprites became coloured pulses (SovDigital removed). `docs/ASSET_REQUESTS.md` lists optional replacements.

185. **Upgrade pass (Robert, 2026-10-24: "some have been simply underwhelming") →** the generic rule gives big stacks two (a status of 5 or more, Foresee 4 or more) instead of one; five legendaries have hand-written upgrades (The Last Oath, The Last Toll, Deck of a Thousand Knives, Overclocked Legion, The Spirit Council). `pnpm upaudit` lists every card's upgrade with weak ones flagged, for the next pass.

186. **Profanity filter on names (Robert, 2026-10-25) →** every player name goes through `cleanName` (`packages/pvp/src/deck.ts`) on the server and in the shared code: an abusive name (disguises like f.u.c.k, sh1t, fuuuck included; strong stems anywhere, short words only as whole words; Scunthorpe and the like pass) is replaced with "Riftwalker". It covers Arena entries and the leaderboards. The word lists are short and easy to extend (`STEMS`, `WORDS`, `INNOCENT`).

187. **Event pass: 70 events, Rift-crossed trades, a two-part story, a coin for chances, and elite trial events (Robert, 2026-10-25: "get the number of events to 70 ... choosing from rift crossed cards, or sacrifice something ... bad ones just like in slay the spire 2"; "re-use the coin-flip UI we added to arena"; "elite events where a mini game can be opt-in ... 10% for now") →** (a) 20 new events (`eventsMore.ts`, data only): choose 1 of 3 Rift-crossed cards for HP or a Static card, trade cards or a Boon or max HP for a Rift-crossed rare, a gamble for one, a buried map that comes back later as a much better dig (`RunState.marks`), and bad ones (hollow feast, tax collector with no walk-away, parasite, cursed forge, sundered bridge). New deal steps: crossed, card, static, loseBoon, losePotion, exchange, boonOf, potionOf, mark; events can be gated on the run (`needs`) and can lay several cards on the table (`cardOffers`). (b) Any chance in an event (`chance` steps, the gambler, the well) is recorded in `RunState.eventFlips` and shown by the client as the Arena coin (`run/ChanceCoin.tsx`) before the result. The rules still decide; the coin only plays it. (c) Elite events (`trials.ts`, `ELITE_EVENT_CHANCE` = 10% of events): an opt-in puzzle for a choice of 3 prizes (Legendary cards, Rift-crossed rares, Boons, rare potions, or a bounty of max HP, upgrades, shards, thinning); a loss pays 25 shards. Six mini-games in `apps/web/src/trials` (Heartbeat Gate, Cipher Door, Archivist's Test, Wanderer's Ledger, Mechanical Maze, Brass Cascade), each a pure seeded puzzle in `logic.ts`. The client reports win or lose (the campaign is single-player; the Arena only takes a deck, so this is accepted). One try only (Robert, 2026-10-25: "they cant re-try it and it just moves on to the next room"): pressing Begin saves the trial as begun (`startTrial`, state `playing`); a run loaded in that state (closed page, dropped connection, reopened or imported run) is settled by `settleInterrupted`: the trial counts as failed, with no consolation, and the node is cleared. Practice page: `#/trials` (linked from Practice). Open: sounds are minimal, balance of win rates and prize value needs playtesting; fights as event rewards are not built.

188. **Trial games reworked (Robert, 2026-10-25: "the only ones i like are the cipher door ... and the heartbeat game ... the other games are tedious") →** Echoes, Mirror Shards and Rune Lock are removed (a run saved mid-way through one is given the Cipher Door). The Cipher Door marks every rune of a guess (✓ right place, ↔ wrong place, ✕ not in the code). The Heartbeat Gate's gold narrows with the Rift Tier (`goldScale`: 6% per tier, floor 45%; `TrialOffer.tier`). New: two quizzes (a card shown without its name: pick the name; a card shown: pick whose it is; 5 right wins, a 4th wrong loses), a mouse maze (13x9, clock 30-70 s set from the shortest path, starts on the first step) and the Brass Cascade (drop 3 balls, nudge them with 6 nudges, 10 points wins). The coin is never used for elite events. Open: tune win rates by playtest.

189. **Elite events are all the Brass Cascade (Robert, 2026-10-25: "lets just use brass cascade for all elite events ... a player can see all possible rewards ... they can only choose 1 of the 3 that they got") →** supersedes #188: the Heartbeat Gate, Cipher Door, quizzes and maze are removed (a run saved in front of one finds an empty place). An elite event is a board of pegs with ten lanes, each lane showing its prize (`TrialOffer.prizes`, ids p0..p9, laid out by one of three layouts so places differ; the edges hold the best). The player drops three balls from the top of the board, from anywhere along it (mouse, touch or held keys, never in steps), the ball bounces freely (real 2D physics in `apps/web/src/trials/physics.ts`, fixed 240 Hz steps, drawn smoothly), they may nudge it 6 times in all, and every lane a ball lands in is a prize earned: they choose one (`TrialOffer.earned`, `finishTrial(run, won, earned)`, `claimTrialPrize`). One try only still holds (#188). The coin is never used. On the practice screen the page is labelled Elite Events. Open: lane prizes and nudge counts need playtesting.

190. **Riftwalker ids are the class (Robert, 2026-10-25: "change all of that to the character class like knight or barbarian as the internal ids ... rename any image file names ... keep current character names visible to the players ... cosmetic purchases and gender purchases") →** `HeroId` is now knight, engineer, hunter, trapper, assassin, totemist, barbarian, spellblade, necromancer, drunkard (each is the character's `role`; a test checks it), so a display name or gender can change without touching an id. Renamed with them: the pool files (`pools/knight.ts`...), the hero figures (`art/figures/hero-knight.png`...), the card-art sheets (`docs/card-art/knight.md`, sheet names `knight-01`, and the local `assets-src/heroes` and `assets-src/cards`), the manifest keys (`hero:knight`), CSS classes (`card--hero-knight`), pool constants (`KNIGHT`) and tests. Card ids are unchanged. Older entries in this log keep the old ids as they were written (maren, quill...). **Compatibility:** `canonicalHero` / `LEGACY_HERO_IDS` (`@sr/content/v2`) turn an old id into the new one wherever one can still arrive: a run save (character, and a visiting merchant), an Arena entry or Vault deck (`checkEntry`), the remembered choice (`heroChoice`), and the server (new writes are converted; the Leaderboard Durable Object updates old rows to the new ids when it starts). An old ghost's id (a hash that included the hero) can appear once more under the new id; harmless. Not changed on purpose: display names, English words in prose, test seed strings.

191. **Every Rift-crossed rare is a choice of three (Robert, 2026-10-25) →** the Brass Cascade's Rift-crossed lane (`TrialPrize.choices`) and every event that gives a Rift-crossed rare (the rift broker, tithe stone, torn grimoire, pale dealer, the buried-map dig) now lay out three different rares from other classes and let the player pick one. New deal step `crossedPick`: it sets `event.choose` and the event waits (the node is not cleared) until `pickEventCard(run, index)`; `chooseEvent` is refused meanwhile. Rift-crossed commons and uncommons from events are still a random card.

192. **A lane two balls land in pays again, differently (Robert, 2026-10-25) →** `TrialOffer.earned` now has one entry per ball (a lane twice is listed twice; a third ball in the same lane adds nothing). Every lane has a second prize built with the first (`TrialOffer.seconds`, ids q0..q9): the same kind but a different card, Boon, potion or set of three Rift-crossed rares (a purse, a rest and the like simply come again). A lane with two balls offers both its prizes, and you still choose one of everything earned. The board marks the lane ×2 and the prize screen labels the second prize. Assumption to confirm with Robert: still one pick in all, rather than taking both prizes.

193. **First visit showed the old placeholder figures (a friend, 2026-10-25) →** `preloadArt` gave up after 2 seconds, but a first visit has to download about 8 MB of hero figures (24 MB of figures in all), so the first screen was drawn with the generated placeholders; a refresh found the files cached and was in time. Now: the manifest and the hero figures load first (`preloadArt`, waits up to 15 s, with an "Opening the rift" line in `index.html` meanwhile), the enemy and companion figures load next (`preloadAllArt`, which a fight waits for, up to 10 s), and if the heroes arrive after the wait the home screen redraws itself (`ART_READY_EVENT`; only at home, so no game is interrupted). Open: the hero PNGs are 700-950 KB each; converting them to WebP would roughly halve a first visit.

194. **Punchier hits, and the stance banner clears the potions (Robert, 2026-10-25, from friends) →** (a) `attackHit` and `hit` in `audio/sfx.ts` are rebuilt: a kick-style low thump (a sine falling from about 175 Hz to 40 Hz, 2 ms attack, soft-clipped), a sharp noise crack and a crunch of body; the master now runs through a compressor so the bass is loud without clipping. By ear only: tune by playtest. (b) The stance banner (`StanceAura`) measures itself against the potion belt after each draw and slides right, just far enough to clear it, when a small screen puts them together. Open: background music (needs a source: licensed tracks, commissioned, or AI-generated with a commercial licence; the player would be a separate music volume, crossfaded per screen, loaded after the first screen).

195. **A reload resumes a campaign fight instead of costing Resolve (a friend refreshed mid-fight and lost progress; Robert, 2026-10-25: "just save deck and progress after each fight/map node") →** the run itself was already saved after every step; what a reload cost was the rule that a fight left unfinished counted as a loss (Resolve -1 or -2, fight restarted). That rule is gone. The engine is deterministic, so the campaign keeps only the fight's seed and the moves the player has made (`run/fightLog.ts`, localStorage `sr.fightlog:<run>:<node>:<won>:<lost>`) and `FightView` replays them silently on the next load (`resume`, `onMove`, `onResumeFailed`). Nothing can be gained by reloading: it is the same fight with the same draws. A move that ended the fight is replayed normally so the result shows. If the saved moves no longer replay (the rules changed in a deploy) the log is dropped and the fight starts over, with no penalty. Forfeit still costs Resolve; the log is cleared when a fight ends or the run is abandoned. Not restored: the battle log text. The Arena already resumed through the server.

196. **Odds on the How to play page, more ways to let a card go, and more shards (Robert, 2026-10-25) →** (a) How to play has an **Odds & numbers** tab (`app/LearnOdds.tsx`, hidden by `SHOW_ODDS` in `app/learnFlags.ts`): card rarity odds by luck for fights and elites, the Warden's Gift, prices, the map's node odds, what fights pay, the share of events that offer each kind of thing (`eventOffers()` in `packages/campaign/src/odds.ts`, computed from the real events), and the Brass Cascade's layouts and where test balls land (measured on the real board). The numbers are read from exported constants (`NODE_ODDS`, `FIGHT_SHARDS`, `POTION_DROP_CHANCE`, `CROSSED_OFFER_CHANCE`, `GIFT_LEGEND_CHANCE`, `BOON_WEIGHTS`, `POTION_WEIGHTS`, `LANE_KINDS`...), and tests keep the lane list and the map odds honest. (b) 16 more ordinary events now offer, as an extra choice, to let a basic card go (`remove('basic')`) for a small gift; the share of events that offer a way to let a plain card or a Wound go rose from about 16% to 34% (a test requires over 30%). New option flag `'optional'`: an option that cannot be taken now (no basic card left, belt full) is left off instead of stopping the event from being offered. (c) Shards are about 30% more: fights pay 15 / 36 / 65 (was 12 / 28 / 50) and event, Cascade-purse rewards went up by about 30% (costs unchanged). Re-measure with `pnpm runsim`.

197. **The Brass Cascade is fair: the engine draws the lanes, the prizes move, one Legendary (Robert, 2026-10-25: "randomize location each time and make sure each location has an even chance ... only one slot should be a legendary card") →** supersedes the physics-decided landing of #189 and the nudges: where each ball lands is a fair draw from the trial's seed (`cascadeLanes(seed)` in `trials.ts`, uniform over the ten lanes; a test checks every lane is within about 8% of 10% over 40,000 trials), the engine computes the earned lanes itself (`endTrial` ignores the client, so a modified client cannot claim better lanes), and the ball is only animated and steered to its lane (`physics.ts` `target`, STEER). Aiming is cosmetic; nudges are gone. The ten prizes of a layout (`LAYOUT_PRIZES`) are shuffled across the lanes for every event, a lane's second prize stands with its first, and only one lane is a Legendary card (the other top prize is now a rare card of your class). The How to play page shows the measured even odds.
198. **Cleansing cards (Robert, 2026-10-25: "cards ... that can dispell the effect of curses ... lessened, not completely removed unless its a higher rarity ... any debuff like brittle") →** new op `cleanse` (`amount` stacks off every debuff on the target; `all` dispels them). The debuffs are `DEBUFFS`: Exposed, Faltering, Seared, Stunned, Brittle, Doom. Neutral line: Shrug It Off (common, 1 off, 4 Barrier), Steady Nerves (uncommon, 2 off, 6 Barrier), Purging Light (rare, dispels, 8 Barrier, Exhaust). Rule, tested: only a rare or better card may dispel; an upgrade of a cleanse lessens one more.
199. **Played cards linger and fly to the discard pile; the battle log can show them (Robert, 2026-10-25) →** a played card stays on show about 1.7 s (it was 0.65 s) and then flies to your discard pile (an opponent's card flies off toward their side); several can be on show at once (`FlourishCard`, Web Animations; the fight never waits for it). The battle log has a Cards button: a small panel beside the log (not a full overlay) with the cards played, Traps sprung and Auras started this fight, newest first, click one to enlarge (`LogLine.card`). Open: tune the 1.7 s by playtest.
200. **Arena: opponents see each other's Traps, and labels clear the potion belt (Robert, 2026-10-25) →** the opponent's set Traps show as face-down tiles beside their character (`SnareRow foe`; what they do is still secret until they spring), not just a count. A player's name tag in the Arena slides clear of the potion belt like the stance banner does (`clearOfBelt.ts`).

201. **A Ghost decks page (Robert, 2026-10-25) →** `#/ghosts` (linked from the home page, the Arena and the Leaderboards Ghosts tab) lists the decks that can be met as ghosts: whose ("<name>'s ghost"), which Riftwalker, how far it got (Rift Tier and stage), its record as a ghost and when its owner played it, and when it last played; sortable (most recent, best as a ghost, furthest) and filterable by Riftwalker. Never the cards, the Boons, the owner's id or the deck's id. Server: `GET /pvp/board/ghostdecks` (the Leaderboard object's `ghostDecks`, newest first, 200 at most, decks from the last 31 days, cached 30 s at the edge).

202. **Ghost Wardens (Robert, 2026-10-25: "a chance that the boss at a stage could be a ghost at any stage that occurs beyond tier 3 ... the ghost doesnt have to be from the realms on the stage ... players dont play the same bosses over and over") →** from Rift Tier 4, each stage's Warden has a 35% chance (`GHOST_WARDEN_CHANCE`, `GHOST_FROM_TIER`; decided with the map: `MapNode.ghost`) of being a ghost: a deck from the Arena, any Riftwalker and realm. (Tiers themselves are not realm-themed; stages are.) The usual Warden stays on the node as the fallback. Flow: entering the node, `ghostWanted(run)` is true, the run screen asks the server (`POST /pvp/board/ghostboss`: one of the better half by wins, never the asker's own deck, its cards and Boons included, 30 asks an hour) and calls `attachGhost`, which checks it (`checkGhostBoss`) and keeps it in the node's context (so a retry or reload meets the same ghost); if none comes (offline, none yet, invalid) the usual Warden is fought. The fight is the Arena's match played in the browser on the PvP fight screen: `run/ghostDuel.ts` is a local match (same rules, the computer opponent `chooseSmartAction`) exposed as a `PvpLink`, its moves kept for a reload (`sr.ghostlog:*`). Its HP is its Riftwalker's base times 1.4 plus 0.06 per tier past 3 (`ghostBossHp`; **untested for balance**: run `pnpm runsim`-style checks). It pays the usual Warden Gift and Boon. In development a ghost can be set by hand: localStorage `sr.debug.ghostboss`.

203. **Practice is called Sandbox (Robert, 2026-10-25) →** the home page button and the Elite Events page say Sandbox. The route (`#/fight`) and the code names are unchanged; older entries in this log and the changelog keep the old name as written.

204. **Ilvane is not a stone golem (Robert, 2026-10-25) →** his hero art is a bearded elder shaman (teal robes, feathers, a hawk-headed totem staff, glowing hawk and bear spirits), so his blurb (`RIFTWALKERS.totemist.blurb`, shown on the character screen) now says "An elder shaman who carves totems and calls the spirits inside them to do his bidding", and the same line was changed in the art briefs and card-art prompts (`docs/art-briefs.md`, `docs/card-art/*.md`) and the generated card list. His trait and how-to text never mentioned a golem. (The Neutral "Shardstone Golem" in the legacy card docs is a different, old card.)

205. **The rest of the card art is in (Robert, 2026-10-25) →** all 81 sheets in `docs/card-art/sheets.json` are in `assets-src/cards`; `tools/pack-cards.ps1` cut them into 676 card pictures (`apps/web/public/art/cards`, 640x480 jpg) and rebuilt the manifest. 60 sheets have gutters too thin to find, so they were cut into equal cells with a small inset; spot checks showed clean cuts, and the empty cells of 7-card sheets are ignored. Cards without a picture keep the painted placeholder: Basic and Common cards (by design) and the three cleansing cards added after the prompts were made (Shrug It Off, Steady Nerves, Purging Light). `pnpm cardprompts` must not simply be re-run to add them: it regroups the cards by rarity and would shift cards between sheets; a separate extra sheet is the safe way.

206. **Art for the last Uncommon/Rare cards (Robert, 2026-10-25) →** `pnpm cardprompts:extra` puts cards no sheet covers on `extra-NN` sheets (the main `cardprompts` now refuses to regroup once art exists, `--force` overrides); `extra-01` (6 cards) is cut with explicit `cells` in `sheets.json`, which `pack-cards.ps1` now honours. **Why:** regrouping would shift every existing cut. Also: the How to play gradient is fixed to the window (`background-attachment: fixed`) because on the body it was only one screen tall and showed its edge on long tabs.

207. **Rift Tiers climb faster, and Tier I is a little harder (Robert, 2026-10-25: "make each tier scale harder each time, even tier 1 a little harder") →** enemy HP per tier is now `1 + 0.12t + 0.006t²` (t = tier − 1: the step grows by 0.012 each tier, so Tier 5 is 1.68×, Tier 10 2.67×, Tier 20 5.55×; it was a flat +0.12); Might per tier now rises to 8 (was 6, so tiers past VII stopped getting meaner). Tier I itself gets +8% HP and +5% damage from the campaign (`TIER_PRESSURE` in run.ts), and every tier after adds 2 more points of damage. The engine's Tier I stays the designed enemy (its tests and PvP are untouched). `pnpm runsim` (Normal, knight and drunkard, 30 runs each): 37% of runs clear all five stages (the target is about a third). Existing runs pick the new numbers up from their next fight; a fight in progress when the update arrives restarts from the top without costing Resolve.

208. **Arena matches are best of three (Robert, 2026-10-25) →** the first to win two games wins the match (`packages/pvp/src/series.ts`, `SERIES_WINS`). Game 1 is decided as before (both choose, a coin settles a tie). After each game the player who LOST it chooses who goes first in the next (go second for +30 HP, as before); the other waits, and a coin decides if the chooser does not answer in 30 s. The seats swap with the order, so the room keeps the score by player id. A resignation or two missed turns end only the game; a player who leaves for over 60 s (or never returns between games) forfeits the whole match. A game drawn by the engine is played again. The leaderboard and ghost records count the match once, when it is decided (its move count is the total of its games). A ghost opponent plays all three games and chooses randomly when it lost.

209. **The campaign board lists every character; usernames are unique and required (Robert, 2026-10-25: "players should be able to have multiple characters on the leaderboard", "require a username before playing campaign, and make sure they are unique") →** (a) `standingsRuns` keeps each player's furthest run per Riftwalker, so a player is on the board once for each character (before, only their single furthest run showed, which hid a Barbarian at Tier 2 stage 3 behind a stronger run). (b) A username is 3 to 20 letters, digits, spaces and _ - . ' with nothing blocked, not a reserved word, and two names are the same if they match ignoring case and punctuation (`checkUsername`). The board server keeps one name per player (`names` table, UNIQUE key; 8 claims a day, 40 per address); claiming or changing a name moves everything the player already has on the boards to it. A run counts on the campaign board only under a claimed name (`counted: false, error: "no-name"`). (c) The campaign asks for a name first (`NameGate`); the Arena claims the typed name when a match is looked for; the leaderboard page can change it. If the server cannot be reached the campaign offers to go on without the leaderboard. Existing names are not reserved: the first player to claim one gets it. Open: a run whose first reports never reached the server is still not accepted later (a run is first seen at 2 stages or fewer, DECISIONS leaderboard guard); a catch-up allowance is possible if players hit it.

210. **Twinned is now called Reverb (Robert, 2026-10-25) →** a display rename only: the status id stays `twinned` (saved data, tests and the engine are untouched); `STATUS_NAME` and the status tooltip say Reverb, and the card list, art briefs and roster doc were updated. "Echo" was not used because six cards already carry it in their names (Echo Chamber, Echo Trap...). Usernames also fold to NFKC and take Latin letters only (so a lookalike from another alphabet cannot pass for a claimed name).

211. **The campaign board catches up with saved runs, and boards turn over at midnight Pacific (Robert, 2026-10-25) →** (a) the browser announces every saved run to the board when the app opens and when the campaign board is looked at (`run/syncRuns.ts`); the board only moves a run forward. To make a catch-up possible the first-sight allowance is now three Rift Tiers (`RUN_START_GRACE` 9; it was 2), then one more stage per minute: so a run whose early reports were missed (or that began before the board knew the player) shows up, while a script cannot claim past Tier III at once. (b) A week now turns over at Monday 00:00 US Pacific time (Sunday night midnight), a month at 00:00 Pacific on the 1st, with daylight saving worked out in `periodStart`/`periodEnd` (it was UTC, which is why a board showed 8 hours left on a Sunday afternoon). The page says so under the countdown. Rows already stored are unaffected (a period is just a time range).

212. **Usernames and Steam (Robert, 2026-10-25) →** on Steam the Steam username is the player's username; for every other login method we ask for a username and never use a real name or email. This is a constraint on the current web username flow (`NameGate`, `claimName`): keep the name separate from the account identity so a Steam persona name can fill it in (and still be claimed uniquely), and never display an email or account name from a sign-in provider. Nothing is built for Steam yet.

213. **Rift Tiers scale harder (Robert, 2026-10-25) →** enemy HP multiplier is now `1 + 0.20t + 0.010t^2` (t = tier - 1; was 0.12/0.006): Tier II x1.21, V x1.96, X x3.61. Might per tier stays +1 but its cap rises 8 -> 12. Campaign extra damage per tier rises 2% -> 5% (`TIER_PRESSURE.dmgPerTier`; Tier X is 150% before stage and difficulty). Why: deeper tiers were too easy; damage scales faster than before so HP sponges do not just mean longer fights. Needs a `pnpm sim` pass to check the high tiers.

214. **Tougher baseline enemies (Robert, 2026-10-25) →** starter decks are meant to be awful and not clear Tier I without reward cards or Boons. `STAGE_SCALE` (packages/campaign/src/run.ts) went from 82/98/114% HP and 86/98/110% damage to 92/108/125% HP and 95/108/122% damage for stages 1/2/3. Measured with `pnpm sim` (starters only, per fight) and `pnpm runsim` (a drafting bot, Normal, 20 runs per hero): starter-only Warden wins are about 24% at stage 1 and about 5% at stage 2 (about 84% at stage 1 before), regular fights stay winnable; the drafting bot's full-run clear rate fell from about 29% to about 11%. A first try at 101/119/141% HP left the drafting bot clearing about 3%, so it was backed off. Also fixed the runsim bot choosing an event option that was not on offer.

215. **One editable tier table (Robert, 2026-10-25) →** enemy strength per Rift Tier now lives in one table, `TIER_TABLE` in `packages/campaign/src/tierTable.ts` (a row per tier: percent more than base enemy HP and damage), so balance is set by hand instead of by formula. Stage 1 of a tier is the row's value; stages 2 and 3 climb a third and two thirds of the way to the next tier's row (tier 20 uses the 19-to-20 gap). The campaign plays fights at engine Tier I (so the engine's `tierHp`/`tierMight` no longer apply there) and passes the result as `hpPct`/`dmgPct`; `STAGE_SCALE`, `TIER_PRESSURE` and Might per tier are gone, and Easy/Hard runs saved earlier keep their difficulty as a ratio to Normal. The seed values were computed from the 0.51.0 formulas with Might folded into damage (assuming an 8-damage attack), so the numbers match the old climb at stage 1 of each tier; within a tier the stages now rise less steeply than before (Tier I stage 3 was 173% HP, now 145%). The How to play page shows the table with the stage values.

216. **Foresee draws one (Robert, 2026-10-25) →** Foresee N used to be "look at the top N and discard any of them", which was confusing next to the other discard choices. It is now **look at the top N cards, draw one of them and discard the rest** (a choice of exactly one; the others are real discards, so payoffs fire; if the hand is full the chosen card burns like any draw). Foresee is now also card advantage, so every card that has it gets a free draw. To avoid drawing twice, the draw(1) was removed from the seven cards that paired it with Foresee: Thread-Cutter, Cut Through Fate, Glimpse Ahead, Read the Room, Trick of the Light, Stolen Moment, Spare Change. Not re-balanced: the other ~13 Foresee cards (Inner Sight, Sunlit Blade, Settled Palms, Cold Read ...) are now cantrips and need a pass.

217. **The merchant upgrades cards (Robert, 2026-10-25) →** every merchant (including a visiting stranger) now has an **Upgrade a card** service next to Remove a card. It costs 60 shards the first time at that shop and 40 more for each upgrade bought there (60, 100, 140, ...); the next merchant starts again at 60 (the count lives in that visit's `merchant.upgrades`, which a shop saved by an older build lacks, so it counts none). `buyUpgrade` in `packages/campaign/src/run.ts`, prices in `rewards.ts` (`UPGRADE_PRICE`, `UPGRADE_STEP`, `upgradePrice`). The price is flat, not by rarity, so it stays easy to read and tune. Shrine upgrades are unchanged (free, one per shrine).

218. **Ambush events and upgrade notices (Robert, 2026-10-25) →** (a) An Event stop is now and then a fight (like Slay the Spire's ? rooms): when entered, 10% of the time it is a plain fight and 5% an Elite (Elite only from the third floor; the first floor has no events), chosen with the run's seeded rng from the stage's realm(s), avoiding the stage's own fights when it can. The map node becomes that fight (`node.kind`/`node.enemyId` are set on the run's own copy), so it is played, rewarded (shards, cards, Elite Boon and heal), retried on a loss and saved exactly like any other fight; a notice says "Ambush!". Odds are `EVENT_FIGHT_ODDS` in mapgen.ts and shown on Odds & numbers. (b) Any step that swaps cards for their upgrades (Shrine, merchant, event, prize, starting choice) now shows "Upgraded: A → A+" (`upgradedCards`, a deck diff, so it covers every source); events that already name the card say so themselves and do not get a second line.

219. **Decks save themselves; one name for the elite game (Robert, 2026-10-25) →** (a) The Save deck button and the cleared-tier Save button are gone. The run's deck is copied into the Vault (and the online copy) by itself after every finished stop on the map (`shouldAutoSave`: a fight once its reward is taken, and every shop, shrine and event, since those change the deck), replacing that run's earlier save (one run, one saved deck, so one Vault slot). The run bar says where it stands ("Arena deck: Tier 2 · Stage 1/3", linking to Saved decks), and the first time it happens a banner explains it (saved after every fight, other players may face it as a ghost, removed if the run collapses). A collapsed run still removes its saved deck (decision kept). If the 10-slot Vault is full the player is told once per stage, with a link to the Saved decks page, and then left alone. (b) All five elite events are titled **The Brass Cascade** (it is one game); their flavour text is all brass now. (c) Cascade ball physics: a peg no longer shoves a ball downward out of its lower half (that was a few px of free drop in one step, read as speeding up), and a ball that reaches the slots off its lane slides across instead of being placed; steering toward the drawn lane is stronger (STEER 110, damping 16) so fewer balls arrive off their lane. The lane is still drawn by the rules, not the physics.

220. **The Brass Cascade becomes the Brass Reel, a spin-for-a-prize machine (Robert, 2026-10-25: "change the brass cascade completely ... a slot machine that players can spend some shards to get a random reward ... take the current reward, or re-roll ... the same rewards as last time, but only one at a time for 25 shards each roll ... like csgo, spin through all of the options and land on the one they get offered") →** supersedes #189, #197, #219 (b)/(c) and the ball physics work. The peg board, balls, physics and the "second prize for two balls in a lane" are gone. An elite event now shows a reel of the same ten prizes (the same three layouts, still shuffled into a different order for every event, one Legendary at most), all visible. Each spin costs 25 shards (`ROLL_COST`), including the first: the rules (`spinReel` in `trials.ts`, `rollTrial` in `run.ts`) take the shards and draw the prize from the run's own seeded stream, which is saved with the run, so reloading never gives a free re-spin. Every prize is equally likely on a first spin; a re-spin never stops on the prize already held (otherwise paying for the same thing is no spin), so each of the other nine is about 11%. The prize on the table (`trial.earned[0]`) is taken with `claimTrialPrize` (the old Rift-crossed pick-one-of-three still applies) or let go by spinning again; walking away forfeits it and the shards spent. The client (`apps/web/src/trials/Reel.tsx`) only animates: a strip of tiles scrolls past a pointer and eases to a stop on the drawn prize over about five seconds (instantly, with reduced motion). All five elite events are now "The Brass Reel" with brass machine flavour text. Old saves: a trial begun but not finished as the pegboard game still settles as failed (`settleInterrupted`); a saved pegboard result with prizes earned can still be claimed. Practice page: a free spin on a sample reel. Not balanced: ten prizes at 25 shards each means about 250 shards on average to hit one particular prize, versus 260 for a Legendary in the shop.
221. **One pool of prizes on the Brass Reel (Robert, 2026-10-25: "i would like to include all of the prizes instead of partitioning them into different reward pools. just one pool with all of them in there") →** supersedes the three layouts (Treasury, Armory, Apothecary) of #189/#197/#220. Every elite event shows the same thirteen prizes (`REEL_KINDS`, `REEL_PRIZES` in `trials.ts`): a Legendary card (only one), a rare card, two Boons, two Pick-1-of-3 Rift-crossed rares, a rare potion, Masterwork (upgrade 3 cards), Masterwork (upgrade 2 cards), a purse of 115 shards, a rest (heal 40%), Vitality (+10 max HP, heal 30%) and a lighter load (2 random cards leave the deck). `EliteSpec.layout` and the `Layout` type are gone; the five events differ only in flavour text. Where each prize stands on the reel is still shuffled per event and a prize that cannot be taken still becomes a purse. Thirteen equal prizes: a first spin is about 7.7% each, a re-spin about 8.3% for each of the other twelve, and about 13 spins (325 shards) to hit one particular prize (it was 250 with ten). Not balanced; 25 shards a spin is the number to revisit. The prizes can now be added to by extending `REEL_KINDS` and the `makers` list.

222. **The lighter load lets you choose, and lets you choose none (Robert, 2026-10-25: "update 'a lighter load' to allow removing two cards optionally if the player wants to") →** the Brass Reel's lighter load prize (`TrialPrize.removeUpTo = 2`, no deal steps) no longer takes two random cards. When it is the prize on the table, the player names up to two cards of their deck (`claimTrialPrize(run, prize, undefined, cards)`, checked in `takePrize`: at most two, each in the deck and not sanctified, and the deck may not fall below the minimum size), and naming none is allowed (the prize is then just taken and the deck kept). On a deck that cannot lose a card the prize is a purse of 130 shards instead. The client (`LighterLoad` in `Pickers.tsx`) reuses the deck picker, one card at a time.

223. **Balance audit: Slay the Spire numbers, no infinite or exponential combos, a gentle tier table (Robert, 2026-10-25: "i had a match with sehra where i did over 450 damage on turn one ... would like the character traits to be balanced, and card strength ... more similar to slay the spire 2's numbers so we can make it simpler ... tier 1 is a challenge, and tier 5 is an accomplishment, and tier 10 is a huge accomplishment, and tier 20 really feels like you have a godly build ... matches that took 10+ turns ... without needing crazy high health numbers") →** measured with a new tool, `pnpm audit:balance` (tools/src/audit*: lane-focused drafting like a person, a beam-search bot, a one-turn burst search and a deck optimizer that hill-climbs towards the most damage, i.e. what a combo-hunting player finds). Why the old sims misled: they drafted random cards and played a one-ply bot that never sets up a combo, so engine characters (Sehra above all) looked weak and were given more HP. The optimizer found decks dealing 1,000-6,000 damage in a round at Tier II, and every one rested on (a) a free card that replaces itself or makes Mana, (b) a doubler that can be played again, or (c) a payoff that multiplies a number that only grows. Changes, all data except three generic rules:
    - **Rules (engine):** Fervor needs **10** to enter Radiant (was 5; Slay the Spire's number). Leaving Serene gives 2 Mana **once a turn** (stepping in and out looped). A cost discount (Cheapen, a free Discover) is **spent when the card is played** (it lasted the whole fight, so Frozen Moment made a hand free for good). Reclaim never returns a card that reclaims. A totem that charges (Elder) spends its charge when awakened, even by an awaken that keeps it (Echo Chamber). A status doubler may carry a cap (`scaleStatus.max`: "Double your Thorns, at most +8"), and every doubler in the pools has one.
    - **Upgrade rule (`plus()`):** "damage/Barrier/draw equal to X" gains a flat +3/+3/+1 (it used to get cheaper, which made free scaling cards: Zenith+, Fatal Dose+); Mana never grows; a card that makes resources (Mana, cards, Discover) or scales with something is never made free and never loses Exhaust (it Holds instead); paying less HP counts as an improvement (Soul Tithe+ had become a free, repeatable +2 Mana).
    - **Guard rails (`packages/content/src/v2/balance.test.ts`):** a free card that nets a card or makes Mana must Exhaust; doublers Exhaust and are capped; payoffs scale at most x2 per point of a growing resource (x3 if consumed); hero HP 70-90.
    - **Cards:** doublers capped and consumed (Fatal Dose 2 Mana + Exhaust, Plague Bloom x2, Slip the Leash +6, Briar Bloom +8, Hasten +20, Compound Fear +3, Compounding Interest is +3 Seared a turn); converters halved (Colossus Blow 6 + Barrier, Impregnable doubles, Dawn Incarnate x1, Retribution x2, Apex Maw x3, Blood Haze x1, Final Reckoning x1, Harvest/Reaping Scythe +1 per 4 Fate, Requiem/Pyre/Gnaw/Last Gasp x1, Boomstick x2, Zenith x2 + Exhaust, Coup de Grace x3, Exploit Weakness/Culling x2, Open Veins x0.5, Elder Tide/Spirit Strike/Siege Protocol x2); loops closed (Feral Instinct, Frenzied Gulp, Prostrate, Quick Orison, Calculated Move, Fold the Hand, Blood Sacrifice, Unleash Exhaust; Quick Hands/Quick Study/Discarded Hand upgrade to "draw 2, discard 2"; Owl awakens for 1 Mana, no draw; Radiant Heart draws only); Nerve Surge is rare; Rift Surge costs 1; Heave 2 Might; Reckless Swing 7; Kindle Fury costs 2 for 9 (Eruption); Rise Radiant costs 3; Berserker costs 2; The Hunger and Berserk give 2 Might (Inflame); Blood Tithe is rare (Offering); Keen Edge 2; Razor Halo and Spent Blades Sing 2; Coiled Strike 2; Killing Flourish 4 a hit; Fevered Communion 3 Spirit.
    - **Heroes (Slay the Spire's 70-88 HP):** Valeria 80 (was 66), Rivet 70 (60), Kesh 80 (88), Vesper 80 (124), Sylas 72 (84), Ilvane 76 (84), Sehra 82 (116), Obsidia 82 (112), KP 88 (106), Dorian 76 (92). **Traits:** Sylas's Weak Spot (a lone boss was Exposed on every turn: +50% to every attack for good) became **Envenomed** (a random enemy gets 2 Bleed each turn); Clutch Genes is +50% at half HP (was +60% at 60%). Ambusher (3 turns), Inner Fire, Oathkeeper and the rest stay.
    - **Enemies:** `ENEMY_SCALE` and its hidden multipliers are gone; units.ts now holds the numbers met at Tier I stage 1. HP is what Tier I already had; attack damage came down to Slay the Spire's level (regulars and Elites x0.65, Wardens x0.8) to match the lower hero HP. Vorthax 270 HP, Collapse 26.
    - **Tier table (supersedes #213-215):** each row now lists HP and damage for each stage explicitly (`{ hp: [s1, s2, s3], dmg: [...] }`); stages climb x1.25/x1.5 HP and x1.12/x1.25 damage inside a tier; tiers climb fast to Tier X (248% HP, 174% damage at stage 1) and slowly to Tier XX (312% / 207%), because a deck that carries its cards and Boons grows a lot over the first loops and then levels off. (Tier XX was 3100% HP / 1100% damage.) Numbers in docs/BALANCE.md, "Balance audit".
    These are rules and character-identity changes that Robert asked for in this request; the Fervor goal, the once-a-turn Serene Mana, one-use discounts and the Sylas trait are the ones to look at first if they feel wrong.
    Also: ghost Wardens' HP multiplier went 1.4 -> 1.65 (+0.06 -> +0.07 a tier) so they keep about the HP they had (`GHOST_BOSS_HP_*`, run.ts). **PvP** uses the same hero HP (x ARENA_HP_MULT) and the same rules, so the arena is rebalanced too, and the server (apps/server) must be deployed with the client (a room replaying moves under other rules ends the match).

224. **The Threshold and Waymarks re-assessed (Robert, 2026-10-25: "the threshold options need to be re-assessed. some are simply underwhelming, and the one with a negative outcome rarely is being taken") →** why: every card reward was a *random* card of that rarity from the whole pool (usually a lane you are not playing), upgrades often landed on a Defend, "+1 Resolve" did nothing at the Threshold (a run starts at full Resolve), and the bargains paid a price the deck carries for good (max HP, Wounds) for a reward that was still a coin flip. Changes (`packages/campaign/src/preludes.ts`):
    - **Cards are a choice:** a new effect `pick` (choose one of three cards of a rarity from your own class; `upgraded` offers the "+" versions; ancestral picks come from the ancestral cards). After the option is taken the run shows the usual card reward screen (take one or skip), with an empty `nodeId` like the prelude itself (`chooseCard` clears no node; the save check allows it).
    - **Upgrades go to your own cards first**, then Strikes and Defends. **Resolve blessings appear only when the run is missing Resolve** (`makePrelude(..., hurt)`).
    - **Blessings** (about 100 shards' worth each): max HP 8 -> 10, purse 100 + 20/stage, Open Hand = choose an uncommon, Keepsake = 2 Potions + 40, Whetstone = upgrade + 60, Sturdy Frame = 6 max HP + 50, Clear-Out = remove one + choose a common, Wayfinder's Gift = choose an upgraded common + 40, Pilgrim 6 max HP + upgrade, cache 120 + 15/stage, Lucky Find + 40.
    - **Bargains** (clearly more than a blessing, paid mostly in HP you can heal): Blood Price 12 HP = choose a rare; A Legend's Price 15 HP = choose a Legendary; Call on the Ancestors 12 HP = choose an ancestral card; The Rift Is Hungry -6 max HP = a rare Boon (+1 Mana or +1 card a turn, for good); The Cursed Crown 10 HP + 1 Wound = a rare Boon; A Hollow Bargain -5 max HP = choose an upgraded rare; Trade Up 8 HP = 3 Attacks/Defends become uncommon or rare cards; Heavy Purse 260 + 30/stage + 1 Wound; Sell the Silver 8 HP = 200 + 25/stage; A Haunted Gift = an uncommon and a common Boon + 1 Wound; Blood for Steel 10 HP = 4 upgrades; Fire Sale 8 HP = remove 3; Reckless Haste 8 HP = 2 Potions + 100; Heavy Heart = +25 max HP + 1 Wound. Gone: Double or Nothing (two random uncommons) and The Rift-Tithe (max HP for shards).
    Not measured by a bot (the run bot always takes the safe option); the values are reasoned in docs/BALANCE.md, "Threshold and Waymarks".

226. **The basic Attack card is renamed Strike (Robert, 2026-10-25: "rename the Attack basic card to Strike") →** supersedes the naming in #154. The shared Neutral basic (6 damage, id `strike`) is now **named Strike**; Defend is unchanged. "Attack" stays as the card *type* (Whenever you play an Attack, Killing Flourish...). Event, odds, prelude and merchant text that said "Attack or Defend" now says "Strike or Defend". `10_CARD_LIST.md` and `art-briefs.md` regenerated.

227. **Fewer Boons, Legendary Boons, card tags, and an event pass (Robert, 2026-10-25: "reduce the number of boons players acquire, or at least slow down how fast they stack them up ... add event options to add tags to cards like hold, consume, starting hand ... make some boons Legendary ... a pass on our events and make sure they feel rewarding or have interesting choices ... some events to have bad outcomes outright, just like slay the spire, and some events should result in fighting encounters") →**
    - **Boons, slower:** Wardens no longer drop a Boon (they give the Gift); Elites still do. 12 Boons became 18: new Hex Lantern (uncommon, enemies Faltering 2 turns), Battle Standard (rare, 2 Might), Hunter's Lens (rare, enemies Exposed 2 turns), Phoenix Ember (rare, heal 15 after fights), and two new **Legendary** Boons, Aegis of Ages (Shielded x3 each fight) and The Gilded Crown (+25 max HP, heal 10 after fights). Rift Core (+1 Mana) and Scholar's Tome (+1 card a turn) became Legendary. Rift Satchel (which was a second "+1 card a turn") now draws 2 more on the first turn. Odds (`BOON_WEIGHTS`): an Elite gives 50/35/12/3% common/uncommon/rare/Legendary; the merchant never sells a Legendary (`offerBoon(..., noLegend)`, priced 400 if one ever appears); beating Vorthax leaves a Legendary Boon you lack 40% of the time (`LOOP_LEGEND_CHANCE`). A rare Boon from a Threshold bargain is now one of the new rares, not +1 Mana.
    - **Card tags (`sanctify.ts`):** an event can engrave one keyword on one card for good: **Hold** (retain), **Opening hand** (innate) or **Flare** (costs 1 less, Consume). Written into the id like a sanctified roll and derived on demand (`hold^strike`, `hi~hold^strike+`), so decks, saves, the Arena and PvP need nothing new; a tagged card upgrades into the tagged upgrade. Never on a Power, an unplayable card, a token or an ancestral card, never twice on one card, and only where it changes something. ("Consume" alone would only be a drawback, so it comes as Flare, with the discount.)
    - **New deal steps:** `choose` (the player picks a card of their deck to upgrade, remove, transform, copy or tag; the event waits, `pickDeckCard`) and `fight` (the stop becomes a regular or Elite fight, played, rewarded and retried like an ambush; winning also pays the step's `bonus`, kept on the map node; the event's Boon is pinned by id when the fight starts).
    - **Event pass (`eventsTuned.ts`, 25 events, 82 in all):** 13 reworked so the card is chosen and the reward is worth it (smith, scrapyard, shifting wall, mirror pool, rift healer, bone collector, moth shrine, frost garden, old soldiers, ash market, unmaker, twin pools, mending tree); 3 that engrave tags (the engraver, the patient monk, the cold kiln); 5 that start fights with spoils (the dead adventurer, the bandits' camp, the guarded hoard, a champion's challenge, the nest); 4 that simply go badly, with no walking away (rift storm, pickpockets, the cave-in, the cursed coin). The old versions are gone (the hand-written option handlers stay, so a run saved mid-event still resolves).
    - **From the review:** a fight an event starts pays the spoils it promises and the usual card reward and shards, but **not** an Elite's Boon (the guarded hoard gave two); the dead adventurer's bad branch pays only a rare card (it out-paid the good branch). A Legendary Boon is never a fallback when a rarity runs out, and never on the Brass Reel. A tagged card shows a ◆ after its name; a tag id an event could not have made (a tagged Power, Hold on a card that already holds) is not a card, so a tampered save or Arena deck cannot use one; random "a Strike or Defend leaves" steps never take a tagged card. PvP accepts tagged ids like sanctified ones (a deck is only checked for real cards, not where its tags came from); worth a cap if the Arena ever sees forged decks.
    - **How to play:** the "A run" tab lists every Threshold/Waymark option (`preludeCatalogue`) and every Boon by rarity; the tier text is corrected (stage 1 of a tier is below stage 3 of the one before since #223). All-Seeing's text: "they cost 0 the next time you play them".

228. **Harder from the start, and every tier a step (Robert, 2026-10-25: "im told the game is still too easy. it needs to be harder from the start, and each tier needs to feel harder") →** the tier table (`tierTable.ts`) only, so the change is one place to edit back. **Tier I now runs above the written enemy numbers:** 115% HP and 112% damage at stage 1 (was 100/100); units.ts stays the base. **The climb is steeper:** about +20 points of HP and +10 of damage a tier to Tier X (296% / 202% at stage 1, was 248 / 174), then +8 / +4.5 a tier to Tier XX (376% / 247%, was 312 / 207); Tiers II-IV are softened a little (130/150/176% HP) so a deck that has just cleared one loop does not meet a wall. Stage ramps are unchanged (x1.25/x1.5 HP, x1.12/x1.25 damage). Measured with `pnpm audit:balance -- matrix` (bot, Warden + Vorthax at full HP, each deck at its own tier): Tier I 56% -> 34%, Tier II 75% -> 50%, Tier V 70% -> 63%, Tier X 49% -> 35%; a fixed Tier V deck drops 96/93/88/75/63% over Tiers I-V. The bot is weaker than a person, which is why players found the old numbers easy; read these as floors.

229. **A much steeper climb (Robert, 2026-10-25: "we need a more steep curve each tier, the sims arent reflective of real players, so dont worry about sims for the tiers right now. just increase the difficultly per tier even more") →** set by hand, not measured: Tier I stays at 115% HP / 112% damage (#228); every tier adds **+30 HP and +14 damage** (percent of the base) to Tier X (385% / 238% at stage 1, was 296 / 202), then **+15 / +7** a tier to Tier XX (535% / 308%, was 376 / 247). Stage ramps unchanged, so Tier XX's Rift is 803% HP / 385% damage. The table test's sanity caps rose to 900% / 400%. Supersedes the slope in #228.

230. **The tier table never takes a step back (Robert, 2026-10-25: "i dont want a situation where stage 3 of a tier is stronger than stage 1 of the next higher tier, that should never happen so please make sure the scaling is progressive and never takes a step back") →** the table is now one rising ladder of 60 steps (Tier I stage 1, 2, 3, Tier II stage 1 ...), each harder than the last in both HP and damage, checked by a test in `tierTable.test.ts`. That rules out a big ramp inside a tier and a step down into the next, so each stage adds a fixed amount: **+15% HP and +6% damage to Tier X** (a whole tier +45% / +18%, steeper than #229's +30 / +14), then +7% / +3% to Tier XX. Tier I runs 115/130/145% HP and 112/118/124% damage; Tier X 520-550% / 274-286%; Tier XX 746-760% / 370-376%. Supersedes #229 and the "step down into the next tier" of #223. Tier I's Rift is gentler than before (145% HP, was 173%), the cost of keeping every tier above the last.

231. **Ghost decks outlive a collapsed run; ghost Wardens chosen near your tier and said plainly (Robert, 2026-10-25: "save decks as ghost decks if theyve been entered into the arena, even if they lose in campaign ... they just cant be entered into arena by the player"; "ghost decks that are stage bosses should clearly state that its a ghost deck"; "ghost decks should only be tier bosses if the ghost deck is tier 3 or above, and ideally we pull a ghost deck thats close in the players tier and dont play the same ghost deck twice in a run") →**
    - **Collapse:** it already worked this way: a saved deck is registered as a ghost on the leaderboard server, and collapse only drops the player's own Vault copy (so they cannot take it to the Arena); the ghost stays for others to meet (kept 70 days from its last game). Every message that said the deck was "removed" now says so; a test checks that collapsing never calls the leaderboard.
    - **Ghost Wardens:** the server picks with `pickGhostBoss` (`@sr/pvp`): only decks that reached **Tier 3** or further (`GHOST_BOSS_MIN_TIER`, campaign mapgen), never the asker's own, never one this run has met (the run keeps `ghostsMet`, the ghosts' server ids, sent with the request), and the nearest to the player's tier (decks within one tier of the nearest, the better half by wins). The client also refuses a ghost below Tier 3. When one is summoned the run says "Ghost Warden! This Warden is X's Necromancer deck from the Arena (Tier N), played by the computer", the fight is titled "Ghost Warden: X's ... deck" and the seat "X's ghost deck".
    - **Layout:** the shared `.setup--wide` rules moved from run.css (which loads only once a run is opened) to play.css, which every screen built on it now imports: before a run was opened, the Sandbox's Elite Events button and Fight button stretched the full width. The Sandbox's Back and Elite Events links sit together in a small row.

232. **Every run is on the campaign board (Robert, 2026-10-25: "i can see sacagweeda's ghost decks now but i dont see him in the campaign leaderboards. can you make sure players are automatically showing in both?") →** two causes. (a) A run was reported only once it had cleared a stage; it is now reported from its first finished stop (and on opening and resuming after that), for players with a username, so a new run shows as Tier 1 stage 0 after its first fight (a run abandoned before then does not use up a new-run slot). (b) The server refused a run the first time it heard of it if it was past 9 stages ("first seen near its start"), so a run played before the board existed, before a username or offline was refused for good, however far it got. The server now takes a new run where it says it is, and judges later progress on the time since the **last** report: at most `RUN_STEP_ALLOWANCE` (3, for a tier change or a missed report) plus a stage a minute since then, not measured from the start. The flood limits (reports per hour, new runs per day, per player and per address) still apply. The trade-off: a tampered browser could claim a high tier on a first report; the board trusted the browser already, and a cap can come back if it is abused.

233. **Random removals name their card; sanctified cards can be removed and show what changed (Robert, 2026-10-25: "update any event option that reads 'a random card' to show the random card that it would remove, this is for removal only. lets update sanctified to allow removing sanctified cards from the deck"; "we also need a good indicator of the numerical difference from the sanctification") →**
    - **Named removals:** a `remove` step now carries `ids`, the exact cards it takes, picked when the event is built (`pinRemovals` in deals.ts, called by eventDeals `make()`), and the label names them (`nameRemovals`: "a random basic card leaves" -> "Defend leaves", "two random cards leave your deck" -> "Defend and Shield Charge leave your deck", "else lose a card" -> "else lose Defend"; an unforeseen wording gets "(X would go)"). Chance branches are pinned too. The bonfire picks its card in `build`, names it and shows it. Taking the option removes exactly the named card; an event saved by an older build (no `ids`) still picks one at random. Only removals are named; random upgrades, transforms and copies are unchanged.
    - **Sanctified cards can be removed:** at a Shrine, the merchant, an event choice, or a random removal (they used to be part of the deck for good). A deck is still sanctified only once. (So random effects that take or change a Strike or Defend can now pick a sanctified one too, and it loses its roll; the label names it with its ▲ / ▼.)
    - **What sanctification changed is shown:** on a sanctified card, every number its roll changed is drawn green (up) or red (down) with how far it moved ("8 +2"), and the tooltip says "Sanctified: 6 -> 8" (`markRollDiff` against the plain card's text, in FightCard). The name keeps its ▲ / ▼.

234. **The Gambler's Shrine, and a higher baseline (Robert, 2026-10-25: "we need better options at the shrines for players past tier 5 ... add the slot machine in there for them to spend shards ... maybe we name it something other than just Shrine"; "lets call it the Gamblers Shrine, and lets add some other options like sacrificing a card for a choice at 3 rift crossed cards ... add a few good scaling options that adds uniqueness"; "you should also be able to walk away from the shrines at any time"; "there should be a max hp increase option ... a stacking book"; "i dont support stacking boons but just trying to figure out how max hp would work"; "baseline monster hp/dmg is still a tad low") →**
