316. **Call Gnashlings removed; Litter brings two Gnashlings (Robert, 2026-10-27: "lets just get rid of the gnashling cards ... the call gnashlings common card can be removed", then "litter can summon gnashlings since they are lower health, only two of them though") →** Call Gnashlings is deleted; Litter (uncommon, 2 Mana) summons 2 Gnashlings (4 HP, hits 3) instead of 3, which also fits her 2-companion limit. Old saves and decks: Heel and Call Gnashlings both become Thick Hide (`RETIRED_CARDS`), since she has no common summon left.
317. **Litter switched off for now (Robert, 2026-10-27: "lets just disable the Litter card for now") →** `source: 'disabled'` keeps it out of every reward, shop, event, Discover and Rift-crossed offer (all of them leave out cards with a source) and the card library hides it; a run or deck that already holds it still plays it as written (2 Gnashlings). Turning it back on is deleting that line.
318. **The run history, Arena match history and username follow the player too (Robert, 2026-10-27: "lets wrap up the syncd data") →** after #309 (runs in progress), the rest of what a player sees as theirs: (a) **Run history and Arena match history**: the player's Vault keeps a copy of each list (`lists` table in apps/server/src/vault.ts, `POST /pvp/vault/listsync`); a device and the server are merged by item id, the newer copy of an item winning, newest first, up to the list's own limit (40 runs, 60 matches), and an item forgotten on one device is remembered as removed so the others forget it too (rules in packages/pvp/src/listsync.ts). The home page and the History page sync on opening; an ended run or a kept Arena game is sent up a few seconds later. (b) **Username**: `POST /pvp/board/id` also answers with the name this player claimed, and a device that has no username yet takes it (`restoreUsername`, on the home page's player chip, again when signing in). Settings (speed, sound) stay per device.
319. **Devour 16; Fell the Prey switched off (Robert, 2026-10-27: "Devour and Feel the Prey are essentially the same card. lets disable Fell The Prey and increase Devour to 16 damage") →** Devour deals 16 (Lifesteal 50%, 2 Mana, uncommon; was 14). Fell the Prey (16, Lifesteal, consumed) gets `source: 'disabled'` like Litter (#317): never offered, hidden from the library, still playable in a deck that holds it.
320. **Review fixes for the device sync (#309, #318) →** (a) a run that ended stays ended: a "gone" copy beats any live copy whatever its time (run ids are never reused), so a device that still had a fallen run open cannot bring it back by saving; (b) a device never drops a run it holds: runs that are only on the server fill free slots, newest first, and otherwise wait there (before, the newest 10 were kept and an unsent run could be lost); (c) ended-run markers: up to 300 kept on the server and on a device (were 60 and 100), oldest let go first; (d) the server's run history keeps the last fight on only the newest 12 runs, as a device does, so it cannot outgrow its storage; (e) signed in, the username this identity claimed wins over one claimed on the device under another identity. Not done (noted): device clocks decide which live copy is newer; a shared browser that changes accounts mixes their local data; history items are only loosely checked on the way in.
321. **Overflow: half of what is left over carries through a fallen minion (Robert, 2026-10-27, after "minions are basically shields and enemies ... cant get through the minions to hit the player": "lets do the 50% damage overflow") →** when a single-target attack (a card or move whose damage goes `to: 'target'` or `'front'`) kills a hero's companion, `OVERFLOW_PCT` (50, packages/combat/src/types.ts) of the damage left over (after the companion's Barrier and HP) hits that hero, as an attack (their Barrier and Shielded still apply). So a 14-damage hit on a 4 HP drone sends 5 through. Area attacks, Traps, Thorns and other direct damage do not overflow (they already reach the hero, or are not attacks); a companion that is Shielded or Ghostly lets nothing through; it works the same on both sides in the Arena. It is a damage event flagged `overflow`, and the battle log says "{name} carries through to you". The name players see is `OVERFLOW_NAME` (a placeholder until Robert picks one: one line to change). The Learn page's Combat tab and the Companion note explain it. Not yet measured by a sim (docs/BALANCE.md): the number is a first guess to tune.
322. **Overflow is 100%, and it is called Overflow (Robert, 2026-10-27: "lets go with 100% overflow damage for now. and yes call it Overflow") →** `OVERFLOW_PCT` is 100 (was 50, #321): everything left over after a companion's Barrier and HP carries through to its hero, so a companion only ever absorbs what it has. A 14-damage hit on a 4 HP drone sends 10 through. The name is final (`OVERFLOW_NAME`). At 100% a drone is worth only its HP and its damage, and a big minion (War Engine, Sentry Turret) is a real blocker: the first thing to watch is whether Rivet's cheap-token decks are still worth playing, and whether Barrier on minions (Rally the Pack and friends) now matters more. The number is "for now": it is the one to tune, ideally after a sim (docs/BALANCE.md).
323. **Kesh's pack grows back after Overflow (Robert, 2026-10-27: "with the overflow change, it seems kesh's hound needs more health again. lets bump it to 8 and let her have 3 minions again, they should have 4 damage again. and lets bring back litter ... two hounds ... but no barrier added. and rally the pack provides all allies 4 barrier") →** the Hound has 8 HP and hits for 4 again (Hound+ 12 and 6); Kesh holds 3 companions at once again (her `maxAllies` override is removed; the per-Riftwalker cap from #315 stays for later). Litter is back (uncommon, 2 Mana): summons 2 Hounds, nothing else, and is offered again. Rally the Pack: a Hound, and each of your allies gains 4 Barrier (was 3). Fell the Prey stays switched off. Reverts parts of #314-317 on the Hound's numbers; the Gnashling is again unused (Call Gnashlings and Heel stay retired).
324. **Kesh's three basics eased (Robert, 2026-10-27: "Rend should only lose 2 hp and blood frenzy should lose 1 hp. savage bite up to 8 damage again") →** Rend: Deal 9, lose 2 HP (was 3), gain 1 Might; Rend+ deals 12, still loses 2. Blood Frenzy: Deal 4 to all enemies, lose 1 HP (was 2), gain 1 Might; Blood Frenzy+ deals 7 and gains 2 Might, still losing 1. Savage Bite: Deal 8 with Lifesteal 50%, 10 upgraded. These are the three that start in her deck (with Strike x4 and Defend x3).
325. **Kesh's rare summons told apart, and a cheap Pup (Robert, 2026-10-27: "the other Rare summons for Kesh need buffs to both hp and damage. they also seem similar except for the cost. lets make den mother an aura that if any minion dies then the den mother strikes back for a good amount of damage ... its upgrade should increase the damage, not reduce cost or add a tag. Alpha Call should summon the Alpha with 16hp and 10 damage, and gain 1 might on summon. there should be some kind of 1 cost summon pup, and there should be a dire wolf summon thats higher cost") →** **Alpha Call** (rare, 2): summons the Pack Alpha, now 16 HP and 10 damage (was 14 and 5), and gains 1 Might (as before; Alpha Call+ 20 HP, 12 damage, 2 Might). **Den Mother** (rare, 3 Mana) is an Aura: whenever one of your allies dies, deal 15 damage to a random enemy; the upgrade deals 20 (a custom upgrade: same cost, no Hold or Opening hand). **Dire Wolf** (new, rare, 3): summons the Dire Wolf, buffed to 24 HP and 14 damage (was 18 and 9; the + card 28 and 16), the big body for the cost. **Whelp** (new, common, 1): summons a Pup (6 HP, 3 damage; Whelp+ 10 and 5). Trigger is `allyDies`: any of her companions that falls, including to Overflow; one released to make room is not a death. Numbers are a first guess.
326. **Summon Pup, a Pup that never outgrows the Hound, and Den Mother hits back at the attacker (Robert, 2026-10-27: "the whelp card should be Summon Pup and the upgrade shouldnt make it stronger than the hound ever. instead of den mother returning damage to a random enemy, make it the enemy that attacked its ally") →** (a) The 1 Mana card is called **Summon Pup** (id `summon-pup`, was Whelp; it never shipped). (b) The Pup's "+" is 8 HP and 3 damage (a `PLUS_STATS` table in units.ts; the usual +4 HP and +2 damage would have made it 10 and 5), so it stays at or below the Hound (8 HP, 4 damage) however it is upgraded; Summon Pup+ brings it. (c) **Den Mother** deals 15 (20 upgraded) to the enemy that killed the ally, not a random one. A companion killed by an attack remembers who killed it (`Unit.killedBy`), and a trigger that fires when an ally dies knows that attacker; an Aura that answers "an ally dies" by striking its `attacker` does nothing when nothing killed it (a sacrifice, a Trap, Doom), or when the killer is already dead. The strike counts Might like any damage (Kesh's trait gives her 1, so it hits for 16). The content validator accepts an `attacker` target in an Aura that answers an ally dying.
327. **A sanctified card is final (Robert, 2026-10-27: "make sure sanctified cards cant be sanctified anymore ... they shouldnt be able to be modified permanently after being sanctified even with tags") →** the dice shrine and the Sanctum already refused a card with a roll; the gap was tags: the Gambler's chisel (`engravable`) and the engraver events (`chooseable(..., 'mark')`, checked again when applied) now leave out a sanctified card, as they already left out a tagged one. The order that is still open: tag first, then sanctify (a card with a tag can still be rolled by the dice). Upgrading a sanctified card is not changed (an upgrade is not a gamble); say if that should be final too.
328. **Valeria's Devotion build path gets its own resource (Robert, 2026-10-27: "the knight class doesnt have a valid 3rd build path. devotion seems to be for healing, and i just dont see how its actulaly good"; "lets add Devotion as a new resource similar to Arcana for Obsidia. With Devotion, once it gets to a high enough number, you can cash in for big damge") →** the old Devotion cards healed and gave Shielded but nothing paid off healing, a heal did nothing at full HP, and two of its cards (Sunforged Edge, Last Stand) wanted her hurt; it failed the build path test (#255). Now **Devotion** is a status on Valeria (`devotion`, lasts the fight): her trait Oathkeeper gains her that much Devotion **whenever she heals, even at full HP** (the whole amount counts, so no heal is wasted). Spenders pay it like Arcana, shown as a gold badge under the Mana cost: **Lightbrand** (common, 1 Mana + 5 Devotion: deal 14), **Hymn of Reckoning** (uncommon, 1 Mana + 8: 12 to every enemy), **Sunfall** (rare, 2 Mana + 10: deal 20 +2 per Devotion left, then lose it all, so 2 damage per Devotion you had). **Sunforged Edge** now deals 8 +1 per 2 Devotion (keeps it); **Last Stand** moves to Bulwark. Engine pieces, all generic: a `heal` trigger (fires for the whole heal, past full HP too) with the metric `healed`; a card field `statusCost` (stacks of a status on your hero, paid on top of the Mana); `scaleStatus` with `mult: 0` reads "Lose all your X". The gain lives in the trait, not the engine, so only Valeria builds Devotion; cards with a `statusCost` are never Rift-crossed (they would be dead in another deck). Numbers are a first pass, no sim run yet.
329. **Kesh's stage 1 brought into line with the others (Robert, 2026-10-27: "test each characters stage 1 clear ... i just want them all to be in the same ball park. the player should need to get a little lucky and should have a hard time beating the boss if they didnt get any decent cards beyond the starter deck"; "all three") →** the new `pnpm stage1sim` (a first run's stage 1, tier 1, Normal, for every Riftwalker and starting kit, played by a drafting bot and by one that never takes a card) had Kesh's Pack and Feast kits clearing 90-95% with the starter deck alone. Three changes, tested one at a time: **Pack Instinct heals 2 after a won fight** (was 6; on its own this barely moved him), **the Pack kit has Savage Command instead of Rally the Pack and the Feast kit Gnaw instead of Devour** (a second Hound or Devour from the first fight was the real cause; both stay in his reward pool), and **no Might at the start of a fight** (was 1). Result (60 runs per kit, drafting / starter only): Bloodrage 32% / 25%, Pack 27% / 18%, Feast 25% / 15%, against 72-73% / 90-95% before. The trait still helps all three build paths through the shared Might. A trait whose only effects are `shareMight` or `healAfterFight` now counts as doing something (validator and test).
330. **Copies by rarity (Robert, 2026-10-27: "only 2 duplicates allowed per deck, and uncommons can have 3, commons can have 4") →** a deck takes at most 4 of a common, 3 of an uncommon, 2 of a rare (and 1 of a Legendary or Ancestral); a card and its "+" share the limit; basics have none (`COPIES_BY_RARITY` / `maxCopiesOf` in `@sr/content/v2`, used by `canAdd`). As with the Legendary limit (#238), a deck checked without the rarity limits (an Arena entry, a ghost, a Vault deck built before) keeps the old flat 4, so no saved deck is refused; it only takes no more.
331. **Continue on top of the fight result (Robert, 2026-10-27: "the victory recap menu should have the Continue button on top of the other replay related options") →** Continue is full width on top; Recap and battle log, Look at the board and Watch the replay sit in a row under it (was side by side, #290).
332. **Devotion beside Mana, a Devotion kit that can spend it, the paladin's judgments, a build path filter (Robert, 2026-10-27: "display it next to her mana just like we do for Arcana"; "her devotion starter deck should come with a card that can spend it. nothing overpowering but a decent amount of damage"; "attacks that spend devotion called stuff like Hand of God or Hand of Justice, or other paladin related attacks"; "the card library grimoire should have a filter for build path too") →** a gold Devotion counter sits beside the Mana orb (next to Arcana if both show) for a Riftwalker whose trait builds it. The Devotion kit is Heartening Hymn + **Hymn of Reckoning**, now 1 Mana + 6 Devotion for 10 to every enemy (was 8 for 12), so one Heartening Hymn (heal 6) pays for it; Ward of Ages leaves the kit. New spenders: **Hand of Justice** (common, 1 Mana + 4: deal 10, Exposed 1), **Consecrated Blade** (uncommon, 2 Mana + 8: deal 9 twice, 6 Barrier), **Hand of God** (rare, 2 Mana + 15: deal 36). The card library has a Build path filter once a Riftwalker is chosen (each of their three, or Shared). The "Fits your Devotion" reward hint already worked (it reads the build path of every card).
333. **Stage 1 balance targets, three-card kits, no heal for Kesh (Robert, 2026-10-27: "lets just give every starting kit 3 uncommon cards to start ... make sure they all fall with the 25% chance to reach the boss with the starter kit, and beating the boss should even be a little lower"; "they shouldnt really be able to beat the boss but i would like them to be around 10-20% if its possible. and with some luck they should be able to beat the boss closer to 50%"; "we can get rid of the heal after every fight for the hunter") →** the targets for a first run's stage 1 (tier 1, Normal), measured with `pnpm stage1sim -- --pilot smart`: with the starting kit alone about 25% of runs reach the Warden and 10-20% beat it; a run that drafts well beats it about half the time. The sim now plays fights with the smart bot (`--pilot smart`, plans a whole turn) as well as the greedy one, which undersold decks that need setup (stances, Arcana, totems), and has experiment knobs (`--duelHp/--duelDmg/--wardenHp/--wardenDmg`). Kits have three cards (see #335 for their make-up). Kesh's Pack Instinct no longer heals after a fight (it was 6, then 2 in #329).
334. **Pools trimmed to the 8/7/5/1 rule (Robert, 2026-10-27: "trim each pool down to about 70"; "every character build path should have 8 common cards, 7 uncommon cards, 5 rares, and 1 legendary"; "definitely dont want any near duplicates and most of them should fall into one of the build paths") →** 298 cards are retired (`packages/content/src/v2/retired.ts`, read through `RETIRED_CARDS`/`canonicalCard`): every near-duplicate pair lost one card, over-full build paths were cut, and each retired card becomes a card of the same build path in any old save, Arena deck or ghost (keeping its upgrade, roll and tag). Every remaining card has a build path: the three Legendaries of each Riftwalker one per path, and the old glue cards placed where they fit (`BY_ID` in tools/src/paths.ts). Rarities were re-tiered inside each path to 8/7/5 keeping the order (a path's strongest rares stay rare; its weakest uncommons became common), 127 cards in all. Short of the rule and needing new cards: KP's Gun (17 + 1, with Rolling Thunder and Pick Up the Brass moved in from Shells), Ilvane's Whittling (17 + 1). `pnpm poolaudit` writes docs/POOL_AUDIT.md (each pool by build path, near-duplicates flagged; what it still flags are cards of one shape that do different things). Rummage stays (the only card that fetches to the draw pile).
335. **A kit is two uncommons and a common (Robert, 2026-10-27: "lets make the starting kits 2 uncommons and 1 common card a pre-built kit to get started with a build path") →** each kit keeps its two uncommons and adds a simple common of its build path that shows the plan (Valeria's Devotion: Heartening Hymn, Hymn of Reckoning, Lightbearer; KP's Gun: Reload, Glass Cover, Pistol Whip). Tested in kits.test.ts.
336. **No copy limit (Robert, 2026-10-27: "id also like to remove the limit on the number of duplicate cards a deck can have") →** a deck may hold any number of copies of a card; a Legendary or Ancestral card is still one of a kind (and a deck still holds one Legendary and two Ancestral cards). Replaces the limits by rarity of #330. The Arena keeps its sanity cap of 10 copies of one card.
337. **The 8/7/5/1 rule is a minimum, glue cards come back, no common doubler, 5 copies at most (Robert, 2026-10-27: "any card that doubles something probably shouldnt be common"; "i wanted to give the 8 7 5 1 rule as a rule of thumb, those should be treated as minimums. the full character decks should still include glue cards, but preferably dont exceed 70 as a rough cap"; "if we are going to limit card duplicates in arena then we may as well enforce that in the campaign as well. no more than 5 copies of a card in a deck") →** every build path holds at least 8 commons, 7 uncommons, 5 rares and 1 Legendary (so at least 63 cards in paths), and a pool may also hold glue cards with no build path, about 70 cards in all. To get there: 20 trimmed cards came back to fill paths (Valeria's Shield Bash, Iron Tithe, Gilded Wall, Endless Vigil, Ember Ward, Mirror Shield, Radiant Oath; Rivet's Scrap Volley, Sync Pulse, Overload, Hardened Chassis; Vesper's Spotlight; Ilvane's Arc Flash, Lattice Collapse, Tuning Fork, Rune Carapace; KP's Birdshot; Dorian's Creeping Dread, Thrall's Cue, Ghostform), 9 new cards fill the two paths that had nothing to bring back (KP's Gun: Buttstroke, Spare Barrel, Gun Oil, Dead Eye; Ilvane's Whittling: Antler, Serpent and Moth Carvings, Whittler's Kit, Endless Whittling), the old glue cards are glue again, and the six Riftwalkers without any got three general cards back each (`NO_PATH` in tools/src/paths.ts). Pools are now 63-71 cards. Hasten the End (a Fate doubler) is uncommon; no doubler is common. A deck holds at most 5 copies of a card (`MAX_COPIES`), basics included, in a run and in the Arena alike (an event that forces a Wound or Static on you is not a deck choice and is not counted); a Legendary or Ancestral card is still one of a kind. Replaces #336.
338. **Every Legendary is a build-around for its build path (Robert, 2026-10-27: "i do want the legendaries to be associated with a build path and they need to be good. a legendary that adds only barrier for one turn is a horrendous waste of a legendary") →** 14 reworked (ids kept): Valeria's Bastion of Dawn becomes **Dawn's Mercy** (an Aura: every heal also strikes a random enemy for the whole amount healed, and she heals 3 every turn); The Last Oath (Fortified, and +2 Thorns every turn); Kesh's Apex Maw (grows with cards consumed, not Might) and Bloodmoon Hunt (two Hounds, 3 Might to the pack, the pack acts); Vesper's Masquerade (the Mana you keep strikes every enemy at turn end, and your hand is held) and The Long Ambush (also Exposes every enemy); Sylas's Death by Inches (hits twice, twice more per card discarded this turn); Ilvane's The Spirit Council (three Carvings in hand every turn); Sehra's Eye of the Storm (in Fury every turn, and it strikes every enemy and gives Barrier); Obsidia's Volcano's Birth (a Mana more every turn instead of 20 Barrier) and Shatterstorm (an Aura: Arcana every turn and a strike on every enemy for each Aura you have); Dorian's Grave Harvest (an Aura: every card consumed strikes and curses every enemy); KP's The Twelve-Gauge Opera (Sharpen 6, and every shot Sharpens again and gives Barrier) and The Glass Palace (also cuts every enemy whenever he hurts himself). The others already were.
339. **Every pool at 70 with uncommon and rare glue (Robert, 2026-10-27: "lets get every character to 70 by adding more uncommon and rare cards that have no build path. every character should have at least 1 rare card with no build path, preferably 2") →** 26 trimmed cards came back as glue, two rares for each Riftwalker. Superseded in size by #341.
340. **Kesh's Feast becomes Berserker (Robert, 2026-10-27: "replace the barbarian consume build with berserker where she gets mana and draw to get through cards faster and burstier"; asked which, he chose Kesh's Feast) →** Berserker: gain Mana, draw hard, play many cards in one turn; its attacks grow with every card played that turn. 16 new cards (Rush In, Pounding Heart, Wild Swings, Shake Loose, Adrenal Surge, Blur of Claws, Battle Rhythm, Reckless Abandon, Burst of Speed, Rip and Tear, Berserk Tempo, Red Frenzy, Bloodstorm, Unbridled, Rising Howl, Killing Spree), Frenzied Gulp back; Feral Instinct, Blood Pact, Throw Scraps, Crimson Pact and Bone Offering stay as Berserker cards; Devour is glue, Devour Ally goes to Pack, Gnarltooth Trap and Gut Trap to Bloodrage; the other Feast cards retire into look-alikes. Apex Maw is **Apex Frenzy** (2 hits, 2 more per card played this turn, Lifesteal 50%, Consume). Kit: Blur of Claws, Reckless Abandon, Rush In.
341. **20 commons, 30 uncommons, 25 rares and 3 Legendaries per Riftwalker (Robert, 2026-10-27: "i would like ours to be more like slay the spire, rare cards are build defining and way more fun than common cards ... 20 commons, 30 uncommons, and 25 rares per character. try to split them between the build paths respectively"; the Legendaries come on top: 78 cards) →** every build path holds 6 commons, 9 uncommons, 7 rares and 1 Legendary, and the glue cards 2/3/4 (Legendaries need not be tied to a path but all have one). Reached by hand, per Riftwalker: 126 rarity changes (a path's strongest commons up to uncommon, its build-defining uncommons up to rare), 59 trimmed cards back (rares only where a card defines a build; no near-duplicate came back), 16 new cards where a path had nothing to bring back (Knotted Briars; Whittle Down, Carver's Rhythm, Kindled Rite, Smoke Offering; Thousand Palms, Unbroken Calm; Sagitta, Aquila, Starlit Duel; Double Tap, Bandolier, Scattergun Symphony, Ricochet; Death's Ledger, Soul Chain). Replaces the 8/7/5/1 minimum of #337 and the 70 of #339.
342. **The build path on the card (Robert, 2026-10-27: "lets add the build path to the top right of the cards. so it just reads like Devotion there") →** a card of a build path shows its name in a small label at the top right of the art, under the cost gem; basics, glue cards and tokens show none.
343. **Kesh's new build path is called Predator (Robert, 2026-10-27: "lets rename the new berserker build for kesh to Predator") →** the build path of #340 reads **Predator** everywhere a player sees it (character picker, card label, hints); the cards keep their names. Review fixes in the same batch: four retired cards that pointed at a Feast card retired later now point at live cards, and `canonicalCard` follows such a chain (with a test); the new Whittling attack is **Notch and Cut** (it shared a name with Whittle Down); Apex Frenzy gains one hit per card played (was two); Berserker (worse than Berserk Tempo), Calibrate (a small Reinforce) and Forge Titan (a near Titan Forge) went back out for Hunger Unbound, Patch Job and Relay Network; the build path label on cards is larger.
344. **Prompts for the card art still missing (Robert, 2026-10-27: "lets get the artwork done. give me some prompts like before for the remaining card art") →** `pnpm cardprompts:missing` lists every card a player can see that has no picture of its own: no art at all, or only a borrowed copy of another card's picture (a card that took a retired card's picture in #339's art reuse, when that card came back later). Same prompt wording, style anchor and 3 x 3 sheets as before, grouped by Riftwalker with the best cards first, sheets named `<hero>-mNN` and added to docs/card-art/sheets.json so `tools/pack-cards.ps1` cuts them last (a new picture replaces a borrowed copy). docs/card-art/missing.md holds every sheet, missing-batch-NN.md the NN-th sheet of each character. The existing sheets were re-cut first, which gave the cards that came back their own pictures again.
345. **Spin to win, the campaign header, the Arena badge, the path label at the bottom (Robert, 2026-10-27: "the spin to win needs to present the 3 rift crossed cards all in a row, they are stacked on each other right now"; "the campaign header has elements wrapping on smaller screens"; "get rid of the button that says arena deck: tier x stage x ... something that simply indicates an arena deck, that doesnt take as much space"; "lets move the build path label to the bottom left of the cards") →** the Brass Reel's choose-a-card prize lays its cards side by side (a CSS rule order bug gave it a one-card width); the campaign header is one line when it fits, two lines below 78rem (who and buttons on top, stats under them) and three on a phone (who, buttons, stats); the Arena deck button is a small ⚔ badge beside Deck whose tip holds the details and links to the saved decks (faded until the deck is first saved); the build path label sits at the bottom left of a card, just right of the HP badge on a Summon card.
346. **Shielded shows as a yellow bubble (Robert, 2026-10-27: "units that are shielded need a better indicator, so lets add yellow bubble barrier around the character that goes away after the shielded buff goes away") →** every unit with Shielded (hero, companion or enemy) has a glowing yellow bubble around its figure (`ShieldBubble`), thicker with 2 stacks or more; it pops in when Shielded arrives and is gone once the last stack is spent. Checked in a practice fight: Ward of Ages raises it, the next hit takes it away.
347. **Fortified is on or off (Robert, 2026-10-27: "any cards that apply fortified should simply say Apply Fortified instead of Apply 1 Fortified. also make sure the effect doesnt stack with multiple sources of Fortified") →** a unit holds at most 1 Fortified (a second source adds nothing; the engine already only asked whether it had any), and card text reads "Gain Fortified" ("Apply Fortified" on another unit). The Devotion orb's caption was also made to fit (it read "EVOTION").
348. **Sunfall and Sunforged Edge spend X Devotion (Robert, 2026-10-27: "Sunfall should be able to spend X devotion, same with sunforged blade") →** like an X card spends all its Mana, both spend all your Devotion and grow with it: Sunfall deals 10 +2 per Devotion (no minimum any more; was 10 Devotion for 20 +2 per Devotion left), Sunforged Edge 6 +1 per Devotion (it used to keep the Devotion). The text says "Spend all your Devotion".
349. **Unbroken Wall costs 3, Retribution's upgrade is clear (Robert, 2026-10-27: "lets make Unbroken Wall cost 3 and upgraded cost 2. fortified is huge so it needs a little nerf. Retribution card upgrade reads confusingly") →** Unbroken Wall costs 3 (2 upgraded; was 2 and 1). Retribution costs 2 and 1 upgraded (Robert: "making its cost 0 is too strong ... make its cost 2 and upgraded it goes to 1"; was 1, and its upgrade added a flat 3), so both versions read "Deal damage equal to 2 times your Thorns" (the old upgrade read "Deal 3 (+2 per Thorns) damage", the same thing in other words; a bigger multiplier would break the payoff guard rail for a card that is not consumed).
350. **A refused board report no longer uses up the limits (Robert, 2026-10-27: "why wouldnt my campaign run for rivet show up on the leaderboard" ... "i am sharkbait. i had a rivet run that got to stage 2 of tier 1") →** the board's rate limiter counted every attempt, refused ones too, and the app repeats a report it has no answer to (after every stop, three retries, and every time the tab is looked at again), so a few runs the board had not taken could use up a player's 10 new runs for the day and the hourly report limit, after which every new run was dropped without a trace until midnight UTC. The board now checks a limit first (`hasRoom`) and counts only a report it lets through: a new run counts against the day's new runs once it is recorded, a progress report once it is accepted. Checked on a local server: after 45 refused tries of an 11th new run, progress on an earlier run is still counted (it was refused before). A run that is still in progress shows up the next time it is opened once this is live; one that ended while it was being refused cannot be recovered.
351. **The Bear totem gives 2 Barrier a turn (Robert, 2026-10-27: "ilvanes bear totem should give 2 shield per turn") →** its passive is 2 Barrier at the end of your turn (was 1; read as Barrier, the shield of every turn: Shielded, which ignores a whole hit, would be far too much every turn). Its Awaken stays 6 Barrier.
352. **Every Warden leaves a Gift, the last one too, and a Gift always has cards (Robert, 2026-10-27: a friend finished a stage and got no reward; "lets make sure the stage 3 warden offers a reward. there should always be cards to offer, why wouldnt a warden have at least a card reward?") →** the stage 3 Warden used to go straight to "The Heart of the Rift is silent" with no card (only shards, Max HP, Resolve, a heal and a small Legendary Boon chance): it now offers the Warden's Gift like the others (rare cards of your class, or one of two Warden's Hearts, or nothing), and the loop is cleared once you choose (`afterGift`). The Gift tops itself up from your uncommons, then commons, should every rare be at its 5-copy limit, so it is never empty; the old path that skipped an empty Gift also dropped the Hearts, and now only a Gift with neither cards nor Hearts is skipped. Tested (run.test.ts).
353. **Ilvane's Whittling becomes Elements (Robert, 2026-10-27: "i dont understand it, it seems like its just the totem build again? why cant we just bring over the claw build"; then "lets make it something spell based with elements, and the damage can scale off of spirit ... aoe spells, big burst damage spells ... lightning, ice, fire, water ... a defensive spell can be earth based. i would like one like Claw that increases in damage every time its used. and the spells could also include mana or card draw") →** Whittling had no verb of its own (its Carvings were named after the totems, two of them planted totems, and its payoffs were Sylas's and Kesh's shapes). Claw itself is not copied (DECISIONS #255: no famous card's name or effect), but its idea is: **Gathering Storm** gains 2 damage on every copy each time one is cast. **Elements**: lightning, ice, fire, water and earth spells whose damage and Barrier grow with Spirit (Ilvane's status, which also powers his totems), 6/9/7 like every build path: Spark Lash, Frost Needle, Ember Toss, Stone Skin, Clear Spring, Elemental Focus; Storm Call (lightning: every enemy, and Exposed), Gathering Storm, Glacial Ward, Tidecaller, Fire Bloom, Arc Lightning, Earthen Rampart, Inner Flame, Quench; Firestorm (fire: every enemy, and 1 Spirit for you), Skybreaker, Deep Freeze, Stormcrown, Mountain's Heart, Spiritfont, Prismatic Cascade. The Legendary is **Avatar of the Elements** (was The Spirit Council): 1 Spirit every turn and a strike on every enemy for all your Spirit. The 22 Whittling cards retire into look-alikes; the kit is Storm Call, Glacial Ward, Spark Lash. Sharpening a weapon stays Obsidia's and KP's; a card that grows only itself may belong to anyone (the pool test now says so), and its text reads "plus all it has grown".
354. **KP's Hangover becomes "drink now, pay tomorrow" (Robert, 2026-10-27: "what do we do about kp's hangover build path? is it any good? i dont really get the idea of it", then "i like that idea ... but i do want to include ways for him to gain hp back ... probably not lifesteal since that is the hunter way ... the heals on some of the cards should be quite nice, i like the idea of his damage/healing to be based on missing hp") →** the old path pulled two ways (missing-HP payoffs next to six heals that undid them, only two cards that hurt him) and overlapped Kesh's Bloodrage. New status **Hangover** (`hangover`, KP's alone): at the start of the unit's turn it loses that much HP, never below 1, then it clears; the loss counts as losing HP to your own cards (Mean Drunk, Glass Jaw, The Glass Palace). It is not a debuff (Steadfast does not eat a drink's price). Drinks: Liquid Lunch, One for the Road, Drunken Swing, Swig, Barroom Brawl, Black Out, Bottoms Up, Last Call. Payoffs: Bottle to the Head (+2 per Hangover), Bender (one more hit per Hangover), Haymaker (+3 per Hangover), Dutch Courage (2 Barrier per Hangover at turn end). Cures: Hair of the Dog (clear it, then 2 more), Sweat It Out (heal that much, clear it; Consumed), Pass Out. After review: Hangover stops at 10 (`HANGOVER_MAX`; at 1 HP a drink costs nothing, so the payoffs need a ceiling) and Swig is Consumed again. Hangover still owed when a fight ends is dropped, like Seared. Heals grow with missing HP (Sob Story, Liquid Dinner, Pass Out, Bottomless Flask), no lifesteal (Kesh's). Free drinks that draw or make Mana are consumed (no loops). Retired: Glass Trap, Glazier, Spare Change, Double Glazing, Trolley Barricade, Glass Underfoot. The id `hair-of-the-dog` was a retired card (into Swig) and is reused. Kit: Swig, Liquid Dinner, Bottle to the Head. Gathering Storm+ fix in the same batch: `forge` matches a card and its upgrade.
355. **Resolve heals you fully; Spirit has no cap (Robert, 2026-10-27: "lets have resolve full heal a player. no cap on spirit") →** a fight lost with Resolve left is retried at full HP (`RETRY_HP_PCT` 100; was the HP you entered with, at least half, #289). Spirit (`resonance`) stays uncapped and permanent, so Ilvane's Elements ramp in long fights is intended.
356. **A heal grows a companion instead of mending it; Valeria's and Rivet's Swarm kits named by Robert (2026-10-27: "i dont even want the overheal, i just would rather change heals to minions to be either max hp increases or barriers instead"; the kits: "for valeria bulwark, bulwark sweep, shield charge, and tower shield. for the thorns build, barbed oath, mirrored aegis, thorn volley. for devotion, lightbreaker, sacred vow, and hymn of reckoning ... for rivet swarm, breach charge, overcharge blast, and call turret"; "dont touch assemble, that can just be the utility spec") →** every heal on an ally (kind `ally`) gives it that much max HP and HP (the same as `growHp`), never just HP; the hero heals as before. Max HP was chosen over Barrier: it lasts, so it rewards keeping a companion alive. The heal event carries the new `maxHp`. Card text says "Heal N. Your companions gain N max HP."; Upgrade Chip and Patch Job say growHp outright; Rivet's Tinkerer reads "you heal 1 and your companions gain 1 max HP". Kits: Bulwark = Bulwark Sweep, Shield Charge, Tower Shield; Thorns = Mirrored Aegis, Thorn Volley, Barbed Oath; Devotion = Sacred Vow, Hymn of Reckoning, Lightbearer ("Lightbreaker" read as Lightbearer, the one already in the kit; Lightbrand was tried and did worse); Swarm = Overcharge Blast, Call Turret, Breach Charge. Assemble keeps its cards: a support build path for the other two, measured unfairly alone against a boss. `pnpm kittry` tries named kits against the `pnpm bosssim` Warden.
357. **Devotion costs are a plain number, Sunfall and Sunforged Edge cost "X", Sunfall strikes two enemies (Robert, 2026-10-27: "for the devotion number on the cards, just get rid of the icon ... for sunforged blade, please add the devotion cost like the others one just the X ... sunfall as well. sunfall should target 2 random enemies"; "did i say sunfall should hit twice?"; "i dont want the text to read 'spend all devotion' thats what the number indicator is for") →** the Devotion cost badge shows only its number (no ⚜). `statusCost` gains `x` (amount 0): the badge reads X, the card's text drops "Spend all your Devotion" (the effects still spend it). Sunfall hits 2 *different* random enemies once each (new damage flag `distinct` with `randomFoe`: each hit finds an enemy not struck yet, and a lone enemy is struck once), at the same 10 +2 per Devotion as before.
358. **Rivet's Swarm and Core feel different (Robert, 2026-10-27: "i dont see the difference in the other two ... can you sharpen up those two paths so they actually feel different") →** Swarm goes wide: everything that counts allies, triggers on a summon or a death, or sacrifices, plus Might for every friend; Heavy Plating (on summon), Repair Bot (a small body) and Reactor (Might to all) move from Core to Swarm. Core goes tall: single-ally upgrades, max HP growth (every heal grows a companion, #356), revives, the big engines, and payoffs that read your biggest companion: new metric `biggestAlly` (max HP of your biggest living companion); new cards Crankshaft (C, 4 +1 per 4 of it), Reinforced Frame (C, an ally gains 5 max HP, draw 1), Heavy Hitter (R, damage equal to it); Reactor Meltdown deals damage equal to the sacrificed ally's HP to every enemy (was a flat 25), so it cashes in a grown construct. Retired: Salvage Bot (into Repair Bot), Swarm Protocol (into Drone Carrier; a third "summon two drones"), Scrap Volley (into Formation Strike; a plain attack). Assemble unchanged (#356). Counts stay 6/9/7 + Legendary per build path.
359. **Rivet's cheaper companions have less HP (Robert, 2026-10-27: "we need to treat hp for the minions like a barrier for the player ... some of rivets cheaper summons probably need a slight nerf of hp. they need to be in line with other forms of barrier/healing"; "plus the bots can do damage so thats even another argument for nerfing the hp") →** a companion's HP lasts until it is broken (Barrier falls each turn), it acts every turn, and every heal now grows it (#356), so a 1-Mana body sits under a Defend's 5: Patrol Drone 7 → 5, Micro Drone 4 → 3, Sentry Turret 10 → 7 (it has Taunt and gives Barrier), Repair Bot 6 → 4, Salvage Bot 6 → 4, Enforcer Mk.II 14 → 12. Unchanged: the Drone Rivet starts with (8, free and her identity), Siege Engine 16 and War Engine 22 (Core's big machines, meant to be grown). Other Riftwalkers' companions are not touched. A "+" companion still has 4 more HP.
360. **Ilvane hits a little harder (Robert, 2026-10-27: "i think ilvane needs a slight damage boost across the board"; on upgrades, "for auras lets leave it as it was") →** every damage number on Ilvane's cards gains 1 (32 numbers, per hit on multi-hit cards, and in custom upgrades so a "+" stays better), and the totems: Hawk 4 a turn (was 3) and Awaken 10 (9); Splinter Awaken 7 (6). Splinter's passive stays 2 (it hits every enemy every turn). Damage "equal to" Spirit, Auras or totems is unchanged. Upgrades: a proposed rule (common/uncommon Auras never get cheaper) was tried and dropped at Robert's word; the upgrade rule is as it was. The full list of cost-lowering upgrades is in docs/UPGRADE_COST_AUDIT.md.
361. **Rivet keeps three "act now" cards; Assemble charges companions up instead (Robert, 2026-10-27: "im worried about this rivet character and all of these allies can act now and some have that text duplicated a number of times on a single card. id rather ... only 2 or 3 and they shouldnt repeat on the same card"; then "yea lets do this build") →** of Rivet's 22 cards that made allies act (7 of them two or three times), only Assemble (act, draw 1), Relay Strike (6 damage, act) and Command Chip (act whenever you play a Summon) still do, once each. The rest give companions **Primed** (`vigor`): engine change, a companion's (or any non-hero unit's) strike spends its Primed, as an enemy's always did. Ping 2 Primed (Consume), Jumpstart 3 + 4 Barrier, Overdrive 1 Might + 2, Arc Relay 6 to all + 3, Overwatch / Clockwork Trap 3 instead of acting, Echo Command 5 (Consume), Chain Reaction draw 2 + 4, Double Time 6, Overload 10 (Consume), Perpetual Motion 2 a turn, Relay Network 3 per Trap sprung, Link Up 4 Barrier to all + draw 1, Sync Pulse heal 3 + draw 1, Surge Protocol X Might, Grand Assembly 2 Might (Consume). Legendaries: Overclocked Legion is an Aura (2 Might to all now, 3 Primed every turn); The Foundry Awakens gives 3 Primed instead of acting; Drone Uprising's drones arrive with 3 Primed. Card text: "Each of your companions gains 3 Primed."; scaled statuses read "Apply Might equal to the Mana spent".
362. **No limit of three build paths (Robert, 2026-10-28: "lets drop the idea that characters in this game only have 3 build paths, we will revisit that later") →** `Riftwalker.buildPaths` is a list of any length (was a tuple of three), `pathSizes` returns one count per path, and the tests ask for at least three paths of at least 12 cards each instead of exactly three. Valeria has five (#364); everyone else still has three. (The three starting kits this note first kept were replaced by a choice of two uncommons in #365.)
363. **Legendary cards are put away for now (Robert, 2026-10-28: "lets also do away with legendary cards for now, we will add those once the base set of common/uncommon/rare is balanced. you can just move the current legendaries to rare for as long as there are no duplicates") →** every card that was Legendary is a Rare (no id or name clashed with a Rare of the same pool); `SHELVED_LEGENDARIES` (`packages/content/src/v2/shelved.ts`) lists them for when Legendary returns, and `lendLegendaries` makes them Legendary again for the length of a test file so the rules around a Legendary (one per deck, the Warden's Gift chance, the reliquary event, the Arena's limit) stay tested. Valeria's Dawn's Mercy went with Devotion (#364), so she has two. The 1% and 3% of card rewards that rolled Legendary go to Rare (duel 55/35/10, Elite 35/42/23); the Warden's Gift offers no Legendary; the Brass Reel's top prize is a rare card of yours; "A Legend's Price" (15 HP for a pick of three Legendary cards) is not offered; the reliquary of legends cannot be met. Legendary **Boons** are not touched. Engine and campaign code that mentions Legendary stays, so bringing it back is a data change. Note: Discover (`discoverOp`) used to skip Legendary cards; it now sees these Rares.
364. **Valeria becomes the Ironclad shape, Devotion is removed (Robert, 2026-10-28: "for the knight class in my game, i want to get rid of devotion entirely. our knight should more closely resemble the ironclad with some different mechanics, but i do want to have a lot of the same cards for now, then i can alter them to make it a little different. so things like all the strikes, whirlwind, draw/card manipulation") →** an on-purpose exception to the roster rule against copying another game's card effects, for the knight only and until Robert has changed them. Names are all original; the shapes follow the Slay the Spire 2 Ironclad list. **Devotion** is gone from the engine (`devotion` status and metric, the heal trigger, the Devotion counter, tips and card orbs; `statusCost` stays as an unused generic cost) and so are the healing and judgment cards that fed it (30 cards, all mapped to a card of the same job in `retired.ts`, so old saves load). **Starter:** 5 Strike, 4 Defend, Sundering Blow (the Bash: 8 damage, 2 Exposed, 2 Mana), 80 HP, Oathkeeper (6 Barrier, 6 Plating) minus the Devotion clause. **Five build paths:** Bulwark and Thorns (hers, kept), and new Might (Twin Cut, Braced Strike, Cleaving Throws, Hilt Strike, Clarion Clap, Rattle, Mastered Strike, Steel Resolve, Challenge, Intimidate, Break Guard, Rising Fist, Pile On, Whirling Blade, Mounting Fury, Hammer Fall, Warlord's Oath, Wildfire, Double Down, Tempered Fury), Consume (Sacrificial Guard, Scorching Blow, Searing Fist, Ram Home, Smoldering Blow, Grim Focus, Numbing Rite, Borrowed Blade, Rite of Recall, Unholy Pact, Burn It All, Forsake) and Blood (Draw Blood, Blood Plate, Breach, Reckless Smite, Scar Over, Blood Fire, Tear Flesh, Sanguine Rush, Crimson Offering, Bloodied Plate). Also new in Bulwark: Field Repairs, Provoke, Stoneskin; Bulwark Blow is now exactly Body Slam (damage equal to your Barrier). New engine piece: the **`strikes` metric** (cards with "Strike" in their name anywhere in your deck, for Mastered Strike's 6 +2 each). **Not copied because the engine has no piece for it:** Cascade, Havoc, Second Wind, Stoke, Primal Force, Drum of Battle, Expect a Fight, Forgotten Ritual, Pillage, Stomp, Unrelenting, Spite, Tear Asunder, Thrash, Mangle, Feed, Cruelty, Vicious, Colossus, Rage, Tank, Unmovable, Juggling, Hellraiser, Stampede, Aggression, Pact's End, Howl From Beyond, Demonic Shield. **Approximations:** Grim Focus (Battle Trance) and Draw Blood (Bloodletting) consume themselves (balance.test forbids a free card that draws or makes Mana and stays); Borrowed Blade (Infernal Blade) adds a random attack that costs 0 for the rest of the fight; Provoke is Taunt as an Exposed hit-and-guard; Intimidate and Break Guard count Exposed in turns. Numbers are the Ironclad's (StS2 v0.103.2) and untested: no sim was run. `RULES_VERSION` 20.
365. **Build paths are hidden for now, mechanics are shown instead, and a run starts with two uncommons you choose (Robert, 2026-10-28: "lets remove the build path thing for now, we will associate cards with build paths later. lets just hide that stuff for now"; "the starting hand just let the player choose two uncommon cards to start the run with"; "instead of displaying build path i would just like to show the mechanics the character uses"; "when ... a player is offered a chance at a card that fits their build path, lets just remove that for now, i have a different plan for that") →** the data stays (`Riftwalker.buildPaths`, `pathOf`, `paths.generated.ts`, `pnpm paths`) behind one switch, `BUILD_PATHS_SHOWN = false` in `@sr/content/v2`. While it is off: a card shows no build path label, the card library and the book have no build path filter or sections, the character picker, the home page and the Learn page show `Riftwalker.mechanics` (a short list of plain-word mechanics per character) instead, the "Fits your Bulwark" / "New build path" hint on offered cards is gone (`fitOf` answers null), and nothing is offered because of a build path (`mainPath` answers null: the merchant's stock is not bent towards one, and the trade events that need a main path, such as The specialist, cannot be met). The working versions are `fitOfPaths` and `mainPathOf`. **The three build-path kits are replaced** by `starters.ts`: at the start of a run you take any two of your Riftwalker's own uncommon cards; they replace two plain cards (an Attack a Strike, anything else a Defend), so the deck keeps its size. The run phase is still called `kit`; its context is `starterPick`; `chooseKit` is now `chooseStarters(run, picks?)` (the first two on offer when none are named, for bots and tests). A run saved at the old choice is given the new one on load. `kitsearch`, `kittry` and `kits.test.ts` are deleted; the sim tools try different pairs of uncommons instead of "kit a/b/c". Knight cards no longer have to fill build paths of 12 (`cards.test.ts` skips her).
366. **The rest of the Ironclad list, and a smaller knight (Robert, 2026-10-28: "can you just add all of those?"; "i thought the character had less total cards in its pool?") →** 29 cards were added for the Ironclad cards the engine could not do, and Valeria's own older cards were trimmed (31 retired into the nearest card kept, see `retired.ts`), so the pool is 95 cards instead of 125. **New engine pieces (all generic):** statuses `sapped` (its attacks deal less; a card can make it wear off at the end of the enemy's next turn: `temp: 'theirs'`), `cruelty` (+25% to Exposed enemies), `sturdy` (Exposed enemies hit you for half until their turn is over), `unmovable` (the first Barrier a card gives you each turn doubles) and `freeattack` (your next Attack costs 0); metrics `hpLostTurn`, `hpLostFight`, `exhaustedTurn`, `attacksInHand`, `nonAttacksInHand`, `exhaustedNow`, `exhaustedDamage`; the trigger `exposeFoe`; ops `playPile` (play the top of your draw pile, a random consumed card, a random Attack in hand, or the card just drawn, for free against a random enemy), `manaLock`, `drawLock`, `drawUntil`, `exhaustTop`, `transform`, `when` (an op that runs only while a metric is in range), `copyPlayed` and `maxHp`; `damage.onKill`; `exhaustCards` can take only, or all but, a card kind; `discover` can scale its count; `fetch` can upgrade; an Aura can be `temp` (ends with the turn); card fields `needsExhausted`, `cheaperPerAttack` and `replayFromExhaust`. Combat view gained `SideView.attacksPlayed`. **The cards (Ironclad card = ours):** Cascade = Unravel, Havoc = Wild Gamble, Second Wind = Bitter Breath, Stoke = Fan the Flames, Primal Force = Stone Fury (and its token Giant Rock), Drum of Battle = War Drums, Expect a Fight = Brace for Battle, Forgotten Ritual = Ritual of Ash, Evil Eye = Cold Stare, Pillage = Plunder, Stomp = Trampling Charge, Unrelenting = Relentless Push, Spite = Spiteful Jab, Tear Asunder = Rending Fury, Thrash = Flailing Blows, Mangle = Crippling Blow, Feed = Bloodfeast, Cruelty = Cruel Streak, Vicious = Scent of Weakness, Colossus = Giant's Stance, Rage = Berserker's Rage, Unmovable = Stone Stance, Stampede = Charging Herd, Hellraiser = Raised Hell, Juggling = Juggler's Rhythm, Aggression = Unforgiving Drill, Howl From Beyond = Howl of the Fallen, Pact's End = Ashen Reckoning, Not Yet = Rally. Battle Trance (Grim Focus) now draws 3 and locks drawing, and no longer consumes itself; Dismantle (Break Guard) now hits twice against an Exposed enemy; Infernal Blade (Borrowed Blade) still makes its Attack free for the rest of the fight, not only the turn. **Not copied:** Tank and Demonic Shield (they only mean something with a second player), Break (an event-only card in their game). **A max HP gain from Bloodfeast is kept for the run** (`finishDuel(..., maxHpGained)`, at most 30 per fight). **What was trimmed:** Valeria's Thorns went from 21 cards to 7 (Thornmail, Barbed Oath, Thorn Lash, Righteous Rebuke, Ember Ward, Retribution, The Last Oath) and her Bulwark cards that were not Ironclad shapes from about 18 to 5 (Stand Firm, Gilded Bastion, Plate Discipline, Anvil Strike, Citadel March); Dawn Smite and Oathbound stay as her glue. The older numbers in #364 describe the first pass. No sim was run.
367. **The knight is exactly the Ironclad's card list, as a yardstick (Robert, 2026-10-28: "i just wanted the knight class to have the same number of cards as ironclad"; "just remove her thorns and traps, and get the other ironclad cards in here to match exactly. i do plan to make alterations but i want this class as a reference point for balancing other classes") →** her 17 cards with no Ironclad counterpart are gone (Thornmail, Barbed Oath, Thorn Lash, Righteous Rebuke, Retribution, The Last Oath, Stand Firm, Gilded Bastion, Plate Discipline, Anvil Strike, Citadel March, Last Stand, Martyr's Grace, Tear Flesh, Sanguine Rush, Oathbound, and Tempered Fury, which is a Slay the Spire 1 card), each retired into the nearest card kept, and the three Ironclad cards she lacked are in: Anger = Hot Temper, Burning Pact = Pact of Embers, Pyre = Inner Furnace. She has 82 cards of her own (Sundering Blow is her Bash) plus Strike and Defend: the Ironclad's 87 (v0.103.2, Grapple removed) less Tank and Demonic Shield (they need a second player) and Break (an event card). The Giant Rock token is extra, as it is theirs. Rarities and numbers were checked against the Ironclad list and brought in line: Howl of the Fallen is Uncommon; Oath of Iron (Shrug It Off) no longer Holds; Bastion Engine (Juggernaut) deals 5 to a random enemy; Immovable Oath (Impervious) is 30 Barrier; Ember Ward (Flame Barrier) costs 2 for 12 Barrier and 4 back; Dawn Smite (Iron Wave) is 5 and 5. **Still not the same, by the engine or the guard rails:** Barricade is Fortified (keeps 75% of Barrier, not all); Bloodletting (Draw Blood) is consumed (a free card that makes Mana must be); Infernal Blade (Borrowed Blade) makes its Attack free for the fight; Molten Fist doubles Exposed up to +3; her trait is still Oathkeeper (6 Barrier, 6 Plating at the start) and not Burning Blood (heal 6 after a fight). Thorns are left only as the 4 back from Ember Ward; she has no Traps (cards.test exempts her from "everyone has a Trap"), no Legendary to lend (the shelved list lost The Last Oath), and her mechanics read Barrier, Might, Exposed, Consume, Self-damage.
368. **Sylas is the Silent's card list (Robert, 2026-10-28: "lets get some additional agents to do this same thing for the other slay the spire inspired characters ... i do mean close copies of the originals, but obviously change names and keep our theme, i will adjust things going forward, but we need good baselines right now") →** **Sylas is the Slay the Spire 2 Silent's card list, as a baseline (Robert, 2026-10-28: "close copies of the originals, but obviously change names and keep our theme, i will adjust things going forward, but we need good baselines right now") →** like Valeria (#364-#367), an on-purpose exception to the rule against copying another game's card effects, until Robert changes them. Names are all original and the theme stays his: Shivs are his **Sleeve Knives** (tagged `knife`; the Silent's Accuracy is Edge), Poison is **Bleed** (`seared`), Weak is Faltering, Vulnerable is Exposed, Dexterity is Bracing, Strength lost is Sapped, Artifact is Steadfast, and Sly is **Sly** (a new keyword: a card an effect discards from your hand is played for free; the end-of-turn discard does not count). **Source:** the Silent card list on slaythespire.wiki.gg as edited 2026-10-27, which includes the v0.110.0 beta branch (July 2026) changes: Haze costs 2 and applies Weak, Sidestep replaced Scare, Expertise draws 2 that Retain, Echoing Slash is Uncommon. Costs the wiki did not state were taken from metabot.gg card pages. (The knight was copied from v0.103.2; the two lists are about three patches apart.) **Bleed = Poison, checked:** at the start of the bleeding unit's turn it takes its stacks as unblockable damage, then loses 1, which is what Poison does. One difference: Bleed applied by an enemy follows the difficulty's damage percent (`scaledByDifficulty`), so it is a little gentler on Easy. **Starter:** 5 Strike, 5 Defend (Neutral), Hobbling Jab (Neutralize: 3 damage, 1 Faltering) and Duck and Weave (Survivor: 8 Barrier, discard 1), 12 cards (v2.test allows 12 for him). **70 HP**, the Silent's. **Trait:** Hidden Blades (a Sleeve Knife every turn) is kept, though it is not the Silent's Ring of the Snake (draw 2 more on the first turn): it is an extra Shiv every turn on top of the Silent's list, so it is the first thing to look at if he is too strong. **The cards (Silent card = ours):** basics Neutralize = Hobbling Jab, Survivor = Duck and Weave; token Shiv = Sleeve Knife (Shiv+ = Sleeve Knife+, 6 damage), Inky Shiv = Inked Knife. Commons: Anticipate = Sense the Blow, Backflip = Backspring, Blade Dance = Knife Juggle, Cloak and Dagger = Shroud and Shank, Dagger Spray = Blade Flurry, Dagger Throw = Flick Knife, Deadly Poison = Open Vein, Deflect = Turn Aside, Dodge and Roll = Roll Away, Flick-Flack = Cartwheel Cut, Leading Strike = Feint Strike, Piercing Wail = Chilling Shriek, Poisoned Stab = Barbed Stab, Predator = Stalker's Lunge, Prepared = Shuffle Up, Ricochet = Glancing Knives, Slice = Quick Slash, Snakebite = Viper's Kiss, Sucker Punch = Dirty Hit, Untouchable = Slip the Blow. Uncommons: Accelerant = Thin the Blood, Accuracy = Whetted Edge, Acrobatics = Nimble Deal, Backstab = Knife in the Back, Blur = Smoke Veil, Bouncing Flask = Tossed Vials, Bubble Bubble = Reopen the Wound, Calculated Gamble = All or Nothing, Dash = Darting Strike, Echoing Slash = Rippling Cut, Escape Plan = Bolt Hole, Expertise = Practiced Hands, Expose = Lay Bare, Finisher = Final Flourish, Flechettes = Needle Rain, Footwork = Fleet Step, Hand Trick = Palm Switch, Haze = Blood Mist, Hidden Daggers = Sleeve Drop, Infinite Blades = Bottomless Sleeves, Leg Sweep = Sweep the Leg, Memento Mori = Graveside Cut, Mirage = Red Mirage, Noxious Fumes = Weeping Cuts, Phantom Blades = Ghost Knives, Pinpoint = Nerve Strike, Pounce = Spring Attack, Precise Cut = Clean Cut, Reflex = Quick Reflexes, Sidestep = Side Slip, Skewer = Run Through, Speedster = Fleet Fingers, Strangle = Garrote, Tactician = Cool Head, Up My Sleeve = Spare Blades. Rares: Abrasive = Razor Skin, Adrenaline = Rush of Blood, Afterimage = Shadow Echo, Assassinate = Killing Stroke, Blade of Ink = Inked Blades, Bullet Time = Stilled Time, Burst = Double Trick, Corrosive Wave = Hemorrhage (now Red Tide, #472), Envenom = Serrated Edges, Fan of Knives = Wide Arc, Grand Finale = Final Bow, Knife Trap = Recall the Blades, Malaise = Sapping Wound, Master Planner = Grand Scheme, Murder = Slow Murder, Nightmare = Recurring Dream, Outbreak = Bloodburst, Serpent Form = Serpent's Coil, Shadow Step = Into the Shadows, Shadowmeld = Mantle of Night, Storm of Steel = Steel Monsoon, The Hunt = Marked for Death, Tools of the Trade = Nimble Routine, Tracking = Blood Scent, Well-Laid Plans = Patient Hands. That is 82 cards of his own (20 common, 35 uncommon, 25 rare, 2 basic) plus Strike, Defend and the two knife tokens. **Not copied:** Blade Symphony, Concoct, Fade, Flanking and Sneaky (multiplayer only: they need another player), and the Ancient cards Suppress and Wraith Form (an event's prize in their game, as the knight's Break is). **Removed:** all 79 of his older cards (and Death by Inches from `pools/legendary.ts`), each retired in `retired.ts` into the card of the same job (Keen Edge into Whetted Edge, Cash In into Bloodburst, Speed Reader into Fleet Fingers...); his three shelved Legendaries (Deck of a Thousand Knives, Death Warrant, Death by Inches) are gone, so `SHELVED_LEGENDARIES` has none for him and the campaign's legendary test exempts him as it does the knight. He has no Traps (cards.test exempts him). **New engine pieces (all generic):** card fields `sly`, `cheaperPerSkill`, `needsEmptyDraw`; card instance fields `sly` ('turn' or 'fight'), `costDown`, `freeTurn`, `held`; statuses `virulent` (Bleed on your enemies burns that many more times; Accelerant), `honed` (knives Hold, the first each turn hits harder; Phantom Blades), `fanned` (knives hit every enemy; Fan of Knives), `tracking` (+50% to Faltering enemies), `guarded` (Barrier at the start of your next turn; Dodge and Roll), `blur` (Barrier kept through one turn change), `meld` (Barrier gained doubled; Shadowmeld), `poised` and `empowered` (next turn your attacks deal double; Shadow Step), `strangled` (HP lost whenever the other side plays a card; Strangle), `freeskill` (next Skill costs 0; Pounce), `planner` (Skills you play gain Sly; Master Planner); metrics `skillsInHand`, `foesSeared`, `discardedNow`, `skillsDrawnNow`; triggers `hitFoe` (an Attack of yours took an enemy's HP; Envenom) and `drawMidTurn` (a draw that is not the turn's opening draw; Speedster); ops `tick` (Bleed burns now; Outbreak), `strip` (Barrier and Steadfast off; Expose), `selfCost` (Up My Sleeve), `freeHand` (Bullet Time), `giveSly` (Hand Trick), `copyNext` with a new choice kind `copy` (Nightmare); `damage.repeatOnKill` (Echoing Slash), `draw.hold` (Expertise), `playPile` `tag`/`all`/`aim`/`upgrade` (Knife Trap). The validator lets an Aura discard at random (Tools of the Trade) and lets `when`/`playPile` count as using the target. Card text: consecutive identical "to a random enemy" statuses read as one line ("Apply 3 Bleed to a random enemy 3 times."); a negative scale reads "13 (-2 per card in hand)". `SideView.skillsPlayed` was added for the cost shown on Nerve Strike. **Deviations from the Silent's numbers or text:** Bolt Hole (Escape Plan) is Consumed, as a free card that draws must be (balance.test); Palm Switch (Hand Trick) gives Sly to a random Skill, not a chosen one (a simplification: a choice of one card, like Recurring Dream's, could be added), and Nimble Routine (Tools of the Trade) discards a random card, because an Aura cannot ask the player; Sly given to one copy (Palm Switch, Grand Scheme) is not yet shown on that card in the hand (the card text reads the card, not the copy); Patient Hands (Well-Laid Plans) holds the whole hand, as the v0.110 text says ("you no longer discard your Hand"; the Slay the Spire 1 card kept 1), and its upgrade costs 0; Final Flourish (Finisher) is one hit of 6 per Attack, not 6 per Attack as separate hits; Empowered and Tracking also boost damage from Auras (Serpent's Coil), as Might does in this engine; Fanned knives spread their damage, not an Inked Knife's Faltering; Run Through+ (Skewer+) is X+1 hits, not 11 damage, as every X card's upgrade is; Marked for Death (The Hunt) has no extra card reward for a kill (there is no such reward in a fight; it is a plain 10 damage, Consume); Chilling Shriek (Piercing Wail) is Sapped until the end of the enemies' turn; Dexterity is Bracing, which also adds to Barrier from Auras such as Shadow Echo (Afterimage), where the Silent's Dexterity does not; Blood Scent and Grand Scheme upgrade to 1 Mana, as their originals do. No sim was run. `RULES_VERSION` should be bumped once on merge (not done here, to avoid clashing with the other branches).
369. **Obsidia is the Regent's card list (Robert, 2026-10-28: "lets get some additional agents to do this same thing for the other slay the spire inspired characters ... i do mean close copies of the originals, but obviously change names and keep our theme, i will adjust things going forward, but we need good baselines right now") →** 
370. **Dorian is the Necrobinder's card list (Robert, 2026-10-28: "lets get some additional agents to do this same thing for the other slay the spire inspired characters ... i do mean close copies of the originals, but obviously change names and keep our theme, i will adjust things going forward, but we need good baselines right now") →** **Dorian becomes the Necrobinder, with several minions (Robert, 2026-10-28: "close copies of the originals, but obviously change names and keep our theme, i will adjust things going forward, but we need good baselines right now"; "lets keep our ability to summon multiple minions") →** like Valeria (#364-#367), an on-purpose exception to the roster rule against copying another game's card effects, until Robert has changed them. Source list: the Slay the Spire 2 Necrobinder, **StS2 v0.111.0** (spire-codex.com data: 88 cards, names, costs, rarities, text and upgrades). Dorian has **85 cards of his own plus Strike and Defend** (Neutral) and two tokens (Gravesoul = her Soul, Baleful Glance = her Sweeping Gaze): her 88 less Strike and Defend (his are Neutral) and **Forbidden Grimoire** (an Ancient Power that removes a card from your deck after the fight; the fight engine has no piece for that). Kept although her game marks them multiplayer-only: Legion of Bone and Glimpse Beyond ("ALL players..." is simply you alone). Protector (an Ancient card) is a Rare. Every card's comment in `pools/necromancer.ts` names the card it copies. **Starter:** 4 Strike, 4 Defend, Raise Thrall (Bodyguard), Off the Leash (Unleash); **70 HP** (hers is 66, below the 70-90 every Riftwalker keeps); he starts with a 5-HP Bone Thrall (her Osty; her starting relic, which Summons 1 every turn, is not copied: his trait stays **Grave Chill**, which does not clash). Mechanics shown: Thralls, Fate, Souls, Fleeting. **How Osty became several minions:** her Summon N is **Raise N** (`raise` op): a new Bone Thrall rises with N HP while a companion slot is free (3 at most); with all three taken, the weakest companion gains N max HP instead (never a release prompt). Thralls never act on their own (`bone-thrall` has an empty act; an ally with nothing to do no longer acts or shows an intent). Every "Osty deals N" card is a **Command** card (`command` op, tag `command`): **each** living companion strikes, as the actor, so its own Might/Primed/Marrow count and "Osty's HP" is each Thrall's own HP; with no companion nothing happens. "Osty heals N" grows every companion; "Osty dies" sacrifices each (Bone Rain) or the chosen one (Fen Offering). Multiplying Osty's damage by the number of Thralls is the one big deviation to watch in balance. **The cards (Necrobinder card = ours):** Bodyguard = Raise Thrall, Unleash = Off the Leash, Afterlife = After the End, Blight Strike = Rot Strike, Defile = Desecrate, Defy = Grave Defiance, Drain Power = Leech Lore, Fear = Cold Dread, Flatten = Bone Crush, Grave Warden = Barrow Keeper, Graveblast = Grave Robbery, Invoke = Call at Dusk, Negative Pulse = Creeping End, Poke = Knucklebone, Pull Aggro = Bone Bulwark, Reap = Harvest, Reave = Soul Rend, Scourge = Tolling Bell, Sculpting Strike = Wraith Strike, Snap = Bone Snap, Sow = Scatter Ashes, Wisp = Marsh Light; Bone Shards = Bone Rain, Borrowed Time = Toll in Advance, Bury = Entomb, Calcify = Ossify, Capture Spirit = Bottled Soul, Cleanse = Bone Sift, Countdown = Inevitable, Danse Macabre = Dance of Bones, Death March = Funeral March, Death's Door = At the Threshold, Deathbringer = The Final Hour, Debilitate = Wither, Delay = Stay of Execution, Dirge = Lament, Dredge = Grave Digging, Enfeebling Touch = Withering Touch, Fetch = Bone Toss, Friendship = Bonded Dead, Haunt = Restless Spirits, High Five = Grasping Hands, Legion of Bone = Army of the Drowned, Lethality = Killing Chill, Melancholy = Mourning, No Escape = Last Rites, Pagestorm = Grimoire Gale, Parse = Spectral Ledger, Pull from Below = Drag Under, Putrefy = Fester, Rattle = Rattle the Bones, Right Hand Hand = Helping Hand, Severance = Sever the Thread, Shroud = Burial Shroud, Sic 'Em = Seize Them, Sleight of Flesh = Fleshcraft, Spur = Goad, Veilpiercer = Soul Lance; Banshee's Cry = Wailing Cry, Call of the Void = Whispers Beyond, Demesne = Phylactery, Devour Life = Soul Harvest, Eidolon = Spirit Walk, End of Days = The Last Toll, Eradicate = Unmaking, Glimpse Beyond = Gather Souls, Hang = Gallows, Misery = Spreading Rot, Necro Mastery = Shared Agony, Neurosurge = Soul Tithe, Oblivion = Mark of Ending, Reanimate = Grave Colossus, Reaper Form = Reaper's Mantle, Sacrifice = Fen Offering, Seance = Call the Departed, Sentry Mode = Ever Watchful, Shared Fate = Common Grave, Soul Storm = Requiem, Spirit of Ash = Ashfall, Squeeze = Death Grip, The Scythe = Reaping Scythe, Time's Up = Final Reckoning, Transfigure = Echo of the Dead, Undeath = Unquiet Rest; Protector = The Drowned King. Many old ids were kept for the copy that fits them (Tolling Bell, Last Rites, Requiem...). **Retired** (42, each mapped to a card of the same job in `retired.ts`): his 41 cards with no counterpart (Grave Touch, Bone Ward, Omen, Deathknell, Hasten the End, Grave Trap, Soul Siphon, Thrall's Cue, Rally the Dead, Twin Thralls (`legion-of-bone`), Rise Again (`reanimate`)...) and the shelved Legendary Grave Harvest; The Last Toll and The Drowned King left `SHELVED_LEGENDARIES` (he has no Legendary to lend). He has no Traps now (cards.test exempts him, as it does Valeria). Grave Colossus and The Drowned King units are kept but nothing summons them. **New engine pieces (all generic, with rules tests in `packages/combat/src/raise.test.ts`):** ops `raise`, `command`, `doomNow` (whoever is at or below its Fate dies now), `spreadDebuffs`, `mark` (a random card in hand gains Fleeting, Hold or Replay, optionally a lasting cost rise), `costTax` (cards cost more this turn); `sacrifice.self`, `discover.ethereal`, `upgrade` scope `discard`, `transform` from the draw pile; power narrowings `once` ("next turn"), `ethereal`, `minCost`, `pin` (aims at the card's target, and never fires for the card that made it); statuses `marrow` (companions hit harder), `reaping`, `debilitated` (Exposed/Faltering count double), `lethality`, `noose`, `quarry`, `freeethereal`; metrics `maxHp`, `dealt`, `lost`, `allyStrikes`, `drawnTurn`, `doomedTurn`, `samePlayed`, `etherealPlayed`, `deaths`, `tagExhausted`, `tagDeck`; `Scale.step` ("5 more for every 10") and `Scale.tag`; triggers `doomFoe`, `debuffFoe`, `allyHurt`; card fields `freeIf`, `cheaperPer`, `recallOnCost`; per-copy `CardInst` flags `ethereal`, `retain`, `replay`, `costUp` (shown on the hand card). `costOf` reads the new counters, and `SideView` carries them. balance.test divides a stepped scale by its step; v2.test counts an Opening-hand upgrade as an upgrade. **Not the same, by the engine or the guard rails:** cards that choose from the draw or discard pile, or choose a hand card for a keyword, take a random one (Cleanse: the top card; Sculpting Strike, Snap, Transfigure, Seance, Graveblast, Dredge); "into your draw pile" is the top of it; Strength lost (Friendship, Shared Fate) is Sapped, since Might cannot go below 0; Enfeebling Touch is Sapped through the enemy's next turn; Intangible 1 (Eidolon) is Ghostly 2; The Scythe grows for the rest of the fight, not the run (the campaign has no piece for a card that grows for good); Squeeze counts itself, so its base is 20 (+5 per Command card; 24 +6 upgraded) for her 25 +5 (30 +6) per other; X cards upgrade by "X is 1 higher" (Dirge's Summon 4 and Souls+, Eradicate's 14 are not copied); Blight Strike and Reaper Form count the HP taken off (not damage soaked by Barrier). Numbers are hers and untested: no sim was run. `RULES_VERSION` should be bumped.
371. **Ilvane is the Defect's card list, and the Totemist is now the Shaman (Robert, 2026-10-28: "lets get some additional agents to do this same thing for the other slay the spire inspired characters ... i do mean close copies of the originals, but obviously change names and keep our theme, i will adjust things going forward, but we need good baselines right now") →** **Ilvane becomes the Defect, card for card (Robert, 2026-10-28: "close copies of the originals, but obviously change names and keep our theme, i will adjust things going forward, but we need good baselines right now") →** as Valeria is the Ironclad (#364-#367), Ilvane's pool is now one card for every Slay the Spire 2 Defect card (source: slaythespire2.gg, **Beta v0.111**, 89 Defect cards), under original names in his shaman theme, with the Defect's cost, type, rarity and numbers wherever the engine allows. Each card's comment in `pools/totemist.ts` names the card it copies. The orbs are his totems and Focus is his Spirit (`resonance`): **Hawk** (`spark`) = Lightning, 3 at the end of the turn to a random enemy, awakens for 8 to a random enemy (was 4 / 10 to the front one); **Bear** (`ward`) = Frost, 2 / 5 Barrier (was 2 / 6); **Elder** (`hollow`) = Dark, planted with 6, charges by 6 + Spirit each turn, awakens for its charge on the enemy with the least HP, and Spirit does not add to that blow (was 0, +2 a turn, the front enemy); **Owl** (`prism`) = Plasma, 1 Mana at the start of your turn, awakens for 2 (was 1 Barrier / 1 Mana); **Splinter** (`splinter`) = Glass, planted with 4, hits every enemy for its charge at the end of the turn and loses 1, awakens for twice its charge (was 2 / 7). **Starter:** 4 Strike, 4 Defend, Hawk Call (Zap), Twin Awakening (Dualcast: the oldest totem twice); 75 HP (was 76). His trait **Totem-bound** (start with a Hawk, a Bear and a Splinter) is kept although the Defect's Cracked Core plants only one Lightning: that is a clash, kept on purpose and noted. Mechanics shown: Totems, Spirit, Auras, Status cards, 0-cost cards. The Elements (Spirit-scaled spells) and Rites (Aura-count) cards that were his are gone, 20 of them, each retired into the nearest kept card (`retired.ts`); his three shelved Legendaries are among them, so `SHELVED_LEGENDARIES` has no totemist cards and `legendary.ts` lost Avatar of the Elements. He has no Trap (cards.test exempts him as it does the knight) and no Legendary (legendary.test likewise). 83 cards of his own plus the Kindling token, 81 of them in the reward pool; first sort of build path headings Totems 29 / Elements 30 / Rites 22 (hidden, #365). **The cards (Defect card = ours, id):** Zap = Hawk Call (`spark-cast`), Dualcast = Twin Awakening (`twin-evocation`); commons: Ball Lightning = Spark Lash (`spark-lash`), Barrage = Totem Volley (`charged-blow`), Beam Cell = Arc Flash (`arc-flash`), Boost Away = Gust Step (`gust-step`), Charge Battery = Tidecaller (`tidecaller`), Claw = Gathering Storm (`gathering-storm`), Cold Snap = Rime Strike (`rime-strike`), Compile Driver = Totem Driver (`driver-of-runes`), Coolheaded = Still Water (`chilled-focus`), Focused Strike = Spirit Strike (`resonant-strike`), Go for the Eyes = Frost Needle (`frost-needle`), Gunk Up = Mudslide (`mudslide`), Hologram = Spirit Memory (`spirit-memory`), Hotfix = Elemental Focus (`elemental-focus`), Leap = Stone Skin (`stone-skin`), Lightning Rod = Storm Pole (`storm-pole`), Momentum Strike = Gale Strike (`gale-strike`), Sweeping Beam = Ember Toss (`ember-toss`), TURBO = Inner Flame (`inner-flame`), Uproar = Storm Call (`storm-call`); uncommons: Boot Sequence = Crystal Ward (`crystal-ward`), Bulk Up = Earthen Might (`earthen-might`), Capacitor = Totem Row (`glyph-lattice`), Chaos = Wild Call (`wild-call`), Chill = Bear Pack (`cold-snap`), Compact = Gather Kindling (`gather-kindling`), Darkness = Gathering Dusk (`gathering-dusk`), Double Energy = Root Tap (`leyline-tap`), Energy Surge = Tidal Surge (`tidal-surge`), Feral = Wild Heart (`wild-heart`), Fight Through = Ironbark (`ironbark`), FTL = Rapids (`rapids`), Fusion = Owl Call (`prism-cut`), Glacier = Frostbark (`frostglass`), Glasswork = Splinter Ward (`shard-chorus`), Hailstorm = Hail Rite (`permafrost`), Iteration = Smoke Reading (`glacial-memory`), Loop = Unending Call (`echoing-loop`), Null = Night Veil (`night-veil`), Overclock = Feed the Fire (`feed-the-fire`), Refract = Prismatic Cascade (`prismatic-cascade`), Rocket Punch = Boulder Fist (`boulder-fist`), Scavenge = Smoke Offering (`smoke-offering`), Scrape = Riverscour (`river-scour`), Shadow Shield = Elder's Shelter (`void-pulse`), Skim = Clear Spring (`clear-spring`), Smokestack = Kindled Rite (`kindled-rite`), Storm = Thunder Rite (`thundercall`), Subroutine = Spirit Drum (`tuning-fork`), Sunder = Skybreaker (`skybreaker`), Synchronize = Chorus of Totems (`chorus-of-glyphs`), Synthesis = Fire Bloom (`fire-bloom`), Tempest = Hawkstorm (`sparkstorm`), Tesla Coil = Arc Lightning (`arc-lightning`), Thunder = Stormcrown (`stormcrown`), White Noise = Spirit Whisper (`white-noise`); rares: Adaptive Strike = Shifting Strike (`shifting-strike`), All for One = Spirit Wave (`harmonic-wave`), Buffer = Spirit Ward (`sentinel-sigil`), Consuming Shadow = Devouring Night (`gathering-dark`), Coolant = Bark Carapace (`rune-carapace`), Creative AI = Spiritfont (`spiritfont`), Defragment = Tune the Spirits (`tune-the-lattice`), Echo Form = Echoing Chant (`echoing-form`), Flak Cannon = Blazing Eye (`blazing-aperture`), Helix Drill = Whirlpool (`whirlpool`), Hyperbeam = Skyfall (`hyperbeam`), Ice Lance = Deep Freeze (`deep-freeze`), Machine Learning = Ancestral Learning (`machine-learning`), Meteor Strike = Falling Star (`falling-star`), Modded = Carve a Notch (`carve-a-notch`), Multi-Cast = Many Voices (`many-voices`), One for All = Wind's Favor (`winds-favor`), Rainbow = Circle of Spirits (`aegis-array`), Reboot = Retune (`retune`), Shatter = Shatter the Row (`shatter-the-row`), Signal Boost = Resounding Call (`overtune`), Spinner = Splinter Wheel (`splinter-wheel`), Supercritical = Heart of Flame (`firestorm`), Trash to Treasure = Ash to Spirits (`biased-weave`), Voltaic = Endless Storm (`echo-cascade`). Tokens and status cards: Fuel = Kindling (`kindling`, his token), Dazed = Haze (`haze`), Burn = Scorch (`scorch`), Slimed = Muck (`muck`), Void = Drain (`void-drain`); Wound is the shared `wound`. Wound and Static are now tagged `status` too, so every status card counts. **Not copied:** Ignition and Imitation Learning (they act on another player); Hibernate (its first half gives every player Block, the rest would be a weaker Glacier); Genetic Algorithm (it grows permanently across the run, which needs a deck entry that remembers a number: no such thing yet); the two Ancient Defect cards, Biased Cognition and Quadcast (event cards in their game, as the knight left out Break). **New engine pieces (generic, all with rules tests in `packages/combat/src/totems.test.ts`):** rune fields `start` (planted charge), `decay` (charge lost per passive), `plainEvoke` (Spirit does not add to the awakening), `atStart` (passive at the start of your turn); selector `weakestFoe`; metrics `runeKinds`, `runesOf` and `channeledOf` (with a `rune` on the scale or `when`), `manaSpent`, `attacking`; trigger `evoke` (narrowed by `rune`; its target is each enemy the awakening struck); ops `pulse` (totems' passives now: all, of a kind, or the oldest, optionally at the target), `recost` (a lasting cost change, stored as `CardInst.costUp`, that a play does not spend), `reshuffle`, `returnPlayed`; op options `evoke.last`, `channel` rune `'*'` (random), `draw.keepCost`, `copySelf.free`, `fetch.maxCost`, `exhaustCards.tag`/`everywhere`, `transform.tag` (kind optional), and on Auras `once` (first time each turn), `maxCost`, `times` (it goes after N answers) and `rune`; `created`/`draw` Auras can be narrowed by kind or tag; statuses `echoing` (Echo Form), `boosted` (Signal Boost), `freepower` (Synthesis) and `deftness` (One for All); card fields `inHandAtEnd` (Burn), `onDraw` (Void) and `cheaperOnCreate` (Rocket Punch); a negative status `temp` now comes back at the end of the turn (only what was really taken: Hyperbeam); `runeGone` events carry the row index. One behaviour change: a charging totem's charge used to reset after each awakening; it now resets only when the totem stays (`keep`), so awakening an Elder twice deals its charge twice, as in their game.
