Also `balance.test` treats a card that costs more each play (`recost` > 0) as unable to loop (Modded), and `v2.test`'s "upgrade changes nothing" check now sees Opening hand and Fleeting. **Deviations from the Defect's numbers or text, and why:** TURBO (Inner Flame) and Overclock (Feed the Fire) are consumed (balance.test forbids a free card that makes Mana or draws and stays, as for the knight's Bloodletting); Burn (Scorch) costs 2 HP rather than 2 damage that Block can stop (the engine's self-damage is unblockable); Hologram (Spirit Memory) returns a random card, not a chosen one; White Noise (Spirit Whisper) makes its Aura free the next time it is played, not only this turn; Bulk Up's Dexterity is Bracing (+1 to every Barrier gained, totems' too); Buffer is Shielded (it stops the next attack's damage, not any HP loss); Echo Form with 2 stacks doubles the first 2 cards; One for All and Energy Surge help only you (no other players); Chaos and Trash to Treasure pick among all five totems; Thunder's extra hit goes to each enemy the Hawk's awakening struck. **Known limits, left for later:** a status card an enemy puts into your deck (Wound, Static) also sets off Smokestack, Trash to Treasure and Rocket Punch (the `created` trigger has always fired for cards added by either side); Echo does not stack with Reverb or Mirrored on the same card (one replay at most). Also fixed on the way: turn-long status changes are now undone newest first, so a Spirit gain and a Spirit loss in the same turn cannot leave a lasting gain. Numbers are the Defect's and untested: no sim was run. `RULES_VERSION` needs a bump (cards and engine changed). **The class is now the Shaman** (Robert: "our totemist, lets change his class name to Shaman"; he chose to rename the id too): the id is `shaman` (role "Shaman"), the pool file is `pools/shaman.ts`; old saves, Arena decks and server rows that say `totemist` (or `ilvane`) become `shaman` through `canonicalHero` (`LEGACY_HERO_IDS`). His card ids did not change.
372. **Characters are shown by their class name, and the Trapper is the Trickster (Robert, 2026-10-28: "lets update each character to just display their class name instead of character names. i may bring them back but lets go class names for now. and change trapper to Trickster") →** every Riftwalker's `hero.name` is its class (Knight, Engineer, Hunter, Trickster, Assassin, Shaman, Barbarian, Spellblade, Necromancer, Drunkard); the character names are kept in `Riftwalker.persona` (Valeria, Rivet, Kesh the Unfed, Vesper Nightlace, Sylas, Ilvane, Sehra, Obsidia, KP, Dorian Blackthorn) for when they come back (set `hero.name` to it again). Screens that showed "name · role" show the name once; a Rift-crossed card "belongs to the Hunter"; the Learn page's totem section is "(Shaman)". **The trapper is now the Trickster, id `trickster`** (pool file `pools/trickster.ts`, figure `hero-trickster.png`), like the Shaman in #371: `trapper` and `vesper` map to `trickster` through `canonicalHero`; card ids (Master Trapper, Trapper's Dividend) did not change. **The server renames old hero ids again whenever the set of renames changes** (`apps/server/src/board.ts`: the kv flag `heroIdsRenamed` holds the set it last applied, not `true`; it had been set by the #190 rename, so rows saying `totemist` were never renamed), and ghost decks it sends out pass through `storedHero`. Review fixes in the same batch: a one-shot free status (Free Attack, Free Skill, a free Fleeting card, a free Aura) is spent only when it is what makes the card free; Blade Echo and Replay also happen when the card asked for a choice first (`extraPlays`); a transformed card loses its permanent discount (`costDown`). `RULES_VERSION` 21.
373. **A class name in a sentence takes "the" (Robert, 2026-10-28: "in places where it says the class name that it says The before the classname. not on the class picker part, but in lines of text where its applicable") →** running text reads "as the Knight", "the Hunter's cards", "The Knight's starter", "Rift-crossed: the Assassin's" (title screen, map, match list, reliquary, deck builder, merchant, events). Names standing alone (the picker, headings, labels, leaderboards) stay bare. "a Knight deck" uses "an" before a vowel ("an Assassin deck").
374. **No starting choice: a run opens at The Threshold (Robert, 2026-10-28: "lets get rid of the first step of the campaign where players can pick uncommon cards") →** a run starts with the Riftwalker's plain starting deck and goes straight to The Threshold; the pick of two uncommons (#365) and its screen, `starters.ts`, `chooseStarters`, the `kit` run phase and `StarterPick` are gone. A run saved at the old choice (the kits of #333, or the two uncommons) loads at The Threshold with the plain starter (`restartAtThreshold`). The sim tools play the plain starter.
375. **Three baseline cards made whole, and a fight-ending kill pays what outlasts the fight (Robert, 2026-10-28: "yes please make those fixes"; the card costing guide, docs/14_CARD_COSTING.md, had flagged them as copies that lost part of the original) →** **Fated Hand** (Foregone Conclusion) is "Choose 2 cards in your draw pile and put them on top. Next turn, draw 2 extra cards." (upgrade: 3 and 3): the original puts 2 cards of your choice from the draw pile into next turn's hand, and a choice at the start of a turn is something the engine never asks, so the choice is made now and the cards wait on top, which gives the same hand. New: `topdeck` takes `from: 'draw'` (the choice is shown sorted, so it does not tell the draw order; with 2 cards or fewer in the pile nothing is asked; played with no one to ask, a Sly or replayed copy, random cards go on top), and `Pending.from` / `PendingView.from`. **Army of the Drowned** (Legion of Bone, "ALL players Summon 6") is Raise 6 twice: alone, "all players" had left a 2-Mana copy of After the End; two Thralls is the card for a pair of players, ~20 points against a 2-cost uncommon's ~26 with Consume. **Marked for Death** (The Hunt) is "Deal 10 damage. If this is fatal: Gain an extra card reward after this fight." (15 upgraded): new op `extraReward` counts `SideState.extraRewards`, the web passes it to `finishDuel` (new last argument; more than `MAX_EXTRA_REWARDS` = 3 are earned but not paid, so a fight with copies or replays of the card still reports; saves check the count and flags; the Arena deck and boards are saved when the extra reward after a Warden is picked), and the run offers each extra reward after the fight's own reward or after a Warden's Gift (`NodeContext.extraRewards`, `extraReward`, `afterWarden`; the node is cleared, or the stage, after the last one). Cards are a fight's or an Elite's, as the fight's own were. **Fix:** a kill that ended the fight skipped its "if this is fatal" effects, so Bloodfeast gave no max HP on the last enemy; now the ones that outlast the fight (`maxHp`, `extraReward`) are still paid. Raise reads "twice" for 2. Tests: `packages/content/src/v2/copyFixes.test.ts`, `packages/campaign/src/extraRewards.test.ts`. `RULES_VERSION` 22.
376. **Might and the other attack statuses touch attack damage only (Robert, 2026-10-28: "is my might applying to damage that my aura blood fire does? is that how slay the spire 2 works?", then "i would like you to fix this issue with might and the damage calculation") →** an attack is an Attack card or a unit's own strike (an enemy's move, a companion's, whatever card made it act); damage from an Aura, a totem, a Trap, a potion or a Skill is the number printed. Might, Sapped, Faltering, Clutch, Fury and Radiant, Empowered, Tracking, Cruelty and Sturdy on the dealer, and Exposed and Fury on the target, now leave it alone (only the difficulty's damage percent still applies), as every one of those statuses says "attacks" and as Slay the Spire does (Strength and Vulnerable only change attack damage). Thorns, Reflect and "when attacked" Traps answer attacks only, and a companion that strikes through a Skill (Savage Command) now counts as attacking (it used to slip past Thorns). Engine: `isAttackAct` and `modifiedDamage(..., attack)`; the hand's damage preview passes the card's kind. Before this, Might added to every Aura's and totem's damage (Blood Fire, Serpent's Coil, Bastion Engine, the Shaman's Hawks), which the baseline copies were never balanced for. Also: the Aura list shows one row per Aura card (Blood Fire has two triggers and showed twice), "x2" for a second copy. Primed, Armored wearing thin, overflow onto a hero and Den Mother's "killed by" follow the same rule. **Cards that lose the most** (none rebalanced here; check with the costing guide when their class is reviewed): the Barbarian's Rampage of Ire (6 to all on entering Fury was 12 inside Fury), the Hunter's Wounded Wrath, the Drunkard's The Glass Palace, the Spellblade's Hungering Void and the Engineer's damage-on-summon Aura, plus every Aura and totem in a Might deck. Tests: `packages/content/src/v2/attackDamage.test.ts` (Might, Exposed, Faltering, Armored, Reflect, totems, a companion striking through a Skill); the knight's Blood Fire test now expects 6, not 7. `RULES_VERSION` goes up with the next deploy (a seed and its moves now play out differently).
377. **The Hunter rebalanced by the card costing guide, with six Traps and a hunt (Robert, 2026-10-28: "using our new basis points ... review the hunters card pool and balance its numbers out, as well as include some other mechanics ... i would like to see some traps, not a crazy number but a few", then "i want you to make these changes to the hunter's card pool") →** every change, with its points before and after, is in `docs/HUNTER_REVIEW.md`. In short: companions are priced as 1.2 x HP + 3 + their hit x their acts (`docs/14_CARD_COSTING.md` §10): Pup 5 HP (Pup+ 7), Pack Alpha 12 HP hitting 6 (Alpha Call no longer gives you 1 Might as well: it was still ~47% over), Dire Wolf 18 HP hitting 9, Litter brings 2 Pups, Bloodmoon Hunt 1 Hound and +2 Might, Rally the Pack 3 Barrier. Starting cards: Rend 6, Blood Frenzy 3 to all, Savage Bite 7. About 40 number changes towards each card's budget (Bone Crack 1 Exposed, Primal Feast heal 3 and +4 max HP for the pack, Devour Ally heal 8 and 2 Might, Slip the Leash 2 Mana and at most +4, Hunger Unbound 3 Mana, The Hunger Unleashed 2 Might and 1 Cadence, Red Frenzy only for cards drawn during your turn and 1 Mana, Ravage 10 to all twice, Savage Command and Hunter's Call 1 Mana, Den Mother 2, Bloodsong 0, and more). 15 hand-written upgrades replace automatic ones that raised every number (§12). Four rares became uncommons (The Hunger, Unchained, Hunter's Call, Frothing Rage): the Hunter had 28 rares to the baselines' 21-25. Retired: Crimson Pact (into Bone Offering) and Unbridled (into Blood Tithe). **New (7):** Bear Trap, Snare Line, Deadfall and Baited Snare (Traps), Hunting Grounds (a Trap springing gives 1 Might), Single Out (1 Exposed, 3 Primed on each companion, draw 1) and Run It Down (7, and the pack strikes a Faltering target). Engine: a `faltering` metric, so a card can ask "if the target is Faltering". The Hunter has 86 cards of her own and 6 Traps (the Trickster 17). Tests: `packages/content/src/v2/hunt.test.ts`; kesh and cards tests follow the new numbers.
378. **A Trap whose damage kills the attacker puts nothing on another enemy (Robert, 2026-10-28: "lets fix that trap issue with exposed/faltering") →** the `attacker` selector used to fall back to the front enemy when the attacker had died, so Bear Trap's Faltering or Deadfall's Exposed landed on an enemy that never attacked. Now an attacker that has died takes the rest of the effect with it; only an effect with no attacker at all (a Trap set off by a summon, such as Alarm Bell) still aims at the front enemy. Test in `packages/content/src/v2/hunt.test.ts`.
379. **Sleeve Knives are Daggers (Robert, 2026-10-28: "lets rename sleeve knives to daggers for the assassin. is that ok or does that infringe too much on slay the spire 2") →** the Assassin's token is shown as **Dagger** and Blade of Ink's as **Inked Dagger**; the ids (`sleeve-knife`, `inked-knife`) and the `knife` tag stay, so saves, decks and the server need nothing. "Dagger" is a plain weapon word: the Silent's term of art is the Shiv, which we never show, and the Silent cards with Dagger in their names (Dagger Spray, Dagger Throw, Cloak and Dagger, Hidden Daggers) are Blade Flurry, Flick Knife, Shroud and Shank and Sleeve Drop here, so no card should take one of those names. The Assassin's build-path label said "Shivs", their word; it is "Daggers" now. Cards with Knife in their own name (Knife Juggle, Flick Knife, Knife in the Back, Ghost Knives) keep it. The Edge, Honed and Fanned texts and the Learn page say Daggers.
380. **Overflow goes through the rest of the pack before the hero; Blood Pact is an Aura (Robert, 2026-10-28: "i was thinking the overflow damage would be tanked by other minions if there are any, then go to the player if there's nothing else to tank the damage"; "Blood Pact sounds like it should be an aura since it says gain mana for the rest of the fight") →** when a single-target attack kills a companion, what is left (`OVERFLOW_PCT` of it) hits the next companion in line, picked as a single attack picks (a Taunt companion first, then the one in front), and so on; the hero takes only what gets past the last companion. Each companion's Barrier soaks first, and Shielded or Ghostly on one stops the rest. A companion killed this way remembers its killer (Den Mother). **Blood Pact** (Hunter) is an Aura, no longer a Skill with Consume, like the Knight's Inner Furnace: an Aura is played once anyway. (The Neutral Attunement has the same shape and is still a Skill.) Test: `packages/combat/src/engine.test.ts`.
381. **Attunement is an Aura; the Hunter heals 2 after a won fight again (Robert, 2026-10-28: "yes and lets add the hunters +2 heal after a fight back to its trait") →** the Neutral Attunement ("from your next turn, gain 1 more Mana every turn for the rest of the fight") is an Aura, like Blood Pact (#380). Pack Instinct gets back `healAfterFight: 2` (taken away in #333). Overflow review fixes: a companion the overflow kills exactly, or through Ghostly, also remembers its killer (Den Mother), and the overflow tooltip says the leftover goes to the next companion before the hero.
382. **Robert lowered the tier numbers (2026-10-28) →** HP_START 90, HP_PER_TIER 20, HP_PER_STAGE 5; DMG_START 90, DMG_PER_TIER 12, DMG_PER_STAGE 3 (were 100 / 33 / 10 and 100 / 15 / 4). Tier I enemies are 90 / 95 / 100% of their written HP and 90 / 93 / 96% of their damage, so Tier I now sits a little below the enemies as written; the table test that asked for at least 100% now checks that Tier I starts at the table's own numbers.
383. **The Engineer rebalanced by the card costing guide, with frames, Salvage and guns (Robert, 2026-10-28: "like we did for the hunter, i want to re-assess the engineer ... minions that occupy 1 to 3 minion slots on the battle field ... give him some cards to do damage himself ... i like the act now stuff but he cant go crazy with it ... primed on his minions for bursty turns", then on the review "1) yes 2) lets go with it for now 3) no 4) i like the pips on the card and the machine ... i want those in the game") →** every change, with its points, is in `docs/ENGINEER_REVIEW.md`. **Frames:** a companion takes `UnitDef.slots` of its side's companion slots (1 Light by default, 2 Heavy, 3 Colossal); `spawn` and `raiseAlly` count slots, and a card whose summons do not all fit (several on one card count together) must release companions: the `release` of a play action is one id, or a list when several have to go, and `legalActions` offers each smallest set that makes room (a Colossal on a full board: one way, every companion). Aura and Trap summons only fill free slots. Shown as 1-3 pips on the summon card's type line and on the companion's plate, and in the text ("Heavy: 2 slots"); Heavy and Colossal machines are drawn larger. This applies to every hero, but only the Engineer has Heavy or Colossal companions, so the other change for others is that a card summoning two (Litter) now needs room for both instead of the second one fizzling. **Scrap and Salvage:** a released companion is *scrapped*; new trigger `allyLost` (an ally dies or is scrapped) for Salvage Rights (draw 1, 2 Barrier) and Scrapyard King. Tinkerer gains `salvage`: the first companion a card summons after scrapping others takes their Might and Primed, and half their HP as extra max HP. **Companions:** Patrol Drone 4 HP hits 3, Micro Drone 3/2, Sentry Turret 5 HP hits 2, Repair Bot heals 2, Enforcer Mk.II Heavy 11/6, Siege Engine Heavy 12 HP hitting 3 twice, War Engine Colossal 20 HP hitting 4 twice; new Spotter Drone (Light, 1 Exposed), Bulwark Walker (Heavy, 12 HP, Taunt, you gain 3 Barrier), Scrap Golem (Colossal, 12/5), Dreadnought (Colossal, 18 HP, 5 to all). **New cards:** Deploy Spotter, Pulse Pistol, Arc Welder (id `welding-arc`: `arc-welder` is a trimmed id, #358), Laser Grid, Particle Cannon (8 + 6 per slot filled, new metric `slots`), Targeting Uplink (an Attack gives each companion 2 Primed), Build Bulwark Walker, Scrap Golem, Dreadnought, Full Salvo (you and your companions 5 Primed, all act now). New selector `biggestAlly`. **Act now:** 4 cards (Assemble, Relay Strike, Command Chip, Full Salvo), once each. About 50 numbers moved toward each card's budget, 24 hand-written upgrades, Swarm Tactics, Factory Floor and Relay Network are uncommon, and Nanoswarm, Drone Uprising (shelved Legendary), Overload and Mob Rule are retired into Scrap Heap, Drone Carrier, Double Time and Overcharge Blast. Two changes from the review: Siege Protocol is 10 + 2 per Might (the balance guard caps a payoff on a resource that only grows at 2 a point), and Rebuild keeps 3 Barrier (a card must do something when nothing has fallen). Tests: `packages/content/src/v2/frames.test.ts`.
384. **The Engineer's utility: Battery Pack, Schematics, Build Generator (Robert, 2026-10-28: "yea lets give it some options, i like the 3 you mentioned except power grid sounds like auras from other classes, id like that to be different somehow") →** her draw was fine but Mana was only Overcharge and nothing chose a card. **Battery Pack** (0, common): gain 1 Mana, Consume (the Necromancer's Marsh Light shape; "+" also draws 1). **Schematics** (0, uncommon): choose a card in your draw pile, put it on top, draw 1, Consume (the balance guard consumes a free card that replaces itself; the existing `topdeck` choice from the draw pile, as Fated Hand). The Mana engine is a **machine, not an Aura**: **Build Generator** (2, rare) summons a Light **Generator** (8 HP) that gives 1 extra Mana next turn each time it acts, so it takes a slot, can be struck down (it stands behind the companions summoned before it), can't stand beside a Colossal, and an act now (Assemble, Full Salvo) makes it work again. Priced 9.6 + 3 + 4 x ~2.5 acts = ~22.6 against a 2-cost rare's 23.8. Also from the review of #383 (`reviews/engineer-frames-review.md`): "dies or is scrapped" Auras fire after the card has left the hand, a card that summons nothing never asks for a release, and a targeted card offers each release set once. Tests in `frames.test.ts`.
385. **Machines that strike twice gain 1 a hit when upgraded; 0.110.0 is RULES_VERSION 25 (Robert, 2026-10-28: "lets get that all worked out and pushed when its ready") →** a "+" companion normally gets +4 HP and +2 damage, which a two-hit machine gets twice (Siege Engine+ and War Engine+ were about +55%). Like a multi-hit Attack card's upgrade (`plus()` adds 1 a hit), `PLUS_STATS` gives Siege Engine+ 16 HP hitting 4 twice and War Engine+ 24 HP hitting 5 twice (about +35%). RULES_VERSION 25 for the frames, Salvage and the Engineer's cards (#383-384): replays and Arena games from before no longer play.
386. **The Trickster rebalanced by the card costing guide; a cancel stops one hit; a copied card costs what the original costs (Robert, 2026-10-28: "i want to do the same balance assessment that we did with hunter and engineer, but i want to do it with trickster next", then "cancel the attack should only block 1 hit of a complete attack, similar to the bubble from shielded. if theres an identical card from a different class then yea make it the same cost. im ok with your wording change") →** every change, with its points before and after, is in `docs/TRICKSTER_REVIEW.md`. **Rules:** a Trap's cancel stops the one hit that sprang it, like Shielded (the engine already did; the text now says "Cancel that hit."). **Text:** a Trap that springs on a summon or on an enemy gaining Barrier names "that enemy", not "the attacker" (`cardText`); "If you have a Trap set" reads as such. **Cards:** Set the Web 7 + 1 Exposed (a basic at 119% of a common); 14 cards over budget lowered (Cripple, Perfect Alibi 13, Slit and Run, Smoke Bomb, Metronome Trap, Dread Gaze 3, Trick of the Light, Spring the Trap +5 a Trap, Opening Strike 8, Patient Guard 2 + 1 a card, Trap Door 2 Mana, The Perfect Trap without Shielded, The Long Ambush 20 / Stun 1 / 2 Exposed; Measured Breath Consumes, as a card that makes more Mana than it costs must); 19 under budget raised (Mirror Veil and Trap Net 1 Mana, False Floor and Poison Pantry 0, Fang Pit 6, Hidden Hand +3 Barrier, Tear the Veil draws 2, Grim Collection 12, Fade Back 6 + 4 next turn (it was worse than the Neutral Steady Guard), Step Aside 6, Mask of Faces +6 Barrier, Master Trapper +3 Barrier, Trapper's Dividend and The Long Game 1 Mana, Coup de Grace 18 + 4 an Exposed, Culling 8 + 3, Between Worlds without the Mana it took; Compound Fear 2 Exposed and a cap of 4, still Consumed because every doubler must be, `balance.test.ts`). **Copies:** Cheap Shot (the Assassin's Dirty Hit word for word) is 6, 1 Faltering, and 1 Exposed if you have a Trap set; Stalk (the Assassin's Stalker's Lunge) is 16, and 2 extra cards next turn if the target is Exposed; Deep Pockets costs 2, as the Knight's Inner Furnace does. 25 hand-written upgrades replace automatic ones that raised two or three numbers. **Retired:** Death Trap (into Bait and Switch: on budget it was Bait and Switch with Exposed, or the Hunter's Deadfall) and Whisper Net (into False Floor: a second summon Trap). Sandbag and Curtain Call (no longer Consumed) are uncommons: 20 commons, 31 uncommons, 25 rares. The guide gained rates for Foresee, a cancelled hit, Ghostly, Steadfast, Brittle and Reverb. Tests: `packages/content/src/v2/trickster.test.ts`. Next (Robert liked it): **Suits**, a playing-card build path in place of Patience; Misdirection (enemies striking each other) was turned down as too strong.
387. **The Trickster's Suits in place of Patience; panels you can move; card rarity colours everywhere (Robert, 2026-10-28: "i like the Suits idea, lets to that. im not sure about misdirection, that sounds like it could be too powerful", then "the suit pip shouldnt be on any cards, it should dynamically show up somwhere around the character, to the left and top of the character model ... give them an effect when all 4 are there. also, can we make some of our information on the battle screen modular? specifically, id like the auras list, battle log to be modular and moveable/expandable, and enemy auras for pvp", and "we need to update the colors for rare uncommon and basic on the saved decks page") →** **Suits** (`docs/TRICKSTER_SUITS.md`): every card has a suit read from what it does (`suitOf` in `packages/combat/src/suits.ts`: an Attack ♠, a Trap ♣, otherwise its first effect: Barrier and protection ♥, cards, Mana and Foresee ♦, a debuff ♣; `CardDef.suit` overrides, 'wild' is every suit). A hero whose trait has `suits` keeps the suits played this turn (`SideState.suitsTurn`, cleared as the turn begins, in the view); the first time in a turn all four show, the trait's **Full Trick** runs (the Trickster's: 5 damage to every enemy, draw 1) and `allSuits` Auras answer. Metrics `suits` (how many show, the card being played included; a cost can read it) and `spades`/`hearts`/`diamonds`/`clubs` (0 or 1). No pips on the cards: four pips above and to the left of the hero light as suits are played (`SuitTrack.tsx`), and the card being aimed shows which it would light. The Patience build path is now **Suits**: kept Cold Read, Patient Blade, Shadow Cloak, Weigh the Odds, Hidden Hand, Fade Back, Long Con, Trick of the Light, Second Act, Pickpocket Master; new Flourish, Card Fan, Card Sharp, Cut the Deck, Queen of Hearts, Ace of Clubs, Wild Card, Royal Flourish, Showstopper, The Joker, The Whole Deck; Masquerade reworked (3 to every enemy per suit shown, at the end of your turn). Retired: Banked Breath, Coiled Patience, Sandbag, Waiting Game, Measured Breath, Fade Away. Saved Breath, Stolen Moment, Deep Pockets, Frozen Moment and The Long Game are glue (the last two uncommon now); Gleeful Trapsmith and The Perfect Trap moved to Traps. The starting deck swaps a Defend for **Pick a Card** (0, Foresee 2, a ♦), so it holds all four suits. Rarities 20 / 35 / 26; build paths 22 / 23 / 22. Robert did not answer whether the starter should change or all six cards should go; those are the recommendation and easy to undo. **Panels:** your Auras, the other side's Auras (an Arena opponent's, an enemy's) and the battle log are `FloatPanel`s: drag the title bar to move one, the corner to resize it, click the title to fold it, double-click to put it back; where each was left is remembered in this browser only. **Rarity colours:** the card frames' colours are tokens in `global.css` (`--rar-basic`, `--rar-common`, `--rar-uncommon` blue, `--rar-rare` gold, `--rar-legendary`, `--rar-ancestral`), used by the cards, the saved decks' rarity counts (which had uncommon green and rare blue) and the Brass Reel's card prizes. Boons and potions keep their own scale (common to mythic). Tests: `packages/content/src/v2/suits.test.ts`.
388. **Backup Core brings back one companion a turn (Robert, 2026-10-28: "back up core seems busted, it just keep reviving the same minion and it keeps tanking the damage. i literally take no damage") →** it answered every ally death, so the machine that died came straight back (at half HP) in front of the next hit, and since overflow goes to the next companion (#380) a hit never reached the hero. It now answers only the first death each turn (the power's existing `once`): "The first time each turn one of your allies dies: Your most recently fallen ally returns with half its HP." That is one revive a turn, about 10 points (a half-HP body) for a 2-cost rare Aura. An Aura with `once` on any trigger now says "The first time each turn" in its text. Test: `packages/content/src/v2/backupCore.test.ts` (a three-hit move against a 2-HP Drone revives it once and reaches the hero).
389. **Review fixes after 0.112.0 (Suits, panels) →** a panel's title folds it again on a click (the drag only captures the pointer once it has moved 4 px; it captured on every press, so the title button never got its click) and its corner resize is saved from a press that starts on the grip; a double-click on the Cards button no longer puts the log back. **Cards that only work in their owner's deck never cross over:** `deadElsewhere` (campaign `rewards.ts`) finds a card that reads the suits (`suits`/a suit metric, `allSuits`, a cost by suits) at any depth; such cards are kept out of the Rift-crossed pool, a visiting merchant's table and the Legend events' crossed picks. **The Full Trick goes off once the card that earned it has finished** (`fullTrickDue`), so its 5 to every enemy never takes the card's own target from it (the pips still light as the card is played, so "per suit" counts it); the suits clear at the very start of a turn; a Trap played for free that cannot be set shows no suit. The client lights the pips on the `suits` event and the log says "makes a Full Trick"; the Trickster's trait text names it. Known and left: Backup Core's "first time each turn" is once per round in a campaign fight (your turn and the enemy's share a turn number), so a companion lost on your own turn uses it up.
390. **The Barbarian rebalanced by the card costing guide (Robert, 2026-10-28: "i feel like its already pretty good, maybe too strong righ tnow. i want you to run it through the card cost analysis, and propose some changes", then "i like your proposed changes ... lets go forward with that") →** every change, with its points, is in `docs/BARBARIAN_REVIEW.md`. The main one: **Inner Fire gives 1 Fervor for each stance entered, no longer 2 Barrier as well** (a Flow deck changes stance two or three times a turn, so the trait paid about 11 points a turn, three times any other trait, on top of Auras that pay per stance). **Still Guard** is 3 Barrier and Serene (7 for 1 Mana was the Watcher's 2-cost Vigilance at half price). 24 cards over budget lowered (Bowing Low 2 Barrier, Red Mist 4, Centre Yourself draws 1, Shifting Guard 2 Barrier, Thread-Cutter 3, Watchful Calm 2 Barrier, Settling Fist 7 without Barrier, Temper Flare 2 x3, Wrathful Guard 6, Hot-Blooded 4, Cornered Beast 8, Unafraid 6, Exhale without the draw, Unmoved Mind and Wheel of Motion 2 Mana (Unmoved Mind 2 Barrier), Morning Prayer 2 Barrier, Surge of Ire 2 Mana and the first Fury each turn, Eye of the Storm 4 and 4, Perfect Balance a card and 2 Barrier, Gentle Return heal 1, Tide of Peace 4 a Mana and draws 2, Rage Unbound costs 3 HP, Burning Litany 3 Mana, Gather Breath Consumes and draws 2). 17 under budget raised (Rending Hands 6 x2 for 1 HP, Blood on the Sand 3 Primed, Final Account 14 + 10 in Fury, Rage of Ages 8 x3, Ashes of Restraint 9, The Offering 1 Mana, Hymn of Embers 5 Fervor 8 Barrier, Crown of Embers 6 Primed 2 Fervor, Faith Kept 3 Fervor, Litany and Radiant Heart 1 Mana (Radiant Heart draws 3), Last Rites 16, Mountain's Stillness 6 + 3 a card, Thousand Palms 3 x7, Quick Palms 4 and a Fervor, Poise 3). **All-Seeing costs 2**: it is the Trickster's Pickpocket Master word for word (#386's rule). 7 hand-written upgrades. The guide gained stance rates (enter Fury 4, enter Serene 4, Fervor 2, enter Radiant ~31). Ideas kept for later (BARBARIAN_REVIEW.md §5): a trigger when Fury ends, Trophies (kills give a lasting buff), heavy blows cheaper in Fury. Robert wants her re-themed as a monk; that is a separate step. Tests: `packages/content/src/v2/barbarianPass.test.ts`.
391. **The Barbarian is the Monk (Robert, 2026-10-28: "i like your proposed changes including retheming to a monk", then, asked whether he meant the class or the cards, "Make the class a Monk") →** her cards (palms, prayers, Serene, Fervor) were the Watcher's monk shape all along, so the class is renamed, as the Trapper became the Trickster (#372): id `monk` (`hero.name` and role "Monk"), pool file `pools/monk.ts` (`MONK`), figure `hero-monk.png`, manifest key `hero:monk`, CSS `card--hero-monk`, the art and tool tables, the card-art sheets (`monk-01`...; the local `assets-src/cards` and `assets-src/heroes` files renamed to match). `barbarian` (and `sehra`) map to `monk` through `canonicalHero`, so saves, Vault decks and server rows load as the Monk; the server renames old rows itself because the set of renames changed. Card ids are unchanged; four names that read as a barbarian are monk names now: Red Mist → **Burning Breath**, Cornered Beast → **Coiled Serpent**, Blood on the Sand → **Iron Body**, Berserk Tide → **Storm of Fists**. Her painted figure, scimitar emblem and flame blade are unchanged (art is for later). Review fixes to #390 in the same batch: an Aura with `once` on a stance says "The first time each turn you enter Fury" (Surge of Ire printed "Whenever"); Quick Palms+ (7 damage) and Gather Breath+ (draws 3, Mana unchanged) have hand-written upgrades (their automatic ones gained over +50%); the Surge of Ire test checks the Barrier and the draw.
392. **The Warden's Gift is the only page after a Warden (Robert, 2026-10-28: "i just beat a tier 1 stage 3 boss and it offered 2 separate rewards. can you make sure its just the 1 page of rewards with the warden hearts? ... i was playing hunter") →** an extra card reward the Warden fight earned (Marked for Death, the only card that gives one; a Hunter can hold it as a Rift-crossed card) used to follow the Gift as a second "Choose another card" page. It is now one more card to choose from on the Gift itself (`giftOffers` count + the extra rewards), and taking a card or a Heart from the Gift ends it. A save made in the middle of the old two-page flow still finishes. Tests: `packages/campaign/src/extraRewards.test.ts`.
393. **The Drunkard rebalanced by the card costing guide, with Stagger (Robert, 2026-10-28: "lets make the balance changes, add stagger mechanic with cards that use it, cut how many shells he gets, hangover raise the drinks number. make sure he has enough utility in the deck") →** every change is in `docs/DRUNKARD_REVIEW.md`. **Shells cut:** Load Shells gives 2 (was 3), Shell Game is an uncommon, One More Round gives 2 a turn, Ironsights Sharpens 3. **Drinks raised** (Hangover is priced as HP, -1.5 a stack): One for the Road draws 3, Drunken Swing 14, Barroom Brawl 16 to all for 2 Hangover, Swig 4 Might for 2, Bottoms Up 4 Hangover, Last Call 1 Mana; Hair of the Dog lost its draw, Liquid Dinner heals 2 (+1 per 5% missing). **Over budget lowered:** Pane Up 5, Powder Load 3, Gun Oil 3, The Twelve-Gauge Opera's per-gun Sharpen 1 and 3 Barrier, Hot Brass 1 Barrier, Dutch Courage = your Hangover, The Glass Palace 3 and 3, Glass Jaw 2 Mana with 3 and 2, Whiskey Miracle heals 10. **Under budget raised:** Rack the Slide 4, Scope 2 a turn, Trigger Discipline, Gunmetal Soul, Scattergun Symphony (3 to all), Ricochet and Rock Bottom 1 Mana, Shard Toss 6, Birdshot 6, Molotov Cocktail 0 Mana, Sawn-Off 9 for 1 HP, Spray and Pray 10 (+1 per 2 made), Boomstick 14, Fire in the Hole 12 (+1 per consumed), Smash the Pane 12, Dumpster Dive heals 4, Booby-Trapped Window 13. 15 hand-written upgrades and Sway+. **Stagger** (new): a card tag (`stagger`); a Stagger card needs no target, its damage lands on a random enemy (`randomFoe`), and it hits about 15% harder; "Stagger." starts its text and a keyword tip explains it. Six cards in the Hangover build path: Haphazard Hook, Drunken Lurch, Stumble, Free-for-All, Drunken Master (2 Barrier per Stagger card), Blind Drunk (twice, and once more per Hangover). **Utility:** Rummage the Bin (0: Foresee 3) and Sway (1: 7 Barrier, draw 1), glue. Bar Stool is an uncommon at 7. His mechanics list says Stagger in place of Traps (he keeps his four). The guide gained rates for the Gun's Sharpen (2.5), a Shell (5.2) and Hangover (-1.5). Tests: `stagger.test.ts`; drunkard and hangover tests follow the new numbers.
394. **Review fixes to the Drunkard pass (#393) →** Fire in the Hole is 12 (+1 per consumed card) to every enemy (Fire in the Hole+ 15) and The Twelve-Gauge Opera's each-gun is Sharpen 1 and 3 Barrier: both were in the review and the notes but had been missed in the code. The Neutral common that was called "Stagger" (aimed, 4 damage, 2 Faltering) is **Off Balance** (id `stagger` kept), so the keyword means one thing and Drunken Master's "Stagger card" never misreads it. Drunken Master also deals 2 to a random enemy (a deck plays one or two Stagger cards a turn; 2 Barrier alone was about 0.4x). Hand-written upgrades: Powder Load+ Sharpen 4, Ironsights+ 3 Shells, Free-for-All+ 9 x3, Gunmetal Soul+ 5 Barrier, Scattergun Symphony+ 4 to all; Trigger Discipline+ and Ricochet+ gain Opening hand (a 1-Mana Aura is never upgraded to 0). Damage at a random enemy hit several times reads "to a random enemy 3 times" (each hit finds its own enemy, as the engine always did), for every such card. Stagger tests now check Blind Drunk's two base hits, that Free-for-All's hits land on different enemies, Stumble, Rummage the Bin and Sway; the Learn page lists Stagger.
395. **New figures for the Monk and the Trickster, art for the last seven companions, and the Monk is a man named Solen (Robert, 2026-10-28: "i think we should revise the monk and trickster character models based on the updated themes"; "the monk and hunter character look similar, not the clothes but the person"; "the trickster should be wearing a mask like a masquerade, just like some of the cards"; "i wanted the monk as a man") →** the Monk's figure was the Barbarian's (twin flaming scimitars) and looked like the same woman as the Hunter, so the new one is a bare-handed, shaved-headed, pale monk in ivory, saffron and gold with fire around his palm and fist; the persona Sehra becomes Solen (still hidden, #372) and the text says "he". The Trickster's figure was the Trapper's witch with a rope; the new one is a stage magician in a tailcoat and top hat with the cards' ivory-and-gold masquerade half-mask (teal gem crest), holding glowing suit cards. Spotter Drone, Generator, Bulwark Walker, Scrap Golem, Dreadnought, Pup and Pack Alpha get real figures (sheets `mechs-2`, `pack-2`, prompts in docs/companion-prompts.md), so every unit now has one. Tooling: every generated prompt asks ChatGPT to save a PNG with the right file name; `pack-heroes.ps1` skips the old class-name sources (totemist, trapper, barbarian); `pack-arenas.ps1` takes a per-sheet `holes` tolerance to clear background seen through gaps inside a figure. No rules change (RULES_VERSION stays 28).
396. **Aura upgrades the Slay the Spire way (Robert, 2026-10-28: "id like to see proposed upgrades for auras thats in line with how slay the spire determines upgrades", then "dont touch the knight. im ok with you proceeding with changes") →** 113 of 195 Auras cost 1 less when upgraded, and almost all of that was the `plus()` fallback: it never looks inside an Aura's `power` op, finds nothing to raise and cuts the cost. Slay the Spire raises a Power's number (about two in three), gives it Innate (one in six) or cuts its cost (one in six, only when the effect is on/off). 62 Auras now have hand-written upgrades: 48 raise their main number by about +30-50% (Rampage of Ire 6 → 9, Swarm Tactics 6 → 9, Masquerade 3 → 4 per suit, The Twelve-Gauge Opera Sharpen 6 → 9, Trap Door makes a Trip Line+...), 10 gain Opening hand where +1 would double them (Shell Game, Battle Rhythm, Legion, Hunter's Call, Trail of Blood, Hair Trigger, Reading the Signs, The Long Game, Patient Hunter, Lineage) and 4 lose some of their drawback (Ancient Fury 3 → 2 HP, Berserk Tempo 2 → 1, Iron Body 2 → 1, Ancient Pact 2 → 1). The Knight is untouched (Robert's), and the four Spellblade and Necromancer copies whose cost cut mirrors the original keep it. 51 Auras still get cheaper; `auraUpgrades.test.ts` lists them, so a new Aura can no longer fall into the cost cut unnoticed. Guide §12 rewritten (its "Aura: 1 less Mana" examples came from the fallback). Proposal and the full list: docs/AURA_UPGRADES_REVIEW.md. RULES_VERSION 29.
397. **The Necromancer gets early power: Grave Chill raises a Thrall every turn, and five small card fixes (Robert, 2026-10-28: "the necromancer feels weak. can you run it through the card cost analysis"; "he felt particularly weak in the first stage where everyone else felt ok or good ... i feel like raise 2 trait should be like raise 5 or something"; "ok lets go with raise 3 and the small card fixes you suggested") →** his cards price on budget (he is a reference class), but his trait was the weakest (~15 points a fight) and his Command cards dealt 0 once his 5-HP Thrall died to the first enemy hit, usually on turn 1. Grave Chill gains "At the start of your turn, Raise 3" (~+33 a fight counting the Fate each dying Thrall feeds it; Raise 5 would have been ~60, past every other trait). Card fixes: Bone Bulwark Raise 5 + 8 Barrier, Bone Sift Raise 5, Grave Robbery 6, Spirit Walk needs 6 consumed cards (9 almost never happened), Toll in Advance 5 Mana. Review: docs/NECROMANCER_REVIEW.md. RULES_VERSION 30.
398. **The Trickster's and the Necromancer's traits toned down (Robert, 2026-10-28: "lets tone down the trickster trait and the necro, for necro lets make the minion death add 5 per death and get rid of the starting fate. for the trickster, lets get rid of the barrier per turn and change the full trick to do more damage each time it get triggered") →** after #397 the two traits priced at ~40-50 (Trickster) and ~48 (Necromancer) against ~15-30 for the rest. Lying in Wait loses its 4 Barrier a turn (about -24 a fight); its Full Trick deals 5 to every enemy plus 3 for every Full Trick before it this fight (5, 8, 11...), counted by a new generic metric `fullTricks` (SideState.fullTricks, incremented after each Full Trick goes off). Grave Chill loses the 3 Fate on every enemy at the start and curses 5 Fate (was 3) whenever an ally dies; a consumed card still curses 3. By the guide's rates the Necromancer change is about even (-2.7 at the start, +1.4 per death), but 5 Fate on one enemy reaches a kill sooner than 3 spread out; Raise 3 is still the bulk of his trait.
399. **Sharpen carries over to new copies; a Sharpen badge for either weapon; the Suits and Boons are movable panels (Robert, 2026-10-28: "sharpen should carry over to other sharpened cards that are newly created. we also should display sharpen as a buff below the character model. the trick suits get overlaped by aura, lets make sure it all fits up there and that those ui display elements are modular and can be moved (same for the boons display)") →** each side keeps a running Sharpen total per card (`SideState.forged`); `addCardTo` gives a new copy of that card (or its "+") the total, so a gun from Double Tap or Spare Barrel is as sharp as the others, and a Sharpen with no copy left makes one with the whole total. The hero's "Sharpened +N" buff chip (under the HP bar) works for the Old Twelve Gauge as well as the Obsidian Blade and names the weapon. The Suits pips no longer float over the hero (where the Auras list covered them): they are a FloatPanel ("Suits", "Their Suits") in that side's column under the trait, and the Boons are one too ("Your Boons", "Their Boons"); like the Auras lists and the battle log they can be dragged, resized, folded and put back with a double-click, remembered per browser. Checked in the browser at 1600 wide: trait, Suits, Auras stack without overlap; a dragged Suits panel stays where it is dropped.
400. **The Old Twelve Gauge's Sharpen is called Precision, a separate stat (Robert, 2026-10-28: "lets go with precision for now, it a separate stat than Sharpened so he cant get an obsidian blade and stack it too") →** a card can name its own word for Sharpen (`CardDef.forgeWord`); the Old Twelve Gauge's is "Precision", so its cards read "Precision 2 (Old Twelve Gauge gains 2 damage)", its tooltip, card badge ("Precision +3"), hero buff and battle-log line say Precision, and the Obsidian Blade keeps "Sharpen". The two were already separate underneath: each forge op names its weapon and the running total is kept per card, so a Precision card never sharpens the Blade and a Sharpen card never adds Precision. Review fix to #399: the carry-over to new copies is for weapons only (`CardDef.weapon` on the Blade and the gun); Mounting Fury, Reaping Scythe and Gathering Storm also grow through `forge`, and a new copy of them still starts fresh.
401. **Grave Chill raises 2 a turn, not 3 (Robert, 2026-10-28: "ok lest do raise 2 and ship it for now") →** after #398 the Necromancer's trait priced at ~53 (Raise 3 ~27, 5 Fate on each of ~5 Thrall deaths ~17, the starting Thrall 9), above every other class; #398 itself had moved him up, not down (it took 3 Fate off the start and added 2 to every death). Raise 2 puts him at ~43, second, beside the Trickster's Suits deck (~55) and above the Engineer (~29), and still leaves a Thrall standing for his Commands every turn.
402. **Skins: cut like hero figures, listed in the art manifest, shown on the Store (Robert, 2026-10-28: "we almost have skins in the heroes/skins folder, but get things ready to display these alternate skins on the store page") →** a skin is `assets-src/heroes/skins/<heroId>-<skin-name>.png` (the class id, then the name in kebab case; `<heroId>-gender-<x>` is the class drawn as the other gender). `tools/pack-heroes.ps1` cuts it like the hero (same background key, the Engineer's drone corner wiped) into `public/art/figures/skins/`, and `pack-manifest.ps1` lists it under `skins` (skin id -> file). The Store's "Riftwalker looks" shelf becomes a gallery of whatever skins exist, grouped by class, named from the file ("Moonlit Regent", "Female Engineer"), each marked "Coming soon": nothing can be bought or worn yet, and how a skin is bought, unlocked and worn in a fight is Robert's to decide (it touches monetisation). Skins in so far, each class with a named outfit and a gender swap: Knight (Moonlit Regent; Male Knight), Engineer (Aether Forgemaster; Female Engineer), Hunter (Tempest Prowler; Male Hunter), then the rest arriving the same day. A skin file named with an old class name (totemist-...) is filed under the class it is now (shaman-...). Cut missing-art sheets move to assets-src/cards/_cut/ with their card lists in docs/card-art/sheets-cut.json, so every picture can still be re-cut (pack-cards.ps1 reads them there); Wrote docs/card-art/missing.md and 0 batch files: 0 cards (0 borrowed), 0 sheets does the moving.
403. **Grave Chill curses 3 Fate on a death again (Robert, 2026-10-28: "Let's update the necromancers fate on minion death to 3 like you said") →** #398's 5 a death had moved him up, not down (about +7 a fight with a Thrall dying most turns); a death and a consumed card both curse 3 now. With Raise 2 (#401) his trait prices at ~36, between the Trickster's Suits deck and the Engineer.
404. **The Full Trick starts at 3 damage (Robert, 2026-10-28: "Let's bring tricksters starting trick damage down from 5 to 3") →** 3 to every enemy, 3 more for every Full Trick before it in the fight (3, 6, 9...), and a card.
405. **A summon card can be played with no room for all of its companions (Robert, 2026-10-28: "make sure players can play summon cards even if all the summons can't make it on the field, just prompt them about not every minion can be played or they have to sacrifice a different minion") →** #383 made a card whose summons did not all fit playable only by scrapping companions to make room. Now it can also be played as it is: what fits is summoned in the card's order, the rest is not (the summon op already skipped a companion with no slot). `legalActions` lists the scrap choices first and the as-is play last; `summonsThatFit(db, side, card)` says how many would come. The fight screen's no-room prompt says so ("Or play it anyway: only 2 of its 3 companions come" / "none of its companions come") beside the choice of companion to scrap, with Play anyway, Scrap them (when every companion must go) and Cancel. RULES_VERSION 32.
406. **Skins on a player's account, got free in the Store and chosen on a Cosmetics page (Robert, 2026-10-28: "Build out the ability to add a skin to a players account and a page to select which cosmetics they want active. Just make the purchase of the skins free for now.") →** the Store's catalog is data (`SKINS` in packages/content/src/v2/cosmetics.ts: id, class, price 0). A player's cosmetics (the skins they own and the look each Riftwalker wears, with when it was chosen) live on their Vault (`cosmetics` action: one JSON row in the Vault's Durable Object, so a Vault code or a signed-in account), and a copy in the browser (works offline, draws at once). Copies merge with `mergeCosmetics` (packages/pvp): owning only grows, and for each Riftwalker the newest choice wins; `cleanCosmetics` keeps only catalog skins worn by their own class. The Store has Get (free) and Wear on each look; `#/cosmetics` (Cosmetics) lists each Riftwalker's own look and the skins owned for it, to choose one. The look you wear is your hero on the home screen, in the character picker and on your side of a fight; an opponent of the same class keeps their own look (an opponent's skin over the network is for later). Because every skin is free, the server accepts any catalog skin a device says it owns; a priced skin will need the server to grant it after a purchase, which is Robert's to decide (monetisation). Review fixes: the skin your hero wears is fixed when a fight starts and only if its figure is already in (a figure that changed mid-fight was drawn at the wrong size); getting or wearing a skin starts loading it; watching a match or replay draws everyone in their own look; a guest with no Vault code and no skins does not sync (no Vault is made just for opening the page). Open: a skin removed from the catalog is dropped from Vaults on their next sync (keep unknown ids before skins are paid for); and #405 lets a summon card count as a played card (its suit, on-play Auras, Consume) with no companion lost, worth costing for the summon classes.
407. **Every chance is shown on the slider reel, with tiles that are the odds; no more coin (Robert, 2026-10-28: "Let's update the coin toss to use the slider slot machine and dynamically load the options on the slot machine to fit the odds of whatever is being rolled for that event. I've seen some 60/40 events use coin toss and that's not good, so let's just get rid of coin toss and use slider slot machine every time. Even in arena coin toss") →** an event's chance (`eventFlips`) is shown by `ChanceReel` (it replaced ChanceCoin, a coin for an even chance and a wheel otherwise) on the Brass Reel. The reel takes a `cycle`: `oddsCycle(pct)` is a run of 20 tiles (100 when the chance is not a multiple of 5) with exactly the chance's share of "Your way" tiles, spread evenly, so any ten tiles of a 60% reel show 6 wins; the strip repeats it, turned so a tile of the result (already decided by the rules) stops under the pointer. The odds are also written under it ("A 60% chance: 12 of every 20 tiles go your way"). The Arena's who-goes-first coin (when both players want the same) is the same reel, a 50/50 run of the two players' names, then the winner's portrait. Presentation only: no rule or roll changed. The Trials practice page spins a 60% chance.
408. **The Knight's own look is a man in sun armour; his skins are Sunwarden and Female Knight (Robert, 2026-10-28: "please get the updated base knight model and the skins for it") →** `assets-src/heroes/knight.png` is now a helmed man in white-and-gold sun plate; the skins are `knight-sunwarden` (the same, crowned) and `knight-gender-female` (the blonde woman in moon armour who was the Moonlit Regent). The catalog drops `knight-moonlit-regent` and `knight-gender-male` (the man is the own look now). `RENAMED_SKINS` maps an old skin id to its new one and `cleanCosmetics` reads it so, so whoever got the Moonlit Regent owns (and wears) the Female Knight. The Knight's blurb no longer says "she" ("a deck that burns down to a point"); the hidden persona name Valeria is Robert's to change or keep.
409. **The Store redesigned, and a Cosmetics Vault for what you own (Robert, 2026-10-28: "lets have a Vault of cosmetics for players and design it so it looks better. the store tabs should all stay at the top while player scroll down and can navigate tabs. the vault should be similar but for the players owned cosmetics. again the store needs to look better becuase its just super long page right now") →** the Store (#/store) and the Cosmetics Vault (#/cosmetics, called that because "Vault" already means a player's saved decks) share one frame (`ShopParts.tsx`, tabs in `shopTabs.ts`): a compact header with a Store / Vault switch, and a bar that stays at the top while the page scrolls, holding the kinds of cosmetics as tabs (Riftwalker looks, Card backs, Battlefields, Card frames, Titles and banners, Emotes) and, under Riftwalker looks, a row of classes (All and each Riftwalker, with its icon; they wrap on a wide screen and scroll on a phone). One kind at a time instead of one long page: looks are a grid of tiles, each a figure on a lit stage with its name, class, a tag (Free, Owned, Wearing) and Get (free) / Wear. A kind with nothing yet shows one Coming soon panel. The Vault: under All, each Riftwalker in the look it wears now (pick one to change it); under a class, its own look and the skins you own for it, to wear, and how many more the Store has. The tab and class you left each page on are remembered in this browser.
410. **Last Call and The Long Memory are Auras (Robert, 2026-10-28: "lets re-balance the drunkard card Last Call, its another one that sounds like it should be an aura. i see there are more too that have similar wording 'for the rest of the fight', please just make those auras with improved wording. and run them through the card cost analysis. i would suspect mana per round for the rest of the fight to be higher cost than 1 even if hangover is applied") →** of the non-Aura cards that say "for the rest of the fight", only these two set up something that lasts (+1 Mana every turn, from a Skill that Consumed); the rest change only themselves (Mounting Fury, Gathering Storm, Reaping Scythe and Crowned Fist grow their own damage; Spare Blades, Gale Strike and Carve a Notch their own cost), which is a card, not an Aura. Priced by the guide: +1 Mana a turn is ~28 over a fight (§5, 7 a Mana for the first two), a 2-cost Aura (Inner Furnace, Ascendant Form, Deep Pockets, Attunement); a 1-cost one needs a real price (Blood Pact: 7 HP, ~-10). Last Call (Drunkard, rare) was 1 Mana, 4 Hangover (~-6, and nearly free in a Hangover deck), Consume: now a 2-cost Aura, "Gain 2 Hangover. At the start of your turn: Gain 1 Mana." (the Hangover is his flavour and feeds his payoffs); Last Call+ gains Opening hand. The Long Memory (ancestral) was 1 Mana with no price, Hold, Consume (~40% over): now a 2-cost Aura with Opening hand, "At the start of your turn: Gain 1 Mana." (Opening hand is its ancestral edge over Attunement); its upgrade costs 1. RULES_VERSION 33.
411. **Five trait changes (Robert, 2026-10-29: "lets update trickster's trait to target a random enemy instead of all of them ... lets add monk 1 barrier when switching stances. lets update drunkard to increased damage per missing hp ... i need you to figure out the right number for how it scales down. the necromancer's starting thrall should be 2 hp. and engineers starting drone should have 6 hp") →** the Full Trick strikes a random enemy (the Trickster keeps AoE on her cards: Masquerade, the Trap payoffs); Inner Fire gives 1 Fervor and 1 Barrier a stance; **Clutch Genes is 1% more attack damage for every HP missing** (`HeroTrait.clutch: { perMissingHp }`; the engine keeps the `clutch` status at missing HP x the rate, rounded down, on every HP change and at turn start). 1 a HP was picked because at 44 of 88 it gives +44%, about the old +50% at half HP, it starts paying from the first HP lost, and it prices at ~20 a fight with ~30 HP missing on average, level with the middle of the roster; 0.75 would be ~16. A hero's `startUnits` entry can carry its own HP (`{ unit, hp }`), so the starting Drone has 6 HP (Deploy Drone's still 8) and the starting Bone Thrall 2. Prices after the change (the guide, 5-turn fight, starter deck): Trickster ~24 (Suits deck ~39), Necromancer ~33, Engineer ~28, Monk ~16 (Flow ~40), Drunkard ~20 (6-35 with HP).
412. **Inner Fire gives Primed on leaving Fury, not Barrier; the Full Trick grows by 2 and the Trickster starts with 1 extra card (Robert, 2026-10-29: "lets remove the barrier for the monks stance change and just give him 1-2 primed when leaving fury stance. pick the number that fits him into the ranges best", then "decreas full trick to increase by 2 damage each time its triggered, and only start the game with 1 additional card drawn") →** a new generic trigger, `stanceLeft` (with a `stance` filter, like `stance`), fires when the hero leaves a stance; Inner Fire is 1 Fervor a stance entered plus **2 Primed** on leaving Fury. 2 over 1: a starter deck leaves Fury about twice a fight, so 1 Primed put the trait at ~13, the bottom of the roster, and 2 puts it at ~15 (a Flow deck, about one Fury exit a turn, ~37: the top of the build-path ceilings, where the Trickster's Suits deck and the Necromancer's Souls deck sit). The Full Trick is 3, 5, 7... (`per('fullTricks', 2)`), and Lying in Wait draws 1 at the start (was 2).
413. **The stance exits are Inner Fire's; a Thrall's death curses 2 Fate (Robert, 2026-10-29: "for necro change the fate on death to 2. and for monk lets make sure the stance changes out of serene and fury are included in the trait passive, this way we can calculate it in our trait strength table") →** leaving Serene's 2 Mana (once a turn) was an engine rule for anyone in Serene; it is now a trait trigger, `on('stanceLeft', [mana(CALM_EXIT_MANA)], { stance: 'calm', once: true })`, beside Leave Fury's 2 Primed, so Inner Fire's text holds every stance exit. The engine's `calmPaid` went (a trait trigger's `once` does the same; trait triggers had been dropping `once`, fixed). Since only the Monk gets the Mana, a card that enters Serene is owner-only (`readsOwnerOnly` in campaign `rewards.ts`) and no longer crosses to other Riftwalkers, who would get nothing for leaving it. Grave Chill: 2 Fate on an ally's death, still 3 on a consumed card. The card costing guide keeps Enter Serene at 4 on the cards, so the Monk's trait price now counts Mana his cards are also priced with (noted in the guide).
414. **The five baseline classes are rebuilt with their own identities, and archived first (Robert, 2026-10-29: "Read docs/BASE_CLASS_REWORKS.md and implement the reworks described there. I approve the recommended Martial Forms/Mastery option for Monk as well ... First archive and verify the existing character versions ... Complete this before changing gameplay") ->** supersedes "the Knight is exactly the Ironclad's card list, as a yardstick" (#367) and the Silent/Regent/Necrobinder/Defect copies kept as baselines (#368-371): the Knight, Assassin, Shaman and Spellblade are rebuilt around new mechanics, and the Monk on Martial Forms and Mastery; the Necromancer stays as he is. The old versions are frozen in `reviews/balance-baselines/2026-10-29-pre-identity-reworks/` (resolved JSON of every card, upgrade, hero, trait and totem; readable card lists and rules; a `git archive` of the engine, content, campaign and PvP source with the lockfile; asset hashes; metadata; `VALIDATION.md`), made and checked by `tools/src/baselineArchive.ts` and `baselineVerify.ts`, ignored by ESLint and Prettier, outside every import and test. Shared choices, made once for all five: **(a) pool size:** each rebuilt pool is ~50-60 cards (the old ones were ~80 because they copied a whole Slay the Spire list card for card); three strategies of 12+ cards each plus glue, no near-duplicates; flagged for Robert as a smaller depth than before. **(b) saves:** a removed card becomes its nearest new card everywhere (`RETIRED_CARDS`, as since #312), and a run of a rebuilt class saved before its rework is migrated on load, never read as new rules: it is stamped (`RunState.reworked`, `CLASS_REWORKS` in content), the player gets a notice saying what changed, and a fight in progress starts again from its first turn (its move log belonged to the old rules). **(c) Arena and replays:** `RULES_VERSION` 34 refuses old matches and replays. **(d) balance:** priced by hand (`docs/14_CARD_COSTING.md` §15 for the new rates, §14 labels the old class rates legacy), every card and upgrade in a generated ledger (`docs/reworks/<class>-ledger.md` from `tools/src/ledger/`); no simulations. **(e) art:** none generated; new cards use the procedural placeholder; `docs/CLASS_REWORK_ART.md` lists reuse, review, new and retired pictures.
415. **The Knight: Guard, Riposte and Thorns (docs/BASE_CLASS_REWORKS.md §4; docs/reworks/KNIGHT.md) ->** a new generic piece, **Guard** (`HeroTrait.openings`): an enemy attack hit of 1 or more that her Barrier soaks completely leaves the attacker Open (`opening`, one per enemy; not a Shielded, Ghostly, Trap-cancelled or overflow hit; checked after the hit's modifiers; it goes at the end of the turn of the side that can spend it). **Riposte** spends the target's Opening: `counter(n, bonus)` (a damage effect with `riposte`: every hit deals the bonus more) or the `riposte` op (does its ops); ordinary attacks never spend one, and a Riposte on a target killed earlier finds nothing. Cards can open an enemy (`leaveOpen`), but only an Opening Guard earns sets off "earn" triggers (`opening`), so no card can feed Bastion Engine or Iron Cadence. New triggers `opening`, `riposte`, `thorns` (your Thorns struck an attacker) and metrics `opening`, `foesOpen`, `foesAttacking`. Trait **Hold and Answer** (6 Barrier at the start, Guard, 3 Barrier the first time each turn an Opening is earned; was Oathkeeper's 6 Barrier and 6 Plating); starter 4 Strike, 4 Defend, Sundering Blow (2: 9, Riposte +6, 1 Exposed), Briarplate (1: 5 Barrier, 2 Thorns). Pool 54 (8 kept, 8 changed, 38 new), 67 removed into their nearest new cards. Rates (guide §15): a Riposte bonus at 0.55 of its value, a card that opens an enemy 4, an earned-Opening trigger 2.6 times over four turns, a spent-Opening trigger 2.2, a once-a-turn Thorns trigger 2.8; 52 of 54 cards within ±15%. Fixed on the way: the "next 1 turn" wording of a one-answer trigger now reads "The next time you ...". Rallying Cry's Mana is next turn's (the first draft refunded itself while an enemy was Open, a loop with several copies). The Knight's shield animation is now for Riposte cards.
416. **The Assassin: Preparation and embedded knives (docs/BASE_CLASS_REWORKS.md §6; docs/reworks/ASSASSIN.md) ->** two connected resources. **Preparation** (`prep`, at most 3, kept between turns): setup cards and the trait give it (`prepare`), finishers spend it (`spend` op, `spendPrep`, read with `prepSpent`); an ordinary attack never clears it. **Knives** (`knives` on an enemy, at most 3): `embed` puts them in after the card's attack, only into a living enemy one of its hits landed on (not a hit a Trap or Shielded stopped; with no attack before it, always); a knife does nothing while it sits there; `extract` (`pull`) takes up to N, exactly what is there, each running its payoff aimed at the enemy it came from, and is gone; the dead keep theirs (no loot), nothing spreads. New generic pieces: the `spend` op and trigger (with a `status` narrowing, reused by the Monk's Mastery), triggers `embed`/`extract`, metrics `knives`, `knivesAll`, `prep`, `extracted`, `prepSpent`, and the preview works a spend or pull out before the damage. Trait **Knife Work** (1 Preparation and a knife in every enemy at the start, 1 Preparation the first time each turn you embed, 3 damage for every knife pulled; was Hidden Blades, a Dagger a turn). Starter 4 Strike, 3 Defend, Quiet Incision, Draw Steel, Finishing Thrust, Patient Footwork (11 cards; was 12). Pool 55 (27 kept or changed, 28 new), 57 removed into their nearest new cards; the Dagger token, Edge, the Poison package and the Sly engine are gone from his pool (Bleed, Sly and Edge stay in the engine for anyone). Rates (guide §15): a knife 2, a pull its payoff minus 2 a knife, Preparation 1.5, a spend its payoff minus 1.5 a point; 53 of 55 within ±15%. The validator now reads a Riposte, an embed or a pull as using the target, and a trait trigger that answers something done to an enemy as aimed. Knight review fixes on the way: the ghost-Warden fight log is cleared too on a migration, the notice is never kept from the save text, a trial is settled on the migrated load, a Riposte's bonus only lands on the Open target, and the live card text says "(Riposte counted.)" instead of a +N on top of a number that already has it.
417. **The Spellblade: Imbue and Discharge (docs/BASE_CLASS_REWORKS.md §7; docs/reworks/SPELLBLADE.md) ->** her Obsidian Blade holds two enchantments (`SideState.enchants`, `ENCHANT_SLOTS` 2, in order, duplicates allowed, kept between turns, cleared at the fight's end). Spells `imbue` Ember (+3 on the discharging attack's first hit), Glass (2 to every enemy once that attack is done, a non-attack shard) or Ward (3 Barrier at once); with both positions taken the player chooses which one is replaced (a replacement is not a discharge); `ifRoom` only into a free one. Blade attacks `discharge(1)` (the player chooses when the two differ) or `discharge(2)` (both, once each); the discharge must come before the card's damage (validator); a card with no attack loses its Ember and breaks its Glass at once. `recast` changes the newest charge's kind; `twinedge` makes the next first charge count twice (never a copy). Triggers `imbue`, `discharge`, `dischargeMixed`; metrics `enchants`, `enchantKinds`, `wards`, `discharged`; the attack preview counts the Ember a discharge would land (a range when the choice is the player's). Trait **Glass and Ember** (a Ward at the start; the first Imbue each turn draws 1 and gives 2 Barrier; was Kindled: Arcana and Sharpen each turn). Starter 4 Strike, 4 Defend, Ember Inscription, Obsidian Cut. Pool 53 (41 kept or changed, 12 new), 46 removed into their nearest new cards (the Sovereign Blade/Sharpen, Arcana/Stars, Court/Retainer and Hollow Form engines); the Obsidian Blade is no longer a Sharpen weapon (the Drunkard's Precision is untouched; the `forge` op and Arcana stay in the engine, unused by her). Rates (guide §15): a charge Ember 3 / Glass 2.6 / Ward 3.6, an Imbue 0.85 of it, a Discharge +1 (one) or +1.5 (both); 52 of 53 within ±15% after ten retunes (Hollow Gaze 7 Barrier, Tyrant Strike 15, Warm Anvil+ 3 Barrier, Call the Blade +3 Barrier, Cleaving Edge+ 5, Dying Ember 10, Banked Embers +3 Barrier, Scheme +2 Barrier, Emberglass Covenant 6 Barrier, Mirror Glass 11). On the way: a card being played is no longer swept into a reshuffle its own draw causes in the display (`display.ts`), and two condition texts read "your blade has no enchantments" / "your blade holds a Ward".
418. **The Shaman: commanded totems (docs/BASE_CLASS_REWORKS.md §5; docs/reworks/SHAMAN.md) ->** a new generic piece, **totems** (`HeroTrait.totems` gives the side three fixed positions, `SideState.totems`; spirits are `TotemDef`s in `db.totems`): a totem does nothing on its own; once a turn the player **commands** one with a new action, `{ type: 'command', totem, target?, empower? }` (offered by `legalActions`, cleaned by the server, accepted by replays): **Hawk** 4 damage to a chosen enemy (not an attack), **Bear** 4 Barrier to a chosen friendly, **Elder** 1 Spirit and a card (never empowered). `plant` goes into the first empty position; with all three taken the player chooses one to replace **or keeps them all**; `plant(x, true)` chooses any position; `plant('*')` chooses the kind (two choices in a row). Routine detail decided: an empty position is filled first, in order, rather than asked for (the positions have no adjacency or order rules, so asking which empty one would be a meaningless prompt); a chosen-position plant asks. **Spirit** (`spirit`, at most 3, kept) is spent one a command for +2 (`empower`); **Rousing** (`rousing`, this turn) adds to the next Hawk or Bear command; **Shelter** spreads the next Bear command to every friendly; `uproot` empties the positions. Triggers `command`/`plant`/`replace` (narrowed by spirit); metrics `totems`, `totemKinds`, `totemsOf`, `spirit`, `commanded`, `uprooted`, `replacedNow`, `spiritSpent`. Trait **Spirit Caller** (the positions, the command, a Hawk at the start; was Totem-bound: a Hawk, a Bear and a Splinter pulsing every turn). Starter 4 Strike, 4 Defend, Wardkeeper's Pact, Guided Talon (was Hawk Call and Twin Awakening). Pool 53 (31 kept or changed, 22 new), 53 removed (the orb queue, Focus, Owl Mana, the 0-cost recursion and status-card packages; the Owl and Splinter are gone). The old runes (`channel`/`evoke`/`pulse`/`runeSlots`/`resonance`) stay in the engine unused; the content no longer loads the rune defs. Rates (guide §15): a command ~4.5 a turn (the trait's), a new kind planted 2 (a probable Hawk 1), Rousing 0.8, Spirit 1.8; 51 of 53 within ±15% (Gale Strike 8, Frost Needle 4, and the Arc Flash/Frost Needle upgrades retuned). The bots command (the greedy bot after its last useful card). Routine details decided against the brief's first sketch: Shared Shelter gives the Bear's full Barrier to every friendly (the brief sketched the hero and one companion at reduced values; he has no companions of his own, so it is the hero alone in almost every fight and a second rule for the rare companion was not worth it); Ancestral Exchange's payoff has no once-a-turn limit (it is a paid 1-cost card, never free swapping; Circle of Ancestors, the free payoff, is once a turn); a Hawk command is bound by Taunt as an aimed card is; Rousing reads "your next Hawk or Bear command" (the Elder neither uses nor wastes it). Review fixes (reviews/shaman-review.md): a command being aimed is let go whenever a card, a potion or another action is chosen, and when its totem is gone; Empower counts only while a command can spend Spirit, never disables the Elder, and resets each turn and on Esc. Spellblade review fixes on the way (#417): the preview counts "per enchantment discharged" (Overcharge Edge, Glass Requiem), Glass Requiem's shards only fire when something was discharged (a 0-damage hit sprang Thorns and Traps), the choice window resets between two choices in a row, and the display applies blade (and totem) changes as their events play.
419. **The Monk: Martial Forms and Mastery (docs/BASE_CLASS_REWORKS.md §12, Option A, approved by Robert in the rework brief; docs/reworks/MONK.md) ->** a new generic piece, **forms** (`HeroTrait.forms` gives the side `SideState.form`: Root, Current or Ember). A form names the two techniques that complete his objective this turn: **Root** two different Defensive techniques, **Current** a Defensive then a Striking technique of another card, **Ember** two different Striking techniques aimed at the same enemy. Only cards tagged `defensive` or `striking` count (card text: "Defensive technique." / "Striking technique."), only when played from hand (a free play from a pile is not a step; an echo replays the effects, not the step), a card is one step, and the objective completes **at most once a turn** however often he changes form; then he gains 1 **Mastery** (`mastery`, at most 3, kept) and `technique` triggers answer. `form` changes form and starts the turn's progress again (`form('*')` asks, keeping is allowed). Spenders use the generic `spend` op (`spendMastery`, at most 2 a card, tested). Routine details decided: the player-facing tags are **Defensive** and **Striking** (the brief's "Guard" is already the Knight's mechanic); he starts in Current (a Defensive and a Striking card are in about 58% of starter hands; Root or Ember about 22%); the starter is 3 Strike, 3 Defend, Still Guard, Planted Heel, Kindle Strike (a small Mastery spender, so the first completion has a use) and Turning Palm, no rare. Trait **Martial Forms** (start in Current; a completion gives 1 Mastery and 3 Barrier; was Inner Fire: Fervor on each stance, Serene's 2 Mana, Fury's Primed); no sigil (he no longer starts Serene). Pool 53 (35 kept or changed, 18 new), 45 removed (the Fury, Flow and Radiant packages; his three shelved Legendaries, Perfect Balance, Still Flame Ascendant and Eye of the Storm, are gone). The stances, Fervor and Radiant stay in the engine, unused by any live card. Rates (guide §15): a completion ~60% of turns (starter) to ~80%, a Mastery spend 0.8 of its payoff at ~1.5 a card, Mastery a card gives 2.5; 53 of 53 within ±15%. Monk similarity to the Watcher is no longer assessed here: the rule system the brief named (Calm refunds, Wrath's multiplier, 10 to Divinity) is gone. Review fixes (reviews/monk-review.md): a technique step remembers the enemy it was aimed at by id, so a strike that kills its target and then asks a choice still completes Ember; Still Water draws after another technique this turn (it never drew on the completing play); Shaman and Monk cards that only their traits make worth anything (totems, commands, Spirit, Rousing, Shelter, forms, techniques, Mastery) are never Rift-crossed offers to another hero; the stances, Fervor and the old Focus are no longer taught on the Learn page; the form choice says "Keep this form".
420. **The Shaman rebuilt again: elements, totems and Trinity (Robert, 2026-10-29: "i like the idea of him starting with the 3 totems. i also want him to cast elements himself and have some trinity factor included ... his trinity should increase spirit power ... triggered by using 3 elements in single turn. flame front and storm, flame is single target, frost is defensive but can be offensive with faltering included, and storm is multi target. he also needs utitlity cards"; then: totems boost spells, "commands can be multiple times in a round", Hawk, Bear and Thunderbird, "spirit power never goes down", "no cap on spirit", "remove the trinity count, its whole purpose is just to increase his spirit power", "make sure the elemtents all come with offensive and defensive capabilities", "i also want the auras, utility, and stacking gathering storm that is multi target", and "lets display trinity similar to how we do with tricksters full trick"; docs/reworks/SHAMAN_ELEMENTS_PROPOSAL.md, SHAMAN.md) ->** replaces #418's Shaman (one aimed command a turn, Empower, Rousing, Shelter), which never shipped. **Elements:** a spell's tag `flame`, `frost` or `storm` (`prism`: the element not cast yet this turn); card text "Flame." / "Frost." / "Storm.". Flame is single-target damage and Bleed, with Thorns (Flame Shield, Pyre Ward, Burning Aegis) for defence; Frost is Barrier and Faltering, with attacks that leave enemies Faltering; Storm hits every enemy or random ones, with Sapped (Wind Wall, Static Veil, Calm Before the Storm) for defence. **Totems** (`TotemDef`: `boost`, `onPlant`, `onTrinity`, `rare`): he starts with a **Hawk**, a **Bear** and a **Thunderbird**; each helps the first spell of its element each turn (+3 Flame, +3 Frost, +2 a Storm hit; two of a kind help twice); two rare ones only from rare cards, the **Elder Tree** (2 Spirit when planted, +2 to the first spell of any element, command 1 Spirit and a card) and the **Moon Owl** (1 Mana when Trinity triggers, command: the next elemental spell free). Positions start at 3 and grow to 5 (`totemSlot`); a totem stays unless he replaces it; "a totem of your choice" never offers a rare one. **Commands** (`SideState.commands`, one a turn from the trait, more from `commands` and `commandAll`): the action is now just `{ type: 'command', totem }` and the spirit aims by itself (Hawk 6 to a random enemy, Bear 5 Barrier to him, Thunderbird 3 to every enemy); the same totem can be commanded again. **Trinity:** the first time each turn he has cast a spell of every element, 1 Spirit (`trinity` trigger; the `elements` event); no count, streak or halving. **Spirit:** no cap, never goes down: +1 to every command, +1 per 2 to every totem's help (an exception to the guide's "no uncapped permanent scaling", by Robert's decision; priced at 5 a point). **Free Spell** (`freespell`): the next elemental spell costs 0. Trait **Spirit and Trinity**; starter 2 Strike, 2 Defend, 2 Ember Bolt, 2 Frost Ward, 2 Gathering Storm (a Flame, a Frost and a Storm spell are in about 40% of its hands). **Gathering Storm** hits every enemy and every copy grows each time one is cast (Tempest Call grows them too; its weapon word is "Gather", not Sharpen). Pool 83 (Flame 17, Frost 15, Storm 16, the rest totem, Trinity and utility glue), 81 of 83 within ±15%; Auras in every element. The first rework's cards that left map to their nearest new ones (`CLASS_REWORKS.shaman` version 2), through a chain from the Defect ids. Ids that collided with other pools were renamed (Ice Shatter, Charged Sky). On the screen: the Totems panel (up to 5 buttons, Spirit, commands left; no aiming, no Empower) and a **Trinity panel shown the way the Trickster's Suits are**: three element pips that light as he casts, the card under the pointer shows its pip, the row flares at Trinity with "+1 Spirit". Pool-shape ownership: growing another card (Tempest Call) is the Drunkard's and his; Bleed stacking (Wildfire, which Consumes and adds at most 10) is his, the Assassin's having gone with #416. Trinity, totem and Spirit cards are never Rift-crossed offers to another hero; his elemental spells may be.
421. **The Shaman: no free command, and Burn instead of Bleed (Robert, 2026-10-29: "he shouldnt have any free command crap, he should need to have a card that commands each one, or all whatever. and he shouldnt bleed it should be burn that works the same") ->** a totem acts **only when a card commands it**: the `command` action, `SideState.commands`, `HeroTrait.commands` and the `commands`/`commandAll` ops are gone; one op, `totemCommand` (`commandTotem(spirit, times)`), commands every totem of a kind ("Command your Hawks"), one of the player's choice (`'choose'`, a `commandPick` choice of position), a random one or all of them. New cards: **Spirit Call** (basic: a totem of your choice, 2 Barrier; in the starter in place of a Defend), **Hawk's Dive**, **Bear's Guard**, **Thunderbird's Cry**; Hawk Call commands the Hawks it joins, Call Again commands a random totem, Rally the Spirits one of your choice twice, Spirit Chorus a random one at the start of each turn, Great Spirit's Call all of them; Storm Sovereign 3 → 4 (a command is rarer now). The Totems panel shows the totems and Spirit and has nothing to press. **Burn** (`burn`) is a status of its own that works exactly as Bleed (ticks its stacks at the start of the unit's turn, then loses 1; scaled by difficulty; a debuff), so his Burn and anyone's Bleed never mix; every Shaman card that applied Bleed applies Burn. Pool 87, 85 within ±15%. Review fixes for #420 (reviews/shaman-v2-review.md): the number shown on a card counts the totems' help it will get (`previewCardDamage`); the totem texts say what the help does (+N to every damage and Barrier number on the first spell of its element); Smoulder burns a random enemy, so a Flame spell with no target still Burns; the new Flashpoint and Wildfire took new ids (`flash-burn`, `spreading-flames`) and the old Assassin and Knight ids `flashpoint` and `wildfire` map to their old replacements again (an old save never picks up a Shaman card); Prism counts as an element for Trinity and the help, not for the element Auras (said on the card).
422. **The Shaman's balance and interaction pass (Robert's review brief, 2026-10-29: fix the Call Again / Elder loop, recost spells by their real totem interactions, review duplicate totems and repeated commands, stop utility spells wasting the help, give Frost a finish, keep the pool; docs/reworks/SHAMAN.md; the earlier cards frozen in reviews/balance-baselines/2026-10-29-shaman-balance-pass/) ->** **Call Again Consumes** (and its upgrade): three Elder Trees and two Call Agains in a thinned deck commanded each other for ever, each Elder drawing the other Call Again back (reproduced in a test, now stopped); every other command card costs Mana, and Spirit stays uncapped. **The help** (a totem's first-spell boost) goes only to a spell with a damage or Barrier number of its own, on every hit and every enemy of it, and **never to an "If ...:" bonus** (an engine rule, `when` runs with no help); a utility spell of the element leaves the help for the next spell and still counts for Trinity; the card's shown number counts it. A conditional bonus on a totem he starts with (Kindle's Hawk, Rime Shield's Bear) is priced as certain.
