Multi-hit and mixed spells pay for the help on their extra hits and numbers at x0.6 (the chance they are the first of their element): Squall 2 x4 (was x6), Chain Lightning 3 x3 (4), Eye of the Tempest 3 x6 (x7), Twin Cinders 4 x2 (5), Prism Spell 4 and 3 Barrier (lost its 1 to all). **Named commands move one totem of the kind** (`commandOne`: Hawk's Dive, Bear's Guard, Thunderbird's Cry, Hawk Call; three Hawks at 4 Spirit made Hawk's Dive+ deal 60 for 1 Mana), priced over a fight at 40% early (one totem, 0 Spirit), 40% mid (two of the kind, 3 Spirit) and 20% late (3-4, 7 Spirit); the duplicate-totem payoff is **Great Spirit's Call** (every totem, now 3 Mana), with Rally the Spirits 2 Mana, Spirit Chorus a 2-Mana Aura (+2 Barrier), Storm Sovereign back to 3 (one card can command several totems, each answering once). **Frost finishes:** Ice Shatter deals 6 + 3 for each Faltering on the target, Absolute Zero 10 + 3 for each Faltering on each enemy before it adds 2 more. Pool unchanged: 87 cards, 83 reward-eligible (four basics), within the range of the five classes that were not rebuilt (81-87 reward-eligible; the other rebuilt four are at 51-52), so nothing was added; 85 of 87 within ±15%. No balance simulation was run.
423. **The Knight's pool expansion (Robert's Duelist brief and its correction, 2026-10-29: nine named cards, then the pool to 85-90 reward-eligible cards with 11-13 Auras, in five directions: the Duelist, the formation fighter, the patient defender, offensive Thorns, the siege fighter; docs/reworks/KNIGHT.md; the earlier cards frozen in reviews/balance-baselines/2026-10-29-knight-duelist-pass/) ->** 35 cards added (Testing Cut, Measured Footwork, Keep Your Distance, Patient Edge, Read the Duel, Closing Argument, Knight's Composure, Answer Them All, Shed the Spines, and 26 more), none removed: **89 cards, 87 reward-eligible** (52 before; the two basics excluded), 13 Auras. Generic engine pieces: a Riposte bonus on a damage effect aimed at **every enemy** (each enemy Open when the card is played spends its own Opening for its own bonus, once; the validator allows it), `shedTemp` (take up to N of the actor's **temporary** stacks of a status, newest first, the expiry entries with them, never lasting stacks) with the `thornsSpent` metric. Knight's Composure needs no engine change: her turn-end triggers already run before her Openings wear off. **Mode-neutral intent cards:** Giant's Stance (1 Faltering, +1 if the target is Open), Deflecting Blow (Riposte: 7 Barrier; 7), Sentinel's Patience (5 Barrier +2 per enemy) no longer read an intent, so no Knight card does, and every card reads the same in the campaign and the Arena. **Barbed Vigil** becomes the held two-turn guard (7 Barrier, 6 Guarded, 4 Thorns, Hold; upgrade 9 / 8 / 4), Ember Ward kept. Departures from the brief's starting numbers, to land on budget or avoid a twin: Read the Duel 5 Barrier (4), Keep Your Distance 8 (7), Closing Argument 16 (15), Answer Them All 10 to all +8 (9 +6), Knight's Composure 6 Barrier (4: a rare Aura at 4 was half its budget). Two would-be loops removed before they shipped: a 0-cost "draw 1 if the target is Open" (it replaced itself while the enemy stayed Open) became Probing Thrust's Exposed, and Bristle has no draw. Arena burst worked through legal actions: at most 113 of 540 (21%) on a five-card, 5-Mana turn. No growing finisher, no permanent damage growth (deferred). No balance simulation was run.
424. **The Assassin's pool expansion (Robert's expansion brief, docs/reworks/ASSASSIN_EXPANSION_PROMPT.md, 2026-10-29: 87 reward-eligible cards, 12 Auras, six strategies; docs/reworks/ASSASSIN.md; the earlier cards frozen in reviews/balance-baselines/2026-10-29-assassin-expansion/) ->** 36 cards added (51 reward-eligible before, verified with rewardPool), none changed or removed: **91 cards, 87 reward-eligible** (the four basics excluded), **12 Auras** (9 new: Economy of Motion, Patient Coil, The Waiting Blade, Lingering Steel, Steel Thicket, Cruel Precision, Clean Extraction, Lie in Wait, Recycled Steel), 14 new non-Aura utility or defence cards. Six strategies: precision execution (partial spenders, and Poised Strike / Patient Coil / The Waiting Blade paying for holding, so spending all 3 is not always right), leave the knife (bounded by 3 a target; nothing deals damage on its own), selective extraction (one knife, one job: damage, Exposed, cards, Barrier), fieldwork (spread then choose; nothing spreads on a death), shadow and timing (Hold, next-turn Mana and cards, a no-Attack turn paying Preparation that only an attack spends), hybrid (knives read into Preparation without a pull; Preparation buys pulls one way: no card turns Preparation into knives, so no Preparation-knife-Preparation loop). No new engine op: everything is the existing DSL (`spendPrep`, `pull`, `embed`, scales on `knives`/`prep`/`prepSpent`/`knivesAll`, `when`, triggers `spend`/`extract`/`debuffFoe`/`turnStart`/`turnEnd`); card text: "equal to the target's knives", "your Preparation", "if you spent Preparation", "if you played no Attack this turn". Every draw, embed or Mana trigger on the new Auras is once a turn or at a turn boundary; the free cards (Steady Hand, Quick Pull) draw and make nothing. Arena burst through legal actions against a 540-HP hero with 3 Preparation and 3 knives built up: at most 75 (14%) on a 3-Mana turn (Pry Loose+, Exacting Pull+, Measured Cut+); Final Cut+ alone 67. No balance simulation was run. **Review (2026-10-29):** The Waiting Blade makes no Mana (a card and 2 Barrier): Preparation persists, so a holding deck keeps 3 nearly every turn and three copies made 3 Mana a turn, a 2-cost Mana Aura each at 1.
425. **The Spellblade's pool expansion (Robert's expansion prompt, docs/reworks/SPELLBLADE_EXPANSION_PROMPT.md, 2026-10-29: 87 reward-eligible cards, 12 Auras, at least 8 new utility/defence cards, six strategies; docs/reworks/SPELLBLADE.md; the cards before it frozen in reviews/balance-baselines/2026-10-29-spellblade-expansion/) ->** 36 cards added, none changed: **89 cards, 87 reward-eligible**, 12 Auras (Edge Discipline, Living Glass, Spell-Etched Steel, Matched Set, Stored Light, Obsidian Resonance, Sentinel Glass new). Generic engine pieces: the discharge op takes a `kind` (spend only that kind, oldest first, no choice, the rest stays); the card's spent kinds are recorded (`Act.charges`, carried over a choice) for the metrics `dischargedPair` / `dischargedMix`; `echoCharges` makes them take effect once more (not a discharge: no trigger, nothing returns to the blade, so no restoring of spent charges); metrics `bladeEmbers`, `bladeGlass`, `bladePair`; a `recast` trigger. Two positions and flat charges still bound everything: the Arena burst worked through legal actions is at most 67 of 540 (12%). The text "If it did" now reads a discharge that happened (Glass Requiem's shards too). No balance simulation was run. **Review (2026-10-29):** Stored Light makes no Mana (a card and 2 Barrier), for the same reason in a Ward-holding deck; Edge Discipline answers once a turn; the preview stops at a second discharge (Double Release); "If you discharged any" replaces "If it did".
426. **The Monk's pool expansion (Robert's expansion prompt, docs/reworks/MONK_EXPANSION_PROMPT.md, 2026-10-29: 87 reward-eligible cards, 12 Auras, six strategies inside Martial Forms; docs/reworks/MONK.md; the cards before it frozen in reviews/balance-baselines/2026-10-29-monk-expansion/) ->** 36 cards added, none changed or removed: **89 cards, 87 reward-eligible** (51 before; the two basics excluded), **12 Auras** (6 before: Stored Strength, Ebb and Flow, Kindled Focus, Inner Reserve, Flowing Release, Unfinished Form), 17 new non-Aura utility or defence Skills. The trait was read from code (Martial Forms: 1 Mastery and 3 Barrier on the once-a-turn completion) and is unchanged, as are the caps (Mastery 3, at most 2 a card), the starting form and the step timing. Two generic metrics: `formProgress` (the panel's 0-2) and `vigor` (Primed on a unit). Primed goes on every hit of the next attack, so the Root route to damage keeps it small (Coiled Stance 3, Stored Strength 4 and only when not Primed); Patient Thunder deals its damage as a skill instead. Hand-priced: all 89 within ±15%. New rates: "+N per Mastery you have" at ~1.5 Mastery (kept, not spent); a missed completion ~0.4 of turns. Arena burst through legal actions: at most 81 of 540 (15%) on 3 Mana and 3 Mastery. No balance simulation was run. **Review (2026-10-29):** Coiled Stance gives Primed only if he is not Primed: Primed adds to every hit, and three copies made Thousand Palms deal 84.
427. **The Knight against big hits, the Shaman's Forks and starter, and choosing totems on the panel (Robert, 2026-10-29: "the knight feels weak against enemies that have powerful attacks that she cant fully block"; "theirs only the one card that has riposte on it and its 2 cost"; "i would rather just spend the command card and highlight the modular ui box for the totems... dont want to slow down the game with another popup prompt"; "gathering storm should not be a basic card... give the shaman somethign else to start with instead, something like Forked Lightning that hits 2 targets and does 25% more to a single target... it shouldnt just be on one card"; Robert chose the half-block rule) ->** **Guard** gives an Opening when her Barrier stops **at least half** of an enemy attack hit (exactly half counts; the rest still gets through), not only a whole one: a core-rule change Robert picked over a flat threshold or new cards only. Her starter swaps a Strike for **Counter Cut** (basic, 1 Mana: 6, Riposte +4). Riposte and "if Open" rate ~0.65 now; her cards keep their numbers as a deliberate buff. **Fork N** for the Shaman: the existing spread (`distinct` random hits) with the existing `lonePct` (25), whose lone check now reads the enemies standing when the spread begins (before, each spread hit found one enemy and took the bonus every time; no card combined the two until now); text "(25% more when only one enemy stands)". He starts with **2 Forked Lightning** (Fork 2 of 5) instead of 2 Gathering Storm, which becomes a **common** reward (4 to all, +3 a copy each cast); new Spark Fork (common 0, Fork 2 of 4) and Thunder Fork (uncommon 2, Fork 3 of 10): 86 reward-eligible Shaman cards (was 83). **Totem choices on the panel:** a command of your choice and planting into a full row are answered on the totem panel (it unfolds and glows, the choosable totems pulse with number keys, the rest dim), not a pop-up; the engine choice is unchanged (`pick` with a position), so replays and the server are unaffected. Snapshots: reviews/balance-baselines/2026-10-29-knight-half-block/ and 2026-10-29-shaman-forks/. No balance simulation was run.
428. **The Knight, Assassin, Spellblade and Monk reworks reverted (Robert, 2026-10-29: "the knight, assassin, monk, and spellblade all need to be reverted. they all suck, they cant fight bosseses at all, and they lost the plot of what i want them to be. ill be more specific next time"; "the one taht came out the best is the shaman, we can work with that") ->** the four classes are back to their pools, traits, starters, build paths and texts of commit 82867b0 (before #415-#419; archived in reviews/balance-baselines/2026-10-29-pre-identity-reworks/), with their old tests; the Shaman keeps his rework (#420-#422, #427). Their rework documents, ledgers and hand prices moved to reviews/balance-baselines/2026-10-29-reverted-reworks/ (the full card data as it last stood is commit b80d803). **Saves:** `CLASS_REWORKS` goes to version 2 for the four ("back to her/his earlier cards"): a run saved under a rework has every rework-only card mapped to the nearest earlier card of the same class (same kind, then rarity, then cost; 240 ids in `RETIRED_CARDS`), a fight under way starts again, and the player is told; a run saved before the reworks keeps its cards, which are live again (the rework's old-to-new mappings were removed). `canonicalCard` now drops a roll or engraving the replacement cannot carry instead of losing the card (and the whole save). Vault and Arena decks go through the same mapping. The generic engine pieces of the reworks (Openings/Riposte, knives/Preparation, enchantments, forms, Fork) stay in the engine; no live card of the four uses them. #427's Knight half (Guard on half a hit, Counter Cut) went with the revert. No balance simulation was run.
429. **The Shaman's totem pictures and spirits, and Clutch Genes by the share of HP missing (Robert, 2026-10-29: "update the totem display to use the images in assets-src/weapons/totem.png and maybe display the spirit form when they are commanded"; "the totem images should expand with the modular window expansion/collapse and the spirit count disply needs to be improved"; "is the drunkards clutch genes calculating correctly? i want it to be an increase in % damage for every 1% of hp missing. i have a game where i have 88 max hp, and i have 19 hp right now, but the bonus only says 69") ->** `tools/pack-totems.ps1` cuts the two sheets (Hawk, Bear, Elder Tree; carved and spirit) into `apps/web/public/art/totems` with an index; the totem panel shows the carved totems (filling the panel, so they grow when it is resized; a kind with no picture yet, the Thunderbird and the Moon Owl, keeps its mark, scaled with its slot), and a `command` event makes that totem's spirit rise over it for about a second (the playback waits 0.34 s; nothing in the rules waits on it). Spirit is a glowing number with what it adds ("Commands +N · Help +N/2"). **Clutch Genes** was working as #411 wrote it (1% per HP missing: 69 missing = +69%); it is now 1% for every 1% of max HP missing (`HeroTrait.clutch.perMissingPct`, rounded down: 19 of 88 HP = 78% missing = +78%), which is also the same in the Arena, where HP is six times as big and per-HP would have reached +500%. At his 88 HP this is about 14% more than before at the same HP lost. No balance simulation was run.
430. **The Shaman's totem cycle (Robert, 2026-10-29: "lets remove trinity altogether... have some good command cards that command the spirit of a totem to take an action... lets go back to the cycle system with an order just like slay the spire 2"; "totems that get commanded are pushed out and 'consumed'"; Hawk "2 primed passively which scales with spirit, and when it is commanded it provides spirit"; "i only want to provide 1 spirit at a time and that part doesnt scale with other spirit"; Thunderbird "3 damage passively that targets 2 random enemies and scales with spirit and when its consumed it does an increased number to all enemies"; "elder tree mana is ok but dont scale with spirit, and commanding it provides 2 mana immediately"; "i like the idea for moon owl") ->** a third Shaman (docs/reworks/SHAMAN.md; the cards of #420-#427 frozen in reviews/balance-baselines/2026-10-29-shaman-cycle/). **Engine (generic):** the totem row is ordered and packed (front = oldest); `plant` goes to the back and, into a full row, pushes the front one out, which acts (its command ops) and leaves; `totemCommand` acts `times` times on 'front', 'newest', a kind or 'all' (front to back) and consumes the totem; `totemRotate` moves the front one to the back; `TotemDef.passive` runs ops at the start or the end of his turn; statuses `spellcut` (the next elemental spell this turn costs N less, spent by it) and `twinspell` (the next elemental spell is played twice); the totem choice prompts are no longer made. **Totems:** Hawk (end of turn 2+S Primed, only if he is not Primed, so it never piles up onto a multi-hit; act +1 Spirit flat), Bear (end of turn 2+S Barrier; act 6+S Barrier), Thunderbird (end of turn a Fork 2 of 3+S; act 8+S to all), Elder Tree (start of turn +1 Mana; act 2 Mana), Moon Owl (start of turn the first elemental spell costs 1 less; act the next elemental spell twice); S = Spirit, through `per('spirit')` in the totem's own ops. Trinity and the totems' help are gone (the trait's `trinity` flag and `boost` stay in the engine, unused). **Cards:** 91 (87 reward-eligible, 12 Auras); Flame is burst (Flare, Pyroclasm, Talon Strike; Thorns gone), Frost Barrier and Faltering, Storm all-enemy or Forks (Eye of the Tempest a Fork of 4, Superstorm 2 hits, Squall and Static Crackle gone: Primed adds to every hit), plants for every totem (three free ones that Consume), commands (front, newest, a kind, twice, all), Turn the Wheel and Wind Whisper (free cycling that draws one and discards one), stacking Auras on a totem's act (Totem Drum, Harmony, Storm Sovereign) and Spirit Chorus (the front totem acts every turn). Numbers moved from the first draft to land on the costing guide: Talon Strike, Thunderbird Call and Spirit Strike cost 2, Call the Elder Tree 3, Ancestral Exchange 2. Saves: `CLASS_REWORKS.shaman` version 3; 14 removed ids mapped. No balance simulation was run.
431. **The Necromancer begins a fight with one Thrall, not two (Robert, 2026-10-29: "necromancer shouldnt start with two thralls to begin a battle, it should just be one") ->** his hero no longer has a starting Bone Thrall of its own (`startUnits`, #411); Grave Chill's Raise 2 at the start of his first turn is the one he begins with (2 HP). Before, the two stacked (the starting Thrall of #370 and the turn-1 Raise of #397). In the Arena, seat b gets his on his first turn, as every other turn. (#430 is the Shaman's cycle rework.)
432. **Damage numbers say how they are made (Robert, 2026-10-29: "we need a better way to show the damage calcs on drunkards cards like the +25% on old twelve gauge, some of the % text arent that easy to read either, like people need to know the exact number its going to be. and my friend said he cant tell if the 25% on twelve gauge is working") ->** the live number on a card already counted Point Blank (and Clutch Genes, Might, Exposed...), but the text still offered "25% more when only one enemy is left" on top, so nobody could tell. Now: when a lone-enemy bonus is in the number, the clause reads "(Point Blank +25% counted.)" (a Fork: "(+25% counted: one enemy)"); when it is not, the clause stays as the condition. Hovering a card in hand shows a **Damage** line, the exact number step by step as the engine works it out, each step with the number it leaves ("5 → Point Blank +25% = 6 → +2 Might = 8 → Clutch Genes +78% = 14 → Exposed +50% = 21"), whenever it is one number against one enemy (a target aimed at, or one enemy standing); a range across several enemies stays a range. The steps come from the engine's own damage function (`modifiedDamage` records them), so they can never disagree with the hit. The Clutch Genes chip's tooltip reads "+78%" and says the cards count it.
433. **Premeditate in place of Sly (Robert, 2026-10-29: "lets update the assassin's sly cards to be Premeditate instead and change how they work. Instead with premeditate, when the card is discarded, it comes back next turn with a cost of 0. this is in adddition to drawing your regular hand. the card keeps its 0 cost until it gets used. any cards that apply Sly should now apply Premeditate") ->** a card with Premeditate that an effect discards (as Sly, not the end-of-turn sweep: Robert's "discarded" read the way Sly read it; easy to widen if he means every discard) is no longer played at once: it is kept aside (`SideState.premeditating`) and comes back to the hand at the start of the next turn, after the hand is drawn (on top of it), and costs 0 until it is played (`CardInst.premeditated`; a full hand sends it to the discard pile, still at 0; playing it clears the 0). Every card with Sly has Premeditate (the DSL flag keeps its name `sly`, so card data, Palm Switch's "gains Premeditate this turn" and Grand Scheme's "for the rest of the fight" carry over); the word on cards, tooltips and the Assassin's how-to says Premeditate. Costing: the card again next turn at 0 (guide §6). No balance simulation was run.
434. **The Drunkard's lose-HP payoffs pay by how much (Robert, 2026-10-29: "drunkard has some cards that do stuff when he takes damage from his own cards. is hangover supposed to trigger that? if not, then he just doesnt have a lot of cards that would trigger that condidtion and we need to evaludate that"; "yea go ahead and fix the drunkard") ->** Hangover always counted (its morning-after HP fires the 'loseHp' trigger), but nothing said so, it fired once a turn however much Hangover was stacked, and only 3 payoffs read it. Now: the trigger reads "Whenever you lose HP to your own cards or Hangover" and carries the HP lost (`hpLostNow`); **Glass Jaw** deals damage equal to the HP lost to every enemy (at most 6; upgrade 8, 3 Barrier), **The Glass Palace** the same (at most 5; 7), **Mean Drunk** 1 Might for the turn per 2 HP lost (at most 3); a generic cap on a scaled amount (`Scale.max`, "at most N" on the card). "HP lost this turn" now starts before the turn's own losses (Hangover, Seared), so they count (it reset just after them before). New: **Bar Fight** (common 1: 7, +3 per time you lost HP this turn, at most +9), **Nurse the Hangover** (common 1: 5 Barrier +1 per Hangover, at most +8), **Take It on the Chin** (uncommon 1: lose 3 HP, 10 Barrier, draw 1). Hand prices: in a Hangover deck the tick is ~3-4 HP once a turn plus ~0.5 self-loss cards, so Glass Jaw ~8.5 a turn x4 = 34 (rare 2-cost Aura 38), Bar Fight ~10 (common 10), Nurse ~9.6, Take It on the Chin ~11 before its payoffs (uncommon 11.2). No balance simulation was run.
435. **The open rift shows its cards (Robert, 2026-10-29: "the open rift event needs to show the cards in the option boxes. its too confusing with the way it is now") ->** an option whose card the event already named (the open rift's three Rift-crossed cards) now shows that card in its box, as random gifts, copies and swaps already did (DECISIONS #273): `pinRemovals` counts a named `card` step as a card the option gives. Every event that offers a named card shows it the same way.
436. **The starting weapons are cards of the deck, and can be removed (Robert, 2026-10-29: "we should allow players to remove tokens like the old twelve gauge and others like it. for players that dont want it they shoudl be able o get rid of it"; he chose "a real starter card" over a per-run opt-out) ->** the Old Twelve Gauge (given by the Drunkard's sigil at the start of every fight) and the Obsidian Blade (made by Kindled's Sharpen on her first turn) are cards of their starting decks (11 cards each) with **Opening hand**, so they are still in the first hand, and can be removed at a shrine or a merchant like any card. **Sharpen / Precision no longer make a weapon when there is none**: its amount waits in the fight's total for a copy that comes later (Double Tap still makes one, as sharp as the total, #399); a card that grows only itself (Gathering Storm) is still made as before. Saves: a run (`RunState.weaponCards`) or a Vault deck (`VaultDeck.weaponCards`) saved before gets its weapon once when it is read, if it has none (`withStartingWeapons`); one saved after keeps what the player chose. The weapons stay out of reward offers (tokens).
437. **The multiverse enemy expansion: 62 new enemies from the enemies art folder, 31 -> 93 (Robert, 2026-10-29: "can you use the art in the enemies folder to create a lot more enemies. hoping to double or triple the amount of enemies") ->** the design is `docs/MULTIVERSE_ENEMY_EXPANSION.md` (roster `assets-src/enemies/multiverse-expansion-v1/enemy-roster.json`): HP and every number are its Tier I stage-1 targets (the tier table scales them as for every enemy), each cycle is its left-to-right loop, a half-HP clause is a second phase, and **everything uses existing ops (no engine change)**: Barrier and healing on the enemy itself unless it says otherwise, Fate on the hero, temporary Thorns, junk cards (Wound/Static) into the discard pile; the Hunger Orchard's summon fails quietly at the four-enemy cap. **48 encounters** (`packages/content/src/v2/index.ts`, after Vorthax): per realm three pairs (two for the Far Rifts), a trio, two Elites and a Warden. Gaps filled the simplest way: (a) **Gloomfen, the Hollow Crown (realm `starfall`) and the Far Rifts (new realm id `beyond`) are not stages**: their fights and Elites join the Rift's pool (the last stage), and their Wardens may end the second stage (`FAR_REALMS`, `wardensFor`); (b) **a realm's new boss is a second Warden for it**, picked at random with its first (the Warden's Gift does not depend on which); (c) **trios only from the second stage** (`Encounter.fromStage`), so a first stage stays pairs and singles. Seeded maps change (the Warden is now a pick). Figures are cut from the seven sheets by `tools/pack-enemies.ps1` (keyed on the navy ground, enclosed ground pockets cleared, every piece given to the cell holding its centre); the procedural stand-ins and attack looks are in `apps/web/src/art/multiverseUnits.ts`. Balance is a first hand check only (stage-1 pairs open for 15-21, as the Squire pair's 18); open: whether the far realms should get stages of their own, and pricing the bosses against the old Wardens. **Review (2026-10-29):** a 1-stack Exposed from an enemy wore off at the end of its own turn, before its next attack, so five moves did nothing (Marble Mantis, Prism Magistrate, Welding Cherub, Mirror Moth, Shard Peacock): they apply 2 Exposed, before the hit of the same move, as the older enemies do (tested: a hit lands on the Exposed hero). Fate goes on the hero past any companion in front (a new generic target, `foeHero`), as the design says. Warden odds: stage 1 picks one of its realm's two Wardens at even odds; stage 2 one of five (its realm's two and the three far realms', a fifth each). Open for Robert: the new pairs' HP (91-119) is above the old stage-1 fights (57-96), the trios are untuned (54-61 over three turns), the new Wardens are front-loaded (their second phase is no harder), and none of the 62 hits every unit, which favours companion classes.
438. **The totems in a fixed block under the Boons (Robert, 2026-10-29: "lets do away with the modular window for totems, we should just put them in order on the left of the character with indicator for which one is next to be commanded"; then "actually maybe just place the totems below the character below the boons") ->** the Shaman's totems are no longer a floating, foldable, resizable panel: they are a fixed block in his side's column, right under his Boons, in order with the front totem (the next a command makes act) first and marked Next, his Spirit under them. Tempest Call and every growth effect say what they do in full ("Every Gathering Storm you have deals 2 more damage for the rest of the fight (with none, one is added to your hand)"; a weapon's Sharpen: "every Old Twelve Gauge deals N more").
439. **The totems in an arc beside the Shaman, with their numbers (Robert, 2026-10-29: "we also need the totem UI to show the number of dmg, shield, etc. and again get rid of the modular window thing, lets just have positions that show the order of where they are and wich is next to go. please display in a radial patern next to the player charcter to the left") ->** the totems stand on the stage in an arc to the left of the Shaman (to the right of an opponent Shaman in the Arena), one position each, sized from his figure: each position shows its order (1, 2, 3...) and the front one, the next to act, is lit and marked NEXT; every totem shows what it does now with his Spirit counted, read from its own data: each turn (↻ "2 Primed", "🛡2", "⚔3 ×2") and when it acts (✦ "+1 Spirit", "🛡6", "⚔8 all"); its full text on hover; a commanded totem's spirit rises from its position; his Spirit sits under the arc. Replaces the block of #438 (and the floating panel before it).
441. **Enemies stand back, damage numbers read, and a new weapon copy starts fresh (Robert, 2026-10-29: "enemies are still too close to the center of the screen, they should be backed up closer to the edge on their side of the screen... the number for damage when it shows needs to be touched up, the font is bad and it should have a black outline like other things do. we also should revert the multiple twelve gauges all stack the same thing, they should only stack if it was in the deck when it started stacking, same with obsidian blade"; he also called off the combat-motion animation pack: "lets just forget this animation stuff", so #440 is unused) ->** the enemy line slides out until its far edge stands at x 1810 (a lone enemy where an Arena opponent stands), order kept; the Arena layout is unchanged. Damage, Barrier and status numbers use the cards' numeric face (Bebas Neue) with a thick black outline. **Sharpen goes only on the copies there when it is played**: a copy made later (Double Tap, Spare Barrel) starts unsharpened (reverts the carry-over of #399); with no weapon, a Sharpen does nothing (#436). The hero's Sharpened buff shows the sharpest copy, and the Obsidian Blade's buff, lost in the revert of #428, is back.
442. **The map's Arena button tells the truth (Robert, 2026-10-29: "make sure the arena status button in the campaign map is representative of if that deck is actually valid for arena") ->** the button runs the Arena's own check (`checkArenaEntry`: deck size, deck rules, copy, Legendary and Ancestral limits) on the run's deck as it stands. Green only when the deck is saved **and** passes; red and struck through when it would not pass, with the reason in its tip ("A deck holds ... cards"), as well as before the first save.
443. **Phones and tablets, and fights held upright (Robert, 2026-10-29: "do another pass on mobile and tablet ... make things smaller so it all fits ... things can be hidden by expandable menus to view things like boons and auras. character models should be smaller on mobile and tablet ... we should be able to make it work with the phone vertical mode, they shouldnt have to do landscape everytime to battle ... re-assess how cards are distributed and hovered on") ->** the fight screen picks one of three layouts from the window's size alone (`apps/web/src/fight/stageLayout.ts`; never from the kind of pointer, so a small desktop window looks like a phone): **wide** (as before), **compact** (shorter than 600 px, or a tablet held sideways under 1200 x 900: figures drawn at about 80%, the sky cropped so the board sits higher, smaller hand cards, plates and intents drawn at 80%, the hero stands further in from the edge, the battle log goes to the top right; on a phone on its side Mana and End turn come down beside the piles) and **portrait** (taller than wide: the "turn your phone" screen is gone; the enemies stand on a row behind and your side on a row in front, the camera frames the middle of the stage (x 550-1370) hung from under the top bar, figures at 84-90%, plates and intents at 64%; under the board a strip with the potion belt (a row) and below it a row of controls (Mana, draw pile, End turn, discard pile) on the hand, so the hand fan uses the whole width; the top bar's buttons fold into a Menu). In both small layouts Boons, Auras, Omens, the enemy's Auras and the class panels fold into one **drawer** opened by a button showing their counts (a press elsewhere closes it, as it does the menu). **Cards held upright:** a touch on a card shows it big above the hand with its notes (as it reads now: Might, Sharpen...); a second touch plays it, or for an aimed card starts aiming (the targets glow, no arrow follows a finger, the bar says "Tap a glowing target"), then a touch on a target plays it; dragging up still plays as before. The page's rem size now shrinks on any short or narrow window, not only under a finger. Elsewhere: the run map runs top to bottom on a screen taller than wide; reward, shop and pick cards are never wider than half a phone screen (two a row); the book of cards has slimmer edges on a phone (two cards across a page, as small as 110 px); the setup pages and the character details keep less padding on a phone.
444. **The hand follows the mouse, and the arrow comes sooner (Robert, 2026-10-29: "the arrow to target enemies doesnt show early enough when trying to target them... sometimes the wrong one is hovered and i have move my mouse around to get back to the one i want") ->** a mouse hovers the card whose resting centre is nearest along the fan (with a little slack at the midpoint so it does not flicker), not whichever tilted, overlapping stand-in the pointer is in; the lifted card's raised top still counts as on it, but no longer reaches over its neighbours (its hold area narrowed). Pressing picks up the lifted card. An aimed card turns into the arrow as it leaves the top of the resting hand (it waited until nearly two card heights above it), the same line a card let go above plays at. Touch is unchanged (#443 is the mobile pass).
445. **A big Thrall looks big (Robert, 2026-10-29: "there are some minions that need new character models like the grave colossus") ->** every Thrall is one unit (bone-thrall) whose HP the raising card sets, so the Grave Colossus card's 20-HP Thrall stood as the small skeleton. A Thrall with 18+ max HP now stands as the Grave Colossus figure (taller again at 30+), and one that grows mid-fight changes figure on the spot (a figure whose picture changes, such as art that finishes loading, swaps its texture). Presentation only. Every other summoned unit already has its own figure. Also: the Sharpen texts (the keyword, the weapon chip and the card line) now say only the copies you have take it, after #441.
446. **The Card library's book is always the full width (Robert, 2026-10-29: "the grimoire is still resizing to be small. lets fix this to always be full width of the screen. clearly we are strugglign with this"; it happened on the Card library when changing the character filter) ->** the cause: the page (.setup--wide) centres its children, so the book was as wide as its contents, and the cards are sized from the book: a filter with fewer cards (one Riftwalker's) gave a narrower book and smaller cards. The library's book now stretches to the page's full width, and its cards are sized for every card in the library (in the base/upgraded/both view chosen; Grimoire's new fitCount), so no filter changes the book or the card size.
447. **The Shaman's element as a coloured icon (Robert, 2026-10-29: "lets add the element type icon to the card somewhere, maybe lets just do top right for now, like where the mana is but it will be the element. and it should be colored" / "we dont need the text of the element on the card if we have the icon with a hover tooltip") ->** a Flame, Frost or Storm card carries a round badge in its top right, opposite the Mana orb, in the orb's size and trim: a flame on orange, a snowflake on ice blue, a yellow bolt on violet; its tip names the element and what that element does. The name banner makes room for it. The element word ("Storm.") is left off the card face (only as its own sentence; "Gathering Storm" stays), but kept in the card's text everywhere else (search, logs). Prism has no badge and keeps its line.
448. **Hovers explain totems and the cards a card makes (Robert, 2026-10-29: "there are some cards that need better tool tips like the shaman totems, the hover tooltips need to explain what they are. and any cards that add cards to your hand like daggers, the cards dont explain what the cards are (or gravesouls, ect.)") ->** a card's hover lists every card it makes (anywhere on it: an Aura's trigger, a choice) by what that card is: "Gravesoul: A 0-cost Skill: Draw 2 cards. Consume.", from the made card itself; a card that only names a made card ("Whenever you play a Dagger") gets the same. A card that plants a totem, or names one ("If you have a Thunderbird"), explains that totem in its own words. The Totem keyword was still the pre-#430 text (help on spells, choosing what to replace): it now describes the row, the command and Spirit. A totem's tip on the board adds what totems are under its own words.
449. **No co-op, and the Defect pass for the Shaman (Robert, 2026-10-30: "we wont support coop so we dont need any of that"; "I want these other cards in here with the engine built to support them, then I will modify them") ->** the game has no co-op, and no card will be built for it: cards that act on "another player" or "ALL players" (Ignition, Energy Surge, Imitation Learning, Hibernate and the like) are not copied. The Shaman gained 44 uncommons and rares from the Slay the Spire 2 Defect (original names, effects mapped onto totems, not priced): Block is Barrier, Energy is Mana, Focus is Spirit, an orb slot is a totem position, Lightning, Frost and Dark orbs are Thunderbird, Bear and Owl, Glass is a Hawk. Their upgrades follow that game's (Aura cost cuts only where it has them: Subroutine, Creative AI, Feral, Trash to Treasure). Engine added: X-cost planting and commanding (the plant and totemCommand ops with a scale), the Owl as a Dark orb (status umbra, 6 each turn, a command spends it on the weakest enemy), Buffer, totemPulse (Loop), a negative totemSlot (Bulk Up). The Hawk now hits a random enemy (3, commanded 7); Spirit Call commands twice; Void is a 1-cost draw-1 consumed status card. Retired: Bear Hide, Hawk Feather, Storm Feather, Call Again, Spark Fork, Turn the Wheel, Rime Shield, Bear's Guard, Thunderbird's Cry, Last Called, Ancestral Exchange, Great Spirit's Call, Chain Lightning, Stone Sling, Searing Lash, Kindle, Flare, and Ember Bolt (to Raise the Hawk).
450. **The Shaman's near-duplicates retired; his build-path check is gone (Robert, 2026-10-30: "retire talon strike...", "retire immolate...", "retire the build check stuff yes") ->** the Shaman went from 111 cards to 92 (88 reward cards: 23 commons, 34 uncommons, 31 rares). Retired, each to a live card for old saves: Talon Strike, Cold Breath, Three Winds, Firebrand, Winter's Embrace, Maelstrom, Eye of the Tempest, Grow the Pole, Spiritstorm, Elder Recall, Totem Mantle, Sorrow's Hammer, Glacial Lance, then Immolate, Storm Front, Totem Drum, Dream Weaving, Raise the Pole and Awakened Spirit. Why: each was a bigger or smaller copy of a card he keeps (Flame Spear, Gale Call, Storm Sovereign, Wild Vision, Carve the Spirit Pole). The element-heading build paths are obsolete for the Shaman: the path-size and on-path-share tests skip him, and his builds are judged by their payoffs (Burn finisher, zero-cost Gathering Storm, Status payoffs, totems and Spirit, Frost control). The review is in docs/SHAMAN_RARE_CONSOLIDATION_PROPOSAL.md; Spirit stacking still has no engine of its own (Spirit Lance is the only card that reads it).
451. **Defer, a second way for the Assassin to set up a turn (Robert, 2026-10-30: "we can just add text to some cards that defers a card for the next turn and it comes back with 1 less cost"; "not sure about price thats on you") ->** a card of your hand that costs 1 or more (never a 0-cost or X card, which are not offered; with nothing to take it does nothing) leaves now and comes back at the start of the next turn, on top of the hand drawn, costing 1 less until it is played (with a full hand it is never discarded: it goes on top of the draw pile instead, still cheaper, and comes with the next draw; it stacks with other cost cuts and never goes below 0). It is set aside, not discarded: Graveside Cut, Fleet Fingers and the like do not hear of it. Engine: the defer op and pending choice, `deferring` on the side, `deferred` on the card; the text is "Defer a card." / "Defer up to 3 cards." Premeditate stays as it was (a discarded Premeditate card comes back at 0). Priced by hand at 3.5 a card (docs/14_CARD_COSTING.md): **Stalker's Lunge** 15 + draw 2 next turn (22, over its 19) became 15 + Defer (18.5), upgraded 20 + Defer; **Side Slip** +1 Mana next turn (4) became Defer a card (3.5); **Palm Switch** 7 Barrier + a random Premeditate became 7 Barrier + Defer (11.9 against 11.2); **Cartwheel Cut** 7 to ALL (9.1) became 5 to ALL + Defer (10.0, upgraded 7 + Defer 12.6); **Glancing Knives** and **Slip the Blow** gained Defer on top (15.5 and 10.7, both under their budgets because they are discard-engine cards); **Quick Reflexes** (3 cost, Premeditate) draws 1 and defers 1 (6.5; it is only worth casting through a discard outlet, so make it 1 cost if it should be played from the hand); **Into the Shadows** now defers up to 3 cards, discards the rest of the hand and gives Poised, and costs 2 instead of 1 (Poised ~12 + Defer ~10.5 = 22.5 against 23.75; upgraded to up to 4 instead of costing 0). Cool Head and Grand Scheme are unchanged. Not built: Defer for the other classes.
452. **The Knight keeps his Mana: Knight's Vow and Mana Reserves (Robert, 2026-10-30: "lets make the mana banking his new trait and call it Knight's Vow"; "lets call the new mana bank Mana Reserves. i dont want the opening barrier anymore") ->** the Knight needed something that is not the Slay the Spire Ironclad and that gives a high-output turn to meet classes like the Drunkard. His trait is now Knight's Vow: at the end of his turn up to 3 unspent Mana is kept and added to the next turn's Mana (so up to 6 on a turn; a turn of saving can follow a turn of saving, but only 3 is kept each time). The kept Mana shows as the hero status Mana Reserves (`reserves`) for the turn it arrives, so cards can read it (`per('reserves')`); it is gone when that turn ends. Oathkeeper (6 Barrier and 6 Plating at the start of a fight) is gone; Plating stays on Stoneskin. Engine: `HeroTrait.reserves` (the cap), `reservesNext` on the side, the `reserves` status and its metric. Mana kept also pays off Mana owed from a borrowed turn first (a debt of 2 and 3 kept leaves 1 over). The Mana gem's tooltip says so. *Reworked the same day in #454: the kept Mana is a separate pool, Reserves, not extra Mana, and the status described here is gone.* Cards that use it were proposed here and built in #454. Check before they are built: Brace for Battle, Draw Blood, Crimson Offering, Ritual of Ash and Inner Furnace now stack with the Reserves toward 10+ Mana turns.
453. **The card-grouping system is gone (Robert, 2026-10-30: "completely scrap" it and never refer to it again) ->** removed from the game and the tools: the per-class named groups (`Riftwalker.buildPaths`), the generated card-to-group table and its `pnpm paths` script, the group filter in the card library, the group names on pages and cards, the "fits your deck" hints on offered cards, the merchant's and the trades' leaning towards a group, and the Retrain trade ("the-specialist", 94 events became 93). Nothing there changed what a player sees today (it was already switched off, #365), so rewards, merchants and fights play the same. The balance audit still drafts three decks per hero, now by a seed number (`--byvariant`), and takes the best cards of the hero's class; the pool audit and the card list no longer group cards. The Shaman's "path" tests went with it. Older entries in this log, the changelog and the patch notes keep their words as history. Pool files keep their `// --- heading ---` comments as plain section headings.
454. **Reserves are their own pool, and the Knight's Vow cards (Robert, 2026-10-30: "lets just call it Max Reserves and assuming we display a new mana-like bubble like we do with Arcana ... have cards consume standard mana first before utilizing Mana Reserves"; "i do want the Reserves cards built, including ones that increase max reserves, and i do still want the barrier and self damage ideas") ->** Knight's Vow keeps unspent Mana as **Reserves** (`SideState.reserves`), up to the **Max Reserves** (`maxReserves`, 3 from the trait `HeroTrait.maxReserves`, plus this turn's bonus `reservesTurn`). At the end of the turn, before anything that answers it (so a Patient Wall counts what was just kept), the leftover Mana moves into Reserves. They stay until spent. A card is paid with the turn's Mana first and Reserves after it; an X card spends all the Mana and then all the Reserves. A new second-resource bubble beside the Mana gem shows `Reserves` (the same place and look as Arcana, only for a class with a Max Reserves). Ops: `reserves` (gain, up to the cap) and `maxReserves` (for this turn or for the fight); the metric `reserves` reads the pool (`per('reserves')`, `when('reserves', ...)`). Eleven original Knight cards (the Knight's pool is the Ironclad's 82 plus these): **Stand Fast** (1: 7 Barrier, Max Reserves +2 this turn; later 5 Barrier + 1 Plating, #456), **Measured Strike** (1: 7, +5 with 2+ Reserves), **Blood Oath** (0: lose 3 HP, gain 3 Reserves), **Vow Kept** (1: 3 + 5 per Reserve), **Unyielding Line** (1 Aura: at the end of your turn with 10+ Barrier, gain 2 Reserves), **Cast the Shield** (1: spend all Barrier, 2 damage each), **Hardened Scars** (1 Aura: lose HP to your own cards, gain 3 Barrier), **Patient Wall** (2 Aura: 4 Barrier per Reserve at the end of the turn), **Oath Unbroken** (2 Aura: Max Reserves +2 for the fight, 3 Might at the end of a turn with 4+ Reserves), **Vow Fulfilled** (3: 18 + 7 per Reserve), **Gilded Bulwark** (2 Aura: with 10+ Barrier, each Attack also hits a random enemy for 6). Priced by hand (docs/14_CARD_COSTING.md, the Reserves row): all within about 15% of budget except Cast the Shield (about 8 net of the Barrier it spends, against 11) and the Vow Fulfilled at typical Reserves (28.5 against 35; 39 full). Four names were changed because they were taken (Blood Tithe, Hold the Line, Scar Tissue, Breaking Point, Gilded Guard). Not built: **Fortify** (Barrier that lasts some turns and decays; Plating already does that) and other self-damage ideas, waiting on Robert.
455. **The Knight's trait is called Mana Reserves (Robert, 2026-10-30: "lets also rename knights vow to Mana Reserves, its more obvious") ->** the trait the log calls Knight's Vow in #452 and #454 is named Mana Reserves for players (`Riftwalker.trait.name`); the pool it makes is still called Reserves, and the cap Max Reserves. Nothing else changed.
456. **Plating stays; some Knight Barrier cards split with it, and Scarred Hide (Robert, 2026-10-30: "lets keep plating as is but adjust some of his cards to split between barrier and plating"; "for Scarred Hide lets make it sap the enemy 3 for each time the player took damage") ->** Plating is unchanged (it keeps your Barrier through the turn up to its number and shrinks by 1 a turn) and is not renamed. Six Barrier cards now give some of it as Plating (one more of it each since #457), 2 Barrier for 1 Plating (the costing guide's 2.4 against 2.5, so their points hardly move): Oath of Iron 6 Barrier + 1 Plating, Dawn Smite 5 damage, 3 Barrier + 1 Plating, Provoke 5 Barrier + 1 Plating, Ember Ward 8 Barrier + 2 Plating, Blood Plate 12 Barrier + 2 Plating, Stand Fast 5 Barrier + 1 Plating. New card **Scarred Hide** (1, uncommon Aura): whenever you lose HP, from an enemy or from your own cards, apply 3 Sapped to a random enemy (4 upgraded); a new trigger `hurt` fires on any HP your hero loses. It makes every later hit of that turn weaker when an enemy hurts you, and the enemy turn after your own self-damage. Priced about 18 against about 22 for a 1-cost uncommon Aura. The Knight has 12 Mana Reserves cards. Not built: Fortify (Plating covers it).
457. **A second Knight basic, and one more Plating everywhere (Robert, 2026-10-30: "the knight needs some more early power ... it really needs at least one defense/offense hybrid"; "the cards with plating probably need 1 additional plating across the board") ->** new basic **Brace and Press** (1 Mana: 3 Barrier, 2 Plating, 1 Exposed; upgraded 5 Barrier, 3 Plating, 1 Exposed) replaces one Defend in the starting deck (five Strikes, three Defends, Sundering Blow, Brace and Press: still ten cards). Every Knight card that gives Plating gives 1 more: Oath of Iron 2, Dawn Smite 2, Provoke 2, Ember Ward 3, Blood Plate 3, Stand Fast 2, Stoneskin 5 (7 upgraded). Priced by the costing guide (Plating 2.5 a stack): Brace and Press 11.6 against a basic's 10 (it is meant to be strong early); Oath of Iron 15.2 against 10, Dawn Smite 13.6, Provoke 14 against 11.2, Stand Fast about 12.5, Blood Plate 18.9 against 19. Oath of Iron (about 12.6 before, now about 50% over budget) and Provoke (14 against 11.2) are the furthest over; the others were already above budget (the Ironclad list is a yardstick, not a budget). Watch them: if Plating dominates, take 1 Barrier off Oath of Iron first. The new basic has borrowed art for now (docs/card-art/placeholder-art-2026-10-30.md).
458. **Starting decks: ten cards, three or four class basics, no doubles (Robert, 2026-10-30: "some characters have more than 4 basic cards, i dont think we should have that"; "every character should start with 10 cards") ->** the Knight, Assassin, Shaman, Monk and Drunkard decks change. **Knight:** 4 Strikes, 2 Defends, Sundering Blow, Brace and Press and two new basics, **Press the Attack** (1: 5 damage, 4 more against an Exposed target; about 7.4 against a budget of 10) and **Heavy Swing** (2: 14 damage; 14 of 19). **Assassin** (was 12 cards): 4 Strikes, 2 Defends, Hobbling Jab, Duck and Weave and two new basics, **Fang and Claw** (1: 4 damage twice, 1 Bleed; about 8.7) and **Pocket Blades** (1: add 2 Daggers to your hand; about 8). **Shaman:** one each of Spirit Call, Raise the Hawk, Frost Ward and Forked Lightning (they were doubled) with 4 Strikes and 2 Defends. **Monk:** 4 Strikes, 3 Defends, Kindle Fury, Still Guard and a new basic **Mindful Palm** (1: 5 damage, 2 Fervor; about 9). **Drunkard** (was 11): one Hammer Swing, not two. New basics have borrowed art (docs/card-art/placeholder-art-2026-10-30.md). Not changed yet: **Obsidia** (11 cards: waiting on Robert, whose "two Defends and a Strike out" does not add up to 10), the Engineer and the Necromancer (2 class basics each, below the 3 to 4 aim) and the Trickster (two Veil Strikes).
459. **Obsidia has ten cards; the totem row grows to eight positions (Robert, 2026-10-30: "just make spellblade have 10 cards. also i want the cap on totems to be 8, and make them smaller to fit in the space if needed") ->** Obsidia's starting deck loses one Defend (4 Strikes, 3 Defends, Meteor Jab, Gather Light, Obsidian Blade), so every starting deck is ten cards (#458). `TOTEM_SLOTS` is 8 (was 5): Carve the Spirit Pole and any other position card can grow the row that far (the Shaman still starts with 3). The totem arc on the stage keeps the height of five positions: from six on each position shrinks (5 over the number of positions of its size) and hides its small numbers, which stay in the hover. More positions means more passives each turn and a longer wait before the front one is pushed out; no card was repriced, and no card gives positions in quantity now (Raise the Pole and Grow the Pole are retired).
460. **The Arena takes decks of 15 cards or more; a campaign ghost is never your own (Robert, 2026-10-30: "lets make a 15 card minimum for arena decks and reflect that in the campaign status indicator"; "ghosts in campaign should only be other players ghosts") ->** `checkArenaEntry` (the Arena's doors: the queue, a ghost match, a friend room) refuses a deck of fewer than `ARENA_MIN_DECK` = 15 cards: "The Arena takes decks of at least 15 cards; this one holds N." A run and a Vault still keep any deck of 8 or more (`V2_DECK_RULES.minSize`), and a ghost plays as it was saved. The run bar's Arena badge (`arenaProblem`) uses the same check, so it says "Not valid for the Arena" with that message until the deck has 15 cards (a starting deck of ten does not pass). Ghosts: the server already never picks a campaign ghost owned by the player (their ids, their Vault code or their username); the browser now drops one too whose name is theirs, whatever the server sent. The Arena's own ghost match (`pickGhost`) still skips only the exact deck being played, so another deck of yours may come up there.
461. **A class basic each for the Engineer, Trickster and Necromancer (Robert, 2026-10-30: "the engineer, trickster, necro are fine so i wasnt going to touch them. but i would like a class card for them") ->** their starting decks stay ten cards and gain a third (Trickster: a fourth kind) class basic in place of one Strike or Defend. **Engineer** (4 Strikes, 3 Defends): **Spot Weld** (1: 5 damage, 2 Barrier to you and your companions; about 8.6 of 10). **Trickster** (3 Strikes, 2 Defends, two Veil Strikes): **Pinprick Trap** (0: a Trap, when you are attacked 6 damage to the attacker; about 5.1 of 6; the starting deck still holds all four suits). **Necromancer** (4 Strikes, 3 Defends): **Marked for the Grave** (1: 6 damage and 4 Fate; about 9 of 10). Borrowed art for now (docs/card-art/placeholder-art-2026-10-30.md). Every starting deck now has 3 or 4 different class basics.
462. **Aegis of Ages gives one Shielded; Ward Stone is gone (Robert, 2026-10-30: "the legendary boon for shielded should only give 1 shielded. if theres another boon for shielded then get rid of it, its too good") ->** the Legendary Aegis of Ages gave Shielded 3 times (the first three hits ignored, every fight); it now gives 1 (the first hit), as its text says. That is what the common Ward Stone already gave, so Ward Stone was taken out (it was the only other plain Shielded Boon). Warded Heart (a Legendary heart: 16 Barrier and Shielded, then Brittle) keeps its single Shielded. A saved run holding a Ward Stone now holds an Iron Sigil instead (6 Barrier at the start of a fight; once only if it had one already), and a ghost deck saved with one plays without it. Watch: Aegis of Ages is now a Legendary with the power of a common.
463. **Backup Core revives one ally, once (Robert, 2026-10-30: "it allows an ally to die once and revive at half hp, it shouldnt keep reviving them") ->** it was the first death each turn (`once`), which still brought a machine back every turn of a long fight; it now answers a single death and is then gone (`times: 1`): "The next time one of your allies dies: Your most recently fallen ally returns with half its HP." Its upgrade (a cost cut) is unchanged.
464. **Heavy Hitter says "living"; Spirit Call checked (Robert, 2026-10-30: "heavy hitter appears to be calcuating damage based on allies that are already dead"; "my friend said he doesnt know if shamans cards to command a totem twice is working") ->** Heavy Hitter (and Crankshaft) read the max HP of the biggest companion with HP above 0: a test now plays one with the Enforcer at 0 HP and the damage is the Drone's. I could not make a dead companion count, so the card text now says "biggest living companion's max HP" and the case is locked by a test; if it still happens in play, the exact turn (who died, how) is what is needed. Spirit Call: a test per totem (Hawk, Bear, Thunderbird, Elder Tree) shows the front totem acts twice, once per act it answers "a totem acts", it leaves, the totem behind it stays, Spirit adds to both acts, and with no totem it does nothing; the Moon Owl case was already tested (6 + 6). Nothing was broken in either.
465. **Foresee needs no Confirm; the Engineer gets ceiling cards and bigger big machines; a Legendary Boon can be given up or swapped (Robert, 2026-10-30: "players shouldnt have to click confirm when in the forsee window"; "he is good early but just steady sustained damage"; "a card or two with allies act twice ... bigger minions should have slightly higher benefits in both hp and dmg"; "a small X button to legendary boons ... or swap one out for the other") ->** (1) Foresee (pick one of N) submits on the click. (2) Three Engineer rares, each spent after one use: **Full Throttle** (2 Mana: 3 Primed, the crew acts twice; the first "act now" card to act twice, so the allyGrow rule now allows exactly it), **Critical Mass** (3 Mana: 10 + 3 per Might, within the guard-rail cap of 3 for a consumed payoff) and **Final Volley** (3 Mana: 6 + 10 per companion). (3) Heavy/Colossal machines: Enforcer 12 HP / 7, Siege Engine 4x2 (+: 16 HP, 5x2), Bulwark Walker 14 HP / 4 Barrier, War Engine 22 HP / 5x2 (+: 26, 6x2), Scrap Golem 14 / 7, Dreadnought 22 / 6 to all; about +10-16% on the section 10 rate, to offset the frame penalty. (4) A Legendary Boon shows a small X on the map (confirm, then it and its Max HP are given up, so another can be offered); an Elite or loop-clear Legendary that arrives while one is held waits as a Swap / Keep mine choice (legendOffer on the run). Events and shops still never offer a second Legendary. The one-Legendary limit (#293) stands. Not yet re-priced in docs/14_CARD_COSTING.md section 10's table.
466. **Merchant card removal doubles in price each time (Robert, 2026-10-30: "update merchants to allow removing a card but the price doubles each time") ->** removal was already sold (one card per visit, 15 dearer each time, capped at 100, #243); it is now 40, 80, 160, 320... for each removal bought in the run, with no cap (it stops doubling at the 20th so a save can hold the number). One removal per visit stays. Run-wide count (run.removals) and the shop text say so.
467. **Every event's named card is in its option box; the elite event button says Spin To Win (Robert, 2026-10-30: "i just got rift cache, the card for me to take should be in the option box. ive brought that up before and dont know why it keeps happening"; "the Elite Events button should just say Spin To Win") ->** #435 only fixed data-driven options (`pinRemovals`). The hand-written options that use `cardOffer` (the rift cache's card, the Ancestors' Offering, the traveller, the bonfire) never set `shows`, so their card stayed above the options. `pinOptions` now fills `shows` for them, the screen no longer lays the card out separately when a box has it, and `eventCardBoxes.test.ts` walks every event so a new one cannot miss it. The rules for spinning are still in the line under the reel.
468. **Only the rare big machines keep their buff (Robert, 2026-10-30: "revert the buff to engineers minions except for the rares, i want those to feel rewarding to play, especially the big boys that take 2-3 slots. a 3 slot minion needs to have some kind of value since the mass minion build is obviously better right now with might/primed") ->** part of #465 is taken back: Enforcer 11 HP / 6, Bulwark Walker 12 HP / 3 Barrier and Scrap Golem 12 HP / 5 are as they were. Siege Engine (12 HP, 4x2), War Engine (22 HP, 5x2) and Dreadnought (22 HP, 6 to all) keep it.
469. **Colossal machines hit several times; the no-room prompt has scrap buttons (Robert, 2026-10-30: "sure give them more hits for those 3 slot machines"; "i wasnt able to play my bulward walker ... i thought i would be able to scrap one but it showed some weird popup and i clicked accept because i just wanted to scrap but i lost the summon") ->** Might and Primed go on every hit, so a Colossal with few hits lost to three Light machines. War Engine is 4x3 (+: 26 HP, 5x3), Dreadnought 4x2 to all (+: 26 HP, 5x2), Scrap Golem 3x2 (+: 16 HP, 4x2; same price, included since it is a 3-slot machine too). The no-room prompt (#405) offered only Play anyway and Cancel, and scrapping meant knowing to click a machine on the board. It now has a button for each way to make room, naming the machine and its HP. Clicking the machine still works. Play anyway is not offered when it would do nothing (a card that only summons, with none coming); the engine still allows it, so #405 stands for every card where something comes of it.
470. **Press the Attack becomes a defensive basic (Robert, 2026-10-30: "lets change press the attack to a defensive basic card, we have enough damage now but need better defenses for the knight") ->** Set Your Shield (1 Mana, skill, basic): 6 Barrier, and 3 more if you have Reserves after paying for it (+: 8 and 4). It is 7.2 points without Reserves (72% of a 1-cost budget, inside the 70-90% for a class basic) and 10.8 with them (108%, over). Reserves never expire, so once a deck holds one the bonus can stay on. Requiring 2 Reserves was considered, but Robert reports "pretty much no mana reserves" on the Knight early on, so 1 stays: the bonus should not be out of reach in the early game, and the over-budget late value is accepted. It always beats Defend (5). The starting deck goes from 51 to 46 damage in a cycle before Exposed. Old saves, Arena decks and ghosts read press-the-attack as set-your-shield. The art is a placeholder, stand-firm (the old Knight model).
471. **The Trickster has no duplicate Veil Strike (Robert, 2026-10-30: "trickster shouldnt have duplicate veil strike in startign deck") ->** the second Veil Strike is a Strike: 4 Strikes, 2 Defends, Veil Strike, Set the Web, Pick a Card, Pinprick Trap. Same damage and the same suit (a spade); the deck loses 1 Exposed a cycle. This overrides the exception in #458, and every class now has each class basic once.
472. **Virulent is renamed Hemorrhage; the card Hemorrhage is renamed Red Tide (Robert, 2026-10-30: "Hemorrhage sounds better"; "sure then rename the card") ->** Virulent is a word for disease or poison, but it makes Bleed tick more times. The Assassin's rare card already had the name, so it gave it up. Only the names players see change: the status id stays virulent and the card id stays hemorrhage, so saves, decks and art still find them. No numbers change.
473. **Cards are chosen on the hand, with no window (Robert, 2026-10-30: "id like to do away with the confirm window for deferring a card or discarding one, id rather just pick the one from the hand. give them some kind of highlight that they need to be selected as the next input") ->** a choice among cards in hand is made by clicking the card; the choosable ones pulse gold, the others dim, and a bar names the choice. Applied to every choice of cards from the hand (discard, Defer, Consume, Put back, Transform, Copy), not only the two named, since they were the same window. A choice of one card goes with the click. A choice of several goes with its last card, a marked card can be unmarked, and an "up to" choice has a Done or Keep them all button. On a touch screen a tap chooses and a long press reads the card, as when playing one (#475). Foresee, the draw pile, totems, enchantments and forms keep the window, because their options are not in the hand. There is no undo, so a misclick stands.
474. **Garrote leaves the Assassin pool (Robert, 2026-10-30: "i think we should remove Garrote from the assassin card pool") ->** old saves, Arena decks and ghosts read it as Graveside Cut, an Assassin uncommon attack at the same cost. It was the only card that applied Strangled, so no card uses that status now; the engine rule and its tooltip stay, as nothing else depends on them and a later card could use them. Its picture stays in the art folder with the other retired cards.
475. **Phones: tap to play, long press to read, you at the bottom left (Robert, 2026-10-30: "long press on a card shows the preview but just pressing on one should play the card or start the arrow selection mode. the player character model also needs to be a little smaller so everythign can fit on the board ... making the player in the bottom left hand corner and the enemies in the top right ... that would also leave ample room on the top left side of the screen for some display info like aura window/boons/etc") ->** this replaces the two-tap card of #443. A tap acts as a click. A press of 350 ms lifts the card to read it until the finger lets go (shown big on a phone; on a tablet held sideways it lifts with its notes), and nothing is played; it also works on the enemy's turn. A finger that wanders up to 12 px still counts as a tap (a mouse keeps 7), and dragging still plays and aims. With no pointer to follow, the arrow on a touch screen points at the first target until one is tapped. Upright, the hero stands at the bottom left at 0.62 of full size and the enemies at the top right at 0.68, and the Shaman's totems stand on the hero's right, since the screen's edge is on the left. The top left shows the trait's name, the number of Boons, each Aura's name and how many effects are against you; tapping it opens the drawer, which keeps the whole text. Sideways phones keep their layout, apart from tap and long press.
476. **A fuller battle log, and Premeditate stands out in a discard (Robert, 2026-10-30: "the battle log should show what card was discarded or premediated. i think we need to log some more stuff that happens"; "if a card has premediate when the discard phase is started then make sure any premeditated card is extra highlighted") ->** the log now tells a discard from an effect, a Defer, Premeditate, Consume, a card sent to the discard pile by a full hand, cards added, a card transformed, a card drawn or Mana gained after something has been played this turn, Foresee, a stopped attack, and Reserves gained or spent. It still leaves out the hand drawn at the start of a turn, the end-of-turn discard and Mana spent, as noise. A hidden card is never named, the opponent's or, when watching a live match, the watched seat's: their discard or Consume reads "a card", their Trap as it goes down gets only "sets a Trap", and the rest get no line. A card consuming itself as it is played gets no second line. The engine marks routine discards and exhausts on the event and has a new premeditate event, for the screen only; no rule changes. In a discard choice, the cards with Premeditate (printed or given in the fight) pulse violet with a ribbon, and the bar says what it does.
477. **Every card an event option surely takes or gives is in its box (Robert, 2026-10-30: "i just ran into the event a Traveller from another world, why is the card not in the option box? can we please make sure all events with options that have cards tied to them, the card needs to be in the option box") ->** a new Traveller event already boxed its card in 0.140.0, and the live builds had that code, so the likely cause is an event laid out before the update and kept in the save, which fell back to the old layout (not confirmed: it was not reproduced on the player's save). Saved events are now repaired on load. A walk over every event then found the master and the student's named swaps unboxed, and options that add Wounds or Static (decided here: the earlier open question). Those are boxed, and a test walks every event with a varied deck. Cards behind a chance (an even chance of two Wounds) are still only in the words, since they may not happen.
478. **Steadfast comes out of the card text of Lay Bare and Cast the Shield (Robert, 2026-10-30: "yea lets trim the steadfast part") ->** enemies only have Steadfast at Rift Tier 17 and up, and Arena opponents only through Mask of Faces, so naming it made these cards read as niche. The effect is unchanged: the strip op still takes Steadfast along with Barrier, it just is not said on the card.
479. **A card's damage number and breakdown match the engine for Honed (Robert, 2026-10-30: "i had honed, and tracking on and it looked like my dagger wasnt calculating everything right"; "it needs to show the calculation correctly in the damage tooltip") ->** the damage dealt was right: Honed was added on the first Dagger of the turn, then Might, Exposed and Tracking. Only the card was wrong, as previewCardDamage left Honed out, so a Dagger showed less than it dealt. It now counts Honed (once a turn), and Edge and Honed are their own steps. The same check found Lethality and Noose missing, and Fanned shown as one enemy; both are fixed. The breakdown was hidden whenever several enemies could be hit, so it now shows the highest number's steps, labelled as such. Tests compare the number shown with the damage dealt.
480. **The Spellblade is rebuilt on Spell Power and Fracture (Robert, 2026-10-31: "instead of Sharpen, i like the idea of Imbue-ing her blade with Spell Power"; "the spell power is the arcana"; "no caps on anything including spells"; "sword rares need real power and need cards that still make the blade hit harder"; the merged list, ChatGPT's draft as the main version, docs/reworks/SPELLBLADE_SPELL_POWER_MERGED.md) ->** Arcana and Sharpen become one reserve, Spell Power (`stars` underneath): Swords read it (`sword` tag, hits scaled by it: the Obsidian Blade 5 damage, 1 hit + 1 per 2, no hit cap) and spend nothing; Spells spend it, "spend any amount" chosen as the card is played (`Action.spend`, read through metric `starSpend`; left out means all of it; the rate per point rises with Mana, 4 at 1, 6 at 2, 8 at 3, so a cheap spell does not beat every finisher); Fracture is a third way to win (a debuff that never fades; at 10, remove 10 and Shatter for 18, plus `shatterBonus`; not an attack, Barrier stops it, it never applies Fracture or sets off a Shatter; a transfer is not an application). Generic pieces: `fracture`, `shatterUp`, `shatterBlast`, `carryFracture`, `empower`, `hitFracture` on damage, `only` on an Aura (checked before a once-a-turn Aura is spent), `growSelf.grown` (a free replay does not grow it, and it fires `grown`), statuses `keen` and `ruinous`, triggers `fracture`, `shatter`, `grown`. **88 reward cards** (27 / 40 / 21, 19 Auras; the other classes hold 78-93): 50 kept and reworked, 30 cut, 38 new, plus the new basic Hairline Hex in place of one Strike; the Retainer tokens go. Trait: Kindled, 3 at the start and 1 a turn. **No caps, by Robert's call:** at 27 Spell Power with Exposed on, Lava Bomb with Measured Ruin deals 435, 81% of an Arena opponent's 540 HP; he will nerf what proves too strong. **Choices made for him:** every "up to N" went (it was a cap) and the rate per point does that job; "first each turn" Aura limits stay, since they stop loops; Weave and Spellstrike stayed out. **Saves:** `CLASS_REWORKS.spellblade` version 3, 33 ids in `RETIRED_CARDS`, a fight in progress restarts, the player is told. No balance simulation was run.
481. **The Spellblade's Sword and Spell are badges, not words (Robert, 2026-10-31: "those should be moved to the top right of cards like we did for the elements on the shaman"; "the color of the resource indicates what it is"; "we dont need the text 'spend any amount' when it clearly shows the X") ->** a card's face drops the word and shows a badge in the top right with a tooltip, as for the Shaman's elements (#447); the rules text `cardText` still says it, so the card list, keyword tips and tests read it. The Spell Power orb shows only its number or X, and "Spend any amount of Spell Power" left the text. Defensive and curse Spells such as Glass Veil stay Spells: Emberstrike, Crosscut, Glass Armory and Recenter read them, so a Barrier Spell sets up a Sword.
482. **No choice of how much Spell Power a card spends (Robert, 2026-10-31: "there shouldnt be a choice for how much spell power gets spent, it should be built into the card. so X should mean all of it. X/2 should be half. those are the only options. get rid of that choice menu") ->** replaces "spend any amount" (#480): `starX` is X (all of it), the new `starHalf` is X/2 (half, rounded down; 1 held spends none). `Action.spend`, the slider and its server field are gone. **X:** Molten Ruin, Arcane Flood, Falling Shards, Lava Bomb, Cataclysm, Thousand Shards. **X/2:** Cinderfall, Spellbolt, Arcane Pulse, Glass Burst, Controlled Release, Dying Ember, Obsidian Drill, Stress the Glass, Arcane Barrier, Glass Wall: the cheap and repeatable ones, so a hand of them does not empty the reserve, with the same prices (they were priced at about half a held reserve). **Mirror Glass** had only "if you spent 2 or more" to read, so it takes a fixed cost of 2 instead: 7 Barrier and 1 Shielded, (20.4 / 24.6). A fixed number stays legal, as on Weight of the Crown. The Spell Power counter beside Mana shows only its number; its hover explains it.
483. **Fracture only adds up, and her basic is defensive (Robert, 2026-10-31: "hairline hex should be a defensive basic card. drop the fracture from the basic cards"; "change fracture to never drop to 0 and just keep adding up ... every 10 fracture it triggers and with an increased effect"; "cool for players to be able to get to 100 fracture"; "make sure the barrier is sufficient because i think thats the part she lacks early"; "cards need to show the full damage calculation") ->** Fracture is never removed: every 10 it crosses is a Shatter, and the k-th deals **15 + 3 x k**: 18 at 10, 21 at 20, 45 at 100, 315 for the first ten, no ceiling (linear so a big application stays readable and 100 is reachable without a one-turn kill; a deck applying 20 a turn deals about 39, 51, 63, 75, 87 a turn, against a Sword deck's 47-53, so it starts behind and passes in a long fight). Deepening still adds to each. A cleanse no longer takes Fracture off. **Carry the Fault** cannot move Fracture now (moving 100 onto a fresh enemy would set off every Shatter again): it applies 4 plus half the most-cracked other enemy's Fracture, which keeps its own; alone, 8. **Rates:** Fracture is 1.6 a point (about 22 per 10 at a mid-fight count, less 20%); Rupture Script drops to 20 (32 / 29.7), the other Fracture cards stay within 15-18%. **Basic:** Hairline Hex becomes **Obsidian Ward** (1: 7 Barrier, gain 1 Spell Power; upgrade 10 Barrier), 11.4 points, 114% of a 1-cost common, over the usual 70-90% on purpose, level with the Knight's Brace and Press (116%): she has 75 HP and it was her weak spot. **Pale Ward** goes to 8 Barrier and 1 (12.6 / 10) so the common still beats the basic. New id `obsidian-ward`, because Hairline Hex's picture shows the curse; it borrows Guarded Cut's art until it has its own. **On the card:** a Sword whose hits grow shows the real count and total, "Deal 5 damage 7 times (35 total)", with Might, Primed and Blade Echo counted (`LiveDamage.hits`, `total`).
484. **Who can spend it all, a rare payoff, and more Spell Power on other cards (Robert, 2026-10-31: "the basic/common cards for spending spell power should max out at half"; "uncommons and rare should be able to use the full amount but can also be half"; "there should be a rare one that is a high pay off"; "more ways to increase spell power genearation built into other cards") ->** a test holds that no basic or common is X; they already were not. **Lava Bomb** is the high payoff: 20 + 10 per point (was 8; 3 Mana, X, Consume; 120 at 10 spent, against 114, within budget), the best rate she has. **Spell Power on eight more cards, paid for by trimming them:** Emberstrike (1 hit + 1 per 3; gain 1, 1 more after a Spell: 10.5 / 10), Shard Strike (1 hit + 1 per 3; gain 1: 11.2 / 11.2), Crosscut (after a Spell also gain 1: 11.5 / 11.2), Tempered Bolt (6 damage, gain 1, 2 more at none: 10.5 / 10), Glass Veil (8 Barrier, 6 next turn, gain 1: 18.6 / 19), Ash Wind (gain 1: 12 / 11.2), Recenter (gain 1: 12.4 / 11.2), Fated Hand (gain 1: 16 / 15.6). **Ceiling:** at 27 Spell Power with Exposed on, Lava Bomb alone is 435 (81% of an Arena opponent's 540 HP), and 516 (96%) with Measured Ruin; left in on purpose, with no caps by his call, for him to nerf in play.
485. **Card text says where a condition stops, on every card (Robert, 2026-10-31: "its unclear if the 2nd part applys to 'the first time' part of the card"; "my vote would be change the order of the sentences, but i want to make sure you address all cards that have this problem") ->** 113 of 952 cards read that way, across every class. Two fixes, both in `cardText`, none by hand: **order** (what always happens first, then the keywords like Consume, then each condition or trigger, so nothing plain follows one; "If this is fatal" moves there too) and **joining** (reordering cannot help when both sentences belong to the trigger, so what one trigger does is one sentence: "Draw 1 card and gain 2 Barrier"). "If A: If B:" reads "If A and B:", an Aura's own condition "At the end of your turn, if ...:", and a summon says what it does inside its own brackets. The text only: no rules changed by it, except Shard Strike, whose 9 Spell Power check now comes after its gain, as it reads. `cards.test.ts` scans every card, so a new one cannot bring it back.
486. **Shatters scale harder, and all her Barrier scales with Spell Power (Robert, 2026-10-31: "we start it at like 10 + 10 for every threshold met"; "all of her cards that are tied to the spellblade class should have some kind of spellpower scaling on the barrier") ->** **Shatter:** the k-th deals 10 + 10 x k: 20 at 10, 30 at 20, 110 at 100, 650 in all by 100 (it was 315). Read literally: one threshold met is 10 + 10; a first Shatter of 10 would be weaker than today's 18. Fracture's rate rises from 1.6 to 2.5 a point, and the cards keep their amounts, so on paper they are about 55% over budget; that is the ask, and the first thing to trim is the amounts on cards, not the curve. **Barrier:** one rule, +1 per 3 Spell Power, on all 21 cards with a fixed Barrier (the shared Defend is not hers). Unbreakable Crown is +1 per 2 and drops its "+8 at 10 Spell Power". Last Ember, Sigil of Glass and Afterglow need her empty or spending, so they read Spell Power spent this turn. Each Barrier reads Spell Power where its sentence sits in the text: before the card's own gain on most (Growing Crucible and Soul of Steel+ reordered for that), after it on Shard Strike, Patient Edge+ and Sigil of Kindling, which say the gain first. Glass Veil's "next turn, gain 6" stays fixed: its Barrier this turn scales, and there is no op for scaling the delayed part. Bases unchanged: each card is 3.2 dearer at the priced 8 SP (1.6 for the three), by his call to increase it. A card in hand now shows the Barrier a scaled effect would really give, on any class, and a preview takes Spell Power already paid off first. **Fracture's place in the kit is still open:** he will play it first (docs/13_PROPOSALS.md).
487. **Echoing Blade stops stacking, and Shatters read Spell Power (Robert, 2026-10-31: "only add multiple hits for obsidian blade 1 per 2 spell power... its just exceptionally strong especially when a player stacks up multiple of them"; "when i say fracture needs to scale with spell power i really meant the shatter damage amount") ->** **Echoing Blade** gives the Blade 1 more hit per 2 Spell Power instead of replaying it. If each copy added that, one copy would barely change (13 hits against today's 14 at 12 Spell Power) and three would still come to 25, so copies do not stack, as with Cleaving Edge. At 12 Spell Power the Blade deals 65 with any number of copies (it was 70, 105, 140 with one, two, three). Primed and Might now reach the extra hits, where Primed used to miss the replay. **Shatter:** the k-th deals 10 + 10k + the Spell Power you hold when it goes off, spending none: +1 a point on every Shatter, about what a Sword gets per Mana (5 damage per 2 Spell Power on a 2-Mana Blade). Numbers Claude's, since he gave no rate. Fracture is 3.1 a point at the priced 8 Spell Power, so its cards are about twice their paper budget; he finds it unappealing beside Swords in play, so this is left for play to judge, and card amounts are the first thing to trim. **Card text:** a growing Sword in hand keeps its rule and adds what it comes to now, "Deal 5 damage, 1 more time per 2 Spell Power (7 hits, 35 total)", because the rule used to vanish and the number could not be followed. The total also now counts Blade Sweep ("total each") and a Shielded target correctly.
488. **The Obsidian Blade's damage grows, not its hits, and Echoing Blade stacks (Robert, 2026-10-31: "can we have spell power simply add damage to obsidian blade, and echoing blade still be allowed to stack") ->** Replaces the Echoing Blade part of #487 the same day, before it shipped. The Blade is one hit of 5 + 2 per Spell Power, spending none (upgrade 8 + 2; +3 because one point on one hit is too small an upgrade). Each Echoing Blade adds 1 Blade hit.
