The rate is Claude's: +1 a point makes it 13 at 8 Spell Power, weaker than two Strikes for the same 2 Mana, while +2 makes 21 against a budget of 19. At 12 Spell Power, with 0 to 3 Echoes: 29, 58, 87, 116 (the first version: 35, 70, 105, 140). At 27: 59, 118, 177, 236. Might and Primed now add once a swing on the Blade, so they matter less until Echoes multiply them; other Swords are unchanged. Stacking still multiplies, so three rares in one deck are the case to watch.
489. **Recall the Blades shows its damage (Robert, 2026-10-31: "needs to show the damage it will do on the card") ->** A card in hand that plays every fitting card in your consumed pile adds how many and what they come to: "(2 played, 8 total)". Each is worked out after what the one before spent (Primed, Honed, a Shielded stack), generically for that op, not for this card. It counts the cards' own damage, not Aura answers such as Serpent's Coil, so a random hit is never given away.
490. **Tiers > Acts > Floors, and Play (Robert, 2026-10-31: "change the idea of Tiers > Stages > Floors to Tiers > Acts > Floors"; "change the text for the New Run button to just say Play") ->** Every word a player reads says Act. The code, saved runs, saved decks, the server and its database keep the name `stage`: they are not seen, and renaming them would break saves and the leaderboards for nothing. Past patch notes stay as written. The home button says Play, with "Start a new campaign run as the [class], through 20 tiers of fights." On a phone, Begin the run on the Sanctum stays in view at the bottom of the screen. The home figure's box no longer depends on the canvas's own size, which is how it could sit off to one side until another Riftwalker was picked.
491. **Merchants remove as many cards as you can pay for (Robert, 2026-10-31: "allow merchants to remove cards but double the cost each time. i thought i already asked for this") ->** He had (#466), but that kept the old limit of one removal a visit, so the second was refused. The limit is gone: 40, 80, 160, 320... across the run, on the same visit too. The deck minimum of 8 still stands, so a starter deck of 10 has room for two until it grows.
492. **On-or-off statuses have no number, and a Fanned Dagger needs no target (Robert, 2026-10-31: "reword 'gain 1 poised' and 'gain 1 fanned' to be just gain poised and gain fanned... check other cards like that"; "if i have fanned then i should have to target an enemy", read as "shouldn't") ->** Checked against the engine: Fanned, Poised, Sturdy, Unmovable, Cruelty, Tracking, Unbound, Planner and Meld are only read as there or not, as Fortified already was (#347), so they say "Gain Fanned". Everything counted keeps its number: Free Attack, Free Skill, Mirrored, Reverb, Blur, Holdfast, Shielded and Steadfast are used up one at a time; Cadence, Hemorrhage, Keen and Noose grow with their stacks. Fanned: a Dagger offers one play, so it plays with no aiming. An Inked Dagger still asks, because its Faltering lands on one enemy and that is a real choice (Tracking pays for it); every other Dagger's only use of the target is its damage. Status chips and floating words on the fight screen drop the number for these statuses too.
493. **Removal doubles only at that merchant stop (Robert, 2026-10-31: "i didnt mean for card removal to double cost and persist through the run, i just meant at that particular merchant stop") ->** Replaces the run-long price of #466 and #491. Every merchant starts at 40 (more under the Omen of Dearer Roads), each removal at that shop doubles the next, and the next shop starts over, as upgrades already do. Cheaper over a run than before, by a lot: the first removal at every shop is 40 again, where the fourth in a run was 320.
494. **Removal starts at 50 at each merchant (Robert, 2026-10-31: "should removing cost 50 to start and double from there? so then its 100 then 200 then 400") ->** Yes. Worked by hand: an act's fights pay about 160 shards (four fights at 15, an Elite at 36, the Warden at 65), before events and Boons. So the first removal at a shop is an easy buy, the second about half an act's fights, the third more than an act's, and the fourth out of reach. It sits between an uncommon card (55) and an upgrade (60). No sim, per the costing rule.
495. **Merchant upgrades cost 75, and 50 more each at the same shop (Robert, 2026-10-31: "make upgrade a card 75 then it increases by 50 each time at the same merchant") ->** It was 60 and 40 more. The next shop starts over at 75, as before. Campaign prices only, so RULES_VERSION stays at 56.
496. **Rift Core is taken out (Robert, 2026-10-31: "id like to remove the legendary boon for extra mana each turn, its completely too strong") ->** Priced by hand: +1 Mana is 7-9 points a turn, so 35-45 a fight of 5 turns with no price to pay, about double Scholar's Tome (~20) and three times Aegis of Ages (12). Extra Mana now comes only from the Ember and Blood Hearts, which charge for it. A saved run holding Rift Core holds a Scholar's Tome instead: the nearest Legendary, and one with no Max HP to add by hand (giving up The Gilded Crown takes 25 Max HP off, which a swapped-in one never gave). A ghost fetched from now on plays without it; one already settled in a saved run plays with the Tome. A fight under way when the save is loaded starts again if its move log no longer plays. Boons are not in the Arena, so RULES_VERSION stays at 56. The rates are in docs/14_CARD_COSTING.md §16. (#497 replaced the Tome here with a Rift Satchel.)
497. **Boons have no rarity (Robert, 2026-10-31: "i dont want any rarity for boons anymore, they get too overpowering. so lets tune down any of non-common boons or get rid of them if they cant be balanced") ->** Every Boon is now worth 7-15 points a fight (docs/14_CARD_COSTING.md §16), equally likely, and 70 shards at a merchant. Kept as they were: Rift Satchel, Crystal Heart, Hex Lantern, Aegis of Ages. Tuned: Vigor Wick heals 1 a turn (was 2), Hunter's Lens is 1 turn of Exposed (was 2), Shard Purse pays 6 (was 10, it now turns up more often and costs less), The Gilded Crown is 6 Max HP and heal 3 (was 25 and 10). Taken out, because tuning them down only makes a Boon already there: Battle Standard (an Idol of Might), Phoenix Ember (a Healing Vial), and Scholar's Tome, since a card every turn cannot be cut down; saved runs get those two commons and a Rift Satchel. The rarity rules went with it: the one-Legendary limit, the X that gave one up, the swap dialog (`legendOffer`). Beating Vorthax still leaves something 40% of the time, now any Boon. Threshold bargains that sold a rare Boon give more to keep their price worth paying: The Rift Is Hungry gives 2 (was 1 rare), The Cursed Crown 3 (was 1 rare); A Haunted Gift still gives 2. An option is not offered if it promises more Boons than the run can still gain. Commons were left as asked, so Thorn Charm (15) stays the top of the band. **Warden's Hearts are not Boons here:** they stay as they are, one at a time, each with its price, and only Thorned Heart is tuned (4 Thorns, was 6; ~38 points came to ~23) because its Exposed ends after two turns. A run already holding the old Crown keeps the Max HP it was given. Campaign only, not in the Arena.
498. **A deck holds at most 2 copies of an Aura (Robert, 2026-10-31: "lets cap at 2. doubling is maintained as a valid strategy, but a deck should be forced into more variety than just one aura, and yes this applies to the deck not the fight. dont worry about those gaps") ->** `MAX_AURA_COPIES` (an Aura is kind `power`), read by `maxCopiesOf` and so by `canAdd`: rewards, merchants, events and deals stop at 2, and a card and its "+" count as one. Every Aura is treated alike: no 1-copy group, no limit on Mana from different Auras, and a card that plays an Aura an extra time (Doubled Vow) is untouched. A run's deck already over the limit keeps its copies and takes no more, as with the Legendary limit (#238); a deck saved outside the run (Arena, Vault, ghost) leaves the extra copies out, as past 5 copies of a card (#337). A card on hold that the deck can no longer take refunds its deposit at the next merchant. It changes what an Arena deck holds, so RULES_VERSION goes up at the next deploy.
499. **Less Fracture, same Shatters (Robert, 2026-10-31: "i have a 2 cost card that applies 20 fracture and it straight does 50 damage, thats just a bit much ... im ok with how much damge shatter does, but need to throttle the fracture amounts a little bit; idk whats fair but that card maybe should be 15") ->** Rupture Script 15 (was 20; "+" 20, was 26), so from nothing it is one Shatter, 20, not 50. The same quarter or so off the rest, setting about 5 Fracture a Mana: Faultline Hex 5/7 (was 7/9), Spreading Fault, Pressure Point and Hairline Crack 4/5 (were 5/7), Crack the Shell 4 (was 5), Glassbrand, Glass Prison and Fault Piercer 2 (were 3), Stress the Glass 3 +2 per Spell Power spent, "+" 5 (was 4 +3, "+" 6), Fault Memory 4/6 (was 5/7), Collapsing Vein 6 to all + 3 ("+" 8 + 3; was 7 + 5, 9 + 5), Carry the Fault 3, or 6 alone ("+" 5, or 8; was 4 or 8, 6 or 10), Breaking Point 6/9 (was 8/12), Hungering Void 2/3 (was 3/4). Unchanged: every Shatter number, Final Shatter (10 for 2 Mana is already the rate), Deep Fault (paid for by its Shatter raise), Etching Edge, Falling Shards. Priced by hand, no sim. At the guide's 3.1 a point these are still over budget: Fracture does nothing until it crosses a 10, and Robert asked for a little, not a rebuild. A card still reaches two Shatters on an enemy already cracked: Rupture Script on 5 or more is 50.
500. **Neutral cards sit at or below class cards, and Ancestral cards were retuned (Robert, 2026-10-31: "none of these should be better than class specific card, they should only be equal to or slightly less optimal. the exception would be ancestral cards which can be competitive"; "update all of them, update rift collapse too") ->** Priced by hand against the class cards, upgrades included. **Neutral:** Plan Ahead is common, +1 Mana and +1 card next turn (upgrade +2 cards), one step behind Burst of Speed. Stolen Time is retired into Rift Surge: it could only copy or beat Overcharge. Heart of the Rift loses its Mana: uncommon, cost 2 (upgrade 1), draw 1 a turn, behind The Long Game until upgraded; it leaves the shelved Legendary list, as its old form is gone. Attunement costs 3 (upgrade 2), one Mana behind the class versions. Nerve Surge is uncommon, draw 1 and +1 Mana (upgrade draw 2), no longer Rush of Blood's exact words. Rift Surge is common. Fever Dream's upgrade adds Hold, not 3 less. Rift Collapse deals 30 to ALL, and its upgrade 36 instead of costing 2 (replacing #271's reason: 30 to ALL for 2 would be far over); his call to change it, the numbers Claude's. **Ancestral:** Echo of Ages is 2 Might and 2 Thorns (upgrade 3 and 2). Ancestral Blade 30 (upgrade 34) and Elder's Bulwark 28 (upgrade 32), because both were below a class rare (Immovable Oath, 30 Barrier). Lineage costs 1. The Long Memory's upgrade draws 1 when played instead of costing 1. Forebear's Gift, Ancient Pact and the others stay. The card list now has an Ancestral section.

501. **Burn goes through Barrier; Wildfire and Flashpoint retuned (Robert, 2026-10-31: "something with the shaman fire deck appears to be crazy burst in just a few round of arena"; "give flashpoint the 12 flat damage and +2 per burn and consumes [half] of burn and costs 2"; "wildfire should be changed to just a big burn skill so find a number thats good"; "i thought burn would have to get thru barrier first, so lets make it work like that") ->** **Burn:** Barrier now soaks its ticks (`applyDamage` kind `burn`, not an attack: Shielded, Ghostly and Thorns-style answers stay out of it; the event still reads `direct`). It still loses 1 a tick, blocked or not. It ticks at the start of the unit's turn **before** that unit's Barrier falls away, or there would be nothing for it to get through; Bleed is unchanged (past Barrier, after the drop), so the two are no longer the same (#421). The `tick` op follows the same rule. **Wildfire** (`spreading-flames`): a skill now, 1 Mana, Consume, Flame: apply 8 Burn (+: 10), no damage, no doubling. Priced: 36 ticks x 0.55 = 19.8 against 14 for a 1-Mana uncommon with Consume; 7 is on budget, 8 is one step over because Robert wants it to read big and Barrier can soak it (10 / 13 was 30 points). **Flashpoint** (`flash-burn`): 2 Mana, 12 damage +2 per Burn on the target (+: 16), then halves that Burn, rounded down; still Consume, which Robert did not ask to change. **Why:** the Sacagaweeda tier 9 ghost (Arena, 540 HP target) could kill on its own turn 3; Flashpoint read 27+ Burn at 3 a stack for 1 Mana and kept it all. **Checked by scripted turns on that deck (a target that plays nothing):** with no Barrier, full engine and ideal draws still kills at the tick after turn 3 (504 by turn 3, was a kill on turn 3); holding 20 Barrier at each tick moves that to 3 idle turns later. Without Echoing Spirit and the Hunters: 5 idle turns later (was 4), or 12 holding 20. Five plain Wildfires with Flashpoint at 3 Mana: 8 idle turns later, and alive holding 20. Open: the rest of the burst is the echoed Inferno with Exposed, and the balance test's exception for Wildfire's uncapped doubling is gone. `shaman-ledger.md` is not regenerated: `ledger/write.ts shaman` already fails on the Defect-shaped cards nobody priced. A doubled Wildfire (Moon Owl, Echoing Spirit, Mirrored) is 16 Burn for 1 Mana, about 75 points: watch it. Barrier a Burn tick brings (a phase rise, a Trap it springs) is new and does not fall away with last turn's. Bots now count Burn (`evaluate`), a step below Bleed; the old one never played a Wildfire that only burns. Costing guide: Burn row. RULES_VERSION goes up at the next deploy.

502. **Hangover is a share of Max HP; Blind Drunk and Bender add fewer hits (Robert, 2026-10-31: "we also need to figure something out for the drunkards combo of stagger and hangover bursting for huge numbers"; "lets adjust blind drunk and bender, and lets adjust hangover to take a percentage of his health instead, but try to keep it in line with how much is in campaign. so in arena it will have some kind of downside") ->** **Hangover:** each stack costs 1% of Max HP, the total rounded up (never the last HP). On his 88 HP that is the old 1 a stack, except 9 stacks cost 8 and 10 cost 9; 8 stacks in the Arena (528 HP) cost 43, not 8. **Blind Drunk:** 3 hits, +1 per 2 Hangover (was 2, +1 per 1). **Bender:** 3 damage, 2 hits, +1 per 2 (was 1, +1 per 1). **Why:** three 0-Mana drinks gave 8 Hangover, +2 Mana, 3 cards and 4 Might, and Might and Primed add to every hit: Blind Drunk did 160 alone, 240 at half HP, for an 8 HP price. A hit-adding `max` is ignored by the engine (`scaleValue`), so the step is what limits it. Scripted on a 540 HP target that plays nothing: full line one turn 223 -> about 154; two turns left it at 6, now about 165. Glass Jaw and Mean Drunk answer a bigger Hangover in the Arena, but they keep their caps. Bots now count what Hangover will cost next turn (`evaluate`), so a ghost does not over-drink. RULES_VERSION goes up at the next deploy.

503. **Shatter grows by 5, Rupture Script 10/13, Lava Bomb 7 a point (Robert, 2026-11-01: "make the change for shatter damage to nerf it to 5 scaling per threshold. update the rupture script, leave echoing spirit. and yea tune down lava bomb rate") ->** The k-th Shatter deals 15 + 5k: 20, 25, 30... 65 at 100 (was 20, 30, 40... 110). Ten of them come to 425 (was 650). **Rupture Script** applies 10, 13 upgraded (was 15, 20). **Lava Bomb** deals 20 + 7 per point (28 + 7 upgraded), still her best rate. Echoing Spirit stays as it is. **Fracture** falls to **2.5** a point in the costing guide (3.3 to ALL), the same 0.72 of its value as before. **Why:** Robert wants burst that needs setup and Arena fights of 5+ turns. Scripted ceilings on a 540 HP target that plays nothing, with the exact cards handed over every turn: three Rupture+ line went from dead on turn 3 to 137 left after turn 4 and dead on turn 5; one Rupture+ with commons from dead on turn 4 to 139 left; commons only from 56 to 196; Lava Bomb+ from one hit of 369 to one of 270 (270 left after turn 3); two Bombs from 64 left after turn 4 to 190. This also weakens her in the campaign, which he accepted when he asked for it. It replaces #499's "Shatter damage is unchanged". RULES_VERSION goes up at the next deploy.

504. **Fate shows on the HP bar (Robert, 2026-11-01: "add a display in the health bar for Fate on a target, so lets have some kind of purple healthbar that builds up on the the targets healthbar like in slay the spire 2... almost be like our current barrier display but on the other side of the hp bar and a different color") ->** A purple band grows from the left of the bar, covering Fate's share of Max HP and stopping where the HP stops. At or past the HP it pulses (still, with reduced motion), because that unit dies at the end of its turn. The HP tip says what it means. Display only: `fateBarPct` in `fight/info.ts`. It shows on every unit that has Fate, a hero included.

505. **Drawn pictures for statuses, Boons and piles (Robert, 2026-11-01: "id like to have real artwork for these that are still displayed small on the screen so they need to be somewhat simple in the design... like a little blood drop for bleed... id even like artwork for the decks of cards so they dont look simply flat") ->** `art/gameIcons.ts` holds an original drawing for every status and every Boon and Heart, one bold object with an ink outline and flat shading, drawn by `ui/GameIcon.tsx`; a test fails if a new status or Boon has none. They are placeholders drawn as vectors in code: Robert wants the real pictures made in ChatGPT, from the nine sheets of prompts in `docs/icon-prompts.md` ("i was hoping you would give me prompts for chatgpt to create the images"). When those sheets come back, they get cut and wired in the way the totems and weapons were. Statuses in one family share a shape and differ by colour (a "0" card for Free Attack, Free Skill, Free Aura, Free Spell). **Auras** use the card's own picture in a small round frame, as there are too many Auras to draw one by one and each already has art. **Piles** (`fight/PileArt.tsx`): face-down cards for Draw, thrown face-up cards for Discard, burnt cards for Consumed, one to five cards tall by size, an outline when empty. The symbol field in `STATUS_INFO` stays but no screen draws it.

506. **Draw on the map (Robert, 2026-11-01: "id also like palyers to be able to draw and erase on the map so they can plan out a path, and they should be able to change the color of the pen") ->** A bar above the map: Draw, six colours, Erase, Clear. The eraser rubs out only what it touches, so a line it crosses becomes two. With a tool in hand a tap on an open stop still goes there; a drag never does. **Kept:** in this browser only (`run/inkDrawing.ts`), per run, act and map layout, because the wide and upright maps place their stops differently; it is never part of the run or its save, and the last 8 maps are kept. The same drawing shows on the map opened during a fight. It does not follow a player to another device.

507. **No card scales its number of hits (Robert, 2026-11-01: "i think i dont want anything that scales more hits per anything, id rather just give increased damage on that one hit. can we balance these suspects with that in mind. this excludes things like reverb or echo cards/auras") ->** Every card that added hits by a count (all X cards, Spell Power, cards played, Hangover, HP lost, Status cards, Traps, Skills in hand, earlier commands) now lands one hit carrying the same total at no buffs, and a test fails if a card scales its hits again. Fixed multi-hit cards ("3 damage 3 times") stay, and so do Reverb, Echo, Mirrored and Blade Echo, which replay or are stacks of their own. **Why:** Might, Primed and Keen add to every hit, so a hit count that grows multiplies them, which is what made Blind Drunk 160 and Rending Fury 341 on paper. **Retuned, priced by hand at mid-fight numbers (`14_CARD_COSTING.md`):** Rending Fury 8 + 3 per HP loss, at most 45 (10 + 4, at most 60): a long Arena fight has no other limit, and every hit or tick counts as a loss. Rattle the Bones 7 + 3 per earlier command (9 + 4). Thousand Shards 6 a point (7), the guide's rate at 2 Mana: Lava Bomb stays bigger, with its base, at a Mana more. Apex Frenzy 8 + 4 per card played, and its upgrade 10 + 5 at the same cost: cheaper at 1 Mana was only fair while more damage gained a burst card little. Obsidian Drill 21 + 6 per point (28 + 6). Obsidian Flurry, Hammer and Anvil and Storm of Glass read every 3 Spell Power, with their base up to stay on budget at the priced 8: Storm's 2.5 a point also broke the guard rail of 2 per point of a resource that only grows. Etching Edge applied Fracture per hit, so it now applies 1 + 1 per 8 Spell Power; a Shielded target now takes that Fracture, as with any other Fracture card. Crosscut's second strike is one hit of 4 (6). **Keen:** Whispering Edge gives 3, as it adds once to a Sword now. **Engine:** damage that is only a count, with nothing counted, lands no hit (as X = 0 did), so no Thorns come back and Primed stays, and the card's live number says so. This is every damage effect with a base of 0, not only the converted ones: Bulwark Blow at 0 Barrier, Heavy Hitter and Spent Strike among them. The engine's `into: 'hits'` and `hitFracture` stay, unused by any card. RULES_VERSION goes up at the next deploy.

508. **A card's aimed attack does not change target (Robert, 2026-11-01: "adjust multi-hit targeting to not auto-target a new target if its a single target attack"; he approved the calls below) ->** When the target of a card's aimed attack dies, the hits left are lost, and so are that card's later damage effects (Crosscut's second strike). It covers cards on either side of a PvP match. Random and ALL attacks are untouched. **Attacks no card aimed carry on to the next target:** an enemy's, and a companion's own end-of-turn strike at whoever is in front, so a 1 HP unit cannot soak a whole attack. **A new play is a new attack:** a Reverb or Echo replay starting after its target fell picks a new one, and each companion of a Command starts fresh (no Command strikes more than once today). Noted for Robert: in PvP a 1 HP Taunt companion that a card must aim at now soaks all of that card's hits. RULES_VERSION goes up at the next deploy.

509. **X cards keep their hits; bigger cards keep a fixed number of hits (Robert, 2026-11-01: "X cost cards still still work as multi hit. and we should allow some multi hit cards but they shouldnt scale so crazy, id rathe scall their damage instead... if you think might based swords are weaker then we might just need those cards to have fixed multi hits on the swords"; he also approved the calls in #507 and #508: enemies carry on to their next target, a new play picks a new target, fixed multi-hit cards stay) ->** Revises #507. **X cards** hit X times again: Mana is their limit, and the test allows only `by: 'x'` to scale hits. **Cards built on several hits** keep that many, and each hit grows instead: Obsidian Flurry 4 x3, Rift Sweep 3 x3, Tyrant Strike 4 x3, Fault Piercer 4 x3 and Hammer and Anvil 3 x4 (each +1 per 3 Spell Power), Crosscut 2 x2 (+1 per 4), Storm of Glass 5 x4 (+1 per 2), Obsidian Drill 7 x3 (+2 per point spent), Bender 4 x2 and Blind Drunk 6 x3 (+1 and +2 per 2 Hangover), Apex Frenzy 4 x2 (+2 per card played), Blighted Chorus 5 x2 (+2 per Status card; 6 x2, +3 upgraded). **Cards that began as one hit stay one hit**: Rending Fury, Wild Swings, Trapline Rider, Needle Rain, Flak Burst, Rattle the Bones, Thousand Shards and the 1-hit Swords. **Whispering Edge** gives 2 Keen, as Keen pays on those hits again. **Guard rail:** "at most 2 per point of a resource that only grows" now counts all of a card's hits, since the rate pays on each. **Why:** a Sword deck that raises Might needs hits to pay it off, and a fixed count keeps that from multiplying without limit: upgraded with 27 Spell Power, 5 Might and 2 Keen, Storm of Glass is 108 (238 before #507, 92 as one hit), Obsidian Flurry 66 (192, 60), Hammer and Anvil 84 (204, 64). At the priced 8 Spell Power and no buffs, each is within about 10% of its budget. **Upgrades:** Tyrant Strike and Fault Piercer 6 x3, Rift Sweep 5 x3, Storm of Glass 7 x4, Hammer and Anvil 5 x4, Obsidian Flurry 6 x3, Bender 6 x2, Blind Drunk 8 x3, Apex Frenzy 6 x2; the scaling does not change. A card's live number now shows its hits and total. An X card at X = 0 lands no hit and keeps Primed. The guide's Sword rates follow: Might 10 a stack, Primed 4. RULES_VERSION goes up at the next deploy.

510. **The painted icon and pile art is in (Robert, 2026-11-01: "i think chatgpt put the artwork in the icons folder for you") ->** The nine sheets in `assets-src/icons` (image_gen in ChatGPT, from `docs/icon-prompts.md`; prompts and hashes kept in `docs/art-provenance/icon-sheets-manifest.json` and `icon-sheets-checks.json`, which Steam's AI disclosure will want, as `assets-src` is not in git) are cut by `tools/pack-icons.ps1` into `public/art/icons/<id>.png`: 91 icons at 96 px and 12 piles at 240 px, 2.7 MB in all. The sheets' magenta is close to #FF00FF, not exact, so the cutter reads the background's colour from the corners and takes what magenta reaches from the edge, or a clean hole of it inside a picture; a violet glow inside an outline stays. The vector drawings of #505 remain for the 8 retired statuses, the map's pens and as a fallback if a file fails to load; the vector piles are gone. A pile picks its stack by count: 1-2, 3-7, 8-17, 18+. The spare plain heart is not cut. A test fails if a live status or Boon has no picture or a picture has no file. **Numbers (Robert: "outlined, and on the bottom right of the artwork"):** a status's stacks and a pile's count have no plate behind them now: white, thick dark outline, on the picture's bottom right corner, smaller from 100 up so they never hide the art.

511. **Held upright, the hand is a grid (Robert, 2026-11-01: "cards also need to be a little bigger in mobile... might be better to just do a pre-dertermined grid layout for cards in mobile so everything can fit. it should still be dynamic to fit the width symetrically"; "the buffs/debuffs on a character are not visible, they are overlapped by the potions UI"; "the mana reserved circle doesnt have room... just display the number") ->** On a screen taller than wide the fan is replaced by centred rows of one card size, no overlap, no tilt: a band of 32% of the screen's height (`portraitHandBand`) holds one row or two, whichever fits bigger cards (`handGrid`, pure and tested). On a 390 x 844 phone: about 90 px cards for up to 8 cards (3 + 2 at five, 4 + 4 at eight; the size does not change as a hand is played down), 72 at 9-10 (5 + 5); the old fan was 82 and overlapped. The band has a fixed height, so the controls and the board do not jump as cards come and go. A tap, a drag and a long press work as before. **The board** is fitted above the taller band; your row stands far enough below the enemies' plates for a companion to fit between (on phones around 844 tall and up; a phone of 640 is tight), and high enough for two rows of buffs above the potion belt. Phones held sideways keep the fan: they are too short for rows. **Mana Reserves:** the circle shows only the number; its tooltip still names it.

512. **Every act is 15 floors, the boss included (Robert, 2026-11-01: "id also like to extend each act to be double in length"; "i want every act to have 15 floors including the boss fight") ->** It was 7 + a Warden, twice, then 8 + Vorthax: 25 floors a run, now 45. **What scales with it:** at most 6 Elites an act (was 3), one or two Merchants in each half (at most 4, was 2), a different Elite each time while any are left, and never two Merchants, Shrines or Elites in a row on a path. **Rest (Robert, later the same day: "i dont want shrines at every floor 8, they should be random like normal until the floor before the boss fight"):** Shrines come by chance, as always, until the floor before the boss, which is all Shrines. **What does not change:** what a fight pays (shards, card picks), enemy numbers and the tier table. So a run pays about 1.8 times the shards, card picks, and Elite Boons and healing, and a deck meets the same Wardens much stronger. That is left for Robert to judge in play, as it touches the economy and difficulty. **The map** fits 15 columns at 1920 wide (from 104 px apart; narrower windows scroll) and opens on where you are. A run saved mid-act keeps its map; its next act is 15 floors.

513. **Plating gives Barrier each turn, counting down (Robert, 2026-11-01: "change plating from what it is currently, do similar to the slay the spire 2 mechanic where it adds barrier each turn counting down from its number. that would be more useful for the knight at this point. fortify can be the one that has armour persist through turns") ->** At the end of its turn a unit with Plating N gains N Barrier, then N falls by 1. It keeps nothing through a turn change any more; Fortified is the one that does, unchanged at 75%. Brittle and Shocked cut Plating's Barrier by 25%, as they do any other. It counts as gaining Barrier for "whenever you gain Barrier" Auras, and comes before the turn ends for the enemy to hit. **Prices (`14_CARD_COSTING.md`):** a fresh stack is N(N+1)/2 x 0.9, added to a running stack about 0.9 x N x the turns it has left, like Bleed. So the cards that give 2 or 3 keep their numbers (worth a little less alone, more when a deck keeps playing them). Stoneskin stays at 5 (15 Barrier, 13.5 against 11.2, accepted as an Aura she builds around), and Stoneskin+ falls from 7 (28 Barrier) to 6 (21). This is a new rule in the same shape as Slay the Spire 2's, which Robert asked for, on a generic status. RULES_VERSION goes up at the next deploy.

514. **Campaign ghosts have twice the HP (Robert, 2026-11-01: "we need to give ghost decks more hp in campaign. maybe something like double them in campaign") ->** `GHOST_BOSS_HP_BASE` goes from 1.3 to 2.6, so the rise with the tier table doubles too (a Knight at Tier IV: 80 x 2.6 = 208, was 104). Arena ghosts have their own HP (x ARENA_HP_MULT) and do not change. The Learn page's Odds tab reads the number.

515. **Omens come sooner and hit harder (Robert, 2026-11-01: "i also want to make each tier more dangerous startign from the 2nd tier. id like to omens that are more punishing and really make the player think about proceeding... i like second wind from tier 2, keep that. maybe we need second wind to be slightly tougher. idk if i like the thorns omen... we dont need one omen per stage"; then "lets do omens like this, tier 2 omen is second wind, tier 3 omen is Times Are Tough (shrine healing, shards per fight, 1 less reward per fight, higher merchant prices). Tier 3 is Rallied Foes. go from there, i dont mind all of your tier suggestions") ->** **Tier II:** Second Wind (20% Barrier + 2 Might, was 15% + 1). **III:** Times Are Tough, one Omen in place of four: Shrines heal 25% less (+2 a tier, at most 60%), fights pay 20% fewer shards (+2 a tier, at most 50%), card rewards offer one card fewer, merchants charge 25% more (+5 a tier, 110% at Tier XX). **IV:** Rallied Foes; Robert's message says Tier 3 twice, read as Tier IV as it follows on. **V:** Rift Scars (new: each new act leaves a Wound in the deck, 2 from Tier XII). **VI:** Dread. **VII:** Rift Static. **VIII:** Ironclad, Brittle Wards. **IX:** Blood Rites. **X:** Swarming, Exposed Soul. **XII:** Vigilant. **XIII:** Third Phase. **XV:** Hunted. **XVII:** Warden's Fury. **XX:** The Rift Awakens. Tiers XI, XIV, XVI, XVIII and XIX bring none (the ones in force still grow, and the tier table still climbs). **Wounds:** a Wound is the run's own, so a banked deck leaves them behind (a deck with one was refused by the deck rules, and dropped on the next load), and Rift Scars leaves no more than 6 in a deck. **Hardened Hides (Thorns) is gone.** **The fourth reward card from Tier IV is gone too:** it would have handed back at once what Times Are Tough takes, so a reward offers 3 cards, 2 from Tier III, and Elite rewards the same. Checked by hand, not simulated; these interact with 15-floor acts (#512). Arena fights are not touched by Omens.

516. **Warden's Hearts give no Mana, and are repriced (Robert, 2026-11-01: "Warden Hears should not give mana, so abalance them correctly. im ok with 1 shielded at the start of a fight or start with a number of Plating. we need warden hearts to be balanced as well") ->** Hearts sit at 15-25 points a fight by `14_CARD_COSTING.md` §16. **Ember Heart:** at the start of each of your turns a random enemy gains 3 Burn, and you start each fight with 2 Burn (about 19, less about 4.5, so 15; it spreads thinner on a crowd, and reaches about 40 in a long Warden fight, as Burn does not shrink with the tier). **Blood Heart:** 3 Might at the start of each fight, lose 2 HP at the start of each of your turns (30 - 15 = 15). **Warded Heart:** Shielded and 7 Plating, Brittle each turn, which cuts Plating's Barrier too (12 + 19 - 12 = 19, was 16 Barrier and Shielded). **Thorned Heart:** unchanged (4 Thorns 30 + 2 Might 20 - Exposed 27 = 23). **Glutton's Heart:** still 1 more card (about 20 a fight), now 20 less Max HP (15 before; Max HP is paid once, not a fight), as the strongest left, and more so across 45 floors. A run holding a Heart keeps its id and gets the new effect.

517. **Thresholds and Waymarks fit the run, and pay more from Tier IV (Robert, 2026-11-01: "we need thresholds and waymarks to be improved. for example a player in tier 4 might have no more strikes or defends to remove, so we shouldnt offer those anymore... some options that only apply once a player as ascended... deserves increased rewards. even things like improved versions of the exisitng options would be ok, like max hp or more shards. players that far probably dont need as many offerings for common cards either") ->** **Fit:** an option is offered only if it can do all it says: enough plain Strikes, Defends or basics to remove or transform, room above the deck's smallest size, enough cards to upgrade, room on the belt, a card of that kind left (`hasRoom`). **Ascended:** Ascendancies are not built yet (`13_PROPOSALS.md`), so "ascended" for now means Tier IV or deeper, the tier reached by beating Tier III's Warden (`ASCENDED_TIER`). From there the rest heals 45% (was 30%); new blessings: Rift-forged (+20 max HP), A Warden's Hoard (350 + 50 per act shards), Masterwork (4 upgrades), A Rare Find, An Ancestor's Gift, Twin Trinkets (2 Boons), Ironblood (+12 max HP, 2 upgrades); new bargains: The Ascendant's Due (12 HP, an upgraded rare), The Rift's Bounty (-8 max HP, 600 + 60 per act shards), A Crown of Blood (15 HP, 2 Boons, 2 upgrades). The blessings these replace, those offering common cards, and the bargains a free blessing there beats (Blood Price, Blood for Steel, Call on the Ancestors, Sell the Silver, The Rift Is Hungry, Heavy Purse, A Haunted Gift) stop appearing there. Fight rewards' rarity is untouched: this is only the Threshold and Waymarks.

518. **Grave Chill curses the killer (Robert, 2026-11-01: "update necromancer's thralls to apply fate only to the attacker that killed them instead of all enemies"; then "the sacrifices for necro should apply fate to a random enemy if there was no attacker") ->** An ally an enemy kills curses that enemy with 2 Fate. An ally with no killer standing (a sacrifice, or a killer that has already fallen) curses a random enemy, as all deaths used to. A consumed card still curses a random enemy with 3. A new selector, `attackerOrRandom`, says so; `attacker` keeps its rule that it does nothing with no attacker.

519. **Ascendancies (Robert, 2026-11-01: "ascendencies are a choice after tier 3 boss, thats their reward instead of the other normal tier rewards. they can be brought into arena... an ascendency choice should be like path of exile where the class name actually changes from Knight to Paladin or something like that. If we need to rename the assassin to Rogue to make this work then so be it") ->** Beating Tier III's last Warden offers one of 3, in place of the Warden's Gift, a Heart and the Boon that Warden may leave; the fight's shards, the Warden's Max HP and heal are still paid. The class name changes everywhere it is shown, a new trait joins the base one, and it lasts for the run (`RunState.ascendancy`). **Not a per-character unlock:** a new run starts unascended, so the proposal's unlock is dropped for what Robert said here. **It travels:** a banked deck, an Arena entry and a ghost all carry it (`Entry.ascendancy`, new `ascendancy` columns on the server's decks and ghosts); the engine takes it on `HeroSetup` and reads the merged hero from `db.ascended`. An id that is not the hero's own is ignored, so an old or forged one plays as the base class. A run saved past Tier III without one is offered it at its next last Warden. **The Rogue:** the class shown as Assassin is now the Rogue, so Assassin can be what one becomes; its id stays `assassin` so saves, decks and ghosts need no change (ids are otherwise class names, DECISIONS #190, #371). **The 30 (`packages/content/src/v2/ascendancies.ts`):** Paladin, Marshal, Blood Knight; Foreman, Golemancer, Overclocker; Beastmaster, Reaver, Predator; Trapmaster, Cardsharp, Seer; Assassin, Bladedancer, Bloodletter; Totemist, Spiritwalker, Pyromancer; Sage, Zealot, Sunwalker; Blademaster, Archmage, Shatterer; Brewmaster, Gunslinger, Deathwish; Bonelord, Fateweaver, Soulreaper. Robert has not finished thinking these through, so the effects are a first pass, built only from generic ops and statuses so that changing one is a line of data; none has been priced with the costing guide.

520. **Past Tier X the climb doubles (Robert, 2026-11-01: "i dont want players to really be able to reach tier 20, that should only really be possible if they have a complely optimized deck and have foudn something completely broken. my goal is for players to really die somewhere before that point and that any tier above 10 is extremly risky if they want to keep the character") ->** Enemy HP and damage rise twice as fast per tier from Tier XI (`STEEP_FROM`): Tier X stays 270% / 198%, Tier XX goes from 470% / 318% to 670% / 438%. Tiers I to X do not change. A collapse already takes the run's banked deck away (#231), which is what makes it a risk to the character. Not simulated; to be judged in play.

521. **The Knight's foundation (Robert, 2026-11-01: "i feel like the knights mana reserves isnt really that viable, at least not in the first act"; options A and B of the proposal were recommended and he went ahead) ->** Start each fight with 2 Reserves (about 7 points a fight), and at the end of your turn gain 1 Barrier per Reserve held (up to 3.6 a turn), so holding Mana back is never a dead turn and the trait works from the first fight. Tell Robert: this one was my reading of "go forward", so it is the first to undo if he disagrees.

522. **The animation and effect sheets are in, with the motion they were waiting for (Robert, 2026-11-01: "the sheets for animation-prompts.md are generated and ready", "process them and push when its all done") ->** The 15 sheets in `assets-src/fx` (image_gen in ChatGPT; prompts and hashes kept in `docs/art-provenance`) are cut by `tools/pack-icons.ps1`: 59 pictures into `public/art/icons` (potions, intents, map stops, crests, the Ascend banner) and 36 six-frame strips into `public/art/fx`. Effects are plain elements on one layer over the page, moved with the Web Animations API (`fight/fxPlayer.ts`), not Pixi sprites: positions come from the same `stageToHost` as the floating numbers, the piles and the hand are already page elements, and the map and the Ascend moment use the same player. Which effect a moment plays is pure and tested (`fight/fxMoments.ts`). Nothing awaits an effect; the fight's own beats are unchanged, and a picture that fails to load shows nothing and leaves the flash and the number. Under reduced motion or reduced effects, a strip shows its fullest frame for a moment and flights are skipped. **Calls made:** a frame keeps its grid position and is never trimmed, as a tight crop would make it jump; effect frames have 40 px of room round the cell, so a big burst is not clipped; a piece far larger than a cell is several frames run together and is split on the grid (this rescued `fx-units`); `fx-curses` and `fx-units` key out magenta while keeping pixels that lean blue, or their violet went indigo. A cleared map stop shows the checked stone, not its kind. An attack intent is the huge blade from `BIG_INTENT` total damage (20) and several small blades when it has several hits; a blow is heavy from 10 HP lost and the biggest kind from 25. The drawn pictures stay as what shows if a file fails to load. Not used yet, kept on the sheets: the empty flasks, the Trap, Charge, Flee and Sleep intents, the chest and door markers, and the spare crests. Not built: the Mana gem has a bump as it is paid from, not a liquid draining, as the gem is a number, not a level.

523. **Shatter grows by 10 again, with less burst on two rares (Robert, 2026-11-01: "i want the fracture thresholds to be similar to mounting fury damage increases, in terms of a damage per mana spent"; then "lets make that change for shatter") ->** The k-th Shatter deals 10 + 10k: 20, 30, 40... 110 at 100, 650 for the first ten (was 15 + 5k, 425). This replaces the step in #503. **The numbers, by hand:** 10 Fracture costs 2 Mana at the usual 5 a Mana, which is two Mounting Fury plays, and a pair of Fury plays deals 20 more than the pair before, so an exact match is -5 + 20k at 8 Spell Power. That was not taken: Fury has to be drawn each time, while every Fracture card feeds the curve. Over a six-turn fight 11 Mana on Fracture deals about 22 a Mana at 8 Spell Power (was 17; the exact match 29), between a deck with 2 Furies, 5 plays (19) and one with 3, 7 plays (24). So this is half of Fury's growth per Mana and about the same damage per Mana in a fight. Fracture is 3.1 a point in the guide (4.1 to ALL). **The burst check:** #503's scripted lines, through the engine on the same 540 HP target that plays nothing, with the exact cards handed over each turn: with the new curve alone, 3 Rupture+ and 1 Rupture+ with Breaking Point+ both killed on turn 4, against Robert's 5+ turns. **Rupture Script+ 11** (was 13) and **Breaking Point 4, upgrade 6** (was 6, 9) put every line back on turn 5: 3 Rupture+ 75 HP left after turn 4, 1 Rupture+ 74, no Breaking Point 64, commons only 65. Either cut alone did not. Rupture Script stays 10, so its upgrade is only +1. Stronger in the campaign too. RULES_VERSION goes up at the next deploy.

524. **Elites grow with the tier (Robert, 2026-11-01: "increased chances for elite fights in campaign the further players get in the tiers. it should start increasing from tier 2 and scaling up. tier 20 should be any fight is an elite, not a normal") ->** A plain fight is an Elite instead with chance (tier - 1) / 19: 5% at Tier 2, 21% at 5, 47% at 10, 74% at 15, 100% from 20. Numbers Claude's, as he gave only the two ends: linear is the simplest, and a rule anyone can read. It is rolled after the map's other limits and goes past them (at most 6 an act, from floor 3, never two in a row), or Tier 20 could not be all Elites; the base odds and those limits still decide the rest, so Tier I maps and replays are unchanged and use no extra rolls. Ambushes and fights an event starts follow the same roll; an event fight still pays the spoils it promised, with no second Boon. A run in progress keeps its current map. **Rewards are unchanged:** each one pays as an Elite does (a Boon, 36 shards against 15, a 75% potion chance against 40, 10% Max HP healed) and costs 2 Resolve if lost. That makes deep tiers much richer as well as harder, and is his to judge.

525. **Immovable Oath+ Holds (Robert, 2026-11-01: "immovable oath upgrade should be adds hold instead of just 3 barrier. the 3 barrier upgrade is not befitting of a rare card upgrade") ->** It stays 30 Barrier for 2 Mana with Consume, and gains Hold. The guide's +2-3 for Hold on a big card you want to time is about what the 3 Barrier was on paper (3.6); the worth here is keeping 30 Barrier for the turn it is needed.

526. **Elite rewards fade, and Tier 20 is all Wardens (Robert, 2026-11-01: "lets just get rid of the elite healing after tier 10, and the rewards they give should be dimishing except for card rarity chance. id also like to make tier 20 all wardens and tier 19 the all elites tier") ->** Replaces #524's top end and its "rewards are unchanged". **Tiers:** a plain fight is an Elite with chance (tier - 1) / 18: 6% at Tier 2, 22% at 5, 50% at 10, 78% at 15, 100% at 19. At Tier 20 every fight stop, ambush and event fight is a Warden's fight. **Healing:** Elites heal 10% of Max HP through Tier 10, nothing from Tier 11. **Rewards fade by tier, not by how many Elites an act has had** (he did not say which; a tier rule can be read on the Learn page, and needs no count kept): evenly from Tier I to Tier 19, shards 36 to 15 and potion chance 75% to 40% (a plain fight's, so an Elite never pays less than one, Omens aside), Boon chance 100% to 10%. The floor is Claude's number. Tier 10 is 26 shards, 58%, 55%. Card rewards keep an Elite's rarity chance at every tier, as he asked. Tier I rolls are unchanged. **A Warden's fight at Tier 20 is an Elite in everything but its enemies:** `kind` stays `elite`, because a `warden` stop ends the act and pays the Gift, Max HP, 30% heal and a Resolve. So it pays the Tier 19 rewards, costs 2 Resolve if lost (a true Warden costs 1), shows as an Elite on the map until it is reached, and does not get the Warden's Fury Omen. The Omen that adds a weakest enemy to an Elite skips them, or each would bring a second Warden. Act 3 draws on the Wardens of its realms and the far realms, never Vorthax, who stays the finale.

527. **Phases and Elites harden at Tiers 5, 10 and 15, and ghost Wardens take the Arena's HP from Tier 10 (Robert, 2026-11-01: "we need fights that have phases to be tougher at tier 5 10 and 15, each threahold tougher than the previous"; "elites should get even tougher at tiers 5 10 and 15 as well"; "ghost decks at tier 10 shoudl get the same hp bump as arena too") ->** Three Omens, tuned in `OMEN_TUNING`, each adding to the last: every phase rises with 5% more of the unit's HP as Barrier and 1 more Might (15% and 3 at Tier 15), on every kind of phase, and Elites have 10% more HP and deal 5% more (30% and 15% at Tier 15). Numbers Claude's, as he gave only the tiers. **What that does to an Elite with the Omens' two phases, by hand:** total HP plus Barrier at Tier 15, against the same tier without this: 1 + 0.33 + 0.24 = 1.57x the tier's HP becomes 1.3 x (1 + 0.48 + 0.39) = 2.43x, about 55% more to chew through, with 15% more damage and 3 more Might. Tier 5 and 10 are about 1.1 x (1 + 0.28) = 1.41x (was 1.23x) and 1.2 x (1 + 0.38) = 1.66x (was 1.28x). **A Warden on an Elite stop does not get the Elite bump** (it still gets the harder phases): with it, a mid-act Warden at Tier 20 had 30% more HP than the act's own Warden and more than Vorthax, so the finale was the softest fight of the act. Those stops also draw on the far realms' Wardens in every act, and avoid the act's own Warden while they can, as the first act otherwise had 2 different Wardens and about half its stops were its boss. The fight's stakes banner shows there too, as losing costs 2 Resolve. **Ghosts:** from Tier 10 a ghost Warden is its Riftwalker's HP x 6 (`ARENA_HP_MULT`; a test in `@sr/pvp` keeps them equal, as campaign cannot import pvp) at Tier 10 act 1, 480 for an 80 HP Riftwalker (was 374), and rises as the tier table does from there: 1191 at Tier 20 act 1 (was 929). Its damage is unchanged.

528. **Six Wardens get a rule of their own, from Tier I (Robert, 2026-11-01: "i like how each boss in sts2 has unque mechanics, we shoudl work some of those into our wardens"; "yea i like the idea of doing those 6 first but i don twan tot lose track of doing the others afterwards. and yea a wardens rule should apply from tier 1") ->** Original names and numbers; the StS2 bosses are where the shape of the idea came from. The other five are tracked in `13_PROPOSALS.md` #7. **Kron** (190 HP, was 270): a Foundry Hatch (30 HP, taunt, 4 damage) stands in front, so aimed attacks must break it; every fourth turn Reopen the Hatch brings it back if it is down and gives him 2 Might. His drones are gone. **The Hollow Benediction** (190, was 230): two Hollow Acolytes (30 each, 2 damage), each giving every enemy here 1 Might a turn. **The Mourning Tide** (190, was 230): Low Tide every fourth turn (third from half HP) makes it Ghostly through your next turn, and it and Grey Wave put a **Beckoning** in your hand (1 Mana, Consume, does nothing; lose 4 HP if held at the end of your turn). **The Applause Below** (190, was 230): **Slippery**, a new status: the next hit deals only 1 and takes a stack off. Every move adds 1, 2 from half HP, and any hit counts, an Attack card's or not; Bleed and Burn do not. **A Shatter gets through and keeps the stacks** (Claude's call): the threshold is crossed once, so a capped Shatter would be gone for good, and this Warden gains stacks every turn. **Seraphine** (225, same): once, at half HP, she spends a turn on The Kneeling, losing all Might and any Steadfast (the Vigilant Omen would stop the Exposed) and gaining 2 Exposed. It takes the Might of the Omen's phase at the same threshold too, and a Stun on that turn skips it. **A phase of an Omen now stops at its percent rounded down, not up** (for every enemy), so a boss at odd Max HP enters its next moves at the same moment. **Mother of Static** (230, same): Power Cut and Signal Jam deal 8 and take a Mana or a drawn card from your next turn. **Pricing, by hand:** the HP cuts pay for the rule. A deck dealing about 22 a turn at Tier I took 270 / 22 = 12 turns on Kron, and with the Hatch up about 40% of the time takes about 190 / (22 x 0.6) + 2 = 16 at worst, less with any AoE. Acolytes add 60 HP to the Benediction, about 3 turns, in which it gains up to 5 Might. Low Tide costs one turn in four, about a third more damage needed: 230 becomes 190 plus the lost turns. Slippery costs about 8-10 damage a stack against one big hit, about 6 against a small one. Its painted picture is a copy of Ghostly's for now. These are first numbers; a long fight is the thing to watch. **Engine:** generic and data-driven: status `slippery`; a cycle's `lead` moves, played once as a phase begins; `summon.ifAbsent`; `foe` on `manaNext` and `drawNext`. `Encounter.rule` is shown as the fight starts. RULES_VERSION goes up at the next deploy.

529. **A run past Tier III with no Ascendancy gets the offer at any Warden (Robert, 2026-11-01: "lets make sure to offer ascendency selection on warden kill if the player doesnt already have an ascendency. this will help players that already have runs past tier 3. it shouldnt happen for any new runs going forward, and i dont want to offer ascendency changes") ->** From Tier IV, killing any act's Warden with no Ascendancy offers the choice in place of that Warden's Gift (#519 waited for the last Warden of a tier). The Tier III rule is unchanged: only its last Warden. So a new run is never offered early: the Tier III offer has no way past it, so no new run reaches Tier IV without one. An Ascendancy cannot be changed: the offer needs none held, and `chooseAscendancy` refuses one who has it. A Warden met at a Tier 20 Elite stop is not a Warden kill for this.

530. **The reel: picking cards is taking the prize, and two more prizes (Robert, 2026-11-01: "i chose to remove two cards and i was still able to spin again. players should not be able to spin again after accepting their reward. also it closed the removal window after i selected one... id like to add 2 more options for the wheel if you can think of some") ->** The rules already ended the reel on a taken prize; choosing cards was not taking it. A separate "Remove 2 cards" button had to be pressed afterwards, with Spin again beside it, and the window closed after each card. Now the window stays open while up to two are chosen, its own button removes them and takes the prize, and cancelling lets the choice go, so nothing is half chosen beside Spin again. Tests pin that no spin follows a taken prize, at an event and at a Gambler's Shrine. **New prizes, so 15 on the reel, each about 100-150 shards' worth like the others:** **+1 Resolve** (a purse if yours is full), and **The Twin**, copy a non-basic card of your choice that the deck rules have room for (a purse if none). Each prize is now a little less likely: 1 in 15 on a first spin. A reel already built in a save keeps its 13 prizes.

531. **No more acts: 20 Rift Tiers (Robert, 2026-11-01: "lets do away with acts altogther. i just want 20 rift tiers now, each one feeling noticeably harder than the last. still ascendency on tier 3 completion, and all the other scaling stuff... it just felt really easy on tier one lasting as long as it does currently to build a whole optimzed deck... id like tier 1 to be 10 floors, and each tier increasing by 2 floors... if you dont want kron on tier 1 then move him to a different tier idc") ->** Replaces #512's three acts of 15 floors. A tier is one map: `floorsOf(tier)` = 8 + 2 x tier floors, then the Warden (Tier I 11 in all, Tier XX 49). A run now reaches Tier III after 24 floors (it was 90), and walks 600 in all (it was 900). **Difficulty:** each tier uses the tier table's Act 1 column: HP +20 and damage +12 a tier, twice that past Tier X, and everything by tier in #524-#527 stands. **Claude's calls: Kron** waits for Tier III (`Encounter.fromTier`), as the Hatch is harsh on a deck with 5-6 new cards. **Vorthax** is Tier XX's Warden only, in the Rift, and never replaced by a ghost, as the climb ends with him; every other tier walks a realm, never the same one twice running. **No full heal between tiers:** going on used to heal fully and give a Resolve on top of the Warden's, once in three Wardens; now every Warden leads to a tier, that would be a full rest every 11 floors, against what he asked for. The Warden's 30% heal and 1 Resolve stay, and the Waymark's rest. **Enemy pools** have no acts to follow, so `depthOf(tier)` opens them: the trios from Tier III, the far realms from Tier VI. **Merchants** are per stretch of 7 floors (one or two), and Elites by the map's own roll at most 3 for 7 floors, so longer maps keep the same density. **A Warden sometimes leaves a Boon** (40%, as Vorthax did once a tier). **Saves:** a run between acts has cleared its tier; one inside an act finishes that map, which clears it. The boards keep their numbers: 3 is a cleared tier, 0 a tier in progress, so old standings stay in order and the server needs no change. `stage` stays on the run as 1, unused. Priced by hand, no sim: the first tiers are the thing to play, as a Warden at Tier I now meets a much thinner deck. RULES_VERSION 62.

532. **Tier I aims at 3 Elites (Robert, 2026-11-02: "as for elite fights on tier one, id like to target 3 and have the chances increase from there... more often 3 than 1 on tier 1") ->** `NODE_ODDS.eliteMore` (the chance of each Elite after a map's first) 12 -> 20; the first stays 22, the cap stays 3 for 7 floors (5 on a Tier I map). Measured over 2,000 Tier I maps: Elites on the map 1: 20% -> 8%, 2: 42% -> 30%, 3: 28% -> 40%, 4: 8% -> 20%; the most on one path 2 or 3 most of the time (1 in 17%, was 32%). Every deeper tier gains the same small step before `eliteFightChance` turns its plain fights, so the rise from Tier I is kept (Tier II most often 4-5 on the map). Map counts, not a balance sim.

533. **Primed wears off by half; the Monk's Fury exit pays once a turn; Thousand Palms 5 x4 (Robert, 2026-11-02: a friend's Monk hit for 600, 300 and 200, the 200 from a 0-cost card; "i dont mind big damage but i want all of the classes to have similar potential damage and it should all take setup and sholdnt be just a turn one one shot thing"; then "primed wears of 50% between turns. inner fire gives primed only the first time you leave fury, just like serene with the mana, we might want to increas the primed number... and thousand palms goes to the numbers you just mentioned") ->** **Why:** Primed never wore off and went on every hit before Radiant x3 and Exposed x1.5; Inner Fire gave 2 on every Fury exit and Iron Body 3 on every entry, so a Monk could bank 20-30 a turn behind Barrier: 40 banked made Quick Palms (0) about 198 in one hit, 130 about 600, and Thousand Palms 7 times that. **Primed:** a hero's or companion's loses half, rounded up, as its turn ends (`decay`); an enemy's comes only from the Omen of the Hunted and waits for its first hit, so it is kept. Gaining g a turn now settles at about g carried in, never more. It touches every class with Primed (Spellblade, Hunter, Engineer, Drunkard, the Knight's Reserves Ascendancy), and only Primed left unspent. **Inner Fire:** 3 Primed the first time each turn he leaves Fury (was 2 every time): a Fury deck leaves once a turn and gains 1; a Flow deck that left two or three times loses 1-3. Claude's number, as Robert asked for one. **Thousand Palms:** 5 damage 4 times (was 3 x7): 20 base for 21, Primed counted 4 times. With 10 Primed, Radiant and Exposed it deals (5 + 10) x 3 x 1.5 x 4 = 268 (unbounded before). **Left as it is, flagged:** Iron Body still gives 3 Primed on every Fury entry, so two of them and several Fury loops in one Radiant turn can still pass 300 (15 Primed: Thousand Palms 360). Priced by hand, no sim.

534. **Iron Body once a turn; a Trap snaps its jaws instead of darkening the screen (Robert, 2026-11-02: "lets update iron body to be once a turn limit. also for the trickster, i dont like the trap animation that greys out the screen... i dont mind the text to say it was triggered, id almost prefere an animation of the trap being springed. and i think the full trick description text says \"it\" instead of \"in\" somewhere") ->** **Iron Body** answers the first time each turn he enters Fury (#533 flagged it: two of them and a few Fury loops still made 15 Primed in a Radiant turn). Once a turn at 2 HP for 3 Primed was about 1 point a turn, far under a 1-cost uncommon Aura (about 15 over four turns), so **Claude's numbers:** lose 1 HP, gain 4 Primed (about 5.2 - 1.5 = 3.7 a turn, 15 over four), and 5 upgraded. **The Trap springing:** `dimScreen` is gone; `trapJaws` (fxPlayer.ts) draws two steel jaws that swing in open and snap shut, teeth between teeth, on the first unit of the other side that the Trap's effects touch (its damage, its status, or the attacker a Prevent stops), with a jolt; none if it touches no one there. Drawn in code like the other placeholders, so a painted strip can take its place. The card still flips up, and the banner reads "Trap sprung: <name>". **The text:** the trait said "2 more for every Full Trick before it this fight", which reads like a typo; it is now "plus 2 for every Full Trick earlier in the fight". Her how-to still said 3 to every enemy and 3 more a Full Trick (from before #404 and #411); it now says a random enemy and 2 more. **Also checked (no change):** Event stops are fights about 1 in 6 at every tier, as before (15% an ambush, a few events whose every choice is a fight); a Trap's Exposed lands on the enemy's turn and lasts through the player's next turn, while its own damage is no attack, so Exposed never adds to it; a Trap's Faltering springs before the hit and weakens it, then wears off as that enemy's turn ends.

535. **Wild cards fill one missing suit; attack boosts are ♠ and cracking an enemy ♣ (Robert, 2026-11-02: "why is echoing blow treated as Cards and Mana? and i played wild card, why did that auto-tirgger all of the suits?"; then "wild card doesnt show any of that text for me, all i see is draw 1 card consume and hold on it. i also like hte idea of it filling a missing suit") ->** **Wild:** a Wild card (Wild Card, The Joker) shows the first suit missing this turn, in the order ♠ ♥ ♦ ♣ (`suitShown`, used by play, the card preview and the pips), not all four: a 0-cost card made a Full Trick alone, and it was priced (#387) as filling one suit, about 3 points. Order, not a choice: a choice would be a window on a 0-cost card, and the order is on the card ("the first suit you are missing this turn, in the order ♠ ♥ ♦ ♣"), so it is best played last; played first it lights ♠, which any Attack would (and Flourish, which reads ♥, gains nothing from it). **Its text:** nothing on the card said it was Wild (the suit lives in `CardDef.suit`, which the text never read); `cardText` now starts with "Wild" and the order it fills in. **The rule (suits.ts):** a status on yourself that makes your attacks better (Twinned, Edge, Honed, Fanned, Cruelty, Tracking, Empowered, Blade Echo, Marrow, Reaping, Lethality, Deftness, Keen, Ruinous, with Might and Primed) is ♠; Fracture and Strip on an enemy, and Carry the Fault, are ♣; a Shatter blast ♠. They all fell through to ♦ before (Echoing Blow, Second Act, the Spellblade's Fracture cards). Spell Power, totems, Spirit and other resources stay ♦ by the rule, Claude's call. Cards still show no suit of their own except through the pips while aimed; flagged. **The next Full Trick's damage** (Robert, same day: "we also should show the number of damage that full trick will do"): the side view carries `fullTricks` beside `suitsTurn`, and the Suits panel shows "Trick N" after the pips and "Your next Full Trick deals N damage" in its tip: the trait's damage plus its per-Full-Trick growth (3, 5, 7...). It is no attack, so nothing else changes it.

536. **Every tier one floor longer (Robert, 2026-11-02: "lets extend each tier by one floor so by tier 20 we are at an even 50 floors") ->** `floorsOf(tier)` = 9 + 2 x tier (was 8 + 2 x tier): Tier I is 11 floors and its Warden, 12 in all (was 11); Tier XX 49 and Vorthax, 50 (was 49). A whole climb is 620 floors (was 600), and Tier III is reached after 26 (was 24). Merchants and Elites follow the map's length as before. A map already made keeps its floors; the next tier's is the new length. **Enemy HP climbs faster (Robert, same day: "i want enemy hp scaling a little faster per tier"; "id like tier 20 to be 1000% hp increase"):** read as the table's number, 1000% of the written HP (it was 670%; if he meant 1100% the steep step is 74). `HP_PER_TIER` 20 -> 30, and a tier past X adds `HP_STEEP` 64 (was twice the step, 40): 90 + 9 x 30 + 10 x 64 = 1000. Tier I stays 90%, Tier X is 360% (was 270), Tier XV 680% (was 470). Damage keeps its old climb (twice the step past X). Ghost Wardens at Arena HP scale from the table, so they follow. RULES_VERSION goes up with the release.

537. **Ascendancies shown where a Riftwalker is chosen; a fixed run header on a phone; the Play line (Robert, 2026-11-02: "the how to play page for riftwalkers needs to have their ascendencies on there. and we should include some ui display for the ascendency names somewhere on the homescreen when toggling between the characters. anywhere where we have that selection UI with the icons and the character model displays. and the sanctum can just say the ascendency names instead of the mechanics of each character on their tiles"; "the description for each character on the sanctum page should include the description of each ascendency"; "mobile should have the header area fixed at the top though so you can see it while scrolling"; "the Play button subtext should be \"Start a new campaign run as the [CLASS_NAME], ascend through 20 rift tiers and take down the final boss, Vorthax\"") ->** **HeroShowcase** (the home page and the Sandbox, the two pickers with the icon row and the figure) lists the three Ascendancies under the trait, each with its crest, its name, and its effect in a tip. **The Sanctum** (CharacterPicker): a tile names the three Ascendancies where it named three mechanics; the description below gains an Ascendancies list, crest, name and effect, after the how-to (the mechanics stay there as chips). **How to play, Riftwalkers:** each one ends with its Ascendancies (the run tab keeps the full list). **A phone or a touch screen** (max-width 52rem, or a coarse pointer): `.runbar` is sticky at the top, so the header stays while a long map scrolls; the map itself scrolls as it did (Robert: scrolling is fine, as in Slay the Spire 2). **Play** says "...ascend through 20 Rift Tiers and take down the final boss, Vorthax." with "Rift Tiers" capitalised as the game writes it.

538. **The Gambler's Shrine gives one thing a visit; ghost Wardens start from their run's Max HP (Robert, 2026-11-02: "we should also make sure the gamblers shrine restricts players to choosing only one option while they are there. and ghost decks from players should benefit from inreases to max hp that they took in the campaign") ->** **The shrine:** `GAMBLER_BLESSINGS` 2 -> 1, and the Brass Reel counts as the one choice: once spun, a blessing is refused ("take its prize, or walk away"), and taking its prize closes the shrine (it stayed open for the blessings). Walking away is always there. The Learn page and the shrine's own words say so. **Ghosts:** a saved deck carries `maxHp`, the run's Max HP when it was saved (Boons, Wardens, the Ledger and events all in it): `VaultDeck`, `Entry` (checked as a whole number 1-999, `cleanMaxHp`), the server's ghost list and Vault (a `max_hp` column each, added the way `ascendancy` was), and the ghost a campaign Warden is sent. `ghostBossHp` starts from it in place of the class's own Max HP, so a run that took +30 Max HP leaves a ghost about 40% tougher on a Knight. **Claude's call, flagged:** only the campaign's ghost Wardens use it. The Arena gives every hero 6x their class's own HP and a player's own deck never brings its campaign Max HP there, so an Arena ghost does not either (it would be stronger than the same deck played by its owner). Decks saved before this have no `maxHp` and play as before until saved again; a deck is saved after every stop of its run, so a run still going catches up at once. The server change ships with the site (one Worker).

539. **The leaderboards show Ascendancies; the Campaign board first (Robert, 2026-11-02: "Let's also have the leaderboards show what ascendancy the player chose. And the campaign leaderboard should swap positions in order with the arena leaderboard. I want people to see the campaign leaderboard first when landing on that page.") ->** **Order:** Campaign, Arena, Ghosts, and the page opens on Campaign (it opened on the Arena's season ranks). **Ascendancies:** the run report carries the run's Ascendancy; the server keeps it on the run (`runs.ascendancy`), recorded the first time it is told even with no new progress (it is taken after the Tier III Warden, before the next stage counts). An Arena result keeps each deck's (`pvp.winner_asc`, `loser_asc`, from the entries that played), and the ghost board takes the ghost's from its deck. Each is checked against the Riftwalker (`ascendancyOf`) on the way in and out. On the boards a Riftwalker with an Ascendancy shows its crest and "Paladin (Knight)"; on the Arena and ghost-hunter boards one Riftwalker played with and without one is two entries, each with its own record. The season ranks board keeps the class only (its ratings are per player, not per deck). Rows written before this have none until the run moves on or the deck plays again. The server change ships with the site.

540. **Phone fixes: long press reads a tip, Consumed and potions in their places, bigger card words (Robert, 2026-11-02: "the mana circle is overlapped by a card pile, either discards or consumed... the potions dont fit in their boxes and they overlap buffs/debuffs. cards need slightly bigger text or need to be slightly bigger... i would like to be able to long hold on elements that act like a hover with a mouse so i can read tooltips on everything") ->** **Long press:** the tooltip layer (ui/Tooltip.tsx) shows a tip after 450 ms of a finger resting on anything with `data-tip` (moving 10 px cancels it); the tip stays when the finger lifts, the click that ends the press is swallowed (so a potion is not drunk, a pile not opened), and the next touch anywhere closes it. An element whose own tip shows on hover or focus (an enemy's intent) carries `data-press-focus` and is focused instead, and blurred by the next touch elsewhere. The hand's cards keep their own long press (lift to read). On a coarse pointer a tipped element cannot be selected or open the browser's menu. A quick tap is unchanged, and so are a mouse and a pen (a pen's press and hold is its right click). A browser that sees the long press first (Chrome, at about 400 ms) shows the tip then, and a press that ends with no click leaves nothing waiting to eat the next tap. **Consumed:** upright, a later `right: auto` had dropped the pile at the left edge, over Mana; it stands left of the discard pile again. On a phone on its side it stood on End turn; it now stands beside the discard pile and End turn moves one pile further in while there is one (over the discard pile it would cover the battle log and the nearest enemy). On a 320 px phone End turn is set smaller, to fit between the piles. **Potions:** the painted bottle was a fixed 42 px, bigger than the upright belt's 41 px slots, so it spilled over the statuses above; it is fitted to its slot now. **Cards:** upright, the hand cannot grow (a taller hand band pushes companions into the enemies' plates: `stageLayout.test.ts` fails from 0.34 of the height), so a card under 130 px wide sets its words bigger: short texts 54 -> 66, then 47 -> 52, 41 -> 45 (design px), each checked to still fit its box on all 1,910 card faces; the longest (over 170 letters) keep 36, as a note or a Sharpen added in a fight can lengthen them. Desktop cards under 130 px (the deck report, small reward cards) get the bigger words too. A phone on its side has cards from 27% of the height (was 24%).

541. **Phones: a small run header, two shop cards a row, no pens; no broken pictures (Robert, 2026-11-02: "the merchant on mobile needs to display content better. like one card per row seems wrong... the mobile map should have a smaller header area thats fixed... restrict boons UI to a scrollable/slideable window. and draw doesnt work on the map, so just remove it for mobile"; "i saw some image on the map at some poitn that was showing a broken image... make sure we dont have any broken images") ->** **Header** (under 40rem): 234 px tall on a 390 x 844 phone with 12 Boons, now 91: the class and where the run is on one line, the buttons at 0.68rem, and the stats in one row that slides sideways (no wrapping), HP, shards and Resolve first, then potions, Boons, the Heart and the Omens, the far edge faded to show it goes on; potions in it fit their circles. **Merchant:** cards two to a row, their width from the screen (`useShopCardWidth`: (width - 72) / 2, 120-210 px), the item as wide as its card, smaller buttons. **Pens:** the map's drawing bar is hidden on a coarse pointer (any touch screen, tablets too), where drawing did not work. **Pictures:** none was missing; `art/pictureFiles.test.ts` now checks every painted icon, potion, crest, intent, map stop, effect and every file the art manifests list exists, spelled exactly (the live sites are case-sensitive) and committed. The two raw pictures with no fallback, the map's "you are here" marker and the Store and Cosmetics look tiles (an empty address before a skin's art arrives), now show nothing rather than a broken-image mark if they fail. What Robert saw was most likely the marker during a deploy or a tile with no picture yet; flagged in case it was something else.

542. **The Ascendancy pass, the Elementalist, Etching Edge (Robert, 2026-11-02: "id like to make these ascendency changes you suggested and i want to see the updated chart afterwards. i also was hoping to have an elementalist ascendency as opposed to a pyromancer. i also feel like etching edge needs to be 1 per 2 instead of 2 per 4, i dont get why we made it harder to use, and it feels hard to get it to apply more fracture") ->** From a hand-priced audit of all 30 (early Tier IV-VI, late Tier XV-XX, where flat numbers are worth about a quarter). **Blood Knight:** `hurt` fired once a turn, and the enemy's turn is a turn, so 1.5-2 Might a round (~19 a turn late); now once a round (a new trigger option, `round`: not again until its side's next turn begins; `SideState.turnBegan`). **Deathwish:** 1.5% a point missing, capped at +100% (`clutch.max`); it reached +180% at 10% HP, which Hangover never takes. **Fateweaver:** 5% of a random enemy's max HP as Fate (`per(maxHp, 0.05, each)`), ~4 a turn at Tier V, ~17 at Tier XX; it was 3. **Gunslinger:** +3 Precision at the start, and +2 every turn (~5.5 a turn). **Shatterer:** Shatters 15% more (`shatterUp.pct`, `SideState.shatterPct`), ~+26 over a fight's first four and growing; it was +5 flat. **Zealot:** the first Fury entry each turn. **Foreman:** companions' attacks 25% more (`allyPct` op, `SideState.allyPct`, counted in their intents), not +1. **The Elementalist (Robert's, in place of the Pyromancer, which was ~2.5 a turn):** the first Flame card each turn adds 3 Burn to a random enemy, the first Frost 4 Barrier, the first Storm 3 damage to ALL (~4-5 each, ~9 a turn in a deck casting two elements; Claude's numbers). A saved run, deck or ghost with `shaman:pyromancer` is read and stored as the Elementalist (`ascendancyOf` follows a rename map), and the engine plays the old id as the Elementalist too (`ascendedHeroes` keeps it as another name), for a ghost already settled in a saved run or an old server row. It reads each card's own element tag (Flame, Frost or Storm), which the Shaman's cards carry since #430; there is no Trinity and no Prism card in the game. Companion intents show the Foreman's 25% on the base and rounded, as it lands. Its crest is the Pyromancer's until one is painted (prompt in docs/animation-prompts.md). **Etching Edge:** 2 damage + 1 per 2 Spell Power (was 2 per 4: the same rate, moving at every second point), upgraded 5 + 1 per 2 (was 3 + 3 per 4: the same at 8); Fracture 1 + 1 per 4 Spell Power (was per 8). At the priced 8 Spell Power: 6 damage + 3 Fracture (3.1 a point) = 15.3 against a 1-cost common's 10, so ~5 over (it was 12.2): over on purpose, as Robert found Fracture too hard to build. **Flagged:** the Engineer's other two (Golemancer, Overclocker) and the flat ones the audit graded C-D late (Paladin, Predator, Bladedancer, Bloodletter, Soulreaper, Brewmaster) are unchanged. Priced by hand, no sim.

543. **The Sanctum's Ascendancy tiles; a phone's Grimoire (Robert, same day: "id like the sanctum class description window to show the ascendencies in boxes like we do on other pages... tiles with subtext... fit the area equally, and it needs to look good on mobile. also the grimoire looks kinda bad in mobile with all the tabs overlapped and not showing any real data") ->** **Sanctum:** each Ascendancy is a tile in the look of a Waymark choice: crest, name, what it leans into as subtext, and what it gives; three of equal width across a wide screen, one a row on a phone. **Grimoire** (under 520 px, where it was a book of four-card pages with ribbon tabs that crowd): the realms are a row of chips that slides sideways, and every card of the open one is in a list two across that scrolls (110-200 px wide). Picking a card works as before; the wider screens keep the book.

544. **No Ascendancy above B in the late game (Robert, 2026-11-02: "i dont want any of them to be above B+ for late game scaling, the best i will accept is B, can you sort that out") ->** On the audit's scale (late = Tier XV-XX, points a turn), B is about 6-10. Over it after #542: Blood Knight A, Beastmaster A, Sunwalker A, Archmage A, Deathwish A, Assassin A-, Fateweaver A-, Sage B+. **Blood Knight:** still once a round, at most 3 Might a fight (`times: 3`): 3 Might on ~2.5 hits = ~7.5 a turn once reached (was ~10 and rising). **Beastmaster:** shares 3/4 of the hero's Might (was all; the class shares half): ~+1/4 Might a companion hit, ~10 late. **Assassin:** Lethality 10% (was 25%), Edge 2 kept: ~8 late. **Sunwalker:** +1 Fervor the first stance each turn (`once`), not every stance: ~1 Fervor a turn, ~5-7 late. **Archmage:** +3 Spell Power and the first Attack each turn 20% more (`lethality 20`, in place of Ruinous 1 a point spent, which grew with what a late deck spends): ~8 late. **Deathwish:** 1.25% a point missing (was 1.5%), at most +100%, never below the class's own 1% until it is past 100%: about +12% over the class at half HP, ~10 late. **Fateweaver:** 2 Fate + 2% of the enemy's max HP (was 5%): ~4 early, ~12 Fate (~8 a turn) at Tier XX. **Sage:** unchanged (+1 Mana on the first Serene exit, ~5 a turn at any tier, as Cardsharp's B); its B+ was a notch too high on the grade, not a stronger effect. Early grades fall a little with them (Blood Knight B+, Beastmaster B, Assassin A-, Sunwalker B, Archmage A-, Deathwish B+, Fateweaver C+). Priced by hand, no sim.

545. **Every Ascendancy's best side about B (Robert, 2026-11-02: "lets buff some of the weaker ones to around B. id prefer to see each of these have their best aspect be about a B grade so that none of them are soo overpowered, they shoudl all be interesting nice picks in the right decks"; then "they should be a B in either waht they provide early or B in what they provide late. im even ok with slightly mor epower early to make the early ones interesting picks vs the late game ones") ->** On the audit's scale (early B ~5-6 points a turn, late B ~6-9), each one's better side is B, an early pick up to B+. **Down (early):** Paladin keeps 60% of last turn's Barrier through the turn change (a trait field, `keepBarrier`; Fortified from a card still keeps 75%, and the larger share wins), was always Fortified (A); Marshal Max Reserves 4 (was 5, A-); Trapmaster 2 Barrier a Trap (was 3, A); Totemist 1 Spirit and 8 Barrier at the start (was 2 Spirit, A); Blademaster Keen 1 and the first Sword each turn draws 1 (was Keen 2, A-); Elementalist Frost 3 Barrier and Storm 2 to ALL (was 4 and 3: two elements a turn made ~9). **Up (to B):** Golemancer +3 max HP a turn (was 2); Reaver also 2 Primed (draw 1 and 2 Primed); Predator 2 Primed a mid-turn draw (was 1, before Primed lost half a turn in #533); Spiritwalker 4 Barrier (was 2); Brewmaster also 1 Might for the turn; Soulreaper 4 Barrier a Consume (was 2). **Kept at B+ early, as early picks:** Blood Knight, Zealot, Deathwish. The rest stand at B on one side. Priced by hand, no sim.

546. **The other five Wardens get a rule of their own, each unlike the rest (Robert, 2026-11-02: "lets get those their unique boss fight mechanics and make sure they are different than the rest") ->** Every Warden now asks something different of you: break a wall (Kron), kill the helpers (the Hollow Benediction), hold back on a turn and pay off junk (the Mourning Tide), hit many times (the Applause Below), hold out for the opening (Seraphine), live with less (Mother of Static), and now: **Gorrmaw** (200, was 240), **the Hunger**: Gorge eats every Maw Spawn (24 HP, Bite 7; were 33 and 10) for 15 HP and 2 Might each; with none to eat it starves and is 2 Exposed. Loop: Spawn, Bite, Spawn, Gorge; from half HP Brood (two), Rend, Gorge, Ravenous Bite.
