The question it asks: kill the food before it eats. **The Upside-Down Stag** (210, was 230), **the Turnover**: The Turnover (12) and, from half HP, Upend (8 x2) first swap its Barrier with yours; an enemy's Barrier falls as its turn starts, so in practice it takes yours and you take the blow unguarded. The question: don't raise Barrier into it (its intent shows the blow, and the rule and the move's words say so). **Queen of the Unmade** (210, was 230), **Unmaking**: Unmaking Decree and Shard Rain put 1 Unmaking on you; once your next hand is drawn, its costliest playable card is Consumed for the fight, then it clears. About one card in three turns, then one in two from half HP: four or five over a fight. The question: race her, or keep your best cards cheap and many. **Event Horizon Regent** (210, was 230), **Gravity**: Gravity Well and Debris Storm add 1 Gravity (it stays all fight); at the end of your turn that many of the cards you would discard go back on top of your draw pile, costliest first, so you redraw what you could not play. The question: play your hand out, keep your deck cheap. **Nyxara** (260, was 278), **the Veil**: every move leaves her Veiled; her intent is hidden (the client gets no intent at all) until a hit takes it off, Bleed and Burn are not hits. The question: hit her first, then decide how to guard. **Changed from the rough ideas in 13_PROPOSALS #7** (Claude's call, so none repeats another Warden): the Stag's turn of healing what you deal was Low Tide again; the Queen's cost rise was Mother of Static's Mana tax again; Nyxara's Trap waited on enemy Traps, never built. **Pricing, by hand:** the HP cuts pay for the rules. Gorrmaw: two Spawns are 48 HP to kill before each Gorge, or it heals 30 and gains 4 Might; 40 off its HP and 9 off each Spawn leave a deck that kills the food about where it was. The Stag: one blow in four (one in three late) can't be guarded, about 12-16 more damage taken each loop; 20 off. The Queen: a Consumed best card costs about one good card's worth each time (~8-10 points); 20 off. The Regent: Gravity costs about one fresh card a turn per stack once it builds; 20 off. Nyxara: an attack first each turn costs little, so 18 off. **Engine:** generic: op `devour` (unit, heal, might, none), op `swapBarrier`, statuses `veiled`, `gravity`, `unmaking` (the last two are debuffs, so a cleanse or Steadfast answers them). Their pictures are stand-ins (Ghostly's, Orbit's, Fracture's) until icons-07 is painted (prompt in animation-prompts.md). **"Costliest"** is a card's own lasting cost: what a status or this turn's count takes off is not counted, an X card counts as 0, and a card that can't be played is never chosen. A Veiled unit's view shows nothing of its next move, not its last moves or its place in its loop. RULES_VERSION goes up at the next deploy.

547. **Gorrmaw eased; Vorthax rebuilt as a finale only the best decks finish (Robert, 2026-11-02: "lets get gormaw back into acceptable range but i do want the final boss to be a daunting task"; "make sure the finale boss is appropriately difficult with respect to how strong player decks will be. my goal is for players to never see vorthax exept in the rarest of top tier decks and players, but if they get there it should be an incredible battle that, like somethign they havent seen before") ->** **Gorrmaw:** Maw Spawn 18 HP (was 24): killing the food costs 36 every four turns, not 48, which brings it from ~13-20 turns to ~13-15, Kron's range. Gorge heals 6% of its max HP per Spawn (12 at Tier I, about the old 15) rather than a flat 15, which was nothing at deep tiers; `devour` gained `healPct`. **Found on the way:** an enemy's Barrier and healing are flat numbers that the Rift Tier does not scale, so at Tier XX (10x HP, 4.38x damage, about 5x with Warden's Fury) a Warden's "12 Barrier" turn is a free turn. Vorthax's are now shares of its max HP (`scaled(barrier(0), per('maxHp', x, 'each'))`); the other Wardens' are left as they are (a wider question for Robert). **Vorthax** (met only at the end of Tier XX): 360 HP (was 270; 3,600 there), **its four quarters stand in for the Omens' rises** (Second Wind and Third Phase added two more walls of Barrier, ~53% and ~43% of its max HP, and Might; the finale's `omenMods` now takes no rise, the Omens' statuses still come: `omenMods(..., ownRises)`), **its side falls with it** (a new `UnitDef.leader`: no fighting on under its Fate once it is dead), and four quarters, each with Wardens' rules, so the last fight asks every question the run has. **I. The Heart Wakes (100-75%):** it starts behind a **Rift Ward** (40 HP, taunt, Resonant Hum: every enemy +1 Might and 4 damage; Kron's Hatch and the Benediction's Acolytes in one), raised again each loop; Crystal Shards 6x3, Raise the Ward (+4% Barrier), Collapse 26, Refract (6% Barrier, +1 Might). **II. The Hunger and the Veil (75-50%):** every move leaves it Veiled (Nyxara); it opens with The Shattering (12 to all, two Rift Shards) and loops Shard Storm 6x3, Feed on the Shards (Gorrmaw: eats every Shard for 4% max HP and 1 Might each, Exposed with none), Shatter Again (10 to all, a Shard), Collapse 30. **III. The Unmaking (50-25%):** opens with The Pull (2 Gravity and 1 Unmaking on you, 6% Barrier) and loops Prismatic Beam 17x2 with Unmaking (the Queen), Turn the Heart (the Stag's Barrier swap, then 22), Rupture (6 HP lost, 12, +1 Gravity (the Regent), 1 Mana less next turn (Mother of Static)), Refract (6%, +1 Might). **IV. The Last Light (below 25%):** opens with Prismatic Core (Shielded 2, 8% Barrier, Slippery 2) and loops Beam 17x2, Rupture (8 lost, 14), The Rift Closes (5% Barrier); every move adds Slippery (the Applause Below) and **curses you with Fate as a share of your max HP**: 10% on the Core, 8% on Beam and Rupture, 12% as the Rift Closes, about 38% in four turns and 60%+ in seven. That is its own rule and the finale's clock: a deck that cannot finish the last quarter in about five turns dies to Fate. **Pricing, by hand, as a run meets it (Tier XX: engine tier 1, 1000% HP, 438% damage x Warden's Fury = 499%, the Omens' Blood Rites +1 Might a turn; no sim):** 3,600 HP, ~1,300 of its own Barrier over the fight, the Ward (400, again each loop of the first quarter), Shards (210 each) it eats for 144 each, and 6-8 hits lost to Slippery and Shielded: ~6,000 to get through, ~14-17 turns at the 350-450 a turn a top late deck reaches, 30+ at 200 a turn. (The old Vorthax at Tier XX was 2,700 HP plus the two Omen rises, ~2,600 more Barrier: ~5,300 of plain HP and Barrier.) Its Might climbs to ~20-25 by the last quarter (Blood Rites, the Ward's Hum, Refract, Feed), so it hits ~100-130 a turn in the first quarter, ~200 in the second (Collapse 200 hidden behind the Veil), ~230 in the third (Turn the Heart ~190 that no Barrier stops) and Beams of ~390 in the last, under a Fate clock: the last 900 HP, ~470 Barrier and its Slippery hits fall in about five turns (~300 a turn) or Fate takes you. The deck output figures are estimates from the costing guide and the Tier IX ghost that killed 540 HP in three turns (#501), not measured; Robert should play it. Its rule is shown as the fight starts. **Engine:** `devour.healPct`, `UnitDef.leader`, a damage event's `fate` (the recap names Fate); an intent that curses you shows the debuff mark, and "of your max HP" / "of its max HP" say whose (Barrier and Fate as shares of max HP use the existing scale).

548. **Enemy Barrier and healing grow with the Rift Tier (Robert, 2026-11-02: "yea we should have their barrier and healing increase with each tier as well") ->** Found in #547: an enemy's HP and damage followed the tier table but its Barrier and healing were the written numbers, so at Tier XX (10x HP) a Warden's "Gain 12 Barrier" turn was a free turn. Now the Barrier and healing an enemy gives its own side (`barrier` and `heal` ops on itself or its friends, Gorge-style `devour` healing, and the Barrier from Armored and Plating) are multiplied by the same factor as its HP (`enemyGuardFactor(tier, hpPct)` = the engine's tier HP x the fight's HP percent), **never below the written number**, so Tier I (90%) plays as before. Roughly: Tier V 2.1x, Tier X 3.6x, Tier XV 6.8x, Tier XX 10x (a 12 Barrier move: 25, 43, 82, 120). Not grown: a share of max HP (`scale`, already in step: Vorthax's Barrier, Gorrmaw's healPct), the Omens' rise Barrier (already a percent), ghost Wardens (a hero's deck), and anything a hero or companion gains. Lifesteal follows damage, which already scales. The intent shows the grown number. **Effect:** every Warden and Elite with Barrier or heal moves gets tougher from about Tier V, most at Tier X+, where Robert wants runs to die (#520); the Shield-heavy ones (Molting Cathedral, Kron's Charge, the Benediction's halos, Nyxara's Web) most. Not re-priced fight by fight; a deep-tier run is the check. **The Ironclad Omen** already adds Armored stacks with the tier (3 at Tier XX), and each stack now grows too: about 30 Barrier a turn at Tier XX against a 3,600 HP Warden, kept on purpose (3 was nothing). Elite fights' extra HP from the Omens raises their factor with it, as it raises their HP.

549. **Two new map stops, the Rift Cache and the Echo; campaign ghosts are never one-shot (Robert, 2026-11-02: asked for ideas for more map stops, then "i like the echo and rift cache ideas, lets add those into the campaign"; "we need to make sure ghosts in campaign dont get one-shotted") ->** Every number here is Claude's: he picked the two ideas without numbers. **Placement:** each map gets one **Rift Cache** and, from Tier IV (`ECHO_FROM_TIER`, ghost Wardens' first tier), one **Echo**, each put on a plain fight on floors 3 to two before the last, never beside each other, after the Merchant and Event passes and before deep tiers turn fights into Elites (so they survive Tier XIX). One a map, not a roll, so a player can plan a route to it. Tier I maps change only in that one stop (the pinned map in eliteTiers.test differs by one letter). **The Cache** is a data-driven event (`cache.ts`, built on `make()`), so it uses the event screen. Open (60%): take the Boon, a rare card of your class (named on the option) or 75 shards. Trapped (`CACHE_TRAP_CHANCE` 40%): the Boon **and** a rare card together, for one of: spring the trap (15% of Max HP), reach past the wire (a Wound) or disarm it (60 shards). With every Boon owned, the Boon's place is the rare card and the stash. Walk away is always there. Its Boon is named with `boonOf`, not the event's `boon`, as an optional option's feasibility is checked without the event's Boon. **Priced by hand (BALANCE's ~100 shards a solid thing, merchant prices):** a Boon is 70 at a shop and a rare 95, a random one worth less (~70), so an open Cache is ~70-75, against the plain fight it replaces (15 shards, 40% potion, a card pick, HP lost). A trapped one is ~165 for a price of 60 shards, ~12 HP or a Wound (~50 to remove): ~100-115 net, the better one if you can pay. **The Echo** stands on a plain fight with `ghost: true`; it is settled with the Warden's ghost as the map opens (`settleGhost`), and with no ghost to be had (offline, none in range) it **becomes that plain fight** (on the map, in a save, and in `attachGhost` for a stop entered before the answer came). The server is never asked for a ghost already waiting on the map (`ghostsToSkip`: met ones plus settled ones), and one sent anyway is refused. Strength: 60% of a ghost Warden's HP (`ECHO_HP_PCT`), its damage without Warden's Fury. Pay: an Elite's shards, potion chance and breath, **no Boon**: its prize is **one of its own cards** (`echoOffers`): its rarest three (ties in a random order), sanctified and engraved copies as they were, never a basic card or token, a card your deck cannot take, or another class's card dead outside its owner (`deadElsewhere`); with none to give, an Elite's card reward. The Omen that takes a choice away does not touch these. Losing costs 2 Resolve, like an Elite (it is a stop you choose), and the fight shows the stakes as a Warden fight does. **Never one-shot:** a new generic status, **Enduring N**: the unit loses at most N% of its Max HP in any one turn, counted from either side's turn start (`Unit.lostTurn`, cleared then), on every HP loss through `applyDamage` (Doom/Fate kills included). A ghost Warden starts Enduring 25 (at least 4 turns) and an Echo Enduring 34 (at least 3), through `HeroSetup.statuses`. Arena ghosts are untouched. The Enduring picture is a copy of Fortified's until its own is painted; the Cache's map marker is the chest on `map-01` (cut by pack-icons as `map-cache`), and the Echo uses the ghost marker. **From the review:** the Cache's rare card is picked once and named on every option (each option had pinned its own); an Echo never offers a card and its "+" together (the upgraded copy is laid out); `attachGhost` refuses a ghost already on the map as `settleGhost` does; the skip list is the latest 25 (what one request carries), with the waiting ghosts last so they are never the ones cut. **Left as they are, flagged:** Enduring also counts what a ghost loses on its own turn (its own HP costs, Hangover), and Thorns or Traps on its turn get that turn's fresh share, so "at least 4 turns" counts both sides' turns; an Echo with no ghost is a plain fight even at Tier XIX-XX, where every other fight is an Elite (offline play mostly). Open for Robert: the percentages; whether an Echo should pay a Boon chance too; whether Enduring should reach the Arena's ghosts.

550. **Echoes may drop a Boon; #549's numbers kept; Enduring stays out of the Arena (Robert, 2026-11-02: "im ok with the numbers you used, i would like echos to have a chance at a boon. enduring should not apply to arena") ->** A won Echo rolls for a Boon by the Elite's chance at that tier (`eliteRewards(tier).boonPct`, 100% at Tier I fading to 10% by Tier XIX, DECISIONS #526), as an Elite does; its card reward is still one of its own cards. Enduring is set only by the campaign's fight plan for a ghost Warden or an Echo, so the Arena's ghosts never have it; nothing to change. With the Boon, an Echo pays a little more than an Elite (the rarest of a real deck's cards instead of an Elite's card pick), for the same 2 Resolve at risk and a full player deck as the enemy.

551. **Ghost Wardens take the Arena's HP from Tier XIII, not Tier X (Robert, 2026-11-02: a friend met a ghost with 1250 HP at Tier XI, "that might be a little high for tier 11. im ok with that a little later") ->** #527's bump (6x the Riftwalker's HP at Tier X's table, about 15% over the usual ghost curve, carried to every tier after) now starts at Tier XIII (`GHOST_ARENA_TIER` 13). It keeps its size: the 6x is still matched to Tier X's table (`GHOST_ARENA_AT`), so the step at Tier XIII is the same 15% up and never a fall. **By hand**, for a ghost whose run had 177 Max HP (the 1250 one): Tier XI 1251 -> 1084, Tier XII 1440 -> 1248, Tier XIII and on unchanged (1628 at XIII, 2950 at XX). A Knight at its own 80: Tier X 480 -> 416, Tier XI 565 -> 490. Echoes are 60% of these and move the same way. Arena ghosts are not touched.

552. **A Fanned Dagger never asks for a target, Inked Daggers and Recall the Blades included (Robert, 2026-11-02: "lets make sure all dagger cards dont need to be targeted if i have fanned... my inked daggers require targetting now, and the recall the blades as well") ->** Overrides #492's exception for the Inked Dagger. **Fanned now spreads everything a Dagger aims at one enemy, not only its damage:** an Inked Dagger's Faltering lands on every enemy. Recall the Blades (and any card whose only aimed effects play Daggers) offers one play with no aiming while you are Fanned, as its Daggers hit every enemy anyway; without Fanned it still asks, and its Daggers go at the chosen enemy. **Pricing, by hand:** Faltering on one enemy is 3, on all 4.5 (costing guide §4), so a Fanned Inked Dagger gains 1.5 with two or more enemies and nothing against one; small next to Fanned's own spread damage. Only Dagger and Inked Dagger carry the Dagger tag today. **Engine:** generic: a `'target'` selector on a Dagger-tagged card resolves to every living enemy while its player is Fanned; `legalActions` asks `aimsOnlyDaggers`. RULES_VERSION goes up at the next deploy.

553. **Campaign ghosts leave you Faltering instead of being Enduring (Robert, 2026-11-02: "lets remove enduring and just apply a number of faultering to players") ->** The Enduring status (#549) is gone from the engine (status, `lostTurn`, its tooltip and icon). In its place `duelPlan` starts you Faltering against a campaign ghost: `ECHO_FALTERING` 2 turns, `GHOST_FALTERING` 3, added to Dread's. Faltering wears off at the end of your own turn, so these are your first 2 or 3 turns at 25% less attack damage. **Claude's numbers, flagged:** Enduring held an Echo to at least 3 turns and a ghost Warden to 4, so the Faltering covers the turns a burst deck would have used to finish it (the last of those turns is free). **By hand:** a deck that puts out 120 attack damage a turn does 90 for its first turns; against a Tier V Echo of 182 HP (a 100 Max HP ghost) that is three turns where it was two, about what Enduring's 34% gave. It no longer stops a deck far above the curve from winning in one or two turns, and it does nothing to Traps, totems, Auras, Bleed or Burn, as they are not attacks. Arena ghosts are untouched, as #550 kept Enduring out of the Arena. RULES_VERSION must go up on the next deploy (the engine changed).

554. **Painted event scenes, a second arena for each realm, an arena for each Warden, and the Crossing for ghosts and PvP (Robert, 2026-11-02: "i want to enhance the display of our events to go beyond just a blank screen with text. slay the spire 2 is the inspiration"; "i also want more battle arena variety"; "i would like epic boss arenas, one unique one for each warden"; "i would like a rift-crossed themed battle arena for fighting ghosts or players") ->** Prompts in `docs/scene-prompts.md`; Robert had all 147 images made. **Events:** every event (95), the two Rift Caches, the merchant and the shrine have a scene (`public/art/events/<id>.jpg`, 1440 x 960, packed by `tools/pack-scenes.ps1`). An event carries its id as `scene` (an optional field of the run's event context, so an older save or an elite event simply has none and looks as before). On a wide screen the picture fills the screen and the event's panel stands in its quiet right part, as the scenes were painted; on a narrower one the panel sits over the picture, dimmed; the merchant and the shrine are dimmed behind their own layouts. **Arenas:** the Hollow Crown and the Midden get their backdrops and scenery, the Rift its scenery. Each realm gets a second site (castle ramparts, a skydock, a jungle canopy, a moonlit garden, a stilt village, a desert bazaar, a tavern cellar) and the Far Rifts (`beyond`) its own observatory, where they fought in the plain Rift before; a realm's regular fights and Elites are split between its two sites by the fight's seed, so a resumed or replayed fight keeps its place (Claude's call: half and half, with no rule tying a site to an encounter). Each of the 11 Wardens and Vorthax fight in their own arena (`BOSS_VENUE`, by encounter id). A ghost Warden, an Echo and every Arena match (and its replay and spectators) fight in **the Crossing**, a symmetric Rift-crossed colosseum; the Arena no longer picks one of the eight realm arenas by seed. Venue art is loaded only for the fight being fought (about 3 MB each, as before). Nothing about the rules changed.

555. **A beatable climb: enemies grow tankier faster than they grow harder-hitting, Barrier and healing at half HP's rate, and Elites pay a choice of spoils (Robert, 2026-11-02: "id like to make them increase and half the rate of the hp scaling. and the damage scaling of fights should be slightly slower. id also like to make the game actually beatable ... a regular fight shouldnt just one shot a full campaign character that has been taking max hp along the way"; "reward palyers with max hp or shards on elite fight wins, let them choose which one"; "id like to maek the game feel harder by making the enemies tankier, not always hitting a ton harder ... the last fiew tiers are extremely challenging but its a linear increase in difficulty up to that point") ->** **Measured first** (Claude's check, not a bot sim: every encounter's real fight plan, Omens and all, against a hero who only ends turns, so it is the worst case with no Barrier): the worst plain fight's opening turn dealt 90 at Tier X, 244 at Tier XV and 418 at Tier XX, and an Elite's up to 538, against a hero of roughly 200 to 350 Max HP there: from Tier XV a plain fight could kill a full-HP hero before they acted. Three causes: damage climbed to 438% (and the percent multiplies Might and every flat Omen bonus too), Hunted gave its first-hit bonus to every enemy (a swarm of six carried it six times), and Exposed Soul grew to three turns. **The tier table:** a straight line to Tier XVII, then the last three tiers steeper (`STEEP_FROM` 17, was 10). HP +40 a tier (was 30, then 64 past Tier X) and +90 over the last three, so Tier X is 450% (was 360), Tier XVII 730%, Tier XX still 1000%. Damage +9 a tier (was 12, then 24) and +20 over the last three (`DMG_STEEP`): Tier X 171% (was 198), Tier XVII 234%, Tier XX 294% (was 438). **Enemy Barrier and healing** (#548) grow at half the rate of HP: `enemyGuardFactor` = 1 + (HP factor - 1) / 2, so 5.5x at Tier XX (was 10x), 2.75x at Tier X. **Omens:** Hunted's first-hit bonus goes to the strongest enemy only (`leadStatuses`, a new slot); Exposed Soul stays at one turn (it grew one every four tiers). **After:** the worst plain fight's opening turn is 78 at Tier X, 119 at Tier XV and 194 at Tier XX; an Elite's 94, 175 and 268 (swarms and multi-hitters; at Tier XX every Elite stop is a Warden's); a Warden's 55, 82 and 141. Against an estimated Max HP of about 210, 340 and 530 for a hero who takes the new Max HP spoil about half the time, the worst opening turn stays near 35-40% of Max HP all the way up, so the climb is in how long enemies take to kill, not in one blow. **Elite spoils:** an Elite's (and an Echo's) shards are no longer paid outright; its reward page first offers one of three: those shards, **+2 Max HP** (`ELITE_SPOIL_MAX_HP`), or a **heal of 15% of Max HP** (`ELITE_SPOIL_HEAL_PCT`; Robert: players are "more open to taking max hp or shards, or even healing after tough fights"), then the card. Sized by count: a full run wins about 320 Elites (counted on generated maps: about 1 at Tier I, 14 at Tier X, 30 a tier by Tier XVIII), so +2 a time builds up to about +600 over a run if always taken, about +300 at half. A card chosen with the spoils untaken takes the shards (as an Elite paid before), so nothing that skips the page loses them. Elite healing up to Tier X (#526) is unchanged. Saves check the spoils' shards are no more than an Elite pays. **Not changed:** the Omens' phases and Might, Warden's Fury, Ritual; ghost Wardens and Echoes follow the new table through `ghostBossHp`/`ghostBossDmg` as before. Not priced deck by deck; a deep run is the check. RULES_VERSION goes up at the next deploy.
556. **Each tier one floor longer than the last, 30 at Tier XX (Robert, 2026-11-03: "id also like to make each tier only one node longer than the last. so tier 20 should end with 30 nodes on the map") ->** `floorsOf(tier)` = 9 + tier (was 9 + 2 x tier): Tier I is 10 floors and its Warden, 11 in all (was 12), Tier X 20 (was 30), Tier XX 30 (was 50). A full run is 410 floors, two thirds of the 620 it was, and wins about 190 Elites in place of about 320 (Elites are a share of the floors, and the deep-tier conversions scale with them). **The Elite Max HP spoil goes to +3** (was +2, #555) so the HP pool a hero can build over a run stays near the one #555 measured against (about +570 if always taken, was about +640). Merchant stretches, Shrines, Caches and Elite limits are per floor or per stretch and need no change; the pinned Tier I map was regenerated. Fewer fights also means fewer shards and card rewards over a run; left as it is until a deep run says otherwise.
557. **Wide Arc makes 2 Daggers (3 upgraded), was 4 (5) (Robert, 2026-11-03: "Wide Arc ... shouldnt give as many daggers, it should probably be 1 or 2 less") ->** Priced by the costing guide: a Fanned Dagger is a Buckshot Shell, 5.2 (4 to ALL); Fanned for the rest of the fight is about 12-15 in a Dagger deck. 4 Daggers came to about 21 + 13 = 34, past a 2-cost rare's 24-30; 2 come to about 23, and the upgrade's third Dagger is +5.2, the guide's +5-6 for a 2-cost card. Two less, not one, because Fanned itself is the card: the Daggers only start it off.
558. **Cinderfall costs 0 Mana and is paid with Spell Power alone (Robert, 2026-11-03: "make spellblades cinderfall cost 0 mana and just spend spell power") ->** Still spends half your Spell Power (X/2) for 4 damage + 4 per point spent. Priced: a 0-cost budget of 6 plus 4.5 per point spent, at about 4 spent (half of the ~8 she holds) = 24; it deals 20 there, 83%, inside a basic's 70-90% (it was 78% at 1 Mana). The upgrade drops from 7 + 4 to 6 + 4 (22, 92%): at 0 Mana the old +3 base would have been 96%. With no Spell Power it is a free 4 damage, below Quick Slash (0 Mana, 6); it makes no Mana and draws nothing, so it passes the loop test.
559. **The Spellblade's early game: Gather Power gains Barrier, and Kindled starts her with 4 Spell Power (Robert, 2026-11-03: "spellblade is a little weak early ... it just takes too much chip damage compared to other classes"; he proposed more starting Spell Power and took Claude's two smaller changes instead) ->** **Gather Power** "Gain 2 (+1 per 3 Spell Power) Barrier. Gain 2 Spell Power." (upgraded 3 Spell Power; the Barrier comes first, as on Obsidian Ward, so it reads what she held before the gain): it was her starter's one card that did nothing the turn it was played, priced at 6 of a 1-cost card's 10 (60%, a plain Strike's share, under a class signature basic's 70-90%). Its Barrier reads Spell Power as every Barrier of hers does (#486): 3 at the 5 she opens with, so about 9.6 (96%) on the early turns this is for, and 11.6 (116%) at the priced 8; over the band on purpose, as it is a basic played on the turns she is not hitting, and one a long run usually removes. **Kindled** starts her with 4 Spell Power (was 3; 5 on her first turn with Kindled's 1): +2 on every Obsidian Blade swing, about 15% on turn 1. Spell Power never drains, so +2 (Robert's first idea) was worth about +4 a swing for the whole fight, some 20-25 damage, and still mostly offence; her chip damage came from slow early turns, not a lack of Barrier cards (3 Defends and Obsidian Ward, like the others). Kept in reserve: her Max HP (75, against 80-82 for the Knight, Monk and Hunter).
560. **Every class's starting deck aimed at B or B+ (Robert, 2026-11-03: "My goal is for each class to be in the B range ... My preference is to see each class as B or B+"; from Claude's hand-priced audit of the ten starters and traits, where the Shaman was A+, the Knight A and the Monk A-, the Trickster and Rogue B-, the Spellblade C+ before #559) ->** **Shaman** starts with a Hawk and a Bear (Robert's call; the Thunderbird went): his starting row did about 9 damage and 2 Barrier every turn for nothing, far past any other trait; with two it is about 3 and 2 (some 6 points a turn), level with the Engineer's Drone and heal. The Thunderbird is still his to plant; **Thunder Memory** now plants one more than it counts ("Plant a Thunderbird, plus one for each Thunderbird you have planted this fight"), as the starting Thunderbird always made that count at least 1, and without it the card did nothing until one was planted. **Knight** starts with 1 Reserve (was 2): his turn 1 is 4 Mana, not 5 (-3.5), and the end-of-turn Barrier is unchanged. **Monk** Max HP 78 (was 82): his Serene-to-Fury opening (+2 Mana and 3 Primed) stays; he had the most HP of the non-Drunkards with three Defends. **Rogue** Max HP 75 (was 70): the lowest HP and the thinnest defence, taking the same chip damage as the Spellblade. **Trickster** starts with 3 Strikes and 3 Defends (was 4 and 2): the same points, more defence, as his Traps and Lying in Wait already supply damage. **Not changed, against the first suggestions:** Pick a Card (Foresee 2 already draws a card, so it is not a dead draw; a free extra draw would put a 0-cost basic at 125%), and Tune-Up (with the Drone's Might counted it is already at its price; the Engineer was already B). Expected ranks after: Knight, Monk, Drunkard, Necromancer, Shaman at B+; Engineer, Hunter, Trickster, Rogue, Spellblade at B. Not simulated; a few runs per class are the check.
561. **Under Blade Sweep the Obsidian Blade needs no target, and the Blade Sweep buff shows no number (Robert, 2026-11-03: "Blade Sweep should make the Obsidian Blade not have to target the enemy"; "why does the Blade Sweep buff say 1 at the end?") ->** The same rule as a Fanned Dagger (#552): while the hero has Blade Sweep, `legalActions` offers a blade-tagged card once, with nothing to choose, and everything it aims at one enemy lands on them all (its damage already did). Blade Sweep joins `ON_OR_OFF`: more stacks change nothing, so its chip and the log show no count. Blade Echo keeps its number, as each stack is another strike.
562. **A Warden's rule folds down to its name once read, and no longer sits over the intents (Robert, 2026-11-03: "the warden fights on mobile have the boss mechanics overlapping some elements like their next moves ... let the user minimize that notification after acknowledging it. It should remain as just the name of the mechanic so they can open it back up") ->** The rule (#528) was a whole sentence pinned under the title, below the Resolve line; on a phone it wrapped to three or four lines across the enemies' intents. Now what is at stake and the rule share one line under the title (`.fight__notes`), and the rule there is only its name (the words before its colon, "The Veil"), a button. As a fight starts the rule shows in full on a card with "Got it": under the title on a wide screen, in the middle of the field on a phone, never pinned to the intents. "Got it" (or the name) folds it; the name opens it again. A rule read once is folded from the start of later fights in the same session (sessionStorage, `sr.rulesRead`), so a retry does not ask again. The card is drawn at the page's top level (a portal), as the title is placed with a translate that would hold a fixed card inside it.

563. **Final Shatter applies 8 Fracture, not 10, and its Barrier rises to 12 (16 upgraded) (Robert, 2026-11-03: "it gives 10 shatter which is 100% going to cause a shatter but the text on it says 'if this causes a shatter' ... id like to make final shatter 8 fracture and balance the barrier with that in mind") ->** Every 10 Fracture Shatters, so 10 always crossed one and "If this caused a Shatter" was never false (only a dead or Steadfast target missed it). At 8 the target needs 2 already, so the Barrier is a payoff for setting it up. Priced against the old card: 2 less Fracture, and the Barrier, now about 70% of plays in a Fracture deck, doubled (6 to 12 + 1 per 3 Spell Power, about 14.7 at 8 held, x1.2 x0.7 = ~12, against ~10.4 always). The upgrade keeps 8 Fracture (more would make the condition near certain again) and raises the Barrier to 16. Rules tests that used it as a plain 10-Fracture card now use Rupture Script.

564. **Fracture cards apply more, and Fault Lines builds it from her other cards (Robert, 2026-11-03: "it doesnt have a lot of ways to build it up and it seems like the other fracture cards dont add enough fracture to be desirable to play over sword or spell cards") ->** A Shatter needs 10, about 2 Mana, and a common Fracture card put in 4-5 for 1 Mana with nothing that turn, so a Sword or a Spell was nearly always the better pick. Now: Faultline Hex 7 (9 upgraded, was 5/7), Pressure Point 6 (8, was 4/5), Spreading Fault 5 to all (7, was 4/5), Hairline Crack 5 (7, was 4/5), Fault Memory 5 and Grows 3 (7, was 4/6 and Grows 2), Hungering Void 3 a turn (4, was 2/3). Fracture is applied at about 7 a Mana on a common now (was 5, #499): still over its 3.1 a point on purpose, as it does nothing until it crosses a 10. **Fault Lines** (new uncommon Aura, 2 Mana, 1 upgraded): "Whenever you play a Sword or a Spell: Apply 2 Fracture to its target (or the enemy with the most Fracture)." About 2.5 Swords and Spells a turn in her decks, so 5 Fracture a turn, priced like Hungering Void+ (4 a turn to every marked enemy for 1 Mana) but with no mark needed; its Fracture cards are Spells, so Faultline Hex under it applies 9. The upgrade is a cost cut (it answers every Sword and Spell, so more a play would be too much). New engine pieces, both generic: a power's `orTag` (cards with either tag answer) and the selector `targetOrMostFracture` (the card's target while it stands, or else the living enemy with the most Fracture, for cards with no target such as Arcane Flood). Her pool is 89 reward cards (20 Auras, 41 uncommons). Art prompt: `docs/card-art/fault-lines.md`.

565. **Swords swing, Spells are cast, and a card that hits every enemy plays across all of them (Robert, 2026-11-03: "i feel like swords should have the sword animation and spells should have a spell animation. it would also be great to have AOE attacks/spells to have a different animation too") ->** The stage gave each hero one attack style, so every Spellblade attack, Lava Bomb included, was a swing of the Obsidian Blade. Now (`cardMotion` in `attackStyles.ts`, presentation only): a card tagged Spell from a hero who swings a blade is cast from the hand instead, an orb in the hero's spell colour (the Spellblade's ember), and its blows land as a spell's, not as a cut (heroes who already cast keep their staff or lantern). A card with damage to ALL enemies (anywhere in its effects), or a Dagger under Fanned or the Blade under Blade Sweep, plays across every enemy: a melee blow is one flat cut running through them all, nearest first, with a streak along the line; a spell flares in the hand and rains fire on each from above; a gun or a thrower sends a shot or a knife to each. A spell's blow now shows the pierce hit, not the slash, for every caster.

566. **The campaign leaderboard shows Class and Ascendancy, not Tiers cleared (Robert, 2026-11-03: "we dont need the Tier Cleared column on leaderboards anymore. so lets make it have a column for Class and Ascendency instead") ->** The runs board was Player, Character ("Paladin (Knight)"), Where they are, Tiers cleared, Status. Tiers cleared said again what "Where they are" already shows. Now it is Player, Class (the class icon and name), Ascendancy (its crest and name, or a dash before one is taken), Tier (just its number; "Where they are" read "Tier 4 · Cleared"), Status. Display only: the board still ranks by the same total, and the ghost board keeps its Character column.

567. **Every Rare card gets its own painted effect (Robert, 2026-11-03: "can you create some prompts for creating art work that we can use for animations for every card that is of the rare rarity. this way we can start working on different animations so they dont all look the same") ->** Each of the 286 Rares (268 class cards, 18 neutral, Warden signature cards among them) has a six-frame effect described in `docs/card-art/rare-fx.json`: `at` is where it plays (on its target, on the hero who played it, or over the whole enemy line) and `row` what the frames show, in its class's look and its realm's palette, each with its own main shape and motion. `pnpm fxprompts:rare` (tools/src/rarefx.ts) lays them out four to a sheet by class, in the frame-strip layout of `docs/animation-prompts.md` (magenta keyed background, frames lined up, no text), and writes `docs/rare-fx-prompts.md` (76 sheets) and `docs/card-art/rare-fx-sheets.json` (the row order, for cutting). A Rare with no description is listed there and fails the run. Sheets are saved as `assets-src/fx/rare/<sheet>.png`. Playing them: `tools/pack-icons.ps1` cuts every sheet that is in into `apps/web/public/art/fx/rare/<card id>.png` with an `index.json` of the cards that have one and where each plays; the fight reads it (`loadCardFx`) and plays a card's strip (620 ms, six frames) as an attack's blow lands or as anything else is played, on its target (or the first enemy it strikes), on the hero, or centred over the enemy line and sized to span it. An upgrade plays its base card's. The first sheet, `rare-knight-01` (Ashen Reckoning, Bastion Engine, Bloodfeast, Bloodied Plate), came in 2026-11-03 and is wired; the other 75 came in the same day, so all 286 Rares (76 sheets) have their own effect. Together they are 38 MB, so a fight fetches only the strips of the Rares in the player's piles as it starts (`preloadCardFx`); one made during the fight is fetched when first played. Traps and Auras that hit later play on the hero, as a thing being set up.
568. **Every Uncommon gets its own painted effect too (Robert, 2026-11-03: "we need prompts for all uncommon cards as well now") ->** The Rare system of #567, by rarity: the 371 Uncommons (362 class cards, 9 neutral) are described in `docs/card-art/uncommon-fx.json` by the same rules (where it plays, what the six frames show, a shape and motion of its own, different from the same class's Rares), and `pnpm fxprompts:uncommon` (the same tool, `tools/src/rarefx.ts uncommon`) writes `docs/uncommon-fx-prompts.md` (98 sheets: four to a sheet, a class's last sheet holds what is left) and `docs/card-art/uncommon-fx-sheets.json`. Sheets are saved as `assets-src/fx/uncommon/<sheet>.png`; `tools/pack-icons.ps1` cuts each rarity into its own folder (`apps/web/public/art/fx/<rarity>/`, with its own `index.json`), and the fight reads both indexes, one failing not stopping the other. A Common still plays the shared effects. The Knight's 10 sheets (40 cards) were the first in, 2026-11-03; the other 88 came in the same day, so all 371 Uncommons have their own effect (41 MB; a fight fetches only its deck's, as for the Rares).
569. **The Overclocker becomes the Swarmwright, the only class with a 4th companion slot (Robert, 2026-11-03: "lets change overclocker ascendency to add a 4th companion slot, the only class in the game with a 4th slot", "you can even rename the ascendency") ->** `Ascendancy.maxAllies` sets the class's companion slots (`HeroDef.maxAllies`, DECISIONS #315, which `allyCap` already reads), and the Engineer's third Ascendancy has 4: four Light machines, a Heavy and two Light, or a Colossal and one Light. Renamed the Swarmwright ("A fourth machine"), as the slot is now what it is about; an Overclocker in a saved run, a banked deck, a ghost or a board row reads as the Swarmwright (`RENAMED`), and it has its own crest (four brass machines in a diamond, `assets-src/fx/crest-swarmwright.png`, 2026-11-03), the Overclocker's gear no longer cut. **Call made:** its Primed goes from 2 to 1 for each companion a turn. It pays per companion, so with a fourth one 2 each would be 8 Primed a turn on top of the extra body, past the Foreman (a second Drone and 25%) and the Golemancer (3 max HP each a turn); at 1 it is 4 Primed with four machines, about what it gave before, and the slot is the new part. The frame tips no longer say "your 3 companion slots".
570. **A Graphics quality setting, High and Low (Robert, 2026-11-03: "i dont want to turn on graphics acceleration and i want the game to work smoothly", "lets just add that as as setting for graphics quality where a player can set it to Low ... and high which is the normal quality") ->** Measured first, on Robert's PC, whose Chrome draws without a graphics card (WebGL renderer "Microsoft Basic Render Driver"): a fight ran at about 48 fps (20.8 ms a frame) on a 72 Hz screen while a menu page ran at full speed. Stopping the board's drawing altogether, or hiding any layer of it or any part of the page, changed nothing; only nothing moving at all brought full speed. So the cost is not the drawing but the browser recomposing the whole 2560x1249 screen in software whenever anything moves, however small (an intent bobbing was enough), and the fight never stood still (fog, motes, pulsing cracks, breathing fighters, card shimmer, playable pulse). **Low** (`sr.graphics`, `GraphicsQuality` in `duel/effects.ts`, `EffectsProfile.low`): no fog drift, motes, crack pulse, shard bob, parallax, fighter breathing or pulsing target rings, render resolution at most 1, and the board is drawn only while a tween, a shake or a hit-stop runs, for a moment after a hover or a resize, and 4 times a second besides (`BattlefieldScene.draw`, in place of Pixi's own draw on its ticker); the looping CSS decorations stop on every screen (`html[data-graphics='low']` in global.css: card shimmer and foil, the playable pulse (its glow stays), intent bob, stance loops, the title haze, the home figure, the Arena glow, map ghosts, Boon rays), while the loops that tell you something stay (a blow coming at you, End turn, a pick to make, lethal Fate, a low clock, open map stops, the next suit, a totem to command) and an entrance (a Trap, a barrier bubble, the title's crack) still plays once. Cards, blows and effects play the same. **High** is the look as it was, and the default. Switched in a fight with the "Graphics: High/Low" button beside Tips and Sound, and it applies at once. Measured on Low on the same PC: the board at rest at full speed (13.9 ms) and a turn of play at 14.0 ms a frame typically (55% of frames at full speed; the rest are the moments something moves). **Calls made:** not chosen for the player automatically, though the game can tell a software renderer, as Robert asked for a setting; the target rings also hold still under reduced motion now.
571. **A Settings menu (Robert, 2026-11-03: "we need to add a settings menu and put things like the graphic setting in there and the audio (any regular game settings stuff belongs there)", "the settings menu should be available on the homescreen somewhere as well") ->** One panel (`ui/SettingsPanel.tsx`), mounted once at the app's root and opened by a Settings button on the title screen (beside the player chip), on the run's bar and in a fight's top bar (where it replaces the Tips, Sound and Graphics toggles). **Audio:** Sound on/off and the volume (0-100%, saved since the start but never shown before). **Graphics:** Quality High/Low (#570); Effects Full/Reduced (the old reduced-effects mode, saved but with no control until now: half the shake, no hit-stop freeze, fewer particles, a painted effect as one still picture); Fullscreen, where the browser allows it (and the choice shows what really happened if it refuses). **Gameplay:** Tips on/off, and Show every tip again (forgets the tips already read). Each preference keeps the storage it always had; a change is announced (`app/settings.ts`), so a fight in progress applies it at once (sound, tips, the board's profile). While it is open no key reaches the screen behind it (a fight's 1-9 and E), Escape or a click outside closes it, and focus goes back to the button that opened it. No backdrop blur, as it would be costly on the computers the Low setting is for. **Calls made:** the fight keeps no quick toggles; the Effects row is named for what it does rather than "reduced motion", which is the OS setting the game also honours.
572. **Fewer first-time stalls, and a performance report (Robert, 2026-11-03: "my friend said he still gets some hitching on low even after enabling gpu accelleration", "yea lets do the fixes plus the report") ->** A brief stutter on a fast computer usually comes from something done the first time it is needed, so: **card art** is painted ahead, one card at a time in idle moments as a fight opens, and compressed off the main thread (`canvas.toBlob`), where a card's first draw into the hand used to paint and encode it there and then (`prewarmCardArt`); **effect strips** (the shared ones, and the Rares' and Uncommons' of the deck) are decoded as well as fetched, and the newest 80 kept decoded (about 1 MB each), where the browser used to decode each on the frame it first played (`warmUp` in fxPlayer); **the battlefield's painted textures** (sky, shards, ruins, floor, fog, glows, vignette, a venue's skyline and an arena's tiled floor, which is built pixel by pixel) are painted once and kept between fights, where every fight painted them all as it opened (about 0.6 s on Robert's machine); each scene makes its own Pixi textures from them (`Texture.from(canvas, true)`), so a closing scene destroys only its own. Taken in with them, the other session's lighter effect strips (an effect's frames move by a transform in a clipping box, not by repainting a background; at most 12 at once, was 40), whose test is fixed. **The report** (Settings, Performance report): Start recording closes the menu and shows a Recording pill at the bottom left; Stop opens the menu with the report to copy: the computer's graphics renderer, browser, window, cores and settings, the frame times (typical, 95th and 99th percentile, counts over 33, 50 and 100 ms), the 20 longest stalls with the scripts behind each (from the browser's long-animation-frame timing) and what the game was doing then, and the game's notes (a fight opening and how long the battlefield took, every move with how long its rules, legal moves and saving took, every event as it is shown). It holds only timings and those details, and nothing is sent by itself. **Weighed and left for the report to decide** (a set of suggestions from ChatGPT that Robert passed on): a 30 fps cap on Low (pacing, not stalls), one flattened image of the scenery (measured: stopping the board's drawing changed nothing, and on a graphics card the layers cost little), fewer re-renders while a card resolves and a lighter "just played" card on Low (worth doing if the report shows them).
573. **A one-time Low suggestion, and the Settings menu on a phone (Robert, 2026-11-03, after two performance reports from his own PC: with hardware acceleration on, an RTX 4070, 6.9 ms a frame and 2 frames over 50 ms in 192 s, the only stalls as a fight opened and closed; with it off, "Microsoft Basic Render Driver", on High, 27.9 ms a frame and 236 over 50 ms in 119 s, none with any script behind it: "lets add the one time low suggestion", "the settings menu looks terrible on mobile") ->** The game asks WebGL for its renderer once (`duel/renderer.ts`, shared with the report, which now marks a software one) and takes Windows' Basic Render Driver, SwiftShader, llvmpipe, softpipe or WARP as drawing without the graphics card (a hidden name counts as a card). In the first fight that draws that way with Graphics on High, a note at the top says the game may stutter on this computer and offers Switch to Low or Keep High (and turning on hardware acceleration); either answer is remembered (`sr.lowHintSeen`) and it never shows again, and without storage to remember it, it is not shown at all. It does not switch by itself, as Robert asked for a setting. **The Settings menu on a phone:** every row is now the setting's name on the left and its control on the right, with the explanation on its own line under both (before, a name beside a long explanation was squeezed and the rows wrapped unevenly); under 40rem the panel is a full-screen sheet whose header, with Close, stays in view while the groups scroll, inside the phone's safe area; on a touch screen its buttons are at least 2.6rem tall.
574. **Approved campaign balance pass (Robert, 2026-11-03: "ok lest make these changes") ->** Implemented the reviewed specification in `BALANCE_PROPOSAL_2026-11-03.md`: Engineer Foreman becomes Artillerist (1 hero Might each turn; 3 Barrier after the first Attack on its turn), with legacy-id aliases for saves/decks/ghosts. Weapon Bench and Rotary Cannon support personal weapons; Jumpstart grants lasting companion Might, Ion Lance grows, Swarm Tactics reads successful summons, Bulwark Frame reads ally max HP, and Rebuild uses 75% HP. Knight gets Measured Conviction and stronger Smoldering Blow; Trickster gets cumulative Trap and Full Trick payoffs; Rogue gets common Side Slip and 2-Mana Slow Murder; Shaman gets cheaper Spirit Strike, Spirit Pact, Burn-scaled Heart of the Pyre, and non-replicating consumed Flame Echo tokens; Monk gets Might on its first actual own-turn stance entry and Foretold Flame reads cumulative Fervor; Drunkard gets Morning After and bounded Drunken Master growth; Necromancer gets growing Restless Spirits, Requiem draw and linear Gallows growth. Hunter and Spellblade numbers stay unchanged. **Generic rules:** successful non-starting ally-spawn and sprung-Trap counters, cumulative hero Fervor, a tagged consumed-card metric excluding the triggering play, own-turn trigger filtering, an actual Hangover-payment trigger, explicit ordered-command completion, and per-effect revive HP percentages (legacy default still rounds half HP up). Ordinary play triggers stay after card effects: Weapon Bench and Drunken Master do not boost the attack that triggered them. Masquerade's combined Full Trick damage is one hit. No card ids were special-cased in combat. **Scope:** no fixed card groups or reward categories; Echo encounter/ghost selection, enemy scaling and economy unchanged. New cards temporarily reuse related existing art, and Artillerist the Foreman crest. Scenario tests and normal project checks validate implementation; no balance simulations or campaign playtests. A-tier grades remain targets, not measured results. Hand prices, late-fight exceptions and ceiling checks are in the specification and costing guide.
575. **Linear campaign Echo and ghost-Warden scaling (Robert, 2026-11-03: "revise teh echo ghost scaling so they are competitive fights but not unbeatable. i want a linear growth of difficulty") ->** Campaign ghost HP now uses class base HP x (2.2 + 0.2 per tier after IV), plus earned Max HP as an additive bonus capped at 50% of class base. Saved HP is no longer multiplied by tier. Damage starts at 100% at IV and adds 3 percentage points per tier, reaching 148% at XX. Removed the Arena-HP switch at XIII and Warden's Fury from ghost decks, so the ghost curve never inherits the normal enemy table's final acceleration. Echoes keep 60% of Warden HP. Removed the extra two/three opening Faltering turns; normal campaign Omens still apply. Campaign ghost selection samples all eligible nearby decks rather than the upper half by wins, and its server query considers recent records rather than the highest-win pool. Eligibility, tier bounds, own/stale/repeated exclusions, rewards, Resolve costs, map odds and Arena rules stay unchanged. Legacy stages divide one tier increment into thirds. Hand examples and rationale are in BALANCE.md; deterministic tests cover all classes/tiers, saved-HP caps, no XIII/XVIII jump, late multiplier exclusion and matchmaking. No balance simulations or campaign playtests; a linear stats curve does not guarantee every random deck matchup is equally difficult. Version 0.176.0.

576. **Linear campaign scaling with bounded damage pressure (Robert, 2026-11-03: gradually tougher tiers, extremely challenging but beatable XVII-XX, especially enemy damage) ->** Scripted enemy HP is 90 + 40 points per tier after I; damage is 90 + 5 points, reaching 850%/185% at XX. Removed late slope acceleration; legacy stages add 5 HP and 1 damage point. Removed Elite HP/damage bumps, V/X/XV phase hardening, Warden's Fury, Swarming adds and opening Exposed Soul. Second Wind grants 15% Barrier +0.5/tier from II (24% at XX), fixed 2 Might; ordinary leader phases 5% +0.5/tier from IV; Third Phase 10% +0.5/tier from XIII and 1 Might; XX add phases grant 10% Barrier and no Might. Blood Rites grants fixed 1 starting Might instead of accumulating Ritual. Hunted is fixed 1 Primed, Ironclad 1 Armored, Faltering/Brittle 1 opening turn, Static 1 card, Scars 1 Wound/tier capped at six in deck. Authored enemy mechanics and buffs, encounter lineups, map lengths, economy and rewards remain. Independent campaign ghost curves and Arena remain; ghosts share the lighter ordinary Omens. Hand damage budgets and all-tier deterministic tests document implementation, without sims or playtesting. Stats are linear, actual difficulty depends on deck and encounter; beatable endgame remains a playtest target. Version 0.177.0.
577. **First Windows Electron wrapper (Robert, 2026-11-03: "lets start with adding the electron wrapper") ->** Add apps/desktop using Electron 45.2.2 and electron-builder 26.18.0, Windows x64 NSIS and unpacked targets. The same client builds separately in desktop mode to apps/web/dist-desktop; the existing web output/deployment is unchanged. A privileged secure local srgame://app protocol serves only bundled assets with path confinement and strict CSP. The desktop entry loads Pixi's no-dynamic-evaluation helpers. Renderer Node access is disabled, sandbox/context isolation remain on, navigation stays in the game, and HTTPS links open externally. A fixed AppData/ShatteredRifts profile keeps existing localStorage campaign saves, Vault codes and settings across versions; window-v1.json keeps bounded window size/fullscreen. No preload or filesystem API is exposed. Native save-file backend/backups and Steam Cloud remain future work; browser saves are separate and existing export/import can transfer runs. Desktop campaign NameGate offers immediate local play without claiming a name; online identity requirements remain unchanged. Google cookie login is not offered in desktop; Vault codes remain usable. Server allows the exact local desktop origin alongside existing web origins. No Steam SDK, account linking, achievements, controller support, paid-content differences or economics change. Root commands build/run/package/smoke the wrapper; the smoke test blocks all network requests and uses an isolated profile, starts a campaign, renders a fight, quits, and verifies the same campaign after a new process starts. Version 0.178.0. Installer is an unsigned prototype, not a Steam submission.
578. **Steam integration, backed-up desktop saves and visible Quit (Robert, 2026-11-04; no Steam app ID yet) ->** Steamworks.js 0.4.0 runs only in the main process. Build-time SR_STEAM_APP_ID writes public out/steam-config.json; absent ID gives standalone mode, invalid/Spacewar IDs fail configuration. Configured Steam initialization identifies the current player before choosing an isolated profile; failure never silently opens another user's saves. Native tickets use identity shattered-rifts and POST to the existing backend. The server validates against its own STEAM_APP_ID and private STEAM_WEB_API_KEY via Valve, hashes the verified Steam ID, and issues the signed Steam provider session. Bearer auth remains in main memory and is attached only to the trusted game's backend routes; a named sandboxed preload bridge exposes no tokens, arbitrary paths or Steam methods. Existing Google cookies and Vault codes remain supported; cross-provider linking is not automatic. Persona names pass existing claim/uniqueness rules; collisions still prompt for another name. Eighteen persisted/retryable campaign achievements: first battle win, Ascendancy, clearing I/V/X/XV/XX, Tier I for each class, all classes. Imported valid campaign progress can backfill these; no reward/economy change. Canonical version-1 checksummed snapshots at AppData/ShatteredRifts/saves/<owner>/save-v1.json include allowed campaign/duel/history/Vault/preferences data, with an atomically rotated previous revision. Steam user IDs isolate account files/profiles; standalone keeps existing profile and migrates localStorage on first save. Machine graphics, window geometry, claimed names, auth and server config are excluded from Cloud. Startup restores canonical files before React; malformed/future/wrong-account data is preserved and blocks overwriting. Quit to Desktop in Settings saves first; native closing snapshots too, and a write failure keeps the game open unless the player explicitly exits unsaved. Steam Auto-Cloud is the chosen syncing mechanism: WinAppDataRoaming, ShatteredRifts/saves/{64BitSteamID}, save-v1*.json, Cross-Platform, 64 MiB/4 files, no recursive scan. Steam handles launch/exit synchronization and user conflict selection. Robert has no App ID; partner setup, achievement definitions/icons, publisher key and actual Steam login/achievement/two-machine Cloud tests remain external prerequisites. No live Steam claim is made from mocks or local restore tests. Version 0.179.0.
579. **One active gameplay device per account, with isolated local offline play (Robert, 2026-11-04) ->** Robert wants other devices to log in/browse while one device has claimed Campaign, but forbids entering either Campaign or Arena elsewhere. Robert explicitly chose internet-required shared-account play with separate local offline saves. The existing Vault Durable Object holds a server-clock 45-second account lease, refreshed every 10 seconds, with unguessable bearer proof and monotonically increasing generations/revisions. No forced takeover while active; home has Save and stop playing, crashes/disconnects expire, and clients pause on failed renewal or deadline expiry. Current signed-in identity wins over a submitted guest Vault code for play ownership; matching-code guests share the same boundary. Authenticated writes cannot target another code's Vault to bypass their account claim. Campaign/duel checkpoints and replay logs are stored together under compare-and-set revision in a SQLite transaction; server timestamps replace client-clock arbitration. On claim, confirmed runs/replays/decks/history replace stale copies; divergent local run texts remain in sr.runConflictCopies. First migration can adopt old local runs only for an empty, never-saved identity. Read-only run/deck/board/history access remains available; destructive run/deck/history actions acquire ownership. Arena admission and every socket gameplay message validate the current claim, including hibernated connections; queue/seat identity comes from the verified account rather than a client-chosen player ID. Watching remains read-only and does not claim gameplay. Existing replay/combat RULES_VERSION remains unchanged because combat rules did not change. Desktop local mode is srgame://local, separate Chromium origin and local-saves/<owner> directory, no backend access, shared progression, Arena, Steam Auto-Cloud matching or local-run Steam unlocks; online account mode remains srgame://app with its existing saves/Cloud path. Explicit mode switches save first. Browser shared play now requires connectivity; future mobile wrappers use the same ownership API. Different provider accounts are not automatically one person: Steam/mobile/Google account linking remains required before claiming cross-provider identity. No Steam app ID exists yet. Functional tests cover competing claims, stale revisions, expiry, transactional validation, old Arena sockets, reconnect identity and isolated offline restore; a local Worker protocol probe performs no battles or balance simulations. Version 0.180.0.
580. **Distinct late-game Ascendancies (Robert, 2026-11-04: approved the identity proposal; defer card pools) ->** Redesign twelve choices around Reserve expenditure, retaliation, large-machine health investment, deliberate marks, prepared execution, standing-row cultivation, elemental development, controlled Fury, recurring Radiant windows, Spell Power recovery, Fermentation and low-health last stand. Retain the other eighteen identities. Exact values and hand budgets in docs/ASCENDANCY_PASS_2026-11-04.md; all growth resets per fight, and bounded triggers/multipliers prevent same-turn farming. Generic data-driven discipline ops run in the deterministic combat engine for Campaign, Arena and ghosts; combat chips show progress, previews/intents include bonuses, and pending choices serialize prepared damage. RULES_VERSION 75 fences old Arena clients/replays; campaign/ghost logs now stamp rules and older unfinished fights restart their encounter checkpoint. Existing run/Ascendancy ids remain readable. No card-pool or encounter scaling changes; no balance simulations. A-tier parity remains a playtest acceptance target. Version 0.181.0.

581. **Soulreaper: Harvest of Souls (Robert, 2026-11-04: approved the proposed offensive Consume payoff) ->** Replace 4 Barrier/Consume with 3. Count genuine consumption events per side for the fight. Once at the end of an own turn with consumption, after Fleeting hand cleanup and companion actions, Reap the lowest-current-HP living enemy for 4 + 2 per card consumed this fight; board order breaks ties. Ordinary non-attack damage is absorbed by Barrier and ignores attack multipliers; no healing, random targeting, extra hits or overkill spill. Base Grave Chill Fate reward remains. Progress persists through save restoration, resets per fight and is visible in combat chips. Generic harvest discipline and turnSettled hook, no card/class ids in engine. Rules 76, version 0.182.0; unfinished old-rule fights restart their encounter checkpoint. No card-pool or other Ascendancy changes. Exact hand budgets and tests in docs/ASCENDANCY_PASS_2026-11-04.md; A-tier remains a playtest target, no balance simulations.

582. **Desktop automatic updates and public downloads (Robert, 2026-11-04) ->** Add electron-updater 6.8.11 to the existing Windows NSIS wrapper, with the public rnimmo13/shattered-rifts GitHub Releases feed. Fixed Shattered-Rifts-Setup.exe filename supports a stable website download link. Check after startup and every six hours, download in background, install on normal quit after save protection, or explicit Settings Restart to update after renderer flush, shared-claim release and a final main-process snapshot. Native input freezes during that snapshot; save failure prevents installation and restores input. Settings/home display progress/readiness; Steam-configured, dev and store/non-Windows modes are excluded. Published metadata/checksum validation and clean source stamping bind installers to pushed source; draft-first publication prevents incomplete releases, and published versions cannot be overwritten. Future version work must publish desktop artifacts, not only deploy the website. Prior <=0.182.0 apps require one bootstrap install. Unsigned test-build status remains; HTTPS/SHA-512 do not replace publisher signing. Actual isolated NSIS two-version test verifies corrupt-download rejection, update install and save-marker preservation. No combat/card changes; rules remain 76. Version 0.183.0. See apps/desktop/UPDATES.md.

583. **Update prompts outside battles and cleaner account/settings layout (Robert, 2026-11-04) ->** Emit an uncached version.json with each web build and poll every minute/focus; offer Save and refresh without deployment-triggered reloads. Native background checks every five minutes; downloaded patches offer Save and restart. A shared mounted-FightView guard covers Campaign, Arena, sandbox and results/series transitions; hide prompts and reject explicit update actions until every fight view unmounts, rechecking after async save/release. Later dismisses that version for the session; Settings retains controls. One installed version plus expandable build details and update controls in Settings > About and updates. Home Account left, Settings right; remove Friends alpha/working title and footer version duplication. Desktop save-mode switch moves into Settings and is disabled during battles. Mobile profile stays within viewport, settings and update actions use 44px touch controls. Version 0.184.0, rules 76 unchanged; no balance changes or simulations.

584. **Use the supplied Shattered Rifts logo and integrate Credits (Robert, 2026-11-04) ->** Generate aspect-preserving transparent PNGs (32/180/192/512) and seven-size ICOs from assets-src/icons/shattered-rifts-logo.png with pnpm brand:assets. Source artwork remains ignored; commit derivatives. Use browser/favicon and mobile home-screen icons, home title, download button, Electron window and Windows executable/installer/uninstaller. Enable Windows executable resource editing while keeping signing disabled with signExecutable=false. Browser-mode manifest supplies mobile icons without adding offline/PWA gameplay behavior. Move Credits into a Settings About navigation row beside Patch notes; remove isolated home footer, retain existing eight-tile navigation. Version 0.185.0; rules 76 unchanged.

585. **Home download beside Settings (Robert, 2026-11-04) ->** Move the Windows installer link from the main menu into the home header action group, immediately before Settings. Account remains left. At phone widths use Download text and a Settings gear with accessible label/title, 44px controls and truncated long account names. Preserve fixed public installer URL, logo and native-app hiding. Version 0.186.0; rules 76 unchanged.

586. **Desktop Google sign-in, persistent sessions and explicit future Steam linking (Robert, 2026-11-04) ->** Use the existing confidential web Google OAuth client in the system browser. The native app supplies a SHA-256 verifier challenge and polls a ten-minute, one-use broker attempt. Browser confirmation uses its own short-lived HttpOnly cookie/form secret, never changing the website session. Identity DO binding/migration v4 holds attempt records, revocable thirty-day game sessions (proof hashes only), and permanent Steam-to-Google aliases. Main process alone stores/attaches native credentials, encrypted with safeStorage outside Cloud/save paths. Google profiles have separate account-saves/google-hash files; switching flushes/releases/freezes/snapshots and restores the selected profile, refusing battles and concurrent changes. Sign-out deletes the local credential and revokes online; expired sessions keep their profile and offer reconnect. Local offline origin remains separate. Configured Steam editions can prove Steam plus Google ownership and explicitly keep Google progression; never merge existing Steam progress. A never-played empty Steam source Vault is sealed before alias creation to fence in-flight requests; old Steam tokens resolve the canonical Google account on every request. No unlink UI; conflicts refuse with progress intact. No Steam app ID exists; automated fixtures do not claim live Valve or real-user Google consent testing. Tests include real Windows encryption, two native processes, separate account/guest saves, battle guard, handoff replay/expiry/revocation, browser-cookie isolation and source Vault fencing. Version 0.187.0, rules 76 unchanged.


587. **Baseline lore: separate worlds, the shattered Ward (Robert, 2026-11-04: "shattered made it sound like the rifts were fine on their own but then the barriers between them were shattred and opened the door to all of this fighting. i would like to have a base-line lore that explains what is happening in the game"; keeps the title Shattered Rifts) ->** `docs/15_LORE.md` is the lore baseline. It replaces the old premise (one Prime World broken by the Sundering) with separate worlds sealed by the Ward, each kept by a Warden; the Ward shattered, Rifts opened, the Heart of the Rift (Vorthax) took the Wardens (matching "the rules of the Wardens it made"), and Riftwalkers fight Wardens and each other for Ward shards, the only thing that can seal a world. Echoes are what the Rift keeps of past Riftwalkers; the Arena is the Crossing. What Vorthax is and what broke the Ward are left open, with the hint that the fighting feeds it. Player text uses class names only. Lore page plan (`#/lore`) and art prompts (`docs/lore-prompts.md`, 26 images) written; no code yet. No dedicated loading screens: the key art is reused for the startup splash, Rift Tier transitions and Steam. Why: Robert's framing gives each class a home to fight for and explains PvP; the Midden having no Warden gets a reason (nothing there is worth guarding).

588. **Illustrated lore, default models, useful startup and repaired desktop approval (Robert, 2026-11-04) ->** Implement the lore baseline (#587) as six story chapters and eight world cards at #/lore, linked from Home and Learn, with chapter navigation and a keyboard/touch artwork viewer. Generate the full 26-image brief with built-in imagegen; reference only the current default assets-src/heroes/<class>.png models, not cosmetic skins or obsolete _source portraits. Raw PNGs stay in assets-src/lore (ignored); commit responsive WebP derivatives and prompt/hash/provenance manifest, recording concept status pending Robert's art review. Keep the Heart and the cause of the Shattering unexplained. Reuse clean key art for static startup HTML/CSS and authentication backdrop, show actual settled hero-asset requests and continue immediately when ready; permit slow-art continuation after four seconds, retain existing bounded waits and placeholder recovery. Lore art stays lazy, not a startup preload. Native save-bootstrap failures retain blocking Retry/Quit controls. No tier-transition interstitials or Steam store uploads yet. Fix browser OAuth confirmation: no-referrer makes Chromium form navigations send Origin:null, so use same-origin referrer policy instead; retain strict origin checking and script-free CSP. Style approval/success/cancel/error pages and return HTML for denied browser confirmation origins. Automated Chromium submits the actual form against the actual Worker/SQLite DO; only Google is simulated. Responsive startup/lore checks cover missing/slow art, ten default portraits, viewer keys and low-motion mode. Version 0.188.0, rules 76 unchanged; no card/combat changes or balance simulations.

589. **Visible Quit and aligned Settings (Robert, 2026-11-04) ->** Put Quit to Desktop in a separate row in the sticky Settings header, below the title and Close control. This keeps one consistent exit location available from every game screen and during scrolling. Retain Close as the initial keyboard focus, use a distinct subdued exit treatment and preserve the existing save-before-quit operation; show failed-save errors in the visible header, never quit after a failed flush. Keep Quit native-only. Rename Desktop settings group to Account and saves; share SettingsRow between preferences and native Google/Steam status/actions, removing the account component's inherited centered PvP layout. Give account popups their own grid; align update actions, allow long status/name wrapping and stack row labels/hints/actions on narrow screens. Review guest, Google connected/expired, long Steam names and linked states with isolated browser fixtures at 320/390/640/844/1440 widths; exercise failed/successful save-and-quit and retain real native account/save smoke coverage. Version 0.189.0; rules 76 and combat unchanged.

590. **Remove the rigid blue Settings header box (Robert, 2026-11-04) ->** Remove the header's solid dark-blue background, shadow and mobile bottom border. Keep the title and top actions on the panel's continuous background. Put preferences in a bounded inner scroll area and leave the transparent header outside it; Close and Quit remain visible without underlying content bleeding through. Retain the outer panel styling, aligned account rows and existing save/quit behavior. Version 0.190.0, rules 76 unchanged.

591. **Engineer opening help, Swarmwright growth and continuous Auras (Robert, 2026-11-04) ->** Improve the opening with an 8-base-HP, 4-damage starting Drone (was 6/3), replace one Strike with existing Pulse Pistol in new ten-card starters, and raise Micro Drone damage 2 to 3. Other machine stats, hero HP/healing/Mana and saved decks stay unchanged. 596. **A cause for the Shattering and approved release (Robert, 2026-11-04: "push and deploy. but we also need to have a reason for why the Ward broke in the lore") ->** Establish that the Hollow Crown's fallen star was exhausting its power. Its Warden drew from her section of the Ward to rekindle it and save her people, knowingly risking her wall but unaware of the other worlds. The connected barrier redistributed the strain until its finite strength could no longer hold any section. The star burned again and the Ward shattered; only afterward did Vorthax take the Wardens, including the guardian now called the Queen of the Unmade. This protective choice anticipates the Riftwalkers' conflict over finite shards. Update docs/15_LORE.md and the existing illustrated chapter; retain all artwork/default class models. Supersedes #587-588 only where they kept the cause unexplained. Vorthax's nature, possible foreknowledge and the hinted benefit from fighting remain mysteries. Included in v0.195.0/rules79 with #595; Robert explicitly authorizes pushing, deploying and publishing this release. Historical balance assessments retain their original local-state labels; no additional combat changes or simulations.

597. **Vorthax caused a partial Shattering (Robert, 2026-11-04: "the lore update about the ward breaking needs to be something to do with vorthax and him being responsible for a cataclysmc event that shattered a portion of the ward") ->** Supersede #596's dying-star/Warden explanation. Vorthax deliberately gathered power against a junction of the Ward and unleashed a cataclysmic blast to force entry into the sealed worlds. A vast section shattered; distant sections remain intact. The affected worlds' Wardens tried to hold the breaches and were taken by Vorthax afterward. Its responsibility is explicit canon; origins, true nature, ultimate purpose and the hinted benefit from fighting remain open. Update the lore baseline, illustrated story, intro and current release notes, retaining existing art/default models. The 0.195.0 source had already been pushed when Robert clarified; stop its desktop package before publication and release the corrected source as 0.196.0. Rules79 and every balance number/assessment are unchanged from #595; preserve those snapshots and record the release mapping in the history index. Continue the already approved push/deployment with this correction.

598. **Vorthax the conqueror (Robert, 2026-11-04: "ok thats good lets go with that") ->** Adopt the proposed tyrant backstory: the strongest being his realm had ever produced, Vorthax conquered its kingdoms and rival powers over centuries. Experiments revealed other worlds; desire for their power and refusal to accept limits drove him to spend years and sacrifice cities forcing his own boundary open. After escaping he attacked a junction around neighboring worlds, causing the partial Shattering already established in #597. He discovered that taking their Wardens gave him access to their worlds' laws, explaining his combined boss rules. His goal is further conquest and control. Riftwalkers can challenge him through unfamiliar powers and combinations from worlds he has not mastered. Preserve distinct worlds, intact distant Ward sections, guardians corrupted after the attack, current class models/art and the existing combat rules. His home realm's name, exact nature and original power source remain open; do not assign an existing homeland. Supersede the prior unresolved-motive framing and reject the unadopted dying-realm survival proposal. Update docs/15_LORE.md, illustrated story (short prose introduction before the Shattering panels), future art guidance and patch notes. Local v0.197.0/rules79; approval is implementation only, with no new push/deploy/publish authorization. No balance changes or new assessment grades.
